Manfred was pacing the little room, his hands behind him, a thick Egyptian cigarette between his lips.

===Chinese===
曼弗雷德正在小房间里踱步，双手负在身后，唇间衔着一支粗大的埃及香烟。

===Original===
"There's a train leaves Paddington for Gloucester at ten forty-five," he said. "Will you telegraph to Miss Goddard, Heavytree Farm, and ask her to meet the train with a cab? After that I shall want two men to patrol the vicinity of the farm day and night."

===Chinese===
「十点四十五分有一班火车从帕丁顿（Paddington）开往格洛斯特。」他说。「你拍个电报给赫维特里农场的戈达德小姐，请她雇辆车到车站去接，好不好？此后我还要两个人，日夜巡逻农场四周。」

===Original===
Poiccart pulled open a drawer of the desk, took out a small book and ran his finger down the index.

"I can get this service in Gloucester," he said. "Gordon, Williams, Thompson and Elfred—they're reliable people and have worked for us before."

===Chinese===
波伊卡特拉开书桌的抽屉，取出一本小册子，手指顺着索引划下去。

「这路人事我在格洛斯特办得来。」他说。「戈登、威廉斯、汤普森和埃尔弗雷德（Gordon, Williams, Thompson and Elfred）——都是可靠的人，从前也替咱们办过事。」

===Original===
Manfred nodded.

"Send them the usual instructions by letter. I wonder who will be in charge of this Barberton case. If it's Meadows, I can work with him. On the other hand, if it's Arbuthnot, we shall have to get our information by subterranean methods."

===Chinese===
曼弗雷德点头。

「给他们去信，交待例行指示。不知这桩巴伯顿案子会归谁主办。若是米多斯，我还合得来。反之，若是阿巴思诺特（Arbuthnot），咱们就得走地下的路子摸消息了。」

===Original===
"Call Elver," suggested Leon, and George pulled the telephone towards him.

It was some time before he could get into touch with Dr. Elver, and then he learnt, to his relief, that the redoubtable Inspector Meadows had complete charge.

===Chinese===
「打给埃尔弗。」莱昂提议，乔治把电话拉到面前。

过了一阵子他才接通埃尔弗医生，随即欣慰地得知，那位令人敬畏的米多斯探长已全权主办此案。

===Original===
"He's coming up to see you," said Elver. "As a matter of fact, the chief was here when I arrived at the Yard, and he particularly asked Meadows to consult with you. There's going to be an awful kick at the Home Secretary's office about this murder. We had practically assured the Home Office that there would be no repetition of the mysterious deaths and that the snake had gone dead for good."

===Chinese===
「他这就过来看你。」埃尔弗说。「不瞒你说，我到苏格兰场的时候长官正在那儿，他特意吩咐米多斯来同你们商议。这桩命案准会在内政大臣的官署里掀起一场大风波。我们实际上已经向内政部打过包票：那类神秘死亡不会再重演，『毒蛇』已经永远消停了。」

===Original===
Manfred asked a few questions and then hung up.

"They are worried about the public—you never know what masses will do in given circumstances. But you can gamble that the English mass does the same thing—Governments hate intelligent crowds. This may cost the Home Secretary his job, poor soul! And he's doing his best."

===Chinese===
曼弗雷德又问了几句，便挂上了听筒。

「他们发愁的是公众——人群在特定情形下会做出什么，你永远摸不准。不过有一点只管下注：英国的民众干的也是同一套——政府恨的就是有头脑的人群。这一来内政大臣的位子怕要砸了，可怜的人！他可是尽了力了。」

===Original===
A strident shout in the street made him turn his head with a smile.

"The late editions have got it—naturally. It might have been committed on their doorstep."

===Chinese===
街上一声刺耳的叫卖，引得他含笑回头。

「晚版报纸已经登出来了——自然。这案子简直就像是在他们自家门口犯下的。」

===Original===
"But why?" asked Poiccart. "What was Barberton's offence?"

"His first offence," said Leon promptly, without waiting for Manfred to reply, "was to go in search of Miss Mirabelle Leicester. His second and greatest was to consult with us. He was a dead man when he left the house."

===Chinese===
「可这是为什么？」波伊卡特问。「巴伯顿犯了什么过？」

「他的头一桩过，」莱昂不等曼弗雷德开口，脱口便答，「是去寻访米拉贝尔·莱斯特小姐。第二桩、也是最重的一桩，是来找我们商量。他走出这幢房子的时候，就已经是个死人了。」

===Original===
The faint sound of a bell ringing sent Poiccart down to the hall to admit an unobtrusive, middle-aged man, who might have been anything but what he was: one of the cleverest trackers of criminals that Scotland Yard had known in thirty years. A sandy-haired, thin-faced man, who wore pince-nez and looked like an actor, he had been a visitor to Curzon Street before, and now received a warm welcome. With little preliminary he came to the object of his call, and Manfred told him briefly what had happened, and the gist of his conversation with Barberton.

===Chinese===
一阵隐约的门铃声把波伊卡特引下楼去门厅开门，放进一位毫不惹眼的中年人——此人怎么看都像别的行当里的人，唯独不像他的老本行：他是苏格兰场三十年来所知最精明的罪犯追踪者之一。他头发沙色，脸庞瘦削，戴一副夹鼻眼镜，模样像个演员。他从前也来过柯曾街，这回受到了热忱的欢迎。没几句寒暄，他便道明来意，曼弗雷德把事情经过、以及他同巴伯顿那次谈话的要点，对他简略说了。

===Original===
"Miss Mirabelle Leicester is—" began Manfred.

"Employed by Oberzohn—I know," was the surprising reply. "She came up to London this morning and took a job as laboratory assistant. I had no idea that Oberzohn & Smitts had a laboratory on the premises."

===Chinese===
「米拉贝尔·莱斯特小姐——」曼弗雷德刚一开口。

「受雇于欧伯佐恩——我知道。」对方的回答出人意料。「她今早进的城，谋了份实验室助理的差事。我原先全然不知欧伯佐恩-斯米茨商行里竟有个实验室。」

===Original===
"They hadn't until a couple of days ago," interrupted Leon. "The laboratory was staged especially for her."

Meadows nodded, then turned to Manfred. "He didn't give you any idea at all why he wanted to meet Miss Leicester?"

===Chinese===
「两天前还没有呢。」莱昂插话。「那实验室是特意为她搭的台。」

米多斯点点头，转向曼弗雷德。「他丝毫没有向你们透露，为什么要见莱斯特小姐？」

===Original===
George shook his head.

"No, he was very mysterious indeed on that subject," he said.

===Chinese===
乔治摇头。

「没有，在那一点上，他神秘得很。」他说。

===Original===
"He arrived by the *Benguella*, eh?" said Meadows, making a note. "We ought to get something from the ship before they pay off their stewards. If a man isn't communicative on board ship, he'll never talk at all! And we may find something in his belongings. Would you like to come along, Manfred?"

===Chinese===
「他是搭『本格拉号』（*Benguella*）来的，嗯？」米多斯一面做笔录一面说。「咱们该赶在船上结清工钱、遣散侍应之前，从那条船上弄到点东西。一个人在船上要是嘴紧，那他这辈子就不会开口了！他的行李里兴许也能翻出点什么。愿意同去一趟吗，曼弗雷德？」

===Original===
"I'll come with pleasure," said George gravely. "I may help you a little—you will not object to my making my own interpretation of what we see?"

===Chinese===
「乐于奉陪。」乔治正色道。「或许能帮你一点忙——我对自己所见的东西另作一番解读，你不会反对吧？」

===Original===
Meadows smiled.

"You will be allowed your private mystery," he said.

===Chinese===
米多斯微微一笑。

「你的私家秘密，准你留着。」他说。

===Original===
A taxi set them down at the Petworth Hotel in Norfolk Street, and they were immediately shown up to the room which the dead man had hired but had not as yet occupied. His trunk, still strapped and locked, stood on a small wooden trestle, his overcoat was hanging behind the door; in one corner of the room was a thick holdall, tightly strapped, and containing, as they subsequently discovered, a weather-stained mackintosh, two well-worn blankets and an air pillow, together with a collapsible canvas chair, also showing considerable signs of usage. This was the object of their preliminary search.

===Chinese===
一辆出租汽车把他们送到诺福克街的佩特沃思旅馆，两人当即被引上楼，进了死者租下却还没住过的那个房间。他那口衣箱还捆着皮带、上着锁，架在一只小木架上；大衣挂在门后；房间一角放着一只厚实的旅行提袋，捆得紧紧的，他们后来打开发现，里面是一件风雨剥蚀的胶布雨衣、两条用旧的毯子和一只充气枕，外加一把折叠帆布椅，也带着用旧的不少痕迹。他们的初步搜查，便从这几样东西做起。

===Original===
The lock of the trunk yielded to the third key which the detective tried. Beyond changes of linen and two suits, one of which was practically new and bore the tab of a store in St. Paul de Loanda, there was very little to enlighten them. They found an envelope full of papers, and sorted them out one by one on the bed. Barberton was evidently a careful man: he had preserved his hotel bills, writing on their backs brief but pungent comments about the accommodation he had enjoyed or suffered. There was an hotel in Lobuo which was full of vermin; there was one at Mossamedes of which he had written:

> "Rats ate one boot. Landlord made no allowance. Took three towels and pillow-slip."

===Chinese===
箱锁在探长试到第三把钥匙时应手而开。除了换洗的内衣和两套西服——其中一套几乎全新，还挂着圣保罗-德-罗安达（St. Paul de Loanda）一家铺子的字号签——能给他们启发的东西寥寥无几。他们找到一只塞满纸片的信封，把它们一一摊开在床上分拣。巴伯顿显然是个细心人：他把旅店的账单全都留着，在背面写上简短而尖刻的评语，评说他所享受或忍受的食宿。洛布奥（Lobuo）有一家旅店满是臭虫虱子；莫萨马德斯也有一家，他写道：

> 「老鼠啃掉一只靴子。店主分文不减。顺走三条毛巾、一只枕套。」

===Original===
"One of the Four Just Men in embryo," said Meadows dryly.

Manfred smiled.

===Chinese===
「四义士的雏形啊。」米多斯干巴巴地说。

曼弗雷德莞尔。

===Original===
On the back of one bill were closely written columns of figures: "126, 1315, 107, 1712, about 24," etc. Against a number of these figures the word "about" appeared, and Manfred observed that invariably this qualification marked one of the higher numbers. Against the 107 was a thick pencil mark.

===Chinese===
一张账单的背面密密地写着一栏栏数字：「126、1315、107、1712、约24」等等。其中若干个数字旁边带着「约」字，曼弗雷德注意到，凡有这个限定词的，无一例外都是较大的数字。107 那个数字旁，则画着一道浓浓的铅笔痕。

===Original===
There were amongst the papers several other receipts. In St. Paul he had bought a "pistol automatic of precision" and ammunition for the same. The "pistol automatic of precision" was not in the trunk.

===Chinese===
纸片中还有另外几张单据。他在圣保罗买过一支「精密自动手枪」及应配的弹药。那支「精密自动手枪」却不在箱子里。

===Original===
"We found it in his pocket," said Meadows briefly. "That fellow was expecting trouble, and was entitled to, if it is true that they tortured him at Mosamodes."

"Moss-**am**-o-dees," Manfred corrected the mispronunciation. It almost amounted to a fad in him that to hear a place miscalled gave him a little pain.

===Chinese===
「在他口袋里找到了。」米多斯简短地说。「那家伙早防着有麻烦，也怪不得他防——要是他们在莫萨莫德斯拷问过他的事属实。」

「莫萨**马**德斯。」曼弗雷德纠正他的误读。听人把地名念错他就隐隐不快，这在他几乎成了一桩怪癖。

===Original===
Meadows was reading a letter, turning the pages slowly.

"This is from his sister: she lives at Brightlingsea, and there's nothing in it except …" He read a portion of the letter aloud:

> "… thank you for the books. The children will appreciate them. It must have been like old times writing them—but I can understand how it helped pass the time. Mr. Lee came over and asked if I had heard from you. He is wonderful."

===Chinese===
米多斯正在读一封信，慢慢翻着页。

「这是他妹妹写来的：她住在布莱特灵西（Brightlingsea），信里没什么要紧的，只是……」他把信的一部分念出声来：

> 「……谢谢你的书。孩子们会喜欢的。写那些书的时候，想必恍如旧日时光吧——我也明白，这总好歹帮你打发了时日。李先生来过，问我是不是收到了你的信。他真是个了不起的人。」

===Original===
The letter was in an educated hand.

"He didn't strike me as a man who wrote books," said Meadows, and continued his search.

===Chinese===
这封信出自受过教养的手笔。

「我看他可不像个写书的人。」米多斯说着，继续搜检。

===Original===
Presently he unfolded a dilapidated map, evidently of Angola. It was rather on the small scale, so much so that it took in a portion of the Kalahari Desert in the south, and showed in the north the undulations of the rolling Congo.

===Chinese===
不多时，他摊开一张破旧的地图，一看便知是安哥拉的。比例尺颇小，小到南面竟收入了卡拉哈里沙漠（Kalahari）的一角，北面则显出滚滚刚果河波状起伏的河道。

===Original===
"No marks of any kind," said Meadows, carrying the chart to the window to examine it more carefully. "And that, I think, is about all—unless this is something."

"This" was wrapped in a piece of cloth, and was fastened to the bottom and the sides of the trunk by two improvised canvas straps. Meadows tried to pull it loose and whistled.

===Chinese===
「上面什么标记也没有。」米多斯说着，把地图拿到窗前细看。「我想，能找的也就这些了——除非这玩意儿算件东西。」

「这玩意儿」裹在一块布里，用两条临时做的帆布带绑在箱底与箱帮上。米多斯想把它拽下来，打了个呼哨。

===Original===
"Gold," he said. "Nothing else can weigh quite as heavily as this."

===Chinese===
「金子。」他说。「旁的东西，压手压不过它。」

===Original===
He lifted out the bundle eventually, unwrapped the covering, and gazed in amazement on the object that lay under his eyes. It was an African *bête*, a nude, squat idol, the figure of a native woman.

"Gold?" said Manfred incredulously, and tried to lift it with his finger and thumb. He took a firmer grip and examined the discovery closely.

===Chinese===
他终于把那捆东西托了出来，解开包裹，对着眼前的物件愕然凝视。那是一件非洲的「兽偶」（*bête*）——一尊赤身、蹲踞的偶像，塑的是一个土著女人的形象。

「金的？」曼弗雷德将信将疑，用拇指和食指去掂它，随即握实了，把这新发现凑近细看。

===Original===
There was no doubt that it was gold, and fine gold. His thumbnail made a deep scratch in the base of the statuette. He could see the marks where the knife of the inartistic sculptor had sliced and carved.

===Chinese===
毫无疑问是金子，而且是足金。他的拇指甲在雕像底座上划出一道深深的划痕。他看得出那位全无艺术细胞的雕刻匠刀削刀刻的痕迹。

===Original===
Meadows knew the coast fairly well: he had made many trips to Africa and had stopped off at various ports en route.

"I've never seen anything exactly like it before," he said, "and it isn't recent workmanship either. When you see this"—he pointed to a physical peculiarity of the figure—"you can bet that you've got something that's been made at least a couple of hundred years, and probably before then. The natives of West and Central Africa have not worn toe-rings, for example, since the days of the Caesars."

===Chinese===
米多斯对那段海岸颇为熟悉：他多次去过非洲，沿途在各港口停留过。

「跟它一模一样的东西，我从前从没见过，」他说，「而且这做工也不新。你看到这个」——他指着那偶像形体上的一处奇特之处——「就可以打赌：手里这东西少说已有两百年，多半还要更早。比方说，西非和中非的土人，自恺撒们的时代以后，就再没戴过脚趾环了。」

===Original===
He weighed the idol in his hand.

"Roughly ten pounds," he said. "In other words, eight hundred pounds' worth of gold."

===Chinese===
他把神像掂在手里。

「十磅上下。」他说。「换句话说，值八百镑的金子。」

===Original===
He was examining the cloth in which the idol had been wrapped, and uttered an exclamation.

"Look at this," he said.

Written on one corner, in indelible pencil, were the words:

> "Second shelf up left Gods lobby sixth."

===Chinese===
他正在检视包神像的那块布，忽然低呼一声。

「看这个。」他说。

布的一角用不褪色的铅笔写着一行字：

> 「上数第二层 左 神像 门厅 第六。」

===Original===
Suddenly Manfred remembered.

"Would you have this figure put on the scales right away?" he said. "I'm curious to know the exact weight."

===Chinese===
曼弗雷德猛然想起来了。

「能不能马上把这尊像放上秤？」他说。「我很想知道它的确切重量。」

===Original===
"Why?" asked Meadows in surprise, as he rang the bell.

===Chinese===
「为什么？」米多斯一边按铃，一边诧异地问。

===Original===
The proprietor himself, who was aware that a police search was in progress, answered the call, and, at the detective's request, hurried down to the kitchen and returned in a few minutes with a pair of scales, which he placed on the table. He was obviously curious to know the purpose for which they were intended, but Inspector Meadows did not enlighten him, standing pointedly by the door until the gentleman had gone.

===Chinese===
旅馆老板亲自来应铃——他知道警方正在进行搜查——应探长之请，赶忙下楼到厨房去，几分钟后拿来一杆秤，摆在桌上。他显然好奇这杆秤要派什么用场，米多斯探长却不肯点破，只自顾自守在门边，直到那位先生退出房去。

===Original===
The figure was taken from under the cloth where it had been hidden whilst the scales were being placed, and put in one shallow pan on the machine.

===Chinese===
秤在安放的当口，神像一直藏在布下，这时取了出来，放进秤上一个浅浅的秤盘。

===Original===
"Ten pounds seven ounces," nodded Manfred triumphantly. "I thought that was the one!"

"One what?" asked the puzzled Meadows.

===Chinese===
「十磅七盎司。」曼弗雷德得意地点头。「我就想准是这一件！」

「哪一件？」米多斯一头雾水地问。

===Original===
"Look at this list."

Manfred found the hotel bill with the rows of figures and pointed to the one which had a black cross against it.

===Chinese===
「看这张单子。」

曼弗雷德找出那张写满数字的旅店账单，指着带黑叉的那一个。

===Original===
"107," he said. "That is our little fellow, and the explanation is fairly plain. Barberton found some treasure-house filled with these statues. He took away the lightest. Look at the figures! He weighed them with a spring balance, one of those which register up to 21 lbs. Above that he had to guess—he puts 'about 24,' 'about 22.'"

===Chinese===
「107。」他说。「咱们这位小家伙就是它，道理也相当明白。巴伯顿找到了一座堆满这类雕像的宝库。他带走的是最轻的一尊。瞧这些数字！他是用弹簧秤称的——就是那种最多称到 21 磅的秤。再往上他就只好估——于是写『约24』『约22』。」

===Original===
Meadows looked at his companion blankly, but Manfred was not deceived. That clever brain of the detective was working.

"Not for robbery—the trunk is untouched. They did not even burn his feet to find the idol or the treasure-house: they must have known nothing of that. It was easy to rob him—or, if they knew of his gold idol, they considered it too small loot to bother with."

===Chinese===
米多斯茫然地望着同伴，曼弗雷德却不上这副表情的当。探长那颗聪明的脑袋正在运转。

「不是谋财——箱子原封没动。他们甚至没有烧他的脚来逼问神像或宝库的下落：想必那些事他们一无所知。要抢他本是轻而易举——又或者，他们纵然知道他有这尊金像，也嫌这点赃太小，犯不着费手脚。」

===Original===
He looked slowly round the apartment. On the mantelshelf was a slip of brown paper like a pipe-spill. He picked it up, looked at both sides, and, finding the paper blank, put it back where he had found it. Manfred took it down and absently drew the strip between his sensitive fingertips.

===Chinese===
他缓缓环视这间屋子。壁炉架上有一小条棕色纸，像一枝点烟斗用的纸捻。他拿起来两面看了看，见纸上空白，便放回原处。曼弗雷德却把它取下来，心不在焉地把那纸条在敏感的指尖之间捻过。

===Original===
"The thing to do," said Meadows, taking one final look round, "is to find Miss Leicester."

Manfred nodded.

"That is one of the things," he said slowly. "The other, of course, is to find Johnny."

===Chinese===
「眼下要办的，」米多斯说着，最后环视一周，「是找到莱斯特小姐。」

曼弗雷德点头。

「那是其一。」他慢条斯理地说。「其二嘛，自然是找到约翰尼（Johnny）。」

===Original===
"Johnny?" Meadows frowned suspiciously. "Who is Johnny?" he asked.

===Chinese===
「约翰尼？」米多斯狐疑地皱起眉。「约翰尼是谁？」他问。

===Original===
"Johnny is my private mystery." George Manfred was smiling. "You promised me that I might have one!"

===Chinese===
「约翰尼是我的私家秘密。」乔治·曼弗雷德笑吟吟的。「你答应过，准我留一个的！」

## VI In Chester Square / 六 切斯特广场
===Original===
When Mirabelle Leicester went to Chester Square, her emotions were a curious discord of wonder, curiosity and embarrassment. The latter was founded on the extraordinary effusiveness of her companion, who had suddenly, and with no justification, assumed the position of dearest friend and lifelong acquaintance. Mirabelle thought the girl was an actress: a profession in which sudden and violent friendships are not of rare occurrence. She wondered why Aunt Alma had not made an effort to come to town, and wondered more that she had known of Alma’s friendship with the Newtons. That the elder woman had her secrets was true, but there was no reason why she should have refrained from speaking of a family who were close enough friends to be asked to chaperon her in town.

===Chinese===
米拉贝尔·莱斯特（Mirabelle Leicester）来到切斯特广场（Chester Square）时，心头是一团奇异的乱麻，惊异、好奇与窘迫绞在一处。那窘迫，来自女伴异乎寻常的热络：对方毫无来由，忽然就摆出了至交密友、总角故人的架势。米拉贝尔心想，这姑娘准是个演员——干那一行的，这种突如其来、烈火烹油似的交情本不算稀罕。她纳闷阿尔玛姨妈（Aunt Alma）怎么没设法进城一趟；更纳闷的是，她竟一直不知道阿尔玛同牛顿家有交情。这位长辈自有她的秘密，这不假；可要是两家亲厚到能托人家在城里照应她，那就实在没有只字不提的道理了。

===Original===
She had time for thought, for Joan Newton chattered away all the time, and if she asked a question, she either did not wait for approval, or the question was answered to her own satisfaction before it was put.

Chester Square, that dignified patch of Belgravia, is an imposing quarter. The big house into which the girl was admitted by a footman had that air of luxurious comfort which would have appealed to a character less responsive to refinement than Mirabelle Leicester’s. She was ushered into a big drawing-room which ran from the front to the back of the house, and did not terminate even there, for a large, cool conservatory, bright with flowers, extended a considerable distance.

===Chinese===
她倒有的是工夫想心事，因为琼·牛顿（Joan Newton）一路喋喋不休；偶或问上一句，要么不等人家答话，要么问题还没出口，她自己心里先有了中意的答案。

切斯特广场是贝尔格莱维亚（Belgravia）区内一片端庄体面的地界，气象森严。姑娘由一名穿号衣的男仆迎进那座大宅；宅中一派奢华安逸的情调，便是雅趣远不及米拉贝尔·莱斯特的人见了，也要动心。她被引进一间大客厅，客厅从宅子前部一直贯到后部，到了尽头还不算完——一座宽大荫凉的花房从那里再往外伸展出去老远，满室繁花，灼灼照眼。

===Original===
“Monty isn’t back from the City yet,” Joan rattled on. “My dear! He’s awfully busy just now, what with stocks and shares and things like that.”

She spoke as though “stocks and shares and things like that” were phenomena which had come into existence the day before yesterday for the occupation of Monty Newton.

“Is there a boom?” asked Mirabelle with a smile, and the term seemed to puzzle the girl.

“Ye-es, I suppose there is. You know what the Stock Exchange is, my dear? Everybody connected with it is wealthy beyond the dreams of avarice. The money they make is simply wicked! And they can give a girl an awfully good time—theatres, parties, dresses, pearls—why, Monty would think nothing of giving a string of pearls to a girl if he took a liking to her!”

===Chinese===
「蒙蒂（Monty）还没从伦敦城回来呢。」琼呱啦呱啦地讲个不停。「哎呀亲爱的！他这阵子忙得要死，又是股票又是证券，尽是那一类事儿。」

她那口气，仿佛「股票证券那一类事儿」是前天才冒出来的新鲜玩意儿，专为供蒙蒂·牛顿一人消遣营生。

「如今行情正火吧？」米拉贝尔含笑问了一句，这个字眼似乎把那姑娘难住了。

「呃——是吧，我想正火着呢。亲爱的，你知道证券交易所是怎么回事吧？凡跟那上头沾边的人，个个富得叫贪心人做梦都想不到。他们挣的那些钱，简直造孽！再说他们能让姑娘过得痛痛快快——看戏呀、赴宴呀、衣裳呀、珍珠呀——嗐，蒙蒂要是瞧上了哪个姑娘，送她一串珍珠连眼都不眨！」

===Original===
In truth Joan was walking on very uncertain ground. Her instructions had been simple and to the point. “Get her to Chester Gardens, make friends with her, and don’t mention the fact that I know Oberzohn.” What was the object of bringing Mirabelle Leicester to the house, what was behind this move of Monty’s, she did not know. She was merely playing for safety, baiting the ground, as it were, with her talk of good times and vast riches, in case that was required of her. For she, no less than many of her friends, entertained a wholesome dread of Monty Newton’s disapproval, which usually took a definitely unpleasant shape.

===Chinese===
其实琼脚下的地皮虚得很。她领到的吩咐简单直截：「把人带到切斯特花园（Chester Gardens），同她交上朋友，别提我认识欧伯佐恩（Oberzohn）这一茬。」把米拉贝尔·莱斯特接到宅子里图的是什么，蒙蒂这一步棋背后又是什么，她一概不知。她不过是在稳中求全，拿快活日子和大把钱财的话头，先在地里撒下一把饵，以防上面真用得着这一手。因为她同她的许多朋友一样，对招蒙蒂·牛顿的不快怀着一份额外十二分的忌惮——而他那不快一旦发作，多半摆出一副明明白白叫人吃不消的架势。

===Original===
Mirabelle was laughing softly.

“I didn’t know that stockbrokers were so rich,” she said dryly, “and I can assure you that some of them aren’t!”

She passed tactfully over the gaucherie of the pearls that Monty would give to any girl who took his fancy. By this time she had placed Joan: knew something of her upbringing, guessed pretty well the extent of her intelligence, and marvelled a little that a man of the unknown Mr. Newton’s position should have allowed his sister to come through the world without the benefit of a reasonably good education.

===Chinese===
米拉贝尔只是暗暗好笑。

「我不知道证券经纪人竟这么有钱，」她干巴巴地说，「而且我可以向您担保，这里头就有不那么有钱的！」

至于蒙蒂瞧上谁就送谁珍珠那份失检，她乖觉地一带而过。到这时，她已把琼掂量出个大概：摸到几分这姑娘的家教来历，把她的智力深浅猜了个八九不离十，只是有点纳罕——那位素未谋面、身份又那样的牛顿先生，怎么竟容他妹妹闯荡人世，连一顿说得过去的好教育都没沾过。

===Original===
“Come up to your room, my dear,” said Joan. “We’ve got a perfectly topping little suite for you, and I’m sure you’ll be comfortable. It’s at the front of the house, and if you can get used to the milkmen yowling about the streets before they’re aired, you’ll have a perfectly topping time.”

When Mirabelle inspected the apartment she was enchanted. It fulfilled Joan’s vague description. Here was luxury beyond her wildest dreams. She admired the silver bed and the thick blue carpet, the silken panelled walls, the exquisite fittings, and stood in rapture before the entrance of a little bathroom, with its silver and glass, its shaded lights and marble walls.

===Chinese===
「上楼到你房间去吧，亲爱的。」琼说。「我们给你备了一套顶呱呱的小房间，保你住得舒舒服服。就在宅子临街的一面，只要你受得了送奶工趁街上还没透进晨气就在那儿嚎叫，保准过得顶呱呱。」

米拉贝尔进房一打量，就看直了眼。琼那番含含糊糊的形容，在这儿样样坐实。这份奢华，远出她平生最大胆的想象。银色大床、厚厚的蓝地毯、蒙绸的护壁墙板、件件精巧的陈设，她一样样看不够，末了立在那间小浴室门口，更叫她心醉神驰——银具与玻璃交映，灯影柔柔，四壁大理石。

===Original===
“I’ll have a cup of tea sent up to you, my dear. You’ll want to rest after your horrible day at that perfectly terrible factory, and I wonder you can stand Oberzohn, though they tell me he’s quite a nice man. …”

She seemed anxious to go, and Mirabelle was no less desirous of being alone.

===Chinese===
「我叫人给你送杯茶上来，亲爱的。你在那家糟透了的厂子里熬过了可怕的一天，也该歇歇了。真不知你怎么受得了欧伯佐恩——虽说人家告诉我，他这人还挺不错。……

她一副急着脱身的样子，米拉贝尔也正巴不得独处。

===Original===
“Come down when you feel like it,” said Joan at parting, and ran down the stairs, reaching the hall in time to meet Mr. Newton, who was handing his hat and gloves to his valet.

“Well, is she here?”

“She’s here all right,” said Joan, who was not at all embarrassed by the presence of the footman. “Monty, isn’t she a bit of a fool? She couldn’t say boo to a goose. What is the general scheme?”

He was brushing his hair delicately in the mirror above the hall-stand.

===Chinese===
「想下来了就下来。」琼临走时说，随即一路奔下楼，赶到门厅，正撞上牛顿先生——他正把帽子和手套递给贴身男仆。

「怎么样，她人到了吧？」

「人到了，没错儿。」琼说，那男仆在场，她一点儿也不觉得拘谨。「蒙蒂，她是不是有点儿傻？她呀，见了鹅都不敢嘘一声。整个盘子到底是怎么个章程？」

他正对着衣帽架上方那面镜子，细细地刷着头。

===Original===
“What’s what scheme?” he asked, after the servant had gone, as he strolled into the drawing-room before her.

“Bringing her here—is she sitting into a game?”

“Don’t be stupid,” said Monty without heat, as he dropped wearily to a low divan and drew a silken cushion behind him. “Nor inquisitive,” he added. “You haven’t scared her, have you?”

“I like that!” she said indignantly.

===Chinese===
等仆人退了下去，他一面先她一步踱进客厅，一面问：「什么章程不章程的？」

「把她带到这里来——是要她坐进哪一局牌里去吗？」

「别犯傻。」蒙蒂说，声气不动，一面乏乏地陷进一张矮长沙发，往身后拢了个绸靠垫。「也别穷打听。」他又补上一句。「你没把她吓着吧？」

「亏你说得出口！」她愤愤然道。

===Original===
She was one of those ladies who speak more volubly and with the most assurance when there is a mirror in view, and she had her eyes fixed upon herself all the time she was talking, patting a strand of hair here and there, twisting her head this way and that to get a better effect, and never once looking at the man until he drew attention to himself.

===Chinese===
她属于那种只要望得见一面镜子、说起话来便格外滔滔、格外有把握的女士。讲话的当儿，她两眼始终定在自己身上，这儿抿抿一绺头发，那儿歪歪脑袋找角度，自始至终没朝那男人看过一眼，直到他出声惹人注意，才算正眼给了他一瞥。

===Original===
“Scared! I’ll bet she’s never been to such a beautiful house in her life! What is she, Monty? A typist or something? I don’t understand her.”

“She’s a lady,” said Monty offensively. “That’s the type that’ll always seem like a foreign language to you.”

She lifted one shoulder delicately.

===Chinese===
「吓着！我敢打赌，她这辈子就没进过这么漂亮的宅子！她到底是干什么的，蒙蒂？打字员那一类的？我摸不透她。」

「她是位淑女。」蒙蒂的话说得呛人。「这一路人，往后在你眼里永远像一门外国语。」

她轻轻耸起一边肩膀。

===Original===
“I don’t pretend to be a lady, and what I am, you’ve made me,” she said, and the reproach was mechanical. He had heard it before, not only from her but from others similarly placed. “I don’t think it’s very kind to throw my education up in my face, considering the money I’ve made for you.”

“And for yourself.” He yawned. “Get me some tea.”

“You might say ‘please’ now and again,” she said resentfully, and he smiled as he took up the evening paper, paying her no more attention, until she had rung the bell with a vicious jerk and the silver tray came in and was deposited on a table near him.

“Where are you going tonight?”

===Chinese===
「我从不冒充淑女，我如今这副样子，还不是你一手造就的。」她说，这句怨责念得像走熟的过场。这一套他早听惯了，不止出自她一人之口，凡处在这般地位的女人，个个都会说。「想想我替你挣了多少钱，你还拿我的教养当面寒碜我，我看这可算不得厚道。」

「也替你自己挣的。」他打了个哈欠。「给我弄点茶来。」

「你偶尔也该说一声『请』。」她怨忿忿地说。他笑了笑，拿起晚报，再不分她半点心思，直到她狠命一拽铃绳，银托盘送进来，搁到他近旁的桌上，才算完。

「今晚你要上哪儿去？」

===Original===
His interest in her movements was unusual, and she was flattered.

“You know very well, Monty, where I’m going tonight,” she said reproachfully. “You promised to take me too. I think you’d look wonderful as a Crusader—one of them—those old knights in armour.”

He nodded, but not to her comment.

“I remember, of course—the Arts Ball.”

His surprise was so well simulated that she was deceived.

===Chinese===
他难得过问她的行踪，她不由得受宠若惊。

「我今晚去哪儿，你心里明镜似的，蒙蒂。」她嗔怪道。「你答应过也带我去的。我看你扮成十字军骑士准定神气极了——就扮那种——那种披挂盔甲的老派骑士。」

他点了点头，却不是应她这句话。

「我自然记得——艺术家舞会（Arts Ball）。」

他那副惊讶装得如此到家，竟把她瞒了过去。

===Original===
“Fancy your forgetting! I’m going as Cinderella, and Minnie Gray is going as a pierrette—”

“Minnie Gray isn’t going as anything,” said Monty, sipping his tea. “I’ve already telephoned to her to say that the engagement is off. Miss Leicester is going with you.”

“But, Monty—” protested the girl.

“Don’t ‘but Monty’ me,” he ordered. “I’m telling you! Go up and see this girl, and put it to her that you’ve got a spare ticket for the dance.”

===Chinese===
「想不到你会忘了个干净！我扮灰姑娘，明妮·格雷（Minnie Gray）扮皮埃罗特（女丑角）——」

「明妮·格雷什么都不扮了。」蒙蒂呷着茶说。「我已经打电话告诉她，那个约会作罢了。莱斯特小姐同你一道去。」

「可是，蒙蒂——」姑娘抗议道。

「少拿『可是蒙蒂』来跟我犟。」他吩咐道。「我这是在交代你！上楼去见那姑娘，就说你手里多出一张舞会票。」

===Original===
“But her costume, Monty! The girl hasn’t got a fancy dress. And Minnie—”

“Forget Minnie, will you? Mirabelle Leicester is going to the Arts Ball tonight.” He tapped the tray before him to emphasize every word. “You have a ticket to spare, and you simply can’t go alone because I have a very important business engagement and your friend has failed you. Her dress will be here in a few minutes: it is a bright green domino with a bright-red hood.”

“How perfectly hideous!” She forgot for the moment her disappointment in this outrage. “Bright green! Nobody has a complexion to stand that!”

Yet he ignored her.

===Chinese===
「可她那一身行头呢，蒙蒂！那姑娘没有化装舞会服。再说明妮——」

「把明妮忘了吧，行不行？米拉贝尔·莱斯特今晚就要去艺术家舞会。」他敲着面前的托盘，一字一顿。「你手里正好多一张票，而你万万不能独自赴会，因为我有一桩顶要紧的商务应酬，你的朋友又放了你的鸽子。她的舞服几分钟内就送到：一件鲜绿色的多米诺斗篷（domino），配一顶鲜红的兜帽。」

「丑得没天理了！」这一气，她一时间连自己那份失望都忘了。「鲜绿色！谁的肤色也架不住那种颜色！」

他却全然不理会她。

===Original===
“You will explain to Miss Leicester that the dress came from a friend who, through illness or any cause you like to invent, is unable to go to the dance—she’ll jump at the chance. It is one of the events of the year and tickets are selling at a premium.”

She asked him what that meant, and he explained patiently.

“Maybe she’ll want to spend a quiet evening—have one of those headaches,” he went on. “If that is so, you can tell her that I’ve got a party coming to the house tonight, and they will be a little noisy. Did she want to know anything about me?”

===Chinese===
「你去对莱斯特小姐解释，说这身衣裳是一位朋友送的，她因病——或者随便你编个什么缘故——去不成舞会了——她准会一口抢下这个机会。这是全年数得着的一桩盛事，票价都炒到市面价以上了。」

她问他这话是什么意思，他便耐心地解释了一番。

「说不定她想清清静静地过一晚——犯一犯那种头痛。」他接着往下说。「若是那样，你就告诉她，我今晚请了一班客人到宅子里来，要闹腾一阵子。她有没有想打听我的什么？」

===Original===
“No, she didn’t,” snapped Joan promptly. “She didn’t want to know about anything. I couldn’t get her to talk. She’s like a dumb oyster.”

Mirabelle was sitting by the window, looking down into the square, when there was a gentle tap at the door and Joan came in.

“I’ve got wonderful news for you,” she said.

“For me?” said Mirabelle in surprise.

===Chinese===
「没有，才没有呢。」琼抢白得飞快。「她什么也不想打听。我撬不开她的话匣子。她活像只哑巴牡蛎。」

米拉贝尔正坐在窗前，俯望楼下的广场，忽听得门上轻轻一叩，琼走了进来。

「我给你带来了天大的好消息。」她说。

「给我的？」米拉贝尔愕然道。

===Original===
Joan ran across the room, giving what she deemed to be a surprisingly lifelike representation of a young thing full of innocent joy.

“I’ve got an extra ticket for the Arts Ball tonight. They’re selling at a—they’re very expensive. Aren’t you a lucky girl!”

“I?” said Mirabelle in surprise. “Why am I the lucky girl?”

Joan rose from the bed and drew back from her reproachfully.

===Chinese===
琼一路奔过房间来，顺带演了一出她自以为逼真得惊人的好戏——一个满怀天真欢喜的小东西。

「我多出一张今晚艺术家舞会的票。票价都卖到——呃，反正贵得很。你多走运呀！」

「我？」米拉贝尔惊异地说。「我走的是什么运？」

琼从床沿站起，嗔怪似的往后退开一步。

===Original===
“You surely will come with me? If you don’t, I shan’t be able to go at all. Lady Mary and I were going together, and now she’s sick!”

Mirabelle opened her eyes wider.

“But I can’t go, surely. It is a fancy dress ball, isn’t it? I read something about it in the papers. And I’m awfully tired tonight.”

===Chinese===
「你总肯同我一道去的吧？你若不去，我干脆就去不成了。本来约好同玛丽小姐（Lady Mary）一道去的，谁知她病倒了！」

米拉贝尔把眼睛睁得更大了。

「可我去不了呀，真的。那是一场化装舞会，对不对？我在报上读到过一些。再说今晚我累得不行。」

===Original===
Joan pouted prettily.

“My dear, if you lay down for an hour you’d be fit. Besides, you couldn’t sleep here early tonight: Monty’s having one of his men parties, and they’re a noisy lot of people—though thoroughly respectable,” she added hastily.

Poor Joan had a mission outside her usual range.

===Chinese===
琼娇俏地撅起了嘴。

「亲爱的，躺上一个钟头，你就缓过来了。再说，你今晚在这儿也早睡不成：蒙蒂要办他那种清一色男客的聚会，那班人闹腾得很——不过倒是规规矩矩的正经人。」她赶忙补了一句。

可怜的琼接下了一桩超出她惯常路数的差使。

===Original===
“I’d love to go,”—Mirabelle was anxious not to be a killjoy—“if I could get a dress.”

“I’ve got one,” said the girl promptly, and ran out of the room.

She returned very quickly, and threw the domino on the bed.

“It’s not pretty to look at, but it’s got this advantage, that you can wear almost anything underneath.”

===Chinese===
「我是乐意去的，」——米拉贝尔一心只怕扫了人家的兴——「只要弄得到一身舞服。」

「我这就有一件。」姑娘应声便答，奔出了房间。

她去去便回，把那件多米诺斗篷往床上一扔。

「模样是不好看，可它有这么一样好处：里头差不多穿什么都成。」

===Original===
“What time does the ball start?” Mirabelle, examining her mind, found that she was not averse to going; she was very human, and a fancy dress ball would be a new experience.

“Ten o’clock,” said Joan. “We can have dinner before Monty’s friends arrive. You’d like to see Monty, wouldn’t you? He’s downstairs—such a gentleman, my dear!”

The girl could have laughed.

===Chinese===
「舞会几点开场？」米拉贝尔在心里掂量了一下，发觉自己并不怎么排斥去；她到底也是个凡人，化装舞会于她是一桩新鲜的阅历。

「十点。」琼说。「咱们可以赶在蒙蒂的朋友到之前先吃晚饭。你想见见蒙蒂，不是吗？他就在楼下——多绅士的一个人，亲爱的！」

姑娘差点儿笑出声来。

===Original===
A little later she was introduced to the redoubtable Monty, and found his suave and easy manner a relief after the jerky efforts of the girl to be entertaining. Monty had seen most parts of the world and could talk entertainingly about them all. Mirabelle rather liked him, though she thought he was something of a fop, yet was not sorry when she learned that, so far from having friends to dinner, he did not expect them to arrive until after she and Joan had left.

The meal put her more at her ease. He was a polished man of the world, courteous to the point of pomposity; he neither said nor suggested one thing that could offend her; they were halfway through dinner when the cry of a newsboy was heard in the street. Through the dining-room window she saw the footman go down the steps and buy a newspaper. He glanced at the stop-press space and came back slowly up the stairs reading. A little later he came into the room, and must have signalled to her host, for Monty went out immediately and she heard their voices in the passage. Joan was uneasy.

===Chinese===
过了一小会儿，有人把她引见给那位威名赫赫的蒙蒂；比起琼那副手忙脚乱、强打精神的逗趣功夫，他这份温文从容的风度着实叫人松快。蒙蒂见过大半个世界，讲起哪儿都能讲得妙趣横生。米拉贝尔对他颇有好感，虽说觉得他多少有几分纨绔气；待听说他根本不是邀朋友来吃饭、料定那班客人要等她和琼走了之后才到，她心里更觉称意。

这顿饭让她愈发自在。他是位圆滑练达的交际场老手，客气得近乎虚浮；没有一句话、没有一丝暗示可能开罪于她。饭吃到一半，街上传来报童的叫卖声。透过饭厅的窗，她看见那男仆走下台阶，买了一份报纸。他扫了一眼报上的最新截稿消息栏，一面读着一面缓缓拾级而上。不多时他进了屋，想必是朝主人递了个眼色，蒙蒂立时走了出去，她听得见走廊里两人说话的声音。琼心神不定起来。

===Original===
“I wonder what’s the matter?” she asked, a little irritably. “It’s very bad manners to leave ladies in the middle of dinner—”

At that moment Monty came back. Was it imagination on her part, or had he gone suddenly pale? Joan saw it, and her brows met, but she was too wise to make a comment upon his appearance.

Mr. Newton seated himself in his place with a word of apology and poured out a glass of champagne. Only for a second did his hand tremble, and then, with a smile, he was his old self.

“What is wrong, Monty?”

===Chinese===
「不知出了什么事？」她问道，语气里带着几分烦躁。「饭吃到一半撇下女客不管，实在太失礼——」

正在这时，蒙蒂回来了。是她看花了眼呢，还是他脸色霎时白了？琼看在眼里，眉头蹙拢，但她乖觉得很，对他的脸色不作一声。

牛顿先生道了一声歉，在原位落座，斟出一杯香槟。他的手只颤了一瞬，随即含笑恢复了常态。

「出什么事了，蒙蒂？」

===Original===
“Wrong? Nothing,” he said curtly, and took up the topic of conversation where he had laid it down before leaving the room.

“It isn’t that old snake, is it?” asked Joan with a shiver. “Lord! that unnerves me! I never go to bed at night without looking under, or turning the clothes right down to the foot! They ought to have found it months ago if the police—”

At this point she caught Monty Newton’s eye, cold, menacing, malevolent, and the rest of her speech died on her lips.

Mirabelle went upstairs to dress, and Joan would have followed but the man beckoned her.

===Chinese===
「事？没事。」他简截地说，把离席前撂下的话头，从撂下的地方原样接了起来。

「该不是那条老『毒蛇』又出洞了吧？」琼打着寒噤问。「老天！那玩意儿叫我心里发毛！如今我夜里不上床便罢，上床必得先往床底下瞧一遍，再把铺盖整个儿翻到床脚！那东西几个月前就该被拿住了，要是警方——」

话到此处，她撞上了蒙蒂·牛顿的目光——冰冷、威吓、狠戾——后半截话登时死在了嘴边。

米拉贝尔上楼更衣去了，琼本要跟着上去，那人却朝她招了招手。

===Original===
“You’re a little too talkative, Joan,” he said, more mildly than she had expected. “The snake is not a subject we wish to discuss at dinner. And listen!” He walked into the passage and looked round, then came back and closed the door. “Keep that girl near you.”

“Who is going to dance with me?” she asked petulantly. “I look like having a hell of a lively night!”

“Benton will be there to look after you, and one of the ‘Old Guard’—”

===Chinese===
「你话多了点儿，琼。」他说，口气比她预想的温和。「『毒蛇』不是我们愿意在饭桌上议论的题目。还有——听着！」他走到走廊里四下张望了一回，又回来关上门。「让那姑娘寸步不离你身边。」

「那谁来陪我跳舞？」她使着性子问。「照这么着，我今晚这个夜可有得『快活』了，见他的鬼！」

「本顿（Benton）会到场照应你，另外还有一位『老近卫军』——」

===Original===
He saw the frightened look in her face and chuckled.

“What’s the matter, you fool?” he asked good-humouredly. “He’ll dance with the girl.”

“I wish those fellows weren’t going to be there,” she said uneasily, but he went on, without noticing her:

“I shall arrive at half-past eleven. You had better meet me near the entrance to the American bar. My party didn’t turn up, you understand. You’ll get back here at midnight.”

===Chinese===
他瞧见她脸上的惧色，咯咯笑了。

「怎么啦，你这个傻瓜？」他和颜悦色地问。「他会陪那姑娘跳舞的。」

「我倒巴不得那帮家伙别上那儿去。」她不安地说，他却毫不在意，径自往下讲：

「我十一点半到场。你最好在美式酒吧入口附近等着我。我请的那班客人来不了了，你明白吧。你半夜十二点回到这儿。」

===Original===
“So soon?” she said in dismay. “Why, it doesn’t end till—”

“You’ll be back here at midnight,” he said evenly. “Go into her room, clear up everything she may have left behind. You understand? Nothing is to be left.”

“But when she comes back she’ll—”

“She’ll not come back,” said Monty Newton, and the girl’s blood ran cold.

===Chinese===
「这么早？」她一时发慌。「哎呀，舞会不到——」

「你半夜十二点回到这儿。」他声调平平地说。「进她房间，把她可能落下的东西收拾个干净。你明白吗？什么也不许留下。」

「可等她回来，她会——」

「她回不来了。」蒙蒂·牛顿说。姑娘只觉浑身的血都凉了。

## VII “Moral Suasion” / 七 「道德劝导」
===Original===
“There’s a man wants to see you, governor.”

It was a quarter-past nine. The girls had been gone ten minutes, and Montague Newton had settled himself down to pass the hours of waiting before he had to dress. He put down the patience cards he was shuffling.

===Chinese===
「老爷，有位先生要见您。」

时间是九点一刻。姑娘们走了已有十分钟，蒙塔古·牛顿安顿下来，准备打发更衣前那几个钟头的等候。他放下了手里正洗着的单人纸牌。

===Original===
“A man to see me? Who is he, Fred?”

“I don’t know: I’ve never seen him before. Looks to me like a ‘busy.’”

A detective! Monty’s eyebrows rose, but not in trepidation. He had met many detectives in the course of his chequered career and had long since lost his awe of them.

“Show him in,” he said with a nod.

===Chinese===
「有人要见我？他是谁，弗雷德（Fred）？」

「不知道：我从前没见过他。依我看像个『包打听』。」

是个侦探！蒙蒂的眉毛扬了起来，倒不是心里发慌。在他那段坎坷起伏的生涯里，侦探见得多了，对这类人的敬畏早就丢光了。

「带他进来。」他点点头说。

===Original===
The slim man in evening dress who came softly into the room was a stranger to Monty, who knew most of the prominent figures in the world of criminal detection. And yet his face was in some way familiar.

“Captain Newton?” he asked.

“That is my name.” Newton rose with a smile.

===Chinese===
那穿晚礼服的瘦长男人轻步走进屋来，对蒙蒂却是个生面孔——而罪案侦缉界的头面人物，他多半是认得的。可这张脸又不知怎的眼熟。

「牛顿上尉？」来人问。

「正是我。」牛顿含笑起身。

===Original===
The visitor looked slowly round towards the door through which the footman had gone.

“Do your servants always listen at the keyhole?” he asked, in a quiet, measured tone, and Newton’s face went a dusky red. In two strides he was at the door and had flung it open, just in time to see the disappearing heels of the footman.

“Here, you!” He called the man back, a scowl on his face. “If you want to know anything, will you come in and ask?” he roared. “If I catch you listening at my door, I’ll murder you!”

The man with a muttered excuse made a hurried escape.

===Chinese===
来客缓缓环视，目光落到男仆方才退出去的那扇门上。

「府上的仆人向来都趴在锁眼上偷听吗？」他用平静而有分寸的语调问。牛顿的脸霎时涨成暗红。他两大步跨到门边，猛地拉开门，正来得及瞧见男仆那双正在消失的脚后跟。

「喂，你！」他沉下脸把那人叫回来。「你想知道什么，何不进来当面问？」他吼道。「再叫我撞见你趴在我门口偷听，我要了你的命！」

那男仆嘟囔了一句托词，慌忙溜之大吉。

===Original===
“How did you know?” growled Newton, as he came back into the room and slammed the door behind him.

“I have an instinct for espionage,” said the stranger, and went on, without a break: “I have called for Miss Mirabelle Leicester.”

===Chinese===
「你怎么知道的？」牛顿走回屋里，随手摔上门，咆哮道。

「我对刺探的勾当天生敏锐。」陌生人说着，话头都不接一顿：「我是来接米拉贝尔·莱斯特小姐的。」

===Original===
Newton’s eyes narrowed.

“Oh, you have, have you?” he said softly. “Miss Leicester is not in the house. She left a quarter of an hour ago.”

“I did not see her come out of the house?”

“No, the fact is, she went out by way of the mews. My—er”—he was going to say “sister” but thought better of it—“my young friend—”

===Chinese===
牛顿眯起了眼睛。

「哦，是吗，是吗？」他软软地说。「莱斯特小姐不在家。她一刻钟前就走了。」

「我怎么没见她从这宅子里出去？」

「没有，实情是，她从后面的马厩巷走的。我的——呃」——他本要说「妹妹」，临出口改了主意——「我的年轻朋友——」

===Original===
“Flash Jane Smith,” said the stranger. “Yes?”

Newton’s colour deepened. He was rapidly reaching the point when his sangfroid, nine-tenths of his moral assets, was in danger of deserting him.

“Who are you, anyway?” he asked.

===Chinese===
「『闪电』简·史密斯（Flash Jane Smith）。往下说？」

牛顿的脸色更深了一层。他那份沉着——占他全套处世本钱的十分之九——眼看就要弃他而去。

「你到底是谁？」他问。

===Original===
The stranger wetted his lips with the tip of his tongue, a curiously irritating action of his, for some inexplicable reason.

“My name is Leon Gonsalez,” he said simply.

Instinctively the man drew back. Of course! Now he remembered, and the colour had left his cheeks, leaving him grey. With an effort he forced a smile.

===Chinese===
那陌生人伸出舌尖舔了舔嘴唇。不知怎的，他这动作叫人莫名地火起。

「我叫莱昂·冈萨雷斯（Leon Gonsalez）。」他淡淡地说。

牛顿下意识地退了半步。当然！这下他想起来了，血色从两颊褪尽，只剩一片灰白。他费力地挤出一丝笑来。

===Original===
“One of the redoubtable Four Just Men? What extraordinary birds you are!” he said. “I remember ten-fifteen years ago, being scared out of my life by the very mention of your name—you came to punish where the law failed, eh?”

“You must put that in your reminiscences,” said Leon gently. “For the moment I am not in an autobiographical mood.”

But Newton could not be silenced.

===Chinese===
「令人闻风丧胆的四义士之一？你们这号人可真是些稀罕鸟儿！」他说。「我记得十到十五年前，单听人提起你们的名字，就吓得魂儿都要出窍——法律罚不到的地方，你们去罚，嗯？」

「这话您务必写进回忆录。」莱昂温和地说。「眼下我可没有谈自传的兴致。」

可牛顿收不住话头。

===Original===
“I know a man”—he was speaking slowly, with quiet vehemence—“who will one day cause you a great deal of inconvenience, Mr. Leon Gonsalez: a man who never forgets you in his prayers. I won’t tell you who he is.”

“It is unnecessary. You are referring to the admirable Oberzohn. Did I not kill his brother …? Yes, I thought I was right. He was the man with the oxycephalic head and the queerly prognathic jaw. An interesting case: I would like to have had his measurements, but I was in rather a hurry.”

He spoke almost apologetically for his haste.

===Chinese===
「我认识一个人」——他说得又慢又稳，字字却透着狠——「莱昂·冈萨雷斯先生，此人早晚要给您添天大的麻烦：这人在祷告里从没忘记过您。他是谁，我偏不说。」

「不必说了。您指的是那位可敬的欧伯佐恩。他哥哥不是我打死的么……？对，我想我没记错。就是那个尖锥形脑袋、下巴古怪前突的家伙。一宗有趣的案例：我本想量下他的各项数据，无奈当时相当匆忙。」

对自己的匆忙，他说话的口气几乎带着歉意。

===Original===
“But we’re getting away from the subject, Mr. Newton. You say this young lady has left your house by the mews, and you were about to suggest she left in the care of Miss—I don’t know what you call her. Why did she leave that way?”

Leon Gonsalez had something more than an instinct for espionage: he had an instinct for truth, and he knew two things immediately: first, that Newton was not lying when he said the girl had left the house; secondly, that there was an excellent, but not necessarily a sinister, reason for the furtive departure.

===Chinese===
「不过咱们扯远了，牛顿先生。您说这位小姐是从马厩巷离开府上的，而且您正要暗示，她是交托给那位小姐照看着走的——该怎么称呼她，我可不知道。她为什么要那样走？」

莱昂·冈萨雷斯对刺探的直觉之外还有一样：他对真相也有直觉。他当即明白了两件事：其一，牛顿说姑娘已经离家，不是撒谎；其二，这场鬼鬼祟祟的离开自有极充分的理由，而这理由未必见不得人。

===Original===
“Where has she gone?”

“Home,” said the other laconically. “Where else should she go?”

“She came to dinner … intending to stay the night?”

===Chinese===
「她去哪儿了？」

「回家了。」对方言简意赅。「不回家还能去哪儿？」

「她是来吃晚饭的……本打算留宿过夜？」

===Original===
“Look here, Gonsalez,” interrupted Monty Newton savagely. “You and your gang were wonderful people twenty years ago, but a lot has happened since then—and we don’t shiver at the name of the Three Just Men. I’m not a child—do you get that? And you’re not so very terrible at close range. If you want to complain to the police—”

“Meadows is outside. I persuaded him to let me see you first,” said Leon, and Newton started.

“Outside?” incredulously.

In two strides he was at the window and had pulled aside the blind. On the other side of the street a man was standing on the edge of the sidewalk, intently surveying the gutter. He knew him at once.

===Chinese===
「听着，冈萨雷斯，」蒙蒂·牛顿凶横地打断。「你们那一伙，二十年前确是了不起的人物，可这二十年来世道变了不少——如今听到三义士的名号，我们可不再打哆嗦。我不是三岁小孩——明白没有？再说，你们凑近了看也没那么吓人。你要是想找警察告状去——」

「米多斯（Meadows）就在外头。我说动了他，让我先进来见您。」莱昂说。牛顿猛地一惊。

「在外头？」他满心不信。

两大步他已抢到窗前，一把撩开窗帘。街对过，一个人正站在人行道边沿，聚精会神地端详着阴沟。他一眼认出了那是谁。

===Original===
“Well, bring him in,” he said.

“Where has this young lady gone? That is all I want to know.”

“She has gone home, I tell you.”

===Chinese===
「好吧，让他进来。」他说。

「这位小姐到底去哪儿了？我只想知道这一件。」

「我说了，她回家了。」

===Original===
Leon went to the door and beckoned Meadows; they spoke together in low tones, and then Meadows entered the room and was greeted with a stiff nod from the owner of the house.

“What’s the idea of this, Meadows—sending this bird to cross-examine me?”

“This bird came on his own,” said Meadows coldly, “if you mean Mr. Gonsalez? I have no right to prevent any person from cross-examining you. Where is the young lady?”

“I tell you she has gone home. If you don’t believe me, search the house—either of you.”

===Chinese===
莱昂走到门边，招手唤进米多斯；两人低声交谈了几句，随后米多斯走进屋来，宅子的主人朝他僵硬地点了点头，算是招呼。

「这是唱的哪一出，米多斯——派这么个家伙来盘问我？」

「这个家伙是自己找上门来的，」米多斯冷冷地说，「如果您指的是冈萨雷斯先生的话。我可没有权力拦着旁人来盘问您。小姐在哪儿？」

「我说了，她回家了。不信就搜这宅子——你们哪一位都请便。」

===Original===
He was not bluffing: Leon was sure of that. He turned to the detective.

“I personally have no wish to trouble this gentleman any more.”

He was leaving the room when, from over his shoulder: “That snake is busy again, Newton.”

===Chinese===
他不是虚声恫吓：这一点莱昂拿得准。他转向探长。

「我个人无意再搅扰这位先生了。」

他正要出屋，忽又从肩头甩回一句：「那条『毒蛇』又出来活动了，牛顿。」

===Original===
“What snake are you talking about?”

“He killed a man tonight on the Thames Embankment. I hope it will not spoil Lisa Marthon’s evening.”

Meadows, watching the man, saw him change colour.

“I don’t know what you mean,” he said loudly.

===Chinese===
「你说的是哪条蛇？」

「他今夜在泰晤士河滨河大道杀了一个人。但愿这事别败了丽莎·马尔通（Lisa Marthon）的晚兴。」

米多斯盯着此人的脸，看着他变了颜色。

「我不知道你在说什么。」他大声说。

===Original===
“You arranged with Lisa to pick up Barberton tonight and get him talking. And there she is, poor girl, all dressed to kill, and only a dead man to vamp—only a murdered man.” He turned suddenly, and his voice grew hard. “That is a good word, isn’t it, Newton—murder?”

“I didn’t know anything about it.”

As Newton’s hand came towards the bell: “We can show ourselves out,” said Leon.

===Chinese===
「你跟丽莎安排好了，今夜由她去接巴伯顿的茬儿，引他开口。可她人在那儿，可怜的姑娘，打扮得千娇百媚，却只剩一个死人好让她施展魅术——只剩一个被谋害的人。」他猛地转过身，声音硬了起来。「这是个好字眼儿，是不是，牛顿——谋害？」

「这里头的事我一概不知。」

牛顿的手刚朝唤人铃伸去：「我们认得路，自己出去。」莱昂说。

===Original===
He shut the door behind him, and presently there was a slam of the outer door. Monty got to the window too late to see his unwelcome guests depart, and went up to his room to change, more than a little perturbed in mind.

The footman called him from the hall.

===Chinese===
他随手带上房门，不多时，外门砰的一声撞上。蒙蒂赶到窗前，已经来不及看见那两位不受欢迎的客人离去，便上楼回房更衣，心里七上八下，好不烦乱。

男仆在门厅里唤住了他。

===Original===
“I’m sorry about that affair, sir. I thought it was a ‘busy’ …”

“You think too much, Fred”—Newton threw the words down at his servitor with a snarl. “Go back to your place, which is the servants’ hall. I’ll ring you if I want you.”

He resumed his progress up the stairs and the man turned sullenly away.

===Chinese===
「方才那件事，请您恕罪，老爷。我还当是个『包打听』……」

「你想得太多了，弗雷德。」——牛顿咬着牙，把这句话劈头扔给他的下人。「回你自己的地方去，下人厅里待着。要用你，我会按铃。」

他接着拾级而上，那男仆悻悻地转身走开。

===Original===
He opened the door of his room, switched on the light, had closed the door and was halfway to his dressing-table, when an arm like steel closed round his neck, he was jerked suddenly backward on to the floor, and looked up into the inscrutable face of Gonsalez.

“Shout and you die!” whispered a voice in his ear.

Newton lay quiet.

===Chinese===
他推开自己房间的门，拧亮电灯，刚掩上门，走到通往梳妆台的半路，一条钢铁般的手臂已经箍住他的脖颈，他猛地被向后一掼，仰面摔倒在地，抬眼正对上冈萨雷斯那张莫测高深的脸。

「喊一声就没命！」耳边有个声音低低地说。

牛顿静静地躺着。

===Original===
“I’ll fix you for this,” he stammered.

The other shook his head.

“I think not, if by ‘fixing’ me you mean you’re going to complain to the police. You’ve been under my watchful eye for quite a long time, Monty Newton, and you’ll be amazed to learn that I’ve made several visits to your house. There is a little wall safe behind that curtain”—he nodded towards the corner of the room—“would you be surprised to learn that I’ve had the door open and every one of its documentary contents photographed?”

===Chinese===
「这事我跟你没完。」他结结巴巴地说。

对方摇了摇头。

「我看完不了，如果您说的『没完』，是指上警察那儿告我去的话。蒙蒂·牛顿，您在我的眼皮底下已经待了颇有些日子了，而且您听了准会大吃一惊：我造访府上已不止一回。那道窗帘后头藏着一具嵌墙的小保险柜」——他朝屋角点了点头——「倘若我再说，柜门我打开过，里头每一份文书都拍下了照片，您可会更吃惊？」

===Original===
He saw the fear in the man’s eyes as he snapped a pair of aluminium handcuffs of curious design about Monty’s wrists. With hardly an effort he lifted him, heavy as he was, threw him on the bed, and, having locked the door, returned, and, sitting on the bed, proceeded first to strap his ankles and then leisurely to take off his prisoner’s shoes.

===Chinese===
他把一副式样古怪的铝质手铐咔地一声铐上蒙蒂的双腕，看见那人眼里闪过恐惧。接着他几乎不费吹灰之力，把这么沉重的一个人整个提起来扔到床上，锁好房门，又回身坐上床沿，先捆住囚徒的两只脚踝，再不慌不忙地脱去他的鞋。

===Original===
“What are you going to do?” asked Monty in alarm.

“I intend finding out where Miss Leicester has been taken,” said Gonsalez, who had stripped one shoe and, pulling off the silken sock, was examining the man’s bare foot critically. “Ordinary and strictly legal inquiries take time and fail at the end—unfortunately for you, I have not a minute to spare.”

“I tell you she’s gone home.”

===Chinese===
「你要干什么？」蒙蒂惊惶地问。

「我要弄明白莱斯特小姐被送到了什么地方。」冈萨雷斯说。这时他已脱下一只鞋，正褪下丝袜，挑剔地端详那人的光脚板。「寻常而又严格合法的查访，既费工夫，到头来还常常落空——可惜我连一分钟也腾不出来，这于您就大为不妙了。」

「我告诉你，她回家了。」

===Original===
Leon did not reply. He pulled open a drawer of the bureau, searched for some time, and presently found what he sought: a thin silken scarf. This, despite the struggles of the man on the bed, he fastened about his mouth.

“In Mosamades,” he said—“and if you ever say that before my friend George Manfred, be careful to give its correct pronunciation: he is rather touchy on the point—some friends of yours took a man named Barberton, whom they subsequently murdered, and tried to make him talk by burning his feet. He was a hero. I’m going to see how heroic you are.”

“For God’s sake don’t do it!” said the muffled voice of Newton.

===Chinese===
莱昂没有答话。他拉开五斗柜的一只抽屉，翻检了一阵，找到了要找的东西：一条薄薄的丝巾。他不顾床上那人的挣扎，把它缠上了他的嘴。

「在莫萨马德斯（Mosamades），」他说——「这个词您若当着我朋友乔治·曼弗雷德的面提起，务必把音念准了：他在这一点上顶真得很——你的一些朋友逮住过一个名叫巴伯顿的人，后来把他谋害了，想逼他开口，就烧他的脚。他是条好汉。我倒要看看你有几分好汉的成色。」

「看在上帝的分上，别这么干！」牛顿隔着堵嘴的东西发出闷声。

===Original===
Gonsalez was holding a flat metal case which he had taken from his pocket, and the prisoner watched him, fascinated, as he removed the lid, and snapped a cigar-lighter close to its blackened surface. A blue flame rose and swayed in the draught.

===Chinese===
冈萨雷斯手里托着一只从口袋里取出的扁金属盒。囚徒像被摄住了似的盯着他：他掀开盒盖，咔地打着一支雪茄打火机，凑近那熏得乌黑的盒面。蓝色的火苗腾起，在穿堂气流里摇曳。

===Original===
“The police force is a most excellent institution,” said Leon. He had found a silver shoehorn on the table and was calmly heating it in the light of the flame, holding the rapidly warming hook with a silk handkerchief. “But unfortunately, when you are dealing with crimes of violence, moral suasion and gentle treatment produce nothing more poignant in the bosom of your adversary than a sensation of amused and derisive contempt. The English, who make a god of the law, gave up imprisoning thugs and flogged them, and there are few thugs left. When the Russian gunmen came to London, the authorities did the only intelligent thing—they held back the police and brought up the artillery, having only one desire, which was to kill the gunmen at any expense. Violence fears violence. The gunman lives in the terror of the gun—by the way, I understand the old guard is back in full strength?”

When Leon started in this strain he could continue for hours.

===Chinese===
「警察部队是一桩顶好的制度。」莱昂说。他已在桌上找到一只银鞋拔，正沉静地架在火焰上加热，用一方丝手帕垫着，握住那迅速烫手的弯钩。「可惜，一旦对付的是暴力罪行，道德劝导也好，温柔款待也好，在敌手胸中激起的，无非是一种觉得有趣、夹带讥诮的轻蔑之感，再痛切些的就没有了。把法律奉若神明的英国人，后来不再把恶徒关进监狱，而是改用鞭子抽，如今恶徒便所剩无几。俄国枪手们进伦敦那回，当局干了唯一一件聪明事——按住警察，调上来大炮，心里只有一个念头：不惜任何代价，把枪手统统击毙。暴力惧怕暴力。玩枪的人就活在枪的恐怖里——对了，我听说那一班老卫队又全须全尾地归队了？」

莱昂一起这种话头，能滔滔不绝讲上几个钟头。

===Original===
“I don’t know what you mean,” mumbled Monty.

“You wouldn’t.” The intruder lifted the blackened, smoking shoehorn, brought it as near to his face as he dared.

“Yes, I think that will do,” he said, and came slowly towards the bed.

The man drew up his feet in anticipation of pain, but a long hand caught him by the ankles and drew them straight again.

===Chinese===
「我不知道你在说什么。」蒙蒂含混地咕哝。

「您自然是不知道。」闯入者举起那烧得乌黑、兀自冒烟的鞋拔，凑到自己脸前，近得刚敢再近。

「嗯，我看火候到了。」他说着，缓缓朝床边走去。

那人料到疼痛将至，慌忙蜷起双脚，却被一只修长的手攥住脚踝，又拉得笔直。

===Original===
“They’ve gone to the Arts Ball.” Even through the handkerchief the voice sounded hoarse.

“The Arts Ball?” Gonsalez looked down at him, and then, throwing the hot shoehorn into the fireplace, he removed the gag. “Why have they gone to the Arts Ball?”

“I wanted them out of the way tonight.”

===Chinese===
「她们去艺术家舞会了。」隔着嘴上的丝巾，那嗓音也透着嘶哑。

「艺术家舞会？」冈萨雷斯低头看他，随即把滚烫的鞋拔掷进壁炉，除去他嘴里的堵口物。「她们为什么去艺术家舞会？」

「我要她们今夜别在这儿碍事。」

===Original===
“Is Oberzohn likely to be at the Arts Ball?”

“Oberzohn!” The man’s laugh bordered on the hysterical.

“Or Gurther?”

This time Mr. Newton did not laugh.

“I don’t know who you mean,” he said.

===Chinese===
「欧伯佐恩可能到艺术家舞会去吗？」

「欧伯佐恩！」此人的笑声近乎歇斯底里。

「还是格瑟（Gurther）？」

这一回牛顿先生笑不出来了。

「我不知道你指的是谁。」他说。

===Original===
“We’ll go into that later,” replied Leon lightly, pulling the knot of the handkerchief about the ankles. “You may get up now. What time do you expect them back?”

“I don’t know. I told Joan not to hurry, as I was meeting somebody here tonight.”

===Chinese===
「那咱们回头再细究。」莱昂轻描淡写地答道，一面解开脚踝上手帕打的结。「您可以起来了。她们几时回来？」

「说不好。我叮嘱琼别急着回来，今夜我约了人在这儿见面。」

===Original===
Which sounded plausible. Leon remembered that the Arts Ball was a fancy dress affair, and there was some reason for the departure from the mews instead of from the front of the house. As though he were reading his thoughts, Newton said: “It was Miss Leicester’s idea, going through the back. She was rather shy … she was wearing a domino.”

“Colour?”

“Green, with a reddish hood.”

===Chinese===
这话听着可信。莱昂记起艺术家舞会本是化装舞会，从马厩巷而不是从宅子正门离开，也就有了几分道理。仿佛看穿了他的心思，牛顿说：「走后头是莱斯特小姐的主意。她脸皮嫩……她披了件化装斗篷。」

「什么颜色？」

「绿的，带一顶泛红的兜帽。」

===Original===
Leon looked at him quickly.

“Rather distinctive. Was that the idea?”

“I don’t know what the idea was,” growled Newton, sitting on the edge of the bed and pulling on a sock. “But I do know this, Gonsalez,” he said, with an outburst of anger which was half fear: “that you’ll be sorry you did this to me!”

===Chinese===
莱昂倏地看了他一眼。

「这可相当扎眼。要的就是这个效果？」

「打的什么主意，我可不知道。」牛顿吼道，坐到床沿，套上一只袜子。「可有一件我知道，冈萨雷斯，」他怒气迸发——那怒气里一半是恐惧——「你这么整治我，早晚要后悔的！」

===Original===
Leon walked to the door, turned the key and opened it.

“I only hope that you will not be sorry I did not kill you,” he said, and was gone.

Monty Newton waited until from his raised window he saw the slim figure pass along the sidewalk and disappear round a corner, and then he hurried down, with one shoe on and one off, to call New Cross 93.

===Chinese===
莱昂走到门边，拧转钥匙，推门而出。

「我只盼您将来不要后悔，我今夜没有杀您。」说罢人已不见。

蒙蒂·牛顿守在推起的窗前，直到望见那颀长的身影沿人行道走远、拐过街角没了影，才匆匆下楼——脚上一只穿着鞋，一只光着——去挂新克罗斯（New Cross）93 号的电话。

## VIII The House of Oberzohn / 八 欧伯佐恩的宅邸
===Original===
In a triangle two sides of which were expressed by the viaducts of converging railroads and the base by the dark and sluggish waters of the Grand Surrey Canal, stood the gaunt ruins of a store in which had once been housed the merchandise of the O. & S. Company. A zeppelin in passing had dropped an incendiary bomb at random, and torn a great ugly gap in the roof. The fire that followed left the iron frames of the windows twisted and split; the roof by some miracle remained untouched except for the blackened edges about the hole through which the flames had rushed to the height of a hundred feet.

===Chinese===
有那么一块三角形的地面：两条边是几条交汇铁路的高架桥，底边是萨里大运河（Grand Surrey Canal）幽暗滞缓的河水；就在这三角形里，立着一座货栈的残骸，嶙峋荒败，从前堆放在这里的是欧伯佐恩-斯米茨商行（O. & S.）的货。一架齐柏林飞艇路过时胡乱丢下一颗燃烧弹，把屋顶炸开了一个又大又丑的缺口。随之而起的大火把窗子的铁架烧得扭曲开裂；屋顶却奇迹般无恙，只是火舌从那窟窿里冲天蹿起百来英尺，窟窿四周熏出了焦黑的边缘。

===Original===
The store was flush with the canal towing-path; barges had moored here, discharging rubber in bales, palm nut, nitrates even, and had restocked with Manchester cloth and case upon case of Birmingham-made geegaws of brass and lacquer.

Mr. Oberzohn invariably shipped his spirituous cargoes from Hamburg, since Germany is the home of synthesis. In the centre of the triangle was a redbrick villa, more unlovely than the factory, missing as it did that ineffable grandeur, made up of tragedy and pathos, attaching to a burnt-out building, however ugly it may have been in its prime.

===Chinese===
货栈紧贴着运河的纤道；驳船从前在此系泊，卸下一捆捆橡胶、棕榈仁，甚至硝酸盐，再装上曼彻斯特的布匹和一箱又一箱伯明翰产的黄铜镶漆小玩意儿，满载而归。

欧伯佐恩先生的烈酒货载向来从汉堡发运，因为德国是合成术的老家。三角形的正中是一座红砖宅子，比那工厂似的货栈更不入眼——烧尽的建筑自有一种难以言喻的庄严，由悲剧与哀怆织成，无论它盛时多么丑陋；这宅子偏偏缺了这一层。

===Original===
The villa was built from a design in Mr. Oberzohn’s possession, and was the exact replica of the house in Sweden where he was born. It had high, gabled ends at odd and unexpected places. The roof was shingled with grey tiles; there were glass panels in the curious-looking door, and iron ornaments in the shape of cranes and dogs flanking the narrow path through the rank nettle and dock which constituted his garden.

===Chinese===
这宅子照着欧伯佐恩先生手里的一张图样建成，同他出生之地瑞典的那座宅子分毫不差。高高的山墙出没在古怪而意想不到的位置。屋顶铺着灰瓦；那扇模样古怪的门上嵌着玻璃格，鹤形与狗形的铁铸饰物分立在一条窄径两旁——小径穿过长得疯野的荨麻与酸模，那便是他的花园。

===Original===
Here he dwelt, in solitude, yet not in solitude, for two men lived in the house, and there was a stout Swedish cook and a very plain Danish maid, a girl of vacant countenance, who worked from sunup to midnight without complaint, who seldom spoke and never smiled. The two men were somewhere in the region of thirty. They occupied the turret rooms at each end of the building, and had little community of interest. They sometimes played cards together with an old and greasy pack, but neither spoke more than was necessary. They were lean, hollow-faced men, with a certain physiognomical resemblance. Both had thin, straight lips; both had round, staring, dark eyes filled with a bright but terrifying curiosity.

===Chinese===
他在此独居，却又不算独居：屋里住着两个男人，还有一位壮实的瑞典厨娘和一个长相很不起眼的丹麦女仆——那姑娘神情空落，从日出干到半夜毫无怨言，难得开口，从不微笑。两个男人年纪三十上下，分住宅子两端的塔楼房间里，彼此少有共同的志趣。有时两人用一副油腻的旧牌打打牌，但除非必要，谁也不多说一个字。两人都是精瘦、脸颊凹陷的模样，面相上有几分相似：都是又薄又直的嘴唇；都是圆睁的黑眼睛，眼里亮闪闪的，却是一种叫人胆寒的好奇。

===Original===
“They look,” reported Leon Gonsalez, when he went to examine the ground, “as if they are watching pigs being killed and enjoying every minute of it. Iwan Pfeiffer is one, Sven Gurther is the other. Both have escaped the gallows or the axe in Germany; both have convictions against them. They are typical German-trained criminals—as pitiless as wolves. Dehumanized.”

===Chinese===
「他们那副长相，」莱昂·冈萨雷斯前去察看地形后回报说，「就像在看杀猪，还看得津津有味，一分钟也舍不得错过。一个是伊万·法伊弗（Iwan Pfeiffer），一个是斯文·格瑟（Sven Gurther）。两个都在德国逃过了绞架或是斧刑；两个都有前科在案。都是典型的德式训练的罪犯——同狼一样毫无怜悯。人性荡然无存。」

===Original===
The “Three,” as was usual, set the machinery of the law in motion, and found that the hands of the police were tied. Only by stretching the law could the men be deported, and the law is difficult to stretch. To all appearance they offended in no respect. A woman, by no means the most desirable of citizens, laid a complaint against one. There was an investigation—proof was absent; the very character of the complainant precluded a conviction, and the matter was dropped—by the police.

===Chinese===
「三义士」照例开动了法律的机器，却发现警察的手足被捆得死死的。只有把法律拉长了用，才能把这两个人驱逐出境，而法律是很难拉长的。从表面看，他们没有一处触犯法度。一个女人——决算不上什么良善之辈——对其中一人提出了控告。查是查了一场——证据阙如；单凭控告人那副人品，定罪便无从谈起，此事只好搁下——被警察搁下了。

===Original===
Somebody else moved swiftly.

===Chinese===
另外有一些人，动作却快得很。

===Original===
One morning, just before daybreak, a policeman patrolling the towpath heard a savage snarl and looked round for the dog. He found instead, up one of those narrow entries leading to the canal bank, a man. He was tied to the stout sleeper fence, and his bare back showed marks of a whip. Somebody had held him up at night as he prowled the bank in search of amusement, had tied and flogged him. Twenty-five lashes: an expert thought the whip used was the official cat-o’-nine-tails.

===Chinese===
一天清晨，天将破晓，一名沿纤道巡逻的警察听见一声凶狠的咆哮，四下张望找是哪条狗。他却在一道通往运河岸的窄巷里找到了一个人。那人被绑在结实的枕木栅栏上，赤裸的背上现出道道鞭痕。原来他夜里在岸边游荡寻乐时，被人截住捆了，抽了一顿。二十五鞭：行家认为，用的是官制的九尾鞭（cat-o’-nine-tails）。

===Original===
Scotland Yard, curious, suspicious, sought out the Three Just Men. They had alibis so complete as to be unbreakable. Sven Gurther went unavenged—but he kept from the towpath thereafter.

===Chinese===
苏格兰场又好奇又起疑，找上了三义士。三人的不在场证明完满无缺，无懈可击。斯文·格瑟的仇没能讨还——但打那以后，他再不沾纤道的边。

===Original===
In this house of his there were rooms which only Dr. Oberzohn visited. The Danish maid complained to the cook that when she had passed the door of one as the doctor came out, a blast of warm, tainted air had rushed out and made her cough for an hour. There was another room in which from time to time the doctor had installed a hotchpotch of apparatus. Vulcanizing machines, electrical machines (older and more used than Mirabelle had seen in her brief stay in the City Road), a liquid air plant, not the most up-to-date but serviceable.

===Chinese===
在他这座宅子里，有几间屋子只有欧伯佐恩博士一人进出。丹麦女仆向厨娘抱怨说，有一回博士正从其中一间屋里出来，她打门前经过，一股温热污浊的气浪扑面涌出，呛得她咳了一个钟头。还有一间屋子，博士不时往里安置一锅大杂烩似的器械：硫化机、电机（比米拉贝尔在城市路那段短短日子所见的更旧、用得更狠），还有一套液化空气设备——谈不上最新式，倒也使得。

===Original===
He was not, curiously enough, a doctor in the medical sense. He was not even a doctor of chemistry. His doctorate was in Literature and Law. These experiments of his were hobbies—hobbies that he had pursued from his childhood.

On this evening he was sitting in his stuffy parlour reading a close-printed and closer-reasoned volume of German philosophy, and thinking of something else. Though the sun had only just set, the blinds and curtains were drawn; a wood fire crackled in the grate, and the bright lights of three half-watt lamps made glaring radiance.

===Chinese===
说来也怪，他并不是医学意义上的博士，甚至连化学博士也不是。他的博士学位挂在文学与法学上。他这些实验是癖好——从孩提时代一直玩到如今的癖好。

这天傍晚，他坐在闷塞的客厅里，读着一卷排版紧密、论证更见缜密的德国哲学，心思却在别处。太阳才刚落山，百叶与窗帘已经拉严；炉栅里柴火噼啪作响，三盏半瓦特的灯打出灼灼刺目的光华。

===Original===
An interruption came in the shape of a telephone call. He listened, grunting replies.

“So!” he said at last, and spoke a dozen words in his strange English.

===Chinese===
一通电话打来，打断了他。他听着，喉咙里咕哝着作答。

「原来如此！」他终于开口，用他那口古怪的英语撂下十来个词。

===Original===
Putting aside his book, he hobbled in his velvet slippers across the room and pressed twice upon the bell-push by the side of the fireplace. Gurther came in noiselessly and stood waiting.

He was grimy, unshaven. The pointed chin and short upper lip were blue. The V of his shirt visible above the waistcoat was soiled and almost black at the edges. He stood at attention, smiling vacantly, his eyes fixed at a point above the doctor’s head.

===Chinese===
他把书搁到一边，趿着天鹅绒拖鞋蹒跚穿过房间，在壁炉旁的电铃钮上按了两下。格瑟悄没声地进来，站着候命。

他一身污垢，胡子也没刮。尖下巴和短上唇一片青黑。马甲上缘露出的一角衬衫 V 形前胸沾满污渍，边缘几乎发黑。他挺直站着，脸上挂着空空的笑，眼睛盯在博士头顶上方的一点。

===Original===
Dr. Oberzohn lifted his eyes from his book.

“I wish you to be a gentleman of club manner tonight,” he said. He spoke in that hard North-German tongue which the Swede so readily acquires.

“*Ja*, Herr Doktor!”

The man melted from the room.

===Chinese===
欧伯佐恩博士从书上抬起眼来。

「今晚，我要你做一位俱乐部派头的绅士。」他说的是那种硬邦邦的北德话——瑞典人学这种话最是不费吹灰之力。

「*Ja*, Herr Doktor！」

那人像化在空气里似的，从房中消失了。

===Original===
Dr. Oberzohn for some reason hated Germans. So, for the matter of that, did Gurther and Pfeiffer, the latter being Polish by extraction and Russian by birth. Gurther hated Germans because they stormed the little jail at Altostadt to kill him after the dogs found Frau Siedlitz’s body. He would have died then but for the green police, who scented a Communist rising, scattered the crowd and sent Gurther byroad to the nearest big town under escort. The two escorting policemen were never seen again. Gurther reappeared mysteriously in England two years after, bearing a veritable passport. There was no proof even that he was Gurther. Leon knew, Manfred knew, Poiccart knew.

===Chinese===
欧伯佐恩博士不知为什么憎恨德国人。说起来，格瑟和法伊弗也一样——后者血统是波兰人，出生在俄国。格瑟恨德国人，是因为群狗找到西德利茨太太（Frau Siedlitz）的尸体之后，德国人冲进了阿尔托施塔特（Altostadt）的小监狱要杀他。若不是绿衣警察嗅出一场共产主义暴动的苗头，驱散人群，把格瑟取小路押往最近的大城，他那时就没命了。随行押送的两名警察从此再无踪影。两年之后，格瑟神秘地重现于英国，持着一本货真价实的护照。甚至没有证据证明他就是格瑟。莱昂知道，曼弗雷德知道，波伊卡特也知道。

===Original===
There had been an alternative to the whipping.

“It would be a simple matter to hold his head under water until he was drowned,” said Leon.

They debated the matter, decided against this for no sentimental or moral reason—none save expediency. Gurther had his whipping and never knew how near to the black and greasy water of the canal he had been.

===Chinese===
当初那顿鞭子之外，本另有一条出路。

「把他的头按进水里，一直按到淹死为止，本是件轻而易举的事。」莱昂说。

三人把这事掂量了一番，否决了这一条——并非出于什么感伤的或道义的理由，全然只依权宜二字。格瑟挨了他的鞭子，却始终不知道，自己离那条运河里又黑又腻的死水，曾经多么近。

===Original===
Dr. Oberzohn resumed his book—a fascinating book that was all about the human soul and immortality and time. He was in the very heart of an analysis of eternity when Gurther reappeared dressed in the “gentleman-club manner.” His dress-coat fitted perfectly; shirt and waistcoat were exactly the right cut. The snowy shirt, the braided trousers, the butterfly bow, and winged collar. …

===Chinese===
欧伯佐恩博士重新拿起他的书——一本引人入胜的书，讲的全是人类灵魂、不朽与时间。他正沉浸在一篇关于永恒的分析的最深处，格瑟回来了，打扮成那副「俱乐部绅士派头」。燕尾服合身至极；衬衫与马甲的剪裁恰到好处。雪白的衬衫、镶辫的长裤、蝶形领结，外加一副翼领……

===Original===
“That is good.” Dr. Oberzohn went slowly over the figure. “But the studs should be pearl—not enamel. And the watch-chain is démodé—it is not worn. The gentleman-club manner does not allow of visible ornament. Also I think a moustache …?”

“*Ja*, Herr Doktor!”

===Chinese===
「这就很好。」欧伯佐恩博士把这身行头从头到脚缓缓检视过去。「只是饰钮应当用珍珠——不是珐琅的。还有那表链 démodé——过时了，如今没人这样戴。俱乐部绅士派头，容不得外露的饰物。另外我想，还该有一撮小胡子……？」

「*Ja*, Herr Doktor！」

===Original===
Gurther, who was once an actor, disappeared again. When he returned the enamel studs had gone: there were small pearls in their place, and his white waistcoat had no chain across. And on his upper lip had sprouted a small brown moustache, so natural that even Oberzohn, scrutinizing closely, could find no fault with it. The doctor took a case from his pocket, fingered out three crisp notes.

===Chinese===
格瑟——他从前做过演员——又消失了。待他回来，珐琅饰钮没了：原处换上了小小的珍珠，白马甲上也不再有表链横过。上唇则冒出一撇棕色小胡，自然得很，连欧伯佐恩凑近细察也寻不出半点破绽。博士从口袋里摸出一只小匣，指间捻出三张挺括的新钞。

===Original===
“Your hands, please?”

Gurther took three paces to the old man, halted, clicked his heels and held out his hands for inspection.

“Good! You know Leon Gonsalez? He will be at the Arts Ball. He wears no fancy dress. He was the man who whipped you.”

“He was the man who whipped me,” said Gurther without heat.

There was a silence, Dr. Oberzohn pursing his lips.

===Chinese===
「请让我看看您的手？」

格瑟朝老人跨出三步，收步立定，靴跟咔的一声一并，伸出双手受检。

「好！你认识莱昂·冈萨雷斯？他会到场艺术家舞会。他不穿化装服。他就是鞭打你的那个人。」

「他就是鞭打我的那个人。」格瑟说，语气平平，不带火气。

一阵沉默，欧伯佐恩博士抿紧了嘴唇。

===Original===
“Also, he did that which brands him as an infamous assassin … I think … yes, I think my dear Gurther … there will be a girl also, but the men of my police will be there to arrange such matters. Benton will give you instructions. For you, only Gonsalez.”

Gurther bowed stiffly.

“I have implored the order,” he said, bowed again and withdrew. Later, Dr. Oberzohn heard the drone of the little car as it bumped and slithered across the grass to the road. He resumed his book: this matter of eternity was fascinating.

===Chinese===
「还有，他干过那件事，那件事给他烙下了臭名昭著刺客的印记……我想……是的，我想，我亲爱的格瑟……到时还会有一位姑娘在场，不过我警察的人自会在场料理这等事。本顿会给你指示。至于你——只有冈萨雷斯。」

格瑟僵硬地一躬身。

「这道命令，我恳求已久了。」他说，又鞠一躬，退了出去。过了一阵，欧伯佐恩博士听见那辆小汽车嗡嗡的声响，一路颠簸打滑地碾过草地驶上大路。他重新拿起书来：这永恒的问题，着实迷人。

---

===Original===
The Arts Ball at the Corinthian Hall was one of the events of the season, and the tickets, issued exclusively to the members of three clubs, were eagerly sought by society people who could not be remotely associated with any but the art of living.

===Chinese===
科林斯大厅（Corinthian Hall）的艺术家舞会是本季社交日历上的一桩盛事；入场券专发给三家俱乐部的会员，可那些社交界人物——他们同任何艺术都沾不上一丝边，唯独精通生活这一门艺术——偏偏削尖了脑袋想弄一张到手。

===Original===
When the girl came into the crowded hall, she looked around in wonder. The balconies, outlined in soft lights and half-hidden with flowers, had been converted into boxes; the roof had been draped with blue and gold tissue; at one end of the big hall was a veritable bower of roses, behind which one of the two bands was playing. Masks in every conceivable guise were swinging rhythmically across the polished floor. To the blasé, there was little difference between the Indians, the pierrots and the cavaliers to be seen here and those they had seen a hundred times on a hundred different floors.

===Chinese===
姑娘走进人头攒动的大厅，惊奇地环顾四周。一座座回廊由柔光勾勒出轮廓、半掩在花丛背后，都改成了包厢；屋顶悬起蓝金两色的薄纱；大厅一端是一座名副其实的玫瑰凉亭，两支乐队之一便在亭后演奏。各色想得出装扮的假面舞客，正合着节拍从光可鉴人的地板上摇曳穿行。在阅尽浮华的人看来，此地的印第安人、皮埃罗小丑和骑士们，同他们在一百块不同的舞池里见过一百回的那些，并没有多大分别。

===Original===
As the girl gazed round in wonder and delight, forgetting all her misgivings, two men, one in evening dress, the other in the costume of a brigand, came from under the shadow of the balcony towards them.

“Here are our partners,” said Joan, with sudden vivacity. “Mirabelle, I want you to know Lord Evington.”

===Chinese===
姑娘正看得又惊又喜，把一肚子的疑虑忘了个干净，两名男子——一个穿晚礼服，一个作强盗装束——从回廊的暗影底下朝她们走来。

「咱们的舞伴来了。」琼忽然来了兴致。「米拉贝尔，我给你介绍，这位是埃文顿勋爵（Lord Evington）。」

===Original===
The man in evening dress stroked his little moustache, clicked his heels and bent forward in a stiff bow. He was thin-faced, a little pallid, unsmiling. His round, dark eyes surveyed her for a second, and then:

“I’m glad to meet you, Miss Leicester,” he said, in a high, harsh voice, that had just the trace of a foreign accent.

This struck the girl with as much surprise as the cold kiss he had implanted upon her hand, and, as if he read her thoughts, he went on quickly: “I have lived so long abroad that England and English manners are strange to me. Won’t you dance? And had you not better mask? I must apologize to you for my costume.” He shrugged his shoulders. “But there was no gala dress available.”

===Chinese===
穿晚礼服的男子捻了捻那撮小胡子，靴跟一并，直僵僵地俯身一躬。他瘦脸，微显苍白，不苟言笑。一双滚圆的黑眼睛把她端详了一秒钟，随即：

「幸会，莱斯特小姐。」他说，嗓音又高又硬，带着一丝若隐若现的外国腔。

这句话给姑娘的惊异，不亚于他印在她手背上的那个冷冰冰的吻；他仿佛看穿了她的心思，赶忙接着说：「我在国外住得太久，英国和英国的礼数于我都生疏了。跳支舞好吗？您是不是还是戴上面具的好？我得为这身穿戴向您道歉。」他耸了耸肩。「只是实在弄不到一件盛装礼服。」

===Original===
She fixed the red mask, and in another second she was gliding through the crowd and was presently lost to view.

===Chinese===
她戴好那红色面具，转眼便滑进人流，不多时就望不见了。

===Original===
“I don’t understand it all, Benton.”

Joan was worried and frightened. She had begun to realize that the game she played was something different … her part more sinister than any role she had yet filled. To jolly along the gilded youth to the green tables of Captain Monty Newton was one thing; but never before had she seen the gang working against a woman.

===Chinese===
「这些事我全弄不明白，本顿。」

琼又是忧又是怕。她已开始意识到，自己玩的这场把戏有些异样……她这回的角色，比她从前扮过的哪一个都凶险。把阔少爷们哄到蒙蒂·牛顿上尉的绿呢赌台前是一回事；可这伙人对付一个女人，她还是头一回见。

===Original===
“I don’t know,” grumbled the brigand, who was not inaptly arrayed. “There’s been a hurry call for everybody.” He glanced round uneasily as though he feared his words might be overheard. “All the guns are here—Defson, Cuccini, Jewy Stubbs …”

“The guns?” she whispered in horror, paling under her rouge. “You mean …?”

“The guns are out: that’s all I know,” he said doggedly. “They started drifting in half an hour before you came.”

===Chinese===
「我不知道。」那强盗咕哝道——他这身行头倒也相称。「来了个十万火急的招呼，人人都叫到场。」他不安地朝四下里扫了一眼，仿佛怕话让人听了去。「枪手全到齐了——德夫森、库奇尼、犹太仔斯塔布斯……」

「枪手？」她惊恐地低声说，脂粉底下的脸色也白了。「你是说……？」

「枪手出动了；我就知道这么多。」他执拗地说。「早你们来之前半个钟头，他们就开始陆陆续续往里渗了。」

===Original===
Joan was silent, her heart racing furiously. Then Monty had told her the truth. She knew that somewhere behind Oberzohn, behind Monty Newton, was a force perfectly dovetailed into the machine, only one cog of which she had seen working. These card parties of Monty’s were profitable enough, but for a long time she had had a suspicion that they were the merest sideline. The organization maintained a regular corps of gunmen, recruited from every quarter of the globe. Monty Newton talked sometimes in his less sober moments of what he facetiously described as the “Old Guard.” How they were employed, on what excuse, for what purpose, she had never troubled to think. They came and went from England in batches. Once Monty had told her that Oberzohn’s people had gone to Smyrna, and he talked vaguely of unfair competition that had come to the traders of the O. & S. outfit. Afterwards she read in the paper of a “religious riot” which resulted in the destruction by fire of a great block of business premises. After that Monty spoke no more of competition. The Old Guard returned to England, minus one of its number, who had been shot in the stomach in the course of this “religious riot.” What particular faith he possessed in such a degree as to induce him to take up arms for the cause, she never learned. She knew he was dead, because Monty had written to the widow, who lived in the Bronx.

===Chinese===
琼不作声了，心口狂跳不止。这么说，蒙蒂对她讲的是实话了。她知道，在欧伯佐恩背后、在蒙蒂·牛顿背后，有一股势力同这部机器严丝合缝地咬合着，她眼见转动的不过是其中一枚齿轮。蒙蒂这些牌局进项固然不坏，可长久以来她就疑心那不过是顶末流的副业。这个组织豢养着一支正经八百的枪手队伍，人手招募自天下四面八方。蒙蒂·牛顿在不大清醒的时刻，有时会念叨起他戏称为「老近卫军」的那班人马。他们受的什么差遣、寻的什么由头、图的什么目的，她从不劳神去想。他们一拨一拨地出入英国。有一回蒙蒂告诉她，欧伯佐恩的人马去了士麦那（Smyrna），还含含糊糊地说起商行买卖人遭了不正当竞争。后来她在报上读到一场「宗教暴乱」，一整片商号楼宇焚毁殆尽。打那以后，蒙蒂再不提竞争二字。老近卫军回了英国，人数少了一个——在那场「宗教暴乱」里叫人打穿了肚子。他究竟信奉哪一门特别的教义、竟信到肯为它拿起武器的地步，她始终无从知晓。她只知道他死了，因为蒙蒂给他遗孀去过一封信——那寡妇住在布朗克斯（the Bronx）。

===Original===
Joan knew a lot about Monty’s business, for an excellent reason. She was with him most of the time; and whether she posed as his niece or daughter, his sister, or some closer relationship, she was undoubtedly the nearest to a confidante he possessed.

===Chinese===
琼对蒙蒂的营生知之甚详，缘由也再好不过：她大部分时间都守在他身边；而不管她顶着的是他的侄女还是女儿、妹妹还是别的更亲近些的名分，毫无疑问，她是蒙蒂所有的相知里最近乎心腹的一个。

===Original===
“Who is that man with the moustache—is he one?” she asked.

“No; he’s Oberzohn’s man—for God’s sake don’t tell Monty I told you all this! I got orders tonight to put him wise about the girl.”

“What about her … what are they doing with her?” she gasped in terror.

“Let us dance,” said Benton, and half guided, half carried her into the throng. They had reached the centre of the floor when, with no warning, every light in the hall went out.

===Chinese===
「那个留小胡子的男人是谁——也是一伙的？」她问。

「不是；他是欧伯佐恩的人——千万看在上帝份上，别告诉蒙蒂是我说的这些！今夜我奉了吩咐，要把这姑娘的底细递给他。」

「那她怎么办……他们要拿她怎么样？」她吓得倒抽一口冷气。

「跳舞吧。」本顿说着，半引半架地把她带入人丛。两人刚走到舞池中央，毫无征兆地，大厅里所有的灯霎时全灭了。

## IX Before the Lights Went Out / 九 熄灯之前
===Original===
The band had stopped, a rustle of hand-clapping came from the hot dancers, and almost before the applause had started the second band struck up “Kulloo.”

===Chinese===
乐队停了下来，舞得浑身发热的宾客中响起一阵拍掌的沙沙，掌声几乎还没成气候，第二支乐队便奏起了《库卢》（Kulloo）。

===Original===
Mirabelle was not especially happy. Her partner was the most correct of dancers, but they lacked just that unity of purpose, that oneness of interest which makes all the difference between the ill- and the well-matched.

===Chinese===
米拉贝尔并不怎么快活。她的舞伴舞步规矩得无可挑剔，可两人偏偏缺了那么一点目标的合一、趣味的相投——正是这一点，分出了舞伴之间的合拍与不合拍。

===Original===
“May we sit down?” she begged. “I am rather hot.”

“Will the gracious lady come to the little hall?” he asked. “It is cooler there, and the chairs are comfortable.”

===Chinese===
「咱们坐下歇歇，好不好？」她恳求道。「我热得很。」

「尊贵的女士可愿移步到小厅里去？」他问。「那边凉快些，椅子也舒服。」

===Original===
She looked at him oddly.

“ ‘Gracious lady’ is a German expression—why do you use it, Lord Evington? I think it is very pretty,” she hastened to assure him.

“I lived for many years in Germany,” said Mr. Gurther. “I do not like the German people—they are so stupid.”

===Chinese===
她古怪地看了他一眼。

「『尊贵的女士』是德国人的说法——您怎么用起它来了，埃文顿勋爵（Lord Evington）？我觉得这说法很动听。」她连忙向他保证。

「我在德国住了许多年，」格瑟先生说，「我不喜欢德国人——他们太蠢。」

===Original===
If he had said “German police” he would have been nearer to the truth; and had he added that the dislike was mutual, he might have gained credit for his frankness.

===Chinese===
他若说的是「德国警察」，倒还离事实近些；倘若再补上一句，这份厌恶原是彼此彼此，他说不定还能凭这份坦率挣得几分称许。

===Original===
At the end of the room, concealed by the floral decorations of the bandstand, was a door which led to a smaller room, ordinarily separated from the main hall by folding doors which were seldom opened. Tonight the annex was to be used as a conservatory. Palms and banked flowers were everywhere. Arbours had been artificially created, and there were cosy nooks, half-hidden by shrubs, secluded seats and tables, all that ingenuity could design to meet the wishes of sitters-out.

===Chinese===
大厅尽头，乐台的花饰掩着一道门，通向一间较小的厅室；那里平日由一道折叠门与大礼堂相隔，折叠门难得一开。今晚这间偏厅辟作了花房。棕榈与堆叠成垄的鲜花随处可见。人工造起了一座座绿荫凉棚，还有一个个舒适的小角落，半掩在灌木丛后，幽僻的座席与小桌错落其间——凡是匠心巧思能为坐着歇脚的舞客设想到的，这里一应俱全。

===Original===
He stood invitingly at the entrance of a little grotto, dimly illuminated by one Chinese lantern.

===Chinese===
他殷殷相邀地立在一处小洞窟的入口，窟中只点着一盏中国灯笼，照出些幽昏的光。

===Original===
“I think we will sit in the open,” said Mirabelle, and pulled out a chair.

“Excuse me.”

===Chinese===
「我想我们还是坐在敞亮处吧。」米拉贝尔说着，抽出一把椅子。

「劳驾。」

===Original===
Instantly he was by her side, the chair arranged, a cushion found, and she sank down with a sigh of relief. It was early yet for the loungers: looking round, she saw that, but for a solitary waiter fastening his apron with one eye upon possible customers, they were alone.

===Chinese===
转眼间他已到她身旁，椅子摆正了，靠垫也寻来了，她如释重负地舒了口气，坐了下去。这会儿还不到闲坐的宾客光临的时辰：她环顾四周，只见除了一名独个儿站着系围裙、一只眼睛却瞟着可能光顾的客人的侍者，这里只有他们两人。

===Original===
“You will drink wine … no? An orangeade? Good!” He beckoned the waiter and gave his order. “You must excuse me if I am a little strange. I have been in Germany for many years—except during the war, when I was in France.”

===Chinese===
「您要喝点儿酒……不要？来一杯橙水？好！」他招手唤来侍者，点了单。「我要是显得有点儿古怪，您务必包涵。我在德国待了许多年——打仗那几年除外，那时我在法国。」

===Original===
Mr. Gurther had certainly been in Germany for many years, but he had never been in France. Nor had he heard a shot fired in the war. It is true that an aerial bomb had exploded perilously near the prison at Mainz in which he was serving ten years for murder, but that represented his sole warlike experience.

===Chinese===
格瑟先生在德国确实待了许多年，可他从未到过法国，整场战争也没听过一声枪响。不错，曾有一枚航空炸弹在美因茨（Mainz）那座监狱的近旁炸开，险之又险——他当时正因杀人罪在狱中服十年徒刑——但这便是他全部的战阵阅历了。

===Original===
“You live in the country, of course?”

“In London: I am working with Mr. Oberzohn.”

“So: he is a good fellow. A gentleman.”

===Chinese===
「您自然是住在乡下的？」

「住在伦敦：我在欧伯佐恩先生手下做事。」

「这样：他是个好人。一位绅士。」

===Original===
She had not been very greatly impressed by the doctor’s breeding, but it was satisfying to hear a stranger speak with such heartiness of her new employer. Her mind at the moment was on Heavytree Farm: the cool parlour with its chintzes—a room, at this hour, fragrant with the night scents of flowers which came stealing through the open casement. There was a fox-terrier, Jim by name, who would be wandering disconsolately from room to room, sniffing unhappily at the hall door. A lump came up into her throat. She felt very far from home and very lonely. She wanted to get up and run back to where she had left Joan and tell her that she had changed her mind and must go back to Gloucester that night … she looked impatiently for the waiter. Mr. Gurther was fiddling with some straws he had taken from the glass container in the centre of the table. One end of the straws showed above the edge of the table, the others were thrust deep in the wide-necked little bottle he had in the other hand. The hollow straws held half an inch of the red powder that filled the bottle.

===Chinese===
那位博士的教养门风，本来就没怎么令她心折，不过听一个陌生人这样热诚地称许她的新东家，心里到底受用。这会儿她的心思已飞回赫维特里农场：那间凉丝丝的客厅，满挂着印花布帘——每到这个时辰，夜花的香气便从敞开的窗扉间悄悄潜入，满室幽芳。家里还有一条猎狐梗，名叫吉姆，此刻想必正蔫蔫地挨着屋子打转，对着门厅的门委屈地嗅个不住。一阵哽咽涌上她的喉头。她只觉得自己离家千里，孤单极了。她真想立起身来，跑回她撇下琼的地方，告诉琼自己改了主意，今夜就非回格洛斯特不可……她焦灼地张望侍者的身影。格瑟先生正摆弄着几根吸管，那是他从桌子中央的玻璃管筒里取出来的。吸管的一头露出桌沿，另一头深深插进他另一只手中握着的一只阔口小瓶。空心的管膛里存着半英寸红色粉末——那瓶子里装的正是满满一瓶这样的红粉。

===Original===
“Excuse!”

===Chinese===
「失陪！」

===Original===
The waiter put the orangeades on the table and went away to get change. Mirabelle’s eyes were wistfully fixed on a little door at the end of the room. It gave to the street, and there were taxicabs which could get her to Paddington in ten minutes.

===Chinese===
侍者把两杯橙水放在桌上，走开取零钱去了。米拉贝尔的眼睛怔怔地望着厅室尽头的一扇小门。那门通向街面，门外就有出租汽车，十分钟能把她送到帕丁顿。

===Original===
When she looked round he was stirring the amber contents of her glass with a spoon. Two straws were invitingly protruding from the foaming orangeade. She smiled and lifted the glass as he fitted a cigarette into his long black holder.

===Chinese===
待她回过头来，他正用一把小匙搅动她杯中琥珀色的饮品。两根吸管从泛着泡沫的橙水里诱人地翘出来。她莞尔一笑，端起杯子，这时他正把一支香烟插进那支长长的黑烟嘴里。

===Original===
“I may smoke—yes?”

The first taste she had through the straws was one of extreme bitterness. She made a wry face and put down the glass.

“How horrid!”

===Chinese===
「我可以抽支烟——可以吧？」

她从吸管里吸到的头一口，苦得钻心。她皱起一张苦脸，放下了杯子。

「真难喝！」

===Original===
“Did it taste badly … ?” he began, but she was pouring out water from a bottle.

“It was most unpleasant—”

===Chinese===
「味道很糟……？」他刚起了个头，她已经在从水瓶里往外倒水。

「难喝极了——」

===Original===
“Will you try mine, please?” He offered the glass to her and she drank. “It may have been something in the straw.” Here he was telling her the fact.

===Chinese===
「请尝尝我这杯，好不好？」他把杯子递给她，她喝了。「说不定是吸管里沾了什么东西。」这一句，他倒是向她道出了实情。

===Original===
“It was …”

===Chinese===
「是……」

===Original===
The room was going round and round, the floor rising up and down like the deck of a ship in a stormy sea. She rose, swayed, and caught him by the arm.

===Chinese===
屋子团团打转，地板像风暴海面上的甲板一样起起伏伏。她站起身，晃了一晃，一把抓住了他的手臂。

===Original===
“Open the little door, waiter, please—the lady is faint.”

===Chinese===
「侍者，劳驾，请开一开那扇小门——这位小姐头晕。」

===Original===
