The waiter turned to the door and threw it open. A man stood there—just outside the door. He wore over his dinner dress a long cloak in the Spanish style. Gurther stood staring, a picture of amused dismay, his cigarette still unlit. He did not move his hands. Gonsalez was waiting there, alert … death grinning at him … and then the room went inky black. Somebody had turned the main switch.

===Chinese===
侍者转身把门猛地拉开。一个人立在那里——就在门外。他的晚礼服外罩着一件西班牙式的长斗篷。格瑟木然瞪视着，那副又惊愕又滑稽的神气活像一幅画，香烟始终不曾点着。他的双手一动不动。冈萨雷斯守候在那里，全神戒备……死神正朝他咧嘴而笑……随后，整间屋子没入一片墨黑。有人拉了总闸。

## X When the Lights Went Out / 十 灯灭之时
===Original===
Five, ten minutes passed before the hall-keeper tripped and stumbled and cursed his way to the smaller room and, smashing down the hired flowers, he passed through the wreckage of earthen pots and tumbled mould to the control. Another second and the rooms were brilliantly lit again—the band struck up a two-step and fainting ladies were escorted to the decent obscurity of their retiring rooms.

===Chinese===
五分钟、十分钟过去了，会场管理员才跌跌绊绊、骂骂咧咧地摸到那间小厅，一路撞翻了租摆的盆花，踏着陶盆碎片和倾泻的泥土走到电闸跟前。转眼工夫，几间厅房重新亮如白昼——乐队奏起一支二步舞曲，晕倒的女士们被搀进休息室那体面的幽暗里去了。

===Original===
The manager of the hall came flying into the annex.

“What happened—main fuse gone?”

“No,” said the hall-keeper sourly, “some fool turned over the switch.”

===Chinese===
会场的经理飞奔着进了边厅。

「出什么事了——总保险丝烧了？」

「没有，」会场管理员没好气地说，「是哪个蠢货把电闸拉了。」

===Original===
The agitated waiter protested that nobody had been near the switch-box.

“There was a lady and gentleman here, and another gentleman outside.” He pointed to the open door.

“Where are they now?”

“I don’t know. The lady was faint.”

===Chinese===
那名心神不宁的侍者连声辩白，说谁也没有挨近过配电箱。

「方才这儿有一位女士和一位先生，外头还有一位先生。」他指着敞开的门说。

「他们人呢？」

「不知道。那位女士发了晕。」

===Original===
The three had disappeared when the manager went out into a small courtyard that led round the corner of the building to a side street. Then he came back on a tour of inspection.

“Somebody did it from the yard. There’s a window open—you can reach the switch easily.”

The window was fastened and locked.

===Chinese===
经理走出去，进了大楼一角拐向侧街的一个小院子，那三个人早已不见踪影。他又巡查了一圈回来。

「是有人从院子里动的手。有扇窗开着——从那儿伸手就能够着电闸。」

那扇窗却关得严严实实，还上了锁。

===Original===
“There is no lady or gentleman in the yard,” he said. “Are you sure they did not go into the big hall?”

“In the dark—maybe.”

===Chinese===
「院子里没有什么女士先生。」他说。「你能肯定他们没进大厅去吗？」

「黑咕隆咚的——兴许吧。」

===Original===
The waiter’s nervousness was understandable. Mr. Gurther had given him a five-pound note and the man had not as yet delivered the change. Never would he return to claim it if all that his keen ears heard was true.

===Chinese===
侍者的紧张事出有因。格瑟先生给了他一张五镑的钞票，他到现在还没把找头送过去。倘若他那只尖耳朵听到的那一切都当真，那笔找头他是死也不会回去讨的了。

===Original===
Four men had appeared in the annex: one shut the door and stood by it. The three others were accompanied by the manager, who called Phillips, the waiter.

“This man served them,” he said, troubled. Even the most innocent do not like police visitations. “What was the gentleman like?”

Phillips gave a brief and not inaccurate description.

===Chinese===
边厅里进来了四个人：一个关上门，守在门边。另外三个由经理陪着，经理唤来了侍者菲利普斯（Phillips）。

「招待他们的就是这个人。」他说，神情不安。即便是最清白无辜的人，也不喜欢警察登门。「那位先生长什么模样？」

菲利普斯简短地描述了一番，倒也八九不离十。

===Original===
“That is your man, I think, Herr Fluen?”

The third of the party was bearded and plump; he wore a Derby hat with evening dress.

“That is Gurther,” he nodded. “It will be a great pleasure to meet him. For eight months the Embassy has been striving for his extradition. But our people at home …!”

He shrugged his shoulders. All properly constituted officials behave in such a manner when they talk of governments.

===Chinese===
「我想，那就是您要找的人了吧，弗卢恩先生（Herr Fluen）？」

同来的第三人蓄着胡须，体态丰肥；一身晚礼服，头上却扣着一顶圆顶硬礼帽。

「正是格瑟。」他点头道。「能同他本人见面，将是一大乐事。八个月来，大使馆一直在设法引渡他。可咱们国内那一头……！」

他耸了耸肩。凡是编制齐整的规矩官员，谈起政府来都是这副做派。

===Original===
“The lady now”—Inspector Meadows was patently worried—“she was faint, you say. Had she drunk anything?”

“Orangeade—there is the glass. She said there was something nasty in the straws. These.”

Phillips handed them to the detective. He wetted his finger from them, touched his tongue and spat out quickly.

“Yes,” he said, and went out by the little door.

===Chinese===
「再说那位女士，」——米多斯探长分明忧心忡忡——「你说她发了晕。她喝过什么没有？」

「橙汁汽水——杯子就在那儿。她说麦秆吸管里有股怪味儿。就是这几根。」

菲利普斯把吸管递给探长。探长用指头在管上蘸了蘸，点在舌尖上，随即飞快啐了出来。

「果然不错。」他说完，从那扇小门走了出去。

===Original===
Gonsalez, of course: but where had he gone, and how, with a drugged girl on his hands and the Child of the Snake? Gurther was immensely quick to strike, and an icy-hearted man: the presence of a woman would not save Leon.

===Chinese===
自然是冈萨雷斯：可是他去了哪里，又怎么脱的身——手里还压着一个被下了药的姑娘，外加那位「蛇之子」（Child of the Snake）？格瑟出手快得出奇，又是个心冷如冰的人：有个女人在场，救不了莱昂。

===Original===
“When the light went out—” began the waiter, and the trouble cleared from Mr. Meadows’ face.

“Of course—I had forgotten that,” he said softly. “The lights went out!”

===Chinese===
「灯灭的那会儿——」侍者刚一开口，米多斯先生脸上的愁云顿时散了。

「当然了——我把这个给忘了。」他轻声说。「灯灭了！」

===Original===
All the way back to the Yard he was trying to bring something from the back of his mind—something that was there, the smooth tip of it tantalizingly displayed, yet eluding every grasp. It had nothing to do with the lights—nor Gonsalez, nor yet the girl. Gurther? No. Nor Manfred? What was it? A name had been mentioned to him that day—it had a mysterious significance. A golden idol! He picked up the end of the thought… Johnny! Manfred’s one mystery. That was the dust which lay on all thought. And now that he remembered, he was disappointed. It was so ridiculously unimportant a matter to baffle him.

===Chinese===
一路回苏格兰场的途中，他一直在设法把一样东西从心底捞上来——那东西分明就在那儿，光滑的尖端撩人地露着一角，却次次都从掌心里溜走。这桩心事同灯无关——同冈萨雷斯无关，同那姑娘也无关。是格瑟？不是。是曼弗雷德？也不是。究竟是什么？那天有人对他提起过一个名字——那名字带着某种神秘的意味。金神像！他捻住了这缕思绪的线头……约翰尼！曼弗雷德那独一无二的秘密。原来蒙在他一切思绪上的那层尘埃，就是它。而这会儿既然想了起来，他反倒败兴了。竟是这么一桩微不足道得可笑的事，害他猜了半天。

===Original===
He left his companions at the corner of Curzon Street and went alone to the house. There was a streak of light showing between the curtains in the upstairs room. The passage was illuminated—Poiccart answered his ring at once.

“Yes, George and Leon were here a little time back—the girl? No, they said nothing about a girl. They looked rather worried, I thought. Miss Leicester, I suppose? Won’t you come in?”

===Chinese===
他在柯曾街街口同同伴们分手，独自走向那幢房子。楼上房间的窗帘缝里透出一线灯光。门道里亮着灯——他一按铃，波伊卡特立刻来开门。

「是的，乔治和莱昂方才回来过一趟——姑娘？没有，他们只字没提什么姑娘。我看他们神色颇为焦虑。是莱斯特小姐的事吧？不进来坐坐？」

===Original===
“No, I can’t wait. There’s a light in Manfred’s room.”

The ghost of a smile lit the heavy face and faded as instantly.

“My room also,” he said. “Butlers take vast liberties in the absence of their masters. Shall I give a message to George?”

“Ask him to call me at the Yard.”

===Chinese===
「不了，我等不得。曼弗雷德的房间里有灯。」

一丝笑影在那张沉甸甸的脸上亮了一亮，随即又即刻隐去。

「我的房间里也有灯。」他说。「主人不在跟前，管家享起特权来可没了边儿。要给乔治留话吗？」

「请他打电话到苏格兰场找我。」

===Original===
Poiccart closed the door on him; stopped in the passage to arrange a salver on the table and hung up a hat. All this Meadows saw through the fanlight and walking-stick periscope which is so easily fitted and can be of such value. And seeing, his doubts evaporated.

===Chinese===
波伊卡特关上了门；又在门道里停下来，把一只托盘在桌上摆好，挂起一顶帽子。这一切，米多斯都透过门楣的扇形气窗、借着一支手杖改造成的潜望镜看在眼里——这种装置装配极易，用处却不可小觑。看罢，他的疑虑烟消云散了。

===Original===
Poiccart went slowly up the stairs into the little office room, pulled back the curtains and opened the window at the top. The next second, the watching detective saw the light go out and went away.

“I’m sorry to keep you in the dark,” said Poiccart.

===Chinese===
波伊卡特缓步上楼，进了那间小办公室，拉开窗帘，把上面的窗子推开。下一秒，暗中守望的探长便看见灯灭了，于是转身离去。

「抱歉，让你们摸黑待了这么久。」波伊卡特说。

===Original===
The men who were in the room waited until the shutters were fast and the curtains pulled across, and then the light flashed on. White of face, her eyes closed, her breast scarcely moving, Mirabelle Leicester lay on the long settee. Her domino was a heap of shimmering green and scarlet on the floor, and Leon was gently sponging her face, George Manfred watching from the back of the settee, his brows wrinkled.

===Chinese===
屋里的人一直等到百叶窗扣严、窗帘拉拢，灯光这才倏地亮起。米拉贝尔·莱斯特躺在那张长沙发上，脸色惨白，双眼紧闭，胸口几乎看不出起伏。她那件多米诺斗篷褪在地板上，堆成一摊闪亮的红红绿绿。莱昂正拿海绵轻轻为她拭脸，乔治·曼弗雷德立在沙发背后注视着，眉头拧了起来。

===Original===
“Will she die?” he asked bluntly.

“I don’t know: they sometimes die of that stuff,” replied Leon cold-bloodedly. “She must have had it pretty raw. Gurther is a crude person.”

“What was it?” asked George.

Gonsalez spread out his disengaged hand in a gesture of uncertainty.

===Chinese===
「她会死吗？」他直截了当地问。

「不好说：挨那种东西的人，有时是会死的。」莱昂冷冷地答道。「她中的这一剂，成色只怕相当糙。格瑟本就是个下手不知轻重的粗人。」

「那是什么？」乔治问。

冈萨雷斯摊开空着的那只手，做了个说不准的手势。

===Original===
“If you can imagine morphia with a kick in it, it was that. I don’t know. I hope she doesn’t die: she is rather young—it would be the worst of bad luck.”

Poiccart stirred uneasily. He alone had within his soul what Leon would call “a trace” of sentiment.

===Chinese===
「你要是想象得出一种带后劲的吗啡，就是那玩意儿了。我也说不上来。但愿她别死：她还这么年轻——真死了，那就是倒霉透顶了。」

波伊卡特不安地挪了挪身子。三人之中，唯有他的灵魂里，还存着莱昂所谓的「一星半点」的多情。

===Original===
“Could we get Elver?” he asked anxiously, and Leon looked up with his boyish smile. “Growing onions in Seville has softened you, Raymondo *mio*!” He never failed in moments of great strain to taunt the heavy man with his two years of agricultural experiment, and they knew that the gibes were deliberately designed to steady his mind. “Onions are sentimental things—they make you cry: a vegetable *muchos simpatico*! This woman is alive!”

===Chinese===
「能不能把埃尔弗请来？」他急切地问。莱昂抬起头来，脸上挂着孩子气的笑。「在塞维利亚（Seville）种洋葱，把你的心肠种软了，我的雷蒙多（Raymondo *mio*）！」每逢情势紧绷的时刻，他从不忘拿那位沉甸甸的先生那两年农业试验的旧事打趣，而他们两人都心知，这些挖苦是存心拿来给他定心用的。「洋葱是多愁善感的东西——它们叫你流泪：一种*muchos simpatico*（极通人情）的蔬菜！这女人活着呢！」

===Original===
Her eyelids had fluttered twice. Leon lifted the bare arm, inserted the needle of a tiny hypodermic and pressed home the plunger.

“Tomorrow she will feel exactly as if she had been drunk,” he said calmly, “and in her mouth will be the taste of ten rank cigars. Oh, senorinetta, open thy beautiful eyes and look upon thy friends!”

The last sentence was in Spanish. She heard: the lids fluttered and rose.

===Chinese===
她的眼皮已颤动了两回。莱昂抬起她裸露的手臂，把一具小巧的皮下注射器的针头刺了进去，将针管推到了底。

「明天她醒过来，那滋味就跟大醉过一场一模一样，」他平静地说，「嘴里还会有十支劣质雪茄的味道。噢，塞尼奥丽内塔（senorinetta），睁开你美丽的眼睛，瞧瞧你的朋友们吧！」

最后这句话是用西班牙语说的。她听见了：眼皮一颤，睁了开来。

===Original===
“You’re a long way from Heavytree Farm, Miss Leicester.”

She looked up wonderingly into the kindly face of George Manfred.

“Where am I?” she asked faintly, and closed her eyes again with a grimace of pain.

“They always ask that—just as they do in books,” said Leon oracularly. “If they don’t say ‘Where am I?’ they ask for their mothers. She’s quite out of danger.”

===Chinese===
「莱斯特小姐，您离赫维特里农场可远着呢。」

她讶异地抬头，望着乔治·曼弗雷德那张和善的脸。

「我这是在哪儿？」她声音微弱地问，随即痛得蹙起眉，又闭上了眼睛。

「他们总免不了要问这一句——跟书里写的一模一样。」莱昂一副先知的口吻。「不问『我这是在哪儿？』的，就要喊妈妈。她已经完全脱离危险了。」

===Original===
One hand was on her wrist, another at the side of her neck.

“Remarkably regular. She has a good head—mathematical probably.”

“She is very beautiful,” said Poiccart in a hushed voice.

“All people are beautiful—just as all onions are beautiful. What is the difference between a lovely maid and the ugliest of duennas—what but a matter of pigmentation and activity of tissue? Beneath that, an astounding similarity of the circulatory, sustentacular, motorvascular—”

===Chinese===
他一只手搭着她的腕脉，另一只手按在她颈侧。

「规律得出奇。她有颗好脑袋——多半是块学数学的料。」

「她真美。」波伊卡特压低声音说。

「人人皆美——正如洋葱个个皆美。可爱的少女同最丑的陪侍嬷嬷（duenna）之间有什么分别——除了色素的深浅、组织的活力，还有什么分别？剥去这一层，那循环的、滋养的、血管运动的诸般系统，相似得叫人惊叹——」

===Original===
“How long have we got?” Manfred interrupted him, and Leon shook his head.

“I don’t know—not long, I should think. Of course, we could have told Meadows and he’d have turned out police reserves, but I should like to keep them out of it.”

“The Old Guard was there?”

“Every man jack of them—those tough lads! They will be here just as soon as the Herr Doktor discovers what is going forward. Now, I think you can travel. I want her out of the way.”

===Chinese===
「咱们还有多少时间？」曼弗雷德打断了他。莱昂摇了摇头。

「说不好——依我看，不长。咱们当然本可以告诉米多斯，他会把后备警力尽数调出来，可我宁可别把警方搅进来。」

「『老近卫军』的人当时都在场？」

「一个不落，全数到齐——那帮能打能扛的小子！只等那位『博士先生』（Herr Doktor）一发觉眼下出了什么事，他们转眼就会摸到这儿。好了，我看你可以上路了。我要把她送出这是非窝。」

===Original===
Stooping, he put his hands under her and lifted her. The strength in his frail body was a never-ending source of wonder to his two friends.

They followed him down the stairs and along the short passage, down another flight to the kitchen. Manfred opened a door and went out into the paved yard. There was a heavier door in the boundary wall. He opened this slowly and peeped out. Here was the inevitable mews. The sound of an engine running came from a garage nearby. Evidently somebody was on the look out for them. A long-bodied car drew up noiselessly and a woman got out. Beside the driver at the wheel sat two men.

===Chinese===
他俯下身，双手从她身下兜住，把她抱了起来。他那羸弱身躯里蕴着的力气，永远是两位朋友惊叹不尽的源泉。

他们跟着他下楼，穿过短短的门道，又下一段楼梯进了厨房。曼弗雷德打开一扇门，走进铺石板的院子。围墙上有一扇更厚实的门。他把门缓缓推开，探头张望。外面照例又是马厩巷。附近的车库里传来引擎运转的声音，显然有人正替他们望风。一辆车身修长的汽车悄没声儿地停住，一个女人下了车。方向盘旁司机身边坐着两个男人。

===Original===
“I think you’ll just miss the real excitement,” said Gonsalez, and then to the nurse he gave a few words of instruction and closed the door on her.

“Take the direct road,” he said to the driver. “Swindon—Gloucester. Good night.”

“Good night, sir.”

He watched anxiously as the machine swung into the main road. Still he waited, his head bent. Two minutes went by, and the faint sound of a motor-horn, a long blast and a short, and he sighed.

“They’re clear of the danger zone,” he said.

===Chinese===
「我看，你们恰好要错过真正热闹的好戏了。」冈萨雷斯说。接着他对那护士交代了几句，随手替她关上了车门。

「走直达的大路。」他对司机说。「斯温登（Swindon）——格洛斯特。晚安。」

「晚安，先生。」

他忧心忡忡地望着汽车拐上大路，仍旧低着头等。两分钟过去，远处传来隐隐的汽车喇叭声，一长一短，他这才舒了口气。

「他们脱离危险地带了。」他说。

===Original===
*Plop!*

He saw the flash, heard the smack of the bullet as it struck the door, and his hand stiffened. There was a thudding sound—a scream of pain from a dark corner of the mews and the sound of voices. Leon drew back into the yard and bolted the door.

“He had a new kind of silencer. Oberzohn is rather a clever old bird. But my air pistol against their gun for noiselessness.”

===Chinese===
*噗！*

他看见火光一闪，听见子弹打在门上的那声脆响，手登时僵住。接着是一阵咚咚的闷响——马厩巷一个黑暗的角落里迸出一声惨叫，跟着响起人语嘈杂。莱昂退回院里，闩上了门。

「那家伙使的是一种新式消音器。欧伯佐恩这只老鸟精得很。不过论无声，我的气手枪对上他们那杆枪，还更胜一筹。」

===Original===
“I didn’t expect the attack from that end of the mews.” Manfred was slipping a Browning back to his pocket.

“If they had come from the other end the car would not have passed—I’d like to get one of those silencers.”

They went into the house. Poiccart had already extinguished the passage light.

===Chinese===
「我没料到他们会从马厩巷那一头打过来。」曼弗雷德正把一支勃朗宁手枪塞回口袋。

「他们要是从另一头过来，那辆车就过不去了——我倒想弄一只那样的消音器来。」

他们回到屋里。波伊卡特已经熄掉了门道里的灯。

===Original===
“You hit your man—does that thing kill?”

“By accident—it is possible. I aimed at his stomach: I fear that I hit him in the head. He would not have squealed for a stomach wound. I fear he is alive.”

===Chinese===
「你打中了你那位——那玩意儿打得死人吗？」

「失手致死——有可能。我瞄的是他的肚子：恐怕打中的是脑袋。肚子上挨一下，他是不会嚎的。我怕他还活着。」

===Original===
He felt his way up the stairs and took up the telephone. Immediately a voice said, “Number?”

“Give me 8877 Treasury.”

He waited, and then a different voice asked: “Yes—Scotland Yard speaking.”

“Can you give me Mr. Meadows?”

===Chinese===
他摸黑上楼，拿起电话。立刻有个声音说道：「几号？」

「给我接财政部 8877。」

他等了等，随即另一个声音问：「喂——这里是苏格兰场。」

「能给我找米多斯先生吗？」

===Original===
Manfred was watching him frowningly.

“That you, Meadows? … They have shot Leon Gonsalez—can you send police reserves and an ambulance?”

“At once.”

Leon hung up the receiver, hugging himself.

===Chinese===
曼弗雷德皱着眉注视着他。

「是你吗，米多斯？……他们开枪打中了莱昂·冈萨雷斯——能不能派后备警力和一辆救护车来？」

「马上。」

莱昂挂上听筒，搂住了自己的双臂。

===Original===
“The idea being—?” said Poiccart.

“These people are clever.” Leon’s voice was charged with admiration. “They haven’t cut the wires—they’ve simply tapped it at one end and thrown it out of order on the exchange side.”

“Phew!” Manfred whistled. “You deceived me—you were talking to Oberzohn?”

“Captain Monty and Lew Cuccini. They may or may not be deceived, but if they aren’t, we shall know all about it.”

===Chinese===
「这是何用意——？」波伊卡特说。

「这些人精明得很。」莱昂的声气里满是叹服。「他们没有剪断线路——只是在线路一头搭了线，又把交换台那一端搅得失了灵。」

「嚯！」曼弗雷德打了个呼哨。「连我都瞒过了——你方才是在同欧伯佐恩通话？」

「是同蒙蒂上尉和卢·库奇尼。他们或许上了当，或许没有；可若是没上当，咱们立刻就会领教到。」

===Original===
He stopped dead. There was a knock on the front door, a single, heavy knock. Leon grinned delightedly.

“One of us is now supposed to open an upper window cautiously and look out, whereupon he is instantly gunned. I’m going to give these fellows a scare.”

===Chinese===
他猛地收住话头。前门上响起叩门声，沉沉的一声，只一下。莱昂咧嘴乐了。

「按他们的算盘，这会儿我们当中该有一个人小心翼翼地推开楼上一扇窗子探头张望，随即便被乱枪打死。我要吓唬吓唬这帮家伙。」

===Original===
He ran up the stairs to the top floor, and on the landing, outside an attic door, pulled at a rope. A fire ladder lying flat against the ceiling came down, and at the same time a small skylight opened. Leon went into the room, and his pocket-lamp located what he needed: a small papier-mâché cylinder, not unlike a seven-pound shell. With this on his arm, he climbed up the ladder on to the roof, fixed the cylinder on a flat surface, and, striking a match, lit a touch-paper. The paper sizzled and spluttered, there was a sudden flash and “boom!” a dull explosion, and a white ball shot up into the sky, described a graceful curve and burst into a shower of brilliant crimson stars. He waited till the last died out; then, with the hot cylinder under his arm, descended the ladder, released the rope that held it in place, and returned to his two friends.

===Chinese===
他奔上楼梯直抵顶楼，在楼梯平台上、一扇阁楼门外拉动一根绳索。一架平贴在天花板上的消防梯应声落下，同时一扇小天窗打开了。莱昂走进房里，手电照见了他要找的东西：一个小小的纸壳圆筒，同七磅炮弹颇有几分相像。他把圆筒夹在臂弯，顺梯子爬上屋顶，将圆筒安放在一处平面上，划根火柴，点燃了引火纸。纸捻咝咝作响、噼啪溅着火星，忽地一道闪光，「嘭！」一声闷响，一颗白球蹿上夜空，划出一道曼妙的弧线，绽作漫天绚烂的猩红星雨。他直等到最后一颗星子熄灭，才夹着滚烫的圆筒爬下梯子，放开固定梯子的绳索，回到两位朋友身边。

===Original===
“They will imagine a secret arrangement of signals with the police,” he said; “unless my knowledge of their psychology is at fault, we shall not be bothered again.”

Ten minutes later there was another knock at the door, peremptory, almost official in its character.

“This,” said Leon, “is a policeman to summon us for discharging fireworks in the public street!”

===Chinese===
「他们会以为咱们同警方有一套秘密的信号联络，」他说，「除非我对他们心理的揣摩全然失灵，否则他们再不敢来搅扰了。」

十分钟后，门上又响起叩门声，声气专断，几乎带着官府的做派。

「这一位嘛，」莱昂说，「是位警察，来传咱们问罪——罪名是当街燃放烟花！」

===Original===
He ran lightly down into the hall and without hesitation pulled open the door. A tall, helmeted figure stood on the doorstep, notebook in hand.

“Are you the gentleman that let off that rocket—” he began.

Leon walked past him, and looked up and down Curzon Street. As he had expected, the Old Guard had vanished.

===Chinese===
他脚步轻快地奔下楼进了门厅，毫不迟疑地拉开门。门阶上立着一个戴警盔的高大身影，手里拿着记事簿。

「放那枚烟花的就是您这位先生吧——」他开口道。

莱昂从他身边走过去，朝柯曾街两头张望。正如他所料，「老近卫军」的人马已经无影无踪。

## XI Gurther / 十一 格瑟
===Original===
Monty Newton dragged himself home, a weary, angry man, and let himself in with his key. He found the footman lying on the floor of the hall asleep, his greatcoat pulled over him, and stirred him to wakefulness with the toe of his boot.

===Chinese===
蒙蒂·牛顿拖着一副疲惫又愠怒的身子挨回家门，掏出钥匙自己开了门。只见男仆躺在门厅地板上睡得正沉，一件厚大衣拉过来盖在身上，他便用靴尖把人捅醒。

===Original===
“Get up,” he growled. “Anybody been here?”

Fred rose, a little dazed, rubbing his eyes.

===Chinese===
「起来。」他低吼道。「有人来过吗？」

弗雷德爬起身来，揉着眼睛，还有几分发懵。

===Original===
“The old man’s in the drawing-room,” he said, and his employer passed on without another word.

===Chinese===
「老头儿在客厅里。」他说。东家一言不发，径自走了过去。

===Original===
As he opened the door, he saw that all the lights in the drawing-room were lit. Dr. Oberzohn had pulled a small table near the fire, and before this he sat bolt upright, a tiny chessboard before him, immersed in a problem. He looked across to the newcomer for a second and then resumed his study of the board, made a move …

===Chinese===
他推开房门，只见客厅里灯火通明。欧伯佐恩博士已把一张小桌拉到炉火近旁，直挺挺地坐在桌前，面前摆着一副小小的棋盘，正沉浸在一道排局里。他朝进来的人望了一秒钟，随即又低头研究起棋局，走了一步……

===Original===
“Ach!” he said in tones of satisfaction. “Leskina was wrong! It is possible to mate in five moves!”

===Chinese===
「啊！」他满含得意地说。「莱斯基纳（Leskina）错了！五步之内是可以将死的！」

===Original===
He pushed the chessmen into confusion and turned squarely to face Newton.

===Chinese===
他把棋子胡乱搅作一堆，转过身来，正对着牛顿。

===Original===
“Well, have you concluded these matters satisfactorily?”

===Chinese===
「嗯，那些事，你办得圆满了吗？」

===Original===
“He brought up the reserves,” said Monty, unlocking a tantalus on a side table and helping himself liberally to whisky. “They got Cuccini through the jaw. Nothing serious.”

===Chinese===
「他把后备警力搬来了。」蒙蒂说着，打开边桌上一座带锁酒柜（tantalus），痛痛快快地给自己斟上一大杯威士忌。「库奇尼叫人打穿了下巴。不碍事。」

===Original===
Dr. Oberzohn laid his bony hands on his knees.

===Chinese===
欧伯佐恩博士把两只嶙峋的手搁在膝上。

===Original===
“Gurther must be disciplined,” he said. “Obviously he has lost his nerve; and when a man loses his nerve also he loses his sense of time. And his timing—how deplorable! The car had not arrived; my excellent police had not taken position … deplorable!”

===Chinese===
「格瑟必须受惩戒。」他说。「他显然失了胆气；人一失了胆气，也就失了时间的感觉。还有他掐的时辰——多可叹！车子还没到；我那支出色的警察还没就位……可叹哪！」

===Original===
“The police are after him: I suppose you know that?” Newton looked over his glass.

Dr. Oberzohn nodded.

===Chinese===
「警方正在追捕他：这个我料想你知道吧？」牛顿的目光从酒杯上方投过去。

欧伯佐恩点了点头。

===Original===
“The extradition so cleverly avoided is now accomplished. But Gurther is too good a man to be lost. I have arranged a hiding-place for him. He is of many uses.”

===Chinese===
「先前避得那般巧妙的引渡，如今是办成了。可格瑟是个人才，损失不得。我已给他安排下一处藏身之地。他有用处的地方多得很。」

===Original===
“Where did he go?”

Dr. Oberzohn’s eyebrows wrinkled up and down.

===Chinese===
「他去哪儿了？」

欧伯佐恩的两道眉毛上下皱动。

===Original===
“Who knows?” he said. “He has the little machine. Maybe he has gone to the house—the green light in the top window will warn him and he will move carefully.”

===Chinese===
「谁知道呢？」他说。「那辆小汽车在他手里。也许他回宅子去了——顶楼窗口的绿灯会向他示警，他会小心行事的。」

===Original===
Newton walked to the window and looked out. Chester Square looked ghostly in the grey light of dawn. And then, out of the shadows, he saw a figure move and walk slowly towards the south side of the square. “They’re watching this house,” he said, and laughed.

===Chinese===
牛顿踱到窗前，向外望去。黎明的灰光里，切斯特广场一片鬼影幢幢。这时，暗影中有一条人影动了起来，缓缓朝广场南侧走去。「他们在盯这座宅子。」他说罢笑了。

===Original===
“Where is my young lady?” asked Oberzohn, who was staring glumly into the fire.

===Chinese===
「我那位年轻的小姐哪儿去了？」欧伯佐恩问道，他正阴沉沉地盯着炉火。

===Original===
“I don’t know … there was a car pulled out of the mews as one of our men ‘closed’ the entrance. She has probably gone back to Heavytree Farm, and you can sell that laboratory of yours. There is only one way now, and that’s the rough way. We have time—we can do a lot in six weeks. Villa is coming this morning—I wish we’d taken that idol from the trunk. That may put the police on to the right track.”

===Chinese===
「我不知道……就在咱们一个弟兄把巷口『封』上的当口，有辆汽车从马厩巷里冲了出去。她多半是回赫维特里农场去了，你那间实验室如今也可以出手卖掉了。眼下只剩一条路，就是来硬的。咱们有的是时间——六个星期里能干成不少事。维拉今天上午就到——当初真该把那尊神像从衣箱里取走的。那东西兴许会把警察引上正路。」

===Original===
Dr. Oberzohn pursed his lips as though he were going to whistle, but he was guilty of no such frivolity.

===Chinese===
欧伯佐恩博士抿起嘴唇，仿佛就要吹出一声呼哨，可他从不曾沾染这等轻浮的过失。

===Original===
“I am glad they found him,” he said precisely. “To them it will be a scent. What shall they think, but that the unfortunate Barberton had come upon an old native treasure-house? No, I do not fear that.” He shook his head. “Mostly I fear Mr. Johnson Lee and the American, Elijah Washington.”

===Chinese===
「他们发现了它，我很高兴。」他一字一顿地说。「在他们，那不过是一缕嗅迹。他们还能怎么想？无非是那位倒霉的巴伯顿撞上了一处土人的古老宝库罢了。不，这个我不怕。」他摇了摇头。「我怕的，主要是约翰逊·李先生和那个美国人伊莱贾·华盛顿。」

===Original===
He put his hand into his jacket pocket and took out a thin pad of letters. “Johnson Lee is for me difficult to understand. For what should a gentleman have to do with this boor that he writes so friendly letters to him?”

===Chinese===
他把手伸进外套口袋，掏出薄薄一叠信。「约翰逊·李这个人，于我是一桩难解之事。一位绅士，同这样一个粗人有何干系，竟给他写这般友好的书信？」

===Original===
“How did you get these?”

“Villa took them: it was one of the intelligent actions also to leave the statue.”

===Chinese===
「这些信你是怎么弄到手的？」

「维拉取来的：把那尊神像留下，也算是一着聪明的举措。」

===Original===
He passed one of the letters across to Newton. It was addressed “Await arrival, Poste Restante, Mosamedes.” The letter was written in a curiously round, boyish hand. Another remarkable fact was that it was perforated across the page at regular intervals, and upon the lines formed by this perforation Mr. Johnson Lee wrote.

===Chinese===
他把其中一封递给牛顿。信封上写的是「候抵埠领取，留局待取（Poste Restante），莫萨马德斯」。信是用一种圆圆的、孩子气十足的笔迹写成的，透着说不出的古怪。还有一桩出奇之处：信纸上每隔相等的距离便横穿着一行针孔，约翰逊·李先生的字，就落在这针孔连成的线上。

===Original===
> “**Dear B.**,” the letter ran, “I have instructed my banker to cable you £500. I hope this will carry you through and leave enough to pay your fare home. You may be sure that I shall not breathe a word, and your letters, of course, nobody in the house can read but me. Your story is amazing, and I advise you to come home at once and see Miss Leicester.
> “Your friend,
> “Johnson Lee.”

===Chinese===
> 「**亲爱的 B.**：」信上写道，「我已嘱托我的银行电汇 £500 与你。但愿此款足以济你困厄，并留有余资、充作归国旅费。你尽可放心，我决不吐露一字；你的来函，自然，舍下除我之外无人能识。你的际遇实在惊人，我劝你即刻归国，往见莱斯特小姐。
> 「你的朋友
> 「约翰逊·李。」

===Original===
The notepaper was headed “Rath Hall, January 13th.”

“They came to me today. If I had seen them before, there would have been no need for the regrettable happening.”

===Chinese===
信笺的抬头上印着「拉思府，1月13日」。

「这些信今天才到我手里。倘若我早些见到它们，那桩可憾之事原本就无需发生。」

===Original===
He looked thoughtfully at his friend. “They will be difficult: I had that expectation,” he said, and Monty knew that he referred to the Three Just Men. “Yet they are mortal also—remember that, my Newton: they are mortal also.”

===Chinese===
他若有所思地望着他的朋友。「他们会是劲敌：这一点我早有预料。」他说。蒙蒂知道，他指的是三义士。「然而他们也是凡人——记住这个，我的牛顿：他们也是凡人。」

===Original===
“As we are,” said Newton gloomily.

“That is a question,” said Oberzohn, “so far as I am concerned.”

===Chinese===
「我们也是。」牛顿阴郁地说。

「那就还是一个问题了，」欧伯佐恩说，「就我而论。」

===Original===
Dr. Oberzohn never jested; he spoke with the greatest calm and assurance. The other man could only stare at him.

===Chinese===
欧伯佐恩博士从不说笑；他说话时怀着最大限度的平静与笃定。对方只能干瞪着眼望着他。

===Original===
Although it was light, a green lamp showed clearly in the turret room of the doctor’s house as he came within sight of the ugly place. And, seeing that warning, he did not expect to be met in the passage by Gurther. The man had changed from his resplendent kit and was again in the soiled and shabby garments he had discarded the night before.

===Chinese===
天色已经大亮，可当博士走近、那座丑陋宅子映入眼帘时，塔楼房间里一盏绿灯依旧清晰可见。望见这一警号，他却没有料到会在门道里迎面遇上格瑟。那人已卸下那身光彩夺目的行头，重新穿回头天夜里扔下的那身又脏又旧的衣裳。

===Original===
“You have come, Gurther?”

“*Ja*, Herr Doktor.”

===Chinese===
「你来了，格瑟？」

「*Ja*, Herr Doktor.」

===Original===
“To my parlour!” barked Dr. Oberzohn, and marched ahead.

===Chinese===
「到我的客厅来！」欧伯佐恩博士喝道，迈开步子走在前头。

===Original===
Gurther followed him and stood with his back to the door, erect, his chin raised, his bright, curious eyes fixed on a point a few inches above his master’s head.

===Chinese===
格瑟跟着进去，背抵着门站定，身子笔直，下巴扬起，一双亮闪闪、盛满好奇的眼睛，盯在主人头顶上方几英寸处的一点。

===Original===
“Tell me now.” The doctor’s ungainly face was working ludicrously.

===Chinese===
「现在说吧。」博士那张粗笨的脸可笑地抽动着。

===Original===
“I saw the man and struck, Herr Doktor, and then the lights went out and I went to the floor, expecting him to shoot. … I think he must have taken the gracious lady. I did not see, for there was a palm between us. I returned at once to the greater hall, and walked through the people on the floor. They were very frightened.”

===Chinese===
「我看见了那个人，便出击了，博士先生，随后灯灭了，我便扑倒在地上，防着他开枪。……我想，那位雍容的小姐必定是被他带走了。我没有看见，因为我们两人中间隔着一棵棕榈。我立即返回大厅，从舞池上的人群中间穿行而过。他们个个吓得不轻。」

===Original===
“You saw them?”

“Yes, Herr Doktor,” said Gurther. “It is not difficult for me to see in the dark. After that I ran to the other entrance, but they were gone.”

===Chinese===
「你看见他们了？」

「是的，博士先生。」格瑟说。「在黑暗里看东西，于我并非难事。此后我奔向另一处入口，可他们已经走了。」

===Original===
“Come here.”

The man took two stilted paces towards the doctor and Oberzohn struck him twice in the face with the flat of his hand. Not a muscle of the man’s face moved: he stood erect, his lips framed in a half-grin, his curious eyes staring straight ahead.

===Chinese===
「过来。」

那人迈着僵硬的步子朝博士走了两步，欧伯佐恩挥掌在他脸上扇了两记。那人脸上竟没有一块肌肉动弹：他笔直地立着，嘴唇噙出半个咧笑，那双好奇的眼睛直勾勾望着正前方。

===Original===
“That is for bad time, Gurther. Nobody saw you return?”

===Chinese===
「这是罚你时辰掐得不准，格瑟。没人看见你回来吧？」

===Original===
“No, Herr Doktor, I came on foot.”

“You saw the light?”

“Yes, Herr Doktor, and I thought it best to be here.”

===Chinese===
「没有，博士先生，我是步行回来的。」

「你看见绿灯了？」

「是的，博士先生，我想最好还是到这里来。」

===Original===
“You were right,” said Oberzohn. “March!”

===Chinese===
「你想得对。」欧伯佐恩说。「开步走！」

===Original===
He went into the forbidden room, turned the key, and passed into the super-heated atmosphere. Gurther stood attentively at the door. Presently the doctor came out, carrying a long case covered with baize under his arm. He handed it to the waiting man, went into the room, and, after a few minutes’ absence, returned with a second case, a little larger.

===Chinese===
他走进那间禁室，拧动钥匙，进入了那片灼热的空气里。格瑟毕恭毕敬地守在门边。不多时，博士出来了，腋下夹着一只包着台面呢的长匣。他把匣子交给候着的人，又进屋去，隔了几分钟，捧出第二只匣子，比先前那只略大些。

===Original===
“March!” he said.

===Chinese===
「开步走！」他说。

===Original===
Gurther followed him out of the house and across the rank, weed-grown “garden” towards the factory. A white mist had rolled up from the canal, and factory and grounds lay under the veil.

===Chinese===
格瑟跟着他出了宅子，穿过那片荒秽的、杂草疯长的「花园」，朝那座烧剩的货栈走去。一团白雾从运河上滚滚涌起，货栈连同整片场地都罩在这层雾幕底下。

===Original===
He led the way through an oblong gap in the wall where once a door had stood, and followed a tortuous course through the blackened beams and twisted girders that littered the floor. Only a halfhearted attempt had been made to clear up the wreckage after the fire, and the floor was ankle-deep in the charred shreds of burnt cloth. Near the far end of the building, Oberzohn stopped, put down his box and pushed aside the ashes with his foot until he had cleared a space about three feet square. Stooping, he grasped an iron ring and pulled, and a flagstone came up with scarcely any effort, for it was well counter-weighted. He took up the box again and descended the stone stairs, stopping only to turn on a light.

===Chinese===
他在前头引路，从墙上一扇门旧日所在的长方形豁口里钻进去，沿着一条七拐八绕的路径，在满地狼藉的焦梁扭柱之间穿行。大火之后，清理废墟的活计只敷敷衍衍做过一回，地板上烧毁布匹的焦屑烂片，深得没过脚踝。挨近大楼另一头，欧伯佐恩停了下来，放下匣子，用脚把灰烬拨开，清出一块三英尺见方的地面。他俯下身，攥住一只铁环往上一提，一块石板几乎不费力气便掀了起来——它配着恰到好处的配重。他重新拎起匣子，顺石阶而下，只停下来开了盏灯。

===Original===
The vaults of the store had been practically untouched by the fire. There were shelves that still carried dusty bales of cotton goods. Oberzohn was in a hurry. He crossed the stone floor in two strides, pulled down the bar of another door, and, walking into the darkness, deposited his box on the floor.

===Chinese===
货栈的地窖库房几乎未受火灾波及。有几排架子上至今还堆着落满灰尘的棉布货包。欧伯佐恩行色匆匆。他两大步跨过石地，拉下另一道门上的门闩，走进黑暗里，把匣子搁在地上。

===Original===
The electric power of the factory had, in the old days, been carried on two distinct circuits, and the connection with the vaults was practically untouched by the explosion.

===Chinese===
当年，货栈的电力由两条各自独立的线路输送，通向地窖的那一路几乎未受爆炸损伤。

===Original===
They were in a smaller room now, fairly comfortably furnished. Gurther knew it well, for it was here that he had spent the greater part of his first six months in England. Ventilation came through three small gratings near the roof. There was a furnace, and, as Gurther knew, an ample supply of fuel in one of the three cellars that opened into the vault.

===Chinese===
他们此刻已在一间小些的屋子里，陈设得相当舒适。格瑟对这里了如指掌，因为他来英国头六个月的大半光阴，便是在此处打发的。通风靠的是屋顶附近三扇小小的格栅。屋里有一只火炉；而且正如格瑟所知，通入库房的那三间小地窖中，有一间囤着充裕的燃料。

===Original===
“Here will you stay until I send for you,” said Oberzohn. “Tonight, perhaps, after they have searched. You have a pistol?”

“*Ja*, Herr Doktor.”

===Chinese===
「你就在这儿待着，等我派人来叫。」欧伯佐恩说。「也许就在今夜，等他们搜完之后。你有手枪吗？」

「*Ja*, Herr Doktor.」

===Original===
“Food, water, bedding—all you need.” Oberzohn jerked open another of the cellars and took stock of the larder. “Tonight I may come for you—tomorrow night—who knows? You will light the fire at once.” He pointed to the two baize-covered boxes. “Good morning, Gurther.”

===Chinese===
「吃的、喝的、铺盖——一应所需。」欧伯佐恩猛地拉开另一间小地窖的门，把存放的食物检视了一遍。「今夜我或许来接你——明夜——谁知道呢？你要立刻把炉火生起来。」他指了指那两只包着台面呢的匣子。「早安，格瑟。」

===Original===
“Good morning, Herr Doktor.”

===Chinese===
「早安，博士先生。」

===Original===
Oberzohn went up to the factory level, dropped the trap and his foot pushed back the ashes which hid its presence, and with a cautious look round he crossed the field to his house. He was hardly in his study before the first police car came bumping along the lane.

===Chinese===
欧伯佐恩上到货栈的地面层，放落那扇活板门，又用脚把灰烬拨回原处，掩去它的踪迹，然后警惕地环顾四周，穿过那片空地回到自家宅子。他前脚刚进书房，第一辆警车便顺着小巷颠簸着开过来了。

## XII Leon Theorizes / 十二 莱昂的推断
===Original===
Making inquiries, Detective-Inspector Meadows discovered that, on the previous evening at eight o’clock, two men had called upon Barberton. The first of these was described as tall and rather aristocratic in appearance. He wore dark, horn-rimmed spectacles. The hotel manager thought he might have been an invalid, for he walked with a stick. The second man seemed to have been a servant of some kind, for he spoke respectfully to the visitor.

“No, he gave no name, Mr. Meadows,” said the manager. “I told him of the terrible thing which had happened to Mr. Barberton, and he was so upset that I didn’t like to press the question.”

Meadows was on his circuitous way to Curzon Street when he heard this, and he arrived in time for breakfast. Manfred’s servants regarded it as the one eccentricity of an otherwise normal gentleman that he invariably breakfasted with his butler and chauffeur. This matter had been discussed threadbare in the tiny servants’ hall, and it no longer excited comment when Manfred telephoned down to the lower regions and asked for another plate.

===Chinese===
米多斯探长四处查访，得知前一天晚上八点钟，有两个人登门拜访过巴伯顿。据描述，头一个生得高大，相貌颇有贵族气派，戴一副深色角框眼镜。旅馆经理琢磨他许是个病弱之人，因为他走路拄着手杖。第二个瞧着像是什么仆从之流，因为他对那位访客说话必恭必敬。

「没有，他没留姓名，米多斯先生。」经理说。「我把巴伯顿先生遭的那桩可怕事情讲给他听，他难过得厉害，我便不好意思再追问下去。」

米多斯听到这话时，正绕着弯儿往柯曾街去，赶到时正好赶上早餐。在曼弗雷德的仆役们眼里，这位其余方面再正常不过的绅士，只有一桩怪癖：他向来同自家的管家和司机共进早餐。这件事在那间小小的仆役厅里早已议论得烂熟，因此当曼弗雷德打电话到楼下、吩咐再添一副餐具时，已没有谁再觉得稀奇。

===Original===
The Triangle were in cheerful mood. Leon Gonsalez was especially bright and amusing, as he invariably was after such a night as he had spent.

“We searched Oberzohn’s house from cellar to attic,” said Meadows when the plate had been laid.

“And of course you found nothing. The elegant Gurther?”

“He wasn’t there. That fellow will keep at a distance if he knows that there’s a warrant out for him. I suspect some sort of signal. There was a very bright green light burning in one of those ridiculous Gothic turrets.”

===Chinese===
「三角侦探社」的三位正心情畅快。莱昂·冈萨雷斯尤其神采飞扬、妙语如珠——凡是熬过他昨夜那种夜晚之后，他一概如此。

「我们把欧伯佐恩的宅子上上下下搜了个遍，从地窖直搜到阁楼。」餐具摆好之后，米多斯说。

「而你们当然什么也没搜着。那位衣冠楚楚的格瑟先生呢？」

「他不在。那家伙只要知道已经对他签发了拘票，自会躲得远远的。我疑心这里头有什么信号。那几座可笑的哥特式角塔里，有一座正亮着一盏极亮的绿灯。」

===Original===
Manfred stifled a yawn. “Gurther went back soon after midnight,” he said, “and was there until Oberzohn’s return.”

“Are you sure?” asked the astonished detective.

Leon nodded, his eyes twinkling. “After that, one of those infernal river mists blotted out observation,” he said, “but I should imagine Herr Gurther is not far away. Did you see his companion, Pfeiffer?”

Meadows nodded. “Yes, he was cleaning boots when I arrived.”

“How picturesque!” said Gonsalez. “I think he will have a valet the next time he goes to prison, unless the system has altered since your days, George?”

===Chinese===
曼弗雷德忍住一个哈欠。「格瑟午夜刚过就回去了，」他说，「一直待到欧伯佐恩回来。」

「你敢肯定？」探长愕然问道。

莱昂点头，眼里闪着狡黠的光。「在那之后，一场可恶的河雾把视线遮了个干净，」他说，「不过依我想，格瑟先生就走不远。你见到他那同伴法伊弗了吗？」

米多斯点头。「见到了，我到的时候，他正在刷靴子。」

「好一幅生动的画面！」冈萨雷斯说。「我想他下回再进监狱，就要带个贴身仆人了——除非打乔治你在那儿的日子起，狱里的规矩改了？」

===Original===
George Manfred, who had once occupied the condemned cell in Chelmsford Prison, smiled.

“An interesting man, Gurther,” mused Gonsalez. “I have a feeling that he will escape hanging. So you could not find him? I found him last night. But for the lady, who was both an impediment and an interest, we might have put a period to his activities.” He caught Meadows’ eye. “I should have handed him to you, of course.”

“Of course,” said the detective dryly.

“A remarkable man, but nervous. You are going to see Mr. Johnson Lee?”

===Chinese===
乔治·曼弗雷德——他从前在切姆斯福德监狱（Chelmsford Prison）坐过死囚牢——微微一笑。

「格瑟这人很有意思。」冈萨雷斯若有所思。「我有一种预感：他会逃过绞刑。这么说，你们找不到他？我昨夜倒是找到他了。要不是那位女士——她既是个妨碍，又是份牵挂——我们本可以给他那摊营生画上句号。」他迎住米多斯的目光。「当然，我本该把他交到你手上。」

「那当然。」探长干巴巴地说。

「一个非同寻常的人物，就是神经过敏。你要去见约翰逊·李先生了吧？」

===Original===
“What made you say that?” asked the detective in astonishment, for he had not as yet confided his intention to the three men.

“He will surprise you,” said Leon. “Tell me, Mr. Meadows: when you and George so thoroughly and carefully searched Barberton’s box, did you find anything that was suggestive of his being a cobbler, let us say—or a bookbinder?”

“I think in his sister’s letter there was a reference to the books he had made. I found nothing particular except an awl and a long oblong of wood which was covered with pinpricks. As a matter of fact, when I saw it my first thought was that, living the kind of life he must have done in the wilderness, it was rather handy to be able to repair his own shoes. The idea of bookbinding is a new one.”

“I should say he never bound a book in his life, in the ordinary sense of the word,” remarked Manfred; “and as Leon says, you will find Johnson Lee a very surprising man.”

===Chinese===
「你怎么会说出这话？」探长惊问，因为他至今还不曾向这三位吐露过自己的打算。

「他会让你吃一惊的。」莱昂说。「我问你，米多斯先生：你同乔治把巴伯顿那口箱子搜得那么彻底、那么仔细，可曾搜出什么东西，叫人联想到他是个鞋匠，或者，打个比方——是个装订书籍的？」

「我记得他妹妹的信里提到过他做的那些本子。除了一把锥子和一块布满针孔的长条木板，我没发现什么特别的物件。说实话，我一见那东西，头一个念头是：他在荒野里过的必是那种日子，能自己动手修鞋，倒也省事。装订书籍这个想法，倒是新鲜。」

「依我看，他这辈子从没装订过一本书——就这个词通常的意义而言。」曼弗雷德说。「而且正如莱昂所说，你会发现约翰逊·李是个非常出人意料的人物。」

===Original===
“Do you know him?”

Manfred nodded gravely.

“I have just been on the telephone to him,” he said. “You’ll have to be careful of Mr. Lee, Meadows. Our friend the snake may be biting his way, and will, if he hears a breath of suspicion that he was in Barberton’s confidence.”

The detective put down his knife and fork.

===Chinese===
「你们认识他？」

曼弗雷德郑重地点头。

「我方才还在同他通电话。」他说。「对李先生你可得当心，米多斯。咱们那位『毒蛇』朋友没准儿正循着踪迹一路咬过去；只要让他听到一丝风声，疑心李先生是巴伯顿的心腹，他就必定下口。」

探长放下了刀叉。

===Original===
“I wish you fellows would stop being mysterious,” he said, half annoyed, half amused. “What is behind this business? You talk of the snake as though you could lay your hands on him.”

“And we could,” they said in unison.

“Who is he?” challenged the detective.

“The Herr Doktor,” smiled Gonsalez.

“Oberzohn?”

===Chinese===
「我真希望你们几位别再这么神神秘秘的了。」他说，半是着恼，半是好笑。「这桩买卖背后到底是什么名堂？你们谈起那条『蛇』来，口口声声就像一伸手就能把他拿住似的。」

「而我们确实拿得住。」三人异口同声。

「他是谁？」探长质问。

「『博士先生』。」冈萨雷斯微笑道。

「欧伯佐恩？」

===Original===
Leon nodded.

“I thought you would have discovered that by connecting the original three murders together—and murders they were. First”—he ticked the names off on his fingers—“we have a stockbroker. This gentleman was a wealthy speculator who occasionally financed highly questionable deals. Six months before his death he drew from the bank a very large sum of money in notes. By an odd coincidence the bank clerk, going out to luncheon, saw his client and Oberzohn driving past in a taxicab, and as they came abreast he saw a large blue envelope go into Oberzohn’s pocket. The money had been put into a blue envelope when it was drawn. The broker had financed the doctor, and when the scheme failed and the money was lost, he not unnaturally asked for its return. He trusted Oberzohn not at all; carried his receipt about in his pocket, and never went anywhere unless he was armed—that fact did not emerge at the inquest, but you know it is true.”

Meadows nodded.

===Chinese===
莱昂点头。

「我原以为，只消把最初那三桩命案串到一处，你们早该发现了——而且那的的确确是谋杀。头一件，」他扳着手指一一点过，「是一位股票经纪人。这位先生是个有钱的投机家，时不时给一些极其可疑的买卖出本钱。死前六个月，他从银行提取了一大笔钞票。说来也巧，那位银行职员出门吃午饭，恰见他的客户同欧伯佐恩同乘一辆出租汽车驶过；两车并齐的一瞬，他看见一只大大的蓝信封塞进了欧伯佐恩的口袋。那笔钱提取的时候，就装在一只蓝信封里。经纪人给博士垫过本钱，等到那桩买卖砸了锅、钱赔了个精光，他开口讨还，原是人情之常。他对欧伯佐恩半点不信；收据成天揣在口袋里，出门必带枪——这个事实验尸庭审没有抖出来，可你知道它是真的。」

米多斯点头。

===Original===
“He threatened Oberzohn with exposure at a meeting they had in Winchester Street, on the day of his death. That night he returns from a theatre or from his club, and is found dead on the doorstep. No receipt is found. What follows?

“A man, a notorious blackmailer, homeless and penniless, was walking along the Bayswater Road, probably looking for easy money, when he saw the broker’s car going into Orme Place. He followed on the off-chance of begging a few coppers. The chauffeur saw him. The tramp, on the other hand, must have seen something else. He slept the next night at Rowton House, told a friend, who had been in prison with him, that he had a million pounds as good as in his hand. …”

Meadows laughed helplessly.

===Chinese===
「送命那天，他们在温彻斯特街（Winchester Street）会过一面，他以揭发相胁。当天夜里，他从戏院或是俱乐部回来，便被人发现死在自家门阶上。收据遍寻无着。往下呢？

「有一个人，一个声名狼藉的敲诈犯，无家可归，身无分文，正沿着贝斯沃特路（Bayswater Road）走，多半在打来得容易的钱的主意，恰好看见经纪人的汽车拐进了奥姆广场。他跟了上去，指望讨几个铜板。司机瞧见了他。而那个流浪汉呢，必定是看见了别的什么。第二夜他宿在罗顿宿舍（Rowton House），对一个同他蹲过一所监狱的朋友说，一百万英镑眼看就攥在他手里了……」

米多斯无可奈何地笑了。

===Original===
“Your system of investigation is evidently more thorough than ours.”

“It is complementary to yours,” said George quietly. “Go on, Leon.”

“Now what happened to our friend the burglar? He evidently saw somebody in Orme Place whom he either recognized or trailed to his home. For the next day or two he was in and out of public telephone booths, though no number has been traced. He goes to Hyde Park, obviously by appointment—and the snakebites!

===Chinese===
「你们这一套侦查路数，显然比我们的彻底。」

「同你们那套正好互补。」乔治不动声色地说。「接着讲，莱昂。」

「那么，咱们那位夜盗朋友又遭了什么？他分明是在奥姆广场看见了什么人——要么认了出来，要么一路尾随到了那人家里。此后一两天，他进进出出的都是公用电话亭，尽管没有一个号码被追查出来。他去了海德公园，显见是应约而去——于是『毒蛇』咬杀！

===Original===
“There was another danger to the confederacy. The bank clerk, learning of the death of the client, is troubled. I have proof that he called Oberzohn on the phone. If you remember, when the broker’s affairs were gone into, it was found that he was almost insolvent. A large sum of money had been drawn out of the bank and paid to ‘X.’ The certainty that he knew who ‘X.’ was, worried this decent bank clerk, and he called Oberzohn, probably to ask him why he had not made a statement. On the day he telephoned the snake man, that day he died.”

The detective was listening in silent wonder.

“It sounds like a page out of a sensational novel,” he said, “yet it hangs together.”

===Chinese===
「对那个团伙来说，还另有一层危险。银行职员得知客户死讯，心下不安。我有证据表明，他给欧伯佐恩打过电话。你若记得，经纪人的账目一经清查，便发现他几乎已经资不抵债。一大笔钱从银行提出，付给了一位『X』。他分明知道『X』是谁——这份知晓压在这位正派的银行职员心头，令他寝食难安，于是他给欧伯佐恩去电，多半是要问他为何至今不发一句声明。他给那位『蛇人』去电话的当天，就在那一天，他死了。」

探长默然听着，惊叹不已。

「听着像惊险小说里裁下来的一页，」他说，「可它环环相扣。」

===Original===
“It hangs together because it is true.” Poiccart’s deep voice broke into the conversation. “This has been Oberzohn’s method all his life. He is strong for logic, and there is no more logical action in the world than the destruction of those who threaten your safety and life.”

Meadows pushed away his plate, his breakfast half eaten.

“Proof,” he said briefly.

“What proof can you have, my dear fellow?” scoffed Leon.

===Chinese===
「它环环相扣，因为它是真的。」波伊卡特低沉的嗓音插了进来。「这是欧伯佐恩一辈子的行事法门。他崇尚逻辑，而普天之下再没有比除掉威胁你安全与性命的人更合乎逻辑的举动了。」

米多斯把盘子推开，早餐只吃了一半。

「证据。」他言简意赅。

「你能有什么证据，我亲爱的伙计？」莱昂嘲道。

===Original===
“The proof is the snake,” persisted Meadows. “Show me how he could educate a deadly snake to strike, as he did, when the victim was under close observation, as in the case of Barberton, and I will believe you.”

The Three looked at one another and smiled together. “One of these days I will show you,” said Leon. “They have certainly tamed their snake! He can move so quickly that the human eye cannot follow him. Always he bites on the most vital part, and at the most favourable time. He struck at me last night, but missed me. The next time he strikes”—he was speaking slowly and looking at the detective through the veriest slits of his half-closed eyelids—“the next time he strikes, not all Scotland Yard on the one side, nor his agreeable company of gunmen on the other, will save him!”

Poiccart rose suddenly. His keen ears had heard the ring of a bell, and he went noiselessly down the stairs.

===Chinese===
「证据就是那条蛇。」米多斯执拗地说。「你倒给我演示演示：被害人处在严密监视之下——巴伯顿一案便是如此——他怎么可能调教出一条致命的毒蛇，教它瞅准时机下口？你演示得出，我就信你。」

三义士彼此对望，一齐微笑。「早晚有一天，我演示给你看。」莱昂说。「他们的的确确驯出了他们那条『蛇』！他动起来快得人眼跟不上；回回咬在最致命的部位，挑最有利的时机下手。昨夜他就朝我出过一次手，可惜没咬着。」他说得很慢，从半阖的眼睑眯成的两道细缝里望着探长。「下一次他再出手——下一次他再出手，这一边，纵然是整个苏格兰场倾巢而来；那一边，纵然有他那伙称心得意的枪手护卫左右——也休想救得了他！」

波伊卡特霍然起身。他一双锐耳听见了门铃响，便悄没声地下楼去了。

===Original===
“The whole thing sounds like a romance to me.” Meadows was rubbing his chin irritably. “I am staring at the covers of a book whilst you are reading the pages. I suppose you devils have the A and Z of the story?”

Leon nodded.

“Why don’t you tell me?”

“Because I value your life,” said Leon simply. “Because I wish—we all wish—to keep the snake’s attention upon ourselves.”

===Chinese===
「这整桩事在我听来就像一部传奇。」米多斯烦躁地搓着下巴。「我瞪着一本书的封皮，你们却在读里头的书页。我猜，你们这班魔鬼手里，准攥着这故事从 A 到 Z 的全本吧？」

莱昂点头。

「为什么不告诉我？」

「因为我惜你这条命。」莱昂答得干脆。「因为我希望——我们全都希望——把那条『蛇』的注意力牢牢引在我们自己身上。」

===Original===
Poiccart came back at that moment and put his head in the door.

“Would you like to see Mr. Elijah Washington?” he asked, and they saw by the gleam in his eyes that Mr. Elijah Washington was well worth meeting.

He arrived a second or two later, a tall, broad-shouldered man with a reddish face. He wore pince-nez, and behind the rimless glasses his eyes were alive and full of bubbling laughter. From head to foot he was dressed in white; the cravat which flowed over the soft silk shirt was a bright yellow; the belt about his waist as bright as scarlet.

He stood beaming upon the company, his white panama crushed under his arm, both huge hands thrust into his trousers pockets.

===Chinese===
正在这时，波伊卡特回来了，在门口探进头来。

「想不想见一见伊莱贾·华盛顿（Elijah Washington）先生？」他问。众人从他眼里的光彩看出，这位伊莱贾·华盛顿先生大有一见的价值。

一两秒钟后他到了：高个子，宽肩膀，一张红彤彤的脸膛。他戴一副无框夹鼻眼镜，镜片后面那双眼睛活泛有神，漾满了咕嘟咕嘟直冒泡的笑意。他从头到脚一身素白；软绸衬衫上披垂下来的领巾是鲜亮的明黄，腰间束的皮带红得赛过猩红。

他站在那儿朝众人满面堆笑，白巴拿马草帽压扁了夹在腋下，两只大手齐齐插在裤兜里。

===Original===
“Glad to know you folks,” he greeted them in a deep boom of a voice. “I guess Mr. Barberton told you all about me. That poor little guy! Listen: he was a he-man all right, but kinder mysterious. They told me I’d find the police chief here—Captain Meadows?”

“Mister,” said the inspector, “I’m that man.”

Washington put out his huge paw and caught the detective’s hand with a grip that would have been notable in a boa constrictor.

“Glad to know you. My name is Elijah Washington—the Natural History Syndicate, Chicago.”

===Chinese===
「很高兴认识大伙儿。」他向众人致意，嗓音低沉得像滚雷。「我猜巴伯顿先生把我的底细都跟你们讲过了。那可怜的小个子！听着：他确实是个响当当的汉子，就是有些神神秘秘。他们跟我说，在这儿能见到警察局长——米多斯上尉？」

「是『先生』，」探长说，「那人正是区区在下。」

华盛顿伸出蒲扇般的大手，一把攥住探长的手——那股握力，连蚺蛇缠起来见了都要自愧弗如。

「幸会幸会。在下伊莱贾·华盛顿——芝加哥自然历史辛迪加（Natural History Syndicate）。」

===Original===
“Sit down, Mr. Washington.” Poiccart pushed forward a chair.

“I want to tell you gentlemen that this Barberton was murdered. Snake? Listen, I know snakes—brought up with ’um! Snakes are my hobby: I know ’um from egg-eaters to ‘tigers’—*notechis sentatus*, moccasins, copperheads, corals, mamba, fer de lance—gosh! snakes are just common objects like flies. An’ I tell you boys right here and now, that there ain’t a snake in this or the next world that can climb up a parapet, bite a man and get away with it with a copper looking on.”

He beamed from one to the other: he was almost paternal.

===Chinese===
「请坐，华盛顿先生。」波伊卡特把一把椅子推上前来。

「我要告诉诸位先生：这个巴伯顿是被人谋害的。蛇？听着，蛇这路东西我懂——打小就是同它们一块儿长大的！蛇是我的癖好：从食蛋蛇到『虎蛇』——*notechis sentatus*、棉口蛇、铜头蝮、珊瑚蛇、曼巴、矛头蛇——嚯！蛇就跟苍蝇一样，不过平平常常的物件。趁现在我当面告诉你们这帮小伙子：无论这个世界还是下个世界，都没有哪条蛇能爬上矮墙咬人一口，还能在一个警察的眼皮子底下逃之夭夭。」

他挨个儿朝众人绽开笑脸，简直带着几分老父般的慈祥。

===Original===
“I’d like to have shown you folks a worse-than-mamba,” he said regretfully, “but carrying round snakes in your pocket is just hot dog: it’s like a millionaire wearin’ diamond earrings just to show he can afford ’em. I liked that little fellow; I’m mighty sorry he’s dead, but if any man tells you that a snake bit him, go right up to him, hit him on the nose, and say ‘Liar!’”

“You will have some coffee?” Manfred had rung the bell.

“Sure I will: never have got used to this tea-drinking habit. I’m on the wagon too: got scared up there in the backlands of Angola—”

===Chinese===
「我本想给诸位瞧瞧一条比曼巴还毒的，」他引以为憾地说，「可把蛇揣在兜里满世界走，纯粹是摆阔：就好比百万富翁戴钻石耳坠，专为显摆他戴得起。我喜欢那个小个子；他死了，我心里非常难受。可要是有什么人对你们说他是让蛇咬死的，你们只管走到他跟前去，照他鼻梁上就是一拳，说一声『骗子！』」

「喝点咖啡吗？」曼弗雷德已按过了铃。

「那敢情好：这喝茶的习惯，我一直没能入乡随俗。酒我也戒了：在安哥拉腹地那地界受过一场惊吓——」

===Original===
“What were you doing there?” asked Leon.

“Snakes,” said the other briefly. “I represent an organization that supplies specimens to zoos and museums. I was looking for a flying snake—there ain’t such a thing, though the natives say there is. I got a new kinder cobra—*viperidae crotalinae*—and yet not!”

He scratched his head, bringing his scientific perplexity into the room. Leon’s heart went out to him.

He had met Barberton by accident. Without shame he confessed that he had gone to a village in the interior for a real solitary jag, and returning to such degree of civilization as Mossamedes represented, he found a group of Portuguese breeds squatting about a fire at which the man’s feet were toasting.

===Chinese===
「你在那儿做什么？」莱昂问。

「找蛇。」对方三言两语。「我代表一个给动物园和博物馆供应标本的组织。我当时在找一种会飞的蛇——尽管土人说有，其实压根儿没有这么个东西。我倒是弄到了一种新式的眼镜蛇——*viperidae crotalinae*——可又不尽然！」

他挠起头来，把他那一团科学的困惑也一并挠进了屋里。莱昂打心眼儿里喜欢上了这个人。

他同巴伯顿的相识纯属偶然。他毫不害臊地承认，自己曾独自进内地一个村子，放开量痛饮了一场；待他回到莫萨马德斯所代表的那一等级的文明时，只见一群葡萄牙混血围着一堆火蹲坐，火上烤着的正是那人的两只脚。

===Original===
“I don’t know what he was—a prospector, I guess. He was one of those what-is-its you meet along that coast. I’ve met his kind most everywhere—as far south as Port Nottosh. In Angola there are scores: they go native at the end.”

“You can tell us nothing about Barberton?”

Mr. Elijah Washington shook his head.

===Chinese===
「他是干什么的，我不清楚——我琢磨着是个探矿的。就是你们在那一带海岸上碰得见的那种说不上名堂的角色。他这一路人，我差不多哪儿都遇上过——往南一直到诺托什港（Port Nottosh）都有。安哥拉一地更是数不胜数：到头来一个个全都活成了土人。」

「关于巴伯顿，你就再没有别的可讲了？」

伊莱贾·华盛顿先生摇了摇头。

===Original===
“No, sir: I know him same as I might know you. It got me curious when I found out the why of the torturing: he wouldn’t tell where it was.”

“Where what was?” asked Manfred quickly, and Mr. Washington was surprised.

“Why, the writing they wanted to get. I thought maybe he’d told you. He said he was coming right along to spill all that part of it. It was a letter he’d found in a tin box—that was all he’d say.”

===Chinese===
「没有了，先生：我同他相识，就跟诸位同我相识差不多。等我打听明白他们为什么对他上刑，我倒起了好奇心：他死活不肯说那东西在哪儿。」

「什么东西在哪儿？」曼弗雷德敏捷地问。华盛顿先生倒吃了一惊。

「嗨，就是他们想弄到手的那件字据呀。我还当他早告诉过你们了。他说过的，他这就来找你们，把这一段和盘托出。那是他在一只铁皮盒子里发现的一封信——他只肯说到这儿。」

===Original===
They looked at one another.

“I know no more about it than that,” Mr. Washington added, when he saw Gonsalez’ lips move. “It was just a letter. Who it was from, why, what it was about, he never told me. My first idea was that he’d been flirting round about here, but divorce laws are mighty generous and they wouldn’t trouble to get evidence that way. A man doesn’t want any documents to get rid of his wife. I dare say you folks wonder why I’ve come along.” Mr. Washington raised his steaming cup of coffee, which must have been nearly boiling, and drank it at one gulp. “That’s fine,” he said, “the nearest to coffee I’ve had since I left home.”

===Chinese===
三人面面相觑。

「我知道的就这么多，再没有别的了。」见冈萨雷斯嘴唇翕动，华盛顿先生补充道。「不过是一封信。谁写的、为什么写、写的什么，他从没告诉过我。我起初的念头是，他莫不是在伦敦这一带拈花惹草留下了把柄；可如今离婚的法条宽大得很，他们犯不着费那个事去搜罗证据。男人要甩开老婆，可用不着什么文书字据。我估摸着，诸位准在纳闷我干嘛找上门来。」华盛顿先生端起那杯热气腾腾的咖啡——那温度准是接近滚沸了——一口灌了下去。「好极了，」他说，「打我离家以来，这是最接近真咖啡的一杯。」

===Original===
He wiped his lips with a large and vivid silk handkerchief.

“I’ve come along, gentlemen, because I’ve got a pretty good idea that I’d be useful to anybody who’s snake-hunting in this little dorp.”

“It’s rather a dangerous occupation, isn’t it?” said Manfred quietly.

Washington nodded.

===Chinese===
他掏出一块色彩鲜亮的大绸手帕，抹了抹嘴唇。

「诸位，我之所以找上门来，是因为我心里相当有数：但凡在这座小城里搜捕『蛇』的人，都用得上我。」

「这可是个相当危险的行当，不是吗？」曼弗雷德淡淡地说。

华盛顿点头。

===Original===
“To you, but not to me,” he said. “I am snake-proof.”

He pulled up his sleeve: the forearm was scarred and pitted with old wounds.

“Snakes,” he said briefly. “That’s cobra.” He pointed proudly. “When that snake struck, my boys didn’t wait for anything, they started dividing my kit. Sort of appointed themselves a board of executors and joint heirs of the family estate.”

“But you were very ill?” said Gonsalez.

===Chinese===
「于你们如此，于我却不然。」他说。「我是蛇毒不侵之身。」

他捋起袖子：小臂上疤痕纵横，旧伤留下的凹坑密密麻麻。

「蛇咬的。」他言简意赅。「那是眼镜蛇。」他自豪地指了指。「那条蛇咬我的时候，我那帮伙计二话没说，当场就动手分起我的行头来。俨然自行拼凑了一个遗嘱执行人兼家产共同继承人委员会。」

「可你病得很厉害吧？」冈萨雷斯说。

===Original===
Mr. Washington shook his head.

“No, sir, not more than if a bee bit me, and not so much as if a wasp had got in first punch. Some people can eat arsenic, some people can make a meal of enough morphia to decimate a province. I’m snake-proof—been bitten ever since I was five.”

He bent over towards them, and his jolly face went suddenly serious. “I’m the man you want,” he said.

“I think you are,” said Manfred slowly.

===Chinese===
华盛顿先生摇头。

「不，先生，不过跟蜜蜂蜇一下不相上下，还不如马蜂抢先进的那一针厉害。有人能拿砒霜当饭吃，有人一顿吃得下足够撂倒一省人的吗啡。我是蛇毒不侵——打五岁起就叫蛇咬。」

他朝众人俯过身来，那张乐呵呵的脸忽然郑重起来。「我就是你们要找的人。」他说。

「我想你正是。」曼弗雷德缓缓地说。

===Original===
“Because this old snake ain’t finished biting. There’s a graft in it somewhere, and I want to find it. But first I want to vindicate the snake. Anybody who says a snake’s naturally vicious doesn’t understand. Snakes are timid, quiet, respectful things, and don’t want no trouble with nobody. If a snake sees you coming, he naturally lights out for home. When momma snake’s running around with her family, she’s naturally touchy for fear you’d tread on any of her boys and girls, but she’s a lady, and if you give her time she’ll Maggie ’um and get ’um into the parlour where the foot of white man never trod.”

Leon was looking at him with a speculative eye.

“It is queer to think,” he said, speaking half to himself, “that you may be the only one of us who will be alive this day week!”

Meadows, not easily shocked, felt a cold shiver run down his spine.

===Chinese===
「因为这条老蛇还没有咬够。这档子事里头哪儿藏着猫腻，我要把它找出来。但头一件，我要替蛇正名。谁要说蛇生性歹毒，那他就是不懂蛇。蛇是胆小、安静、懂礼数的东西，同谁都懒得结怨生事。蛇一眼瞧见你来，自然撒腿就往家里溜。蛇妈妈领着一家老小出门转悠的时候，自然格外提防，生怕你踩了她的哪个儿子闺女；可她是一位淑女，只要你给她工夫，她自会『玛吉』（Maggie）一声声把他们唤拢，领进那白人的脚从不曾踏过的客厅里去。」

莱昂正若有所思地打量着他。

「说来也真古怪，」他半像自言自语地说，「下星期的今天，我们这些人里还活着的，兴许就只剩你一个！」

米多斯不是轻易受惊的人，却也觉着一股寒气顺着脊梁骨直溜下去。

## XIII Mirabelle Goes Home / 十三 米拉贝尔回家
===Original===
The prediction that Leon Gonsalez had made was not wholly fulfilled, though he himself had helped to prevent the supreme distress he prophesied. When Mirabelle Leicester awoke in the morning, her head was thick and dull, and for a long time she lay between sleeping and waking, trying to bring order to the confusion of her thoughts, her eyes on the ceiling towards a gnarled oak beam which she had seen before somewhere; and when at last she summoned sufficient energy to raise herself on her elbow, she looked upon the very familiar surroundings of her own pretty little room.

===Chinese===
莱昂·冈萨雷斯当初的预言并未完全应验——他自己出了力，防住了他预言的那场大难。米拉贝尔·莱斯特清晨醒来，头脑昏沉发木，好半晌都半睡半醒地躺着，想把纷乱的思绪理出个头绪；她两眼望着天花板，望着一根虬结的橡木房梁——这梁她先前在什么地方见过。待她终于攒足力气，用胳膊肘撑起上身，映入眼帘的，是自己那间漂亮小屋再熟悉不过的陈设。

===Original===
Heavytree Farm! What a curious dream she had had! A dream filled with fleeting visions of old men with elongated heads, of dance music and a crowded ballroom, of a slightly overdressed man who had been very polite to her at dinner. Where did she dine? She sat up in bed, holding her throbbing head.

===Chinese===
赫维特里农场！她做了个多么离奇的梦！梦里满是浮光掠影般的幻象：脑袋细长的老头儿，舞曲，拥挤不堪的舞厅，还有一个穿得略嫌花哨的男人——吃饭时对她客气得了不得。是在哪儿吃的饭来着？她坐起身，捧着突突直跳的脑袋。

===Original===
Again she looked round the room and slowly, out of her dreams, emerged a few tangible facts. She was still in a state of bewilderment when the door opened and Aunt Alma came in, and the unprepossessing face of her relative was accentuated by her look of anxiety.

===Chinese===
她又把房间四下打量了一遍，几个确凿的事实这才从梦境里缓缓浮出。她兀自懵懂着，房门开了，阿尔玛姨妈走了进来；这位亲眷那张本不讨喜的脸，又教一脸的焦灼衬得愈发分明。

===Original===
“Hullo, Alma!” said Mirabelle dully. “I’ve had such a queer dream.”

Alma pressed her lips tightly together as she placed a tray on a table by the side of the bed.

“I think it was about that advertisement I saw.” And then, with a gasp: “How did I come here?”

===Chinese===
「喂，阿尔玛！」米拉贝尔有气无力地说。「我做了个好古怪的梦。」

阿尔玛紧紧抿住嘴唇，把托盘搁在床边的小桌上。

「我想梦的是我见过的那则广告。」随即她倒抽一口气：「我怎么会到这儿来的？」

===Original===
“They brought you,” said Alma. “The nurse is downstairs having her breakfast. She’s a nice woman and keeps press-cuttings.”

“The nurse?” asked Mirabelle in bewilderment.

“You arrived here at three o’clock in the morning in a motorcar. You had a nurse with you.” Alma enumerated the circumstances in chronological order. “And two men. First one of the men got out and knocked at the door. I was worried to death. In fact, I’d been worried all the afternoon, ever since I had your wire telling me not to come up to London.”

“But I didn’t send any such wire,” replied the girl.

===Chinese===
「是他们送你回来的。」阿尔玛说。「护士在楼下吃早饭呢。人挺不错，还爱收剪报。」

「护士？」米拉贝尔一头雾水地问。

「你凌晨三点钟坐汽车到的家，身边带着位护士。」阿尔玛按先后次序一桩桩数说。「还有两个男人。先是一个男人下车敲门。我担心得要死。其实打下午起我就一直提心吊胆——打接到你的电报、叫我别上伦敦来的那一刻起。」

「可我没打过那样的电报呀。」姑娘答道。

===Original===
“After I came down, the man—he was really a gentleman and very pleasantly spoken—told me that you’d been taken ill and a nurse had brought you home. They then carried you, the two men and the nurse, upstairs and laid you on the bed, and nurse and I undressed you. I simply couldn’t get you to wake up: all you did was to talk about the orangeade.”

“I remember! It was so bitter, and Lord Evington let me drink some of his. And then I… I don’t know what happened after that,” she said, with a little grimace.

===Chinese===
「我下楼之后，那人——倒真是位绅士，说话和和气气——告诉我你病倒了，由护士送回了家。随后他们，两个男人加上护士，把你抬上楼安放在床上，护士同我一道给你脱了衣裳。我怎么也叫不醒你：你翻来覆去只念叨那杯橙水。」

「我想起来了！那橙水苦得要命，还是埃文顿勋爵让我喝了几口他的。再后来我……后来出了什么事，我就不知道了。」她说着，微微做了个苦脸。

===Original===
“Mr. Gonsalez ordered the car, got the nurse from a nursing home,” explained Alma.

“Gonsalez! Not my Gonsalez—the—the Four Just Men Gonsalez?” she asked in amazement.

“I’m sure it was Gonsalez: they made no secret about it. You can see the gentlemen who brought you: he’s about the house somewhere. I saw him in Heavytree Lane not five minutes ago, strolling up and down and smoking. A pipe,” added Alma.

===Chinese===
「汽车是冈萨雷斯先生吩咐雇的，护士是他从疗养院里请来的。」阿尔玛解释道。

「冈萨雷斯！该不会是我那个冈萨雷斯吧——就是——就是四义士里的那个冈萨雷斯？」她惊异地问。

「我敢肯定就是冈萨雷斯：他们对这事毫不隐瞒。送你来的那位先生你也见得着：他就在宅子附近什么地方。不出五分钟前，我还瞧见他在赫维特里巷（Heavytree Lane）里踱来踱去，抽着烟。抽烟斗。」阿尔玛又补了一句。

===Original===
The girl got out of bed; her knees were curiously weak under her, but she managed to stagger to the window, and, pushing open the casement still farther, looked out across the patchwork quilt of colour. The summer flowers were in bloom; the delicate scents came up on the warm morning air, and she stood for a moment, drinking in great draughts of the exquisite perfume, and then, with a sigh, turned back to the waiting Alma.

===Chinese===
姑娘下了床；膝弯里虚软得出奇，可她还是挪到了窗前，把窗扉又推开了些，朝外望去——满眼是百衲被似的斑斓色块。夏花正盛，清幽的香气乘着清晨的暖风涌上来。她伫立片刻，深深吸了几口这沁人的芬芳，然后叹了口气，回身走向等候着的阿尔玛。

===Original===
“I don’t know how it all happened and what it’s about, but my word, Alma, I’m glad to be back! That dreadful man…! We lunched at the Ritz-Carlton.… I never want to see another restaurant or a ballroom or Chester Square, or anything but old Heavytree!”

She took the cup of tea from Alma’s hand, drank greedily, and put it down with a little gasp.

“That was wonderful! Yes, the tea was too, but I’m thinking about Gonsalez. If it should be he!”

===Chinese===
「这一切是怎么发生的、为的什么事，我全不明白，可老天在上，阿尔玛，回来真好！那个可怕的男人……！我们在丽思卡尔顿饭店吃的午饭……我这辈子再也不想见什么饭馆、舞厅、切斯特广场，什么都不想见，只想老赫维特里！」

她从阿尔玛手里接过那杯茶，贪馋地一口气喝下，放下杯子，轻轻舒了口气。

「美极了！对，茶也是，可我心里想的是冈萨雷斯。万一真是他呢！」

===Original===
“I don’t see why you should get excited over a man who’s committed I don’t know how many murders.”

“Don’t be silly, Alma!” scoffed the girl. “The Just Men have never murdered, any more than a judge and jury murder.”

The room was still inclined to go round, and it was with the greatest difficulty that she could condense the two Almas who stood before her into one tangible individual.

===Chinese===
「我不明白，一个手上不知背着多少条人命的男人，有什么好让你动心的。」

「别犯傻了，阿尔玛！」姑娘嗤笑一声。「义士们从来没杀过人——正如法官和陪审团谈不上杀人一样。」

房间还在打转似的；她面前立着两个阿尔玛，得费好大的劲，才能把这两位收敛成实实在在的一个。

===Original===
“There’s a gentleman downstairs: he’s been waiting since twelve.”

And when she asked, she was to learn, to her dismay, that it was half-past one.

“I’ll be down in a quarter of an hour,” she said recklessly. “Who is it?”

===Chinese===
「楼下有位先生：从十二点起就一直等着。」

待她问起时辰，才知道已是一点半——这可让她心头一沉。

「一刻钟我就下去。」她大大咧咧地说。「是哪一位？」

===Original===
“I’ve never heard of him before, but he’s a gentleman,” was the unsatisfactory reply. “They didn’t want to let him come in.”

“Who didn’t?”

“The gentlemen who brought you here in the night.”

Mirabelle stared at her.

===Chinese===
「这人我从没听说过，不过是位绅士。」这回答含含糊糊，解决不了问题。「他们本不想放他进来。」

「谁不想放？」

「夜里送你来的那几位先生。」

米拉贝尔直勾勾地望着她。

===Original===
“You mean… they’re guarding the house?”

“That’s how it strikes me,” said Alma bitterly. “Why they should interfere with us, I don’t know. Anyway, they let him in. Mr. Johnson Lee.”

The girl frowned.

===Chinese===
「你是说……他们在守着这宅子？」

「我瞧着就是这么回事。」阿尔玛没好气地说。「他们凭什么来搅扰我们，我不知道。反正，他们放他进来了。约翰逊·李先生。」

姑娘皱起了眉头。

===Original===
“I don’t know the name,” she said.

Alma walked to the window.

“There’s his car,” she said, and pointed.

===Chinese===
「这名字我没听说过。」她说。

阿尔玛走到窗前。

「那就是他的车。」她说着，抬手一指。

===Original===
It was just visible, standing at the side of the road beyond the box hedge, a long-bodied Rolls, white with dust. The chauffeur was talking to a strange man, and from the fact that he was smoking a pipe Mirabelle guessed that this was one of her self-appointed custodians.

She had her bath, and with the assistance of the nurse, dressed and came shakily down the stairs. Alma was waiting in the brick-floored hall.

===Chinese===
那车停在黄杨树篱外头的路旁，影影绰绰望得见，是一辆车身颀长的劳斯莱斯，蒙着一层白蒙蒙的尘土。司机正同一个陌生男人说话；从那人抽着烟斗这一点，米拉贝尔猜出，这人便是自封看守她的人之一。

她洗了澡，在护士搀扶下穿好衣裳，颤巍巍地下了楼。阿尔玛在砖地的门厅里候着。

===Original===
“He wants to see you alone,” she said in a stage whisper. “I don’t know whether I ought to allow it, but there’s evidently something wrong. These men prowling about the house have got thoroughly on my nerves.”

Mirabelle laughed softly as she opened the door and walked in. At the sound of the door closing, the man who was sitting stiffly on a deep settee in a window recess got up. He was tall and bent, and his dark face was lined. His eyes she could not see; they were hidden behind dark green glasses, which were turned in her direction as she came across the room to greet him.

===Chinese===
「他要单独见你。」她用念台词似的耳语声说。「该不该放他见，我拿不准，可这里头分明有蹊跷。这帮人在宅子四下转悠，搅得我心烦意乱。」

米拉贝尔轻声笑着，开门走了进去。听见关门声，那个直挺挺坐在窗龛深陷长沙发上的男人站起身来。他高个子，背有点驼，黝黑的脸上刻着一道道皱纹。他的眼睛她看不见；藏在一副墨绿色眼镜后头——她穿过房间迎上前去时，那副眼镜正对着她的方向。

===Original===
“Miss Mirabelle Leicester?” he asked, in the quiet, modulated voice of an educated man. He took her hand in his.

“Won’t you sit down?” she said, for he remained standing after she had seated herself.

===Chinese===
「是米拉贝尔·莱斯特小姐吗？」他问道，嗓音平和沉稳，是有教养的人的声口。他把她的手握在自己手心里。

「您请坐，好么？」她说，因为她落座之后，他仍旧站着。

===Original===
“Thank you.” He sat down gingerly, holding between his knees the handle of the umbrella he had brought into the drawing-room. “I’m afraid my visit may be inopportune, Miss Leicester,” he said. “Have you by any chance heard about Mr. Barberton?”

Her brows wrinkled in thought. “Barberton? I seem to have heard the name.”

===Chinese===
「谢谢。」他小心翼翼地坐下，把随身带进客厅的那把雨伞的伞柄夹在两膝之间。「恐怕我这趟来访不大凑巧，莱斯特小姐。」他说。「巴伯顿先生的事，您可有耳闻？」

她蹙眉思索。「巴伯顿？这名字好像听见过。」

===Original===
“He was killed yesterday on the Thames Embankment.”

Then she recollected.

“The man who was bitten by the snake?” she asked in horror.

The visitor nodded.

===Chinese===
「他昨天在泰晤士河畔的滨河大道遇害了。」

她这才想了起来。

「就是被蛇咬死的那个人吗？」她惊恐地问。

来访者点了点头。

===Original===
“It was a great shock to me, because I have been a friend of his for many years, and had arranged to call at his hotel on the night of his death.” And then abruptly he turned the conversation in another and a surprising direction. “Your father was a scientist, Miss Leicester?”

She nodded.

“Yes, he was an astronomer, an authority upon meteors.”

“Exactly. I thought that was the gentleman. I have only recently had his book read to me. He was in Africa for some years?”

===Chinese===
「这对我是个莫大的打击，因为我是他多年的朋友，本还约好在他遇害当晚到旅馆去看他。」说到这里，他忽然把话头一转，转到了另一个出人意料的方向。「您父亲是位科学家吧，莱斯特小姐？」

她点了点头。

「是的，他是位天文学家，研究流星的权威。」

「正是。我料想就是这位先生。他那部书，我近来才请人读给我听。他在非洲住过几年，是吧？」

===Original===
“Yes,” she said quietly, “he died there. He was studying meteors for three years in Angola. You probably know that a very large number of shooting stars fall in that country. My father’s theory was that it was due to the ironstone mountains which attract them—so he set up a little observatory in the interior.” Her lips trembled for a second. “He was killed in a native rising,” she said.

===Chinese===
「是的，」她轻声说，「他就死在那里。他在安哥拉研究流星，待了三年。落在这个国家的流星多得异乎寻常，这一点您多半知道。我父亲的理论是，那是因为当地的铁矿山把流星吸引了过去——于是他在内陆设了一座小小的天文台。」她的嘴唇颤了一颤。「他死于一场土著暴动。」她说。

===Original===
“Do you know the part of Angola where he had his observatory?”

She shook her head.

“I’m not sure. I have never been in Africa, but perhaps Aunt Alma may know.”

She went out to find Alma waiting in the passage, in conversation with the pipe-smoker. The man withdrew hastily at the sight of her.

===Chinese===
「他设天文台的那处地方，在安哥拉哪个部位，您知道吗？」

她摇了摇头。

「我说不准。我没去过非洲，不过阿尔玛姨妈兴许知道。」

她走出去，只见阿尔玛正在过道里等着，同那个抽烟斗的人说话。那人一见她，慌忙避开了。

===Original===
“Alma, do you remember what part of Angola father had his observatory?” she asked.

Alma did not know offhand, but one of her invaluable scrapbooks contained all the information that the girl wanted, and she carried the book to Mr. Lee.

“Here are the particulars,” she said, and laid the book open before them.

“Would you read it for me?” he requested gently, and she read to him the three short paragraphs which noted that Professor Leicester had taken up his residence in Bishaka.

===Chinese===
「阿尔玛，父亲在安哥拉设天文台，是在哪一带，你还记得吗？」她问。

阿尔玛一时说不上来，可她那些少不得的宝贝剪报簿里，正有姑娘要的全部资料，她便把簿子捧给了李先生。

「资料都在这儿了。」她说着，把簿子摊开在他们面前。

「能请你念给我听吗？」他轻声请求；她便把那三小段念给他听——段中记载，莱斯特教授已在比沙卡（Bishaka）安顿下来。

===Original===
“That is the place,” interrupted the visitor. “Bishaka! You are sure that Mr. Barberton did not communicate with you?”

“With me?” she said in amazement. “No—why should he?”

He did not answer, but sat for a long time, turning the matter over in his mind.

===Chinese===
「就是这地方。」来访者插话道。「比沙卡！您敢肯定巴伯顿先生没有同您联系过？」

「同我？」她惊异地问。「没有——他为什么要同我联系？」

他没有作答，只是久久坐着，在心里把这件事翻来覆去地掂量。

===Original===
“You’re perfectly certain that nobody sent you a document, probably in the Portuguese language, concerning”—he hesitated—“Bishaka?”

She shook her head, and then, as though he had not seen the gesture, he asked the question again.

“I’m certain,” she said. “We have very little correspondence at the farm, and it isn’t possible that I could overlook anything so remarkable.”

===Chinese===
「您百分之百肯定，没有人给您寄过一份文件——多半是葡萄牙文写的，关于」——他迟疑了一下——「比沙卡的？」

她摇了摇头；他却仿佛没有看见这个动作，又把问题原样问了一遍。

「我肯定。」她说。「农场里来信很少，这么惹眼的东西，我不可能看漏。」

===Original===
Again he turned the problem over in his mind.

“Have you any documents in Portuguese or in English… any letters from your father about Angola?”

“None,” she said. “The only reference my father ever made to Bishaka was that he was getting a lot of information which he thought would be valuable, and that he was a little troubled because his cameras, which he had fixed in various parts of the country to cover every sector of the skies, were being disturbed by wandering prospectors.”

===Chinese===
他又在心里把这问题掂量了一遍。

「您手里有没有什么文件，葡萄牙文的或是英文的……比如您父亲谈安哥拉的信件？」

「没有。」她说。「父亲提到比沙卡，统共只有一回，说他正搜集到大量资料，认为很有价值；还有一桩叫他有点烦心——他把照相机安在全国各处，把天空的每个方位都照管到，却总被四处游荡的探矿人搅扰。」

===Original===
“He said that, did he?” asked Mr. Lee eagerly. “Come now, that explains a great deal!”

In spite of herself she laughed. “It doesn’t explain much to me, Mr. Lee,” she said frankly. And then, in a more serious tone: “Did Barberton come from Angola?”

“Yes, Barberton came from that country,” he said in a lower voice. “I should like to tell you”—he hesitated—“but I am rather afraid.”

===Chinese===
「他是这么说的？」李先生急切地问。「啊，这样一来，许多事就说得通了！」

她不由自主地笑了。「这可没向我解释清楚多少，李先生。」她坦率地说。随后口气郑重了些：「巴伯顿是从安哥拉来的吗？」

「是的，巴伯顿是从那个地方来的。」他压低了嗓音。「我很想告诉您」——他迟疑着——「可我又相当害怕。」

===Original===
“Afraid to tell me? Why?”

He shook his head.

“So many dreadful things have happened recently to poor Barberton and others, that knowledge seems a most dangerous thing. I wish I could believe that it would not be dangerous to you,” he added kindly, “and then I could speak what is in my mind and relieve myself of a great deal of anxiety.” He rose slowly. “I think the best thing I can do is to consult my lawyer. I was foolish to keep it from him so long. He is the only man I can trust to search my documents.”

===Chinese===
「怕告诉我？为什么？」

他摇了摇头。

「近来可怜的巴伯顿和其他人身上出了这么多可怕的事，知识这东西看去竟成了顶顶危险的货色。但愿我能相信它于您并无危险，」他好意补了一句，「那样我就能把心里的话说出来，也好卸下心头一大块焦虑。」他缓缓起身。「我想，我顶好还是去找我的律师商量。这么久瞒着他，实在是我糊涂。要搜检我的文件，我只信得过他一个人。」

===Original===
She could only look at him in astonishment.

“But surely you can search your own documents?” she said good-humouredly.

“No, I’m afraid I can’t. Because”—he spoke with the simplicity of a child—“I am blind.”

“Blind?” gasped Mirabelle, and the man laughed gently.

===Chinese===
她只能愕然望着他。

「可您自己的文件，难道还不能自己搜检吗？」她和颜悦色地说。

「不能，恐怕办不到。因为」——他说话带着孩童般的坦白——「我是个瞎子。」

「瞎子？」米拉贝尔倒抽一口气。男人温和地笑了。

===Original===
“I am pretty capable for a blind man, am I not? I can walk across a room and avoid all the furniture. The only thing I cannot do is to read—at least, read the ordinary print. I can read Braille: poor Barberton taught me. He was a schoolmaster,” he explained, “at a blind school near Brightlingsea. Not a particularly well-educated man, but a marvellously quick writer of Braille. We have corresponded for years through that medium. He could write a Braille letter almost as quickly as you can with pen and ink.”

===Chinese===
「一个瞎子能干到我这样，也算挺能干的了，是不是？我能穿过一间屋子而不碰着一件家具。唯独做不成的事是读书——至少，读寻常的铅印文字。盲文我读得：可怜的巴伯顿教我的。他从前当教员，」他解释说，「在布莱特灵西附近一所盲人学校。算不得多么有学问的人，可写起盲文来快得出奇。我们靠这个媒介通了多年信。他写一封盲文信，差不多赶得上您用纸笔写一封信那么快。」

===Original===
Her heart was full of pity for the man: he was so cheery, so confident, and withal so proud of his own accomplishments, that pity turned to admiration. He had the ineffable air of obstinacy which is the possession of so many men similarly stricken, and she began to realize that self-pity, that greatest of all afflictions which attends blindness, had been eliminated from his philosophy.

===Chinese===
她心里对这人充满怜惜：他这样开朗，这样自信，又这样为自己的本领自豪，怜惜不觉化作了钦佩。他身上有一股难以言喻的执拗之气——遭逢同样不幸的人多有这份气质；她也渐渐明白：自怜——失明者最大的一种苦楚——早已从他的处世哲学里剔除干净了。

===Original===
“I should like to tell you more,” he said, as he held out his hand. “Probably I will dictate a long letter to you tomorrow, or else my lawyer will do so, putting all the facts before you. For the moment, however, I must be sure of my ground. I have no desire to raise in your heart either fear or—hope. Do you know a Mr. Manfred?”

“I don’t know him personally,” she said quickly. “George Manfred?”

He nodded.

“Have you met him?” she asked eagerly. “And Mr. Poiccart, the Frenchman?”

===Chinese===
「我很想把更多的告诉您。」他伸出手来，说道。「明天我很可能向您口授一封长信，或者由我的律师代笔，把全部事实摆在您面前。不过眼下，我还得先把脚下的地踩实。我不愿在您心里唤起恐惧，也不愿唤起——希望。您认识一位曼弗雷德先生吗？」

「我本人不认识他。」她急忙说。「乔治·曼弗雷德？」

他点了点头。

「您见过他吗？」她急切地问。「还有波伊卡特先生，那个法国人？」

===Original===
“No, not Mr. Poiccart. Manfred was on the telephone to me very early this morning. He seemed to know all about my relationships with my poor friend. He knew also of my blindness. A remarkable man, very gentle and courteous. It was he who gave me your address. Perhaps,” he mused, “it would be advisable if I first consulted him.”

“I’m sure it would!” she said enthusiastically. “They are wonderful. You have heard of them, of course, Mr. Lee—the Four Just Men?”

He smiled.

“That sounds as though you admire them,” he said. “Yes, I have heard of them. They are the men who, many years ago, set out to regularize the inconsistencies of the English law, to punish where no punishment is provided by the code. Strange I never associated them.…”

===Chinese===
「没有，波伊卡特先生没见过。曼弗雷德今天一大清早给我来了电话。他对我同我那可怜朋友之间的渊源，似乎了如指掌。他知道我目盲的事。一位非凡的人物，极温和，极有礼。就是他把您的地址给了我。也许，」他沉吟道，「我先去请教他，才是稳妥的办法。」

「那当然再稳妥不过！」她满腔热忱地说。「他们了不起极了。您当然听说过他们的，李先生——四义士？」

他微微一笑。

「听这话音，您很敬慕他们。」他说。「是的，我听说过。多年前，就是这几个人，着手匡正英国法律的种种失当，在法典不设惩罚之处施加惩罚。说来奇怪，我竟从来没把他们联想在一起……」

===Original===
He meditated upon the matter in silence for a long while, and then: “I wonder,” he said, but did not tell her what he was wondering.

===Chinese===
他就这事默默思量了半晌，然后说：「真不知……」但他没有告诉她，他不知的是什么。

===Original===
She walked down the garden path with him into the roadway and stood chatting about the country and the flowers that he had never seen, and the weather and such trivialities as people talk about when their minds are occupied with more serious thoughts which they cannot share, until the big limousine pulled up and he stepped into its cool interior. He had the independence which comes to the educated blind and gently refused the offer of her guidance, an offer she did not attempt to repeat, sensing the satisfaction he must have had in making his way without help. She waved her hand to the car as it moved off, and so naturally did his hand go up in salute that for a moment she thought he had seen her.

===Chinese===
她陪他沿花园小径走到路上，站着闲谈——谈乡野，谈他从未见过的花，谈天气，谈人们心头压着不便与人共说的正事时照例要谈的那一类闲话——直谈到那辆大轿车驶到跟前停住，他跨进车里清凉的车厢。他有受过教育的盲人特有的那份自恃，温和地谢绝了她引路的好意；她也不再提第二次，觉察得出，不用人扶、自行走路，于他必有一番快意。车子开动时，她朝它挥手；他那只手抬起来作别，自然得很，一时间她竟以为，他看见了她。

===Original===
So he passed out of her sight, and might well have passed out of her life, for Mr. Oberzohn had decreed that the remaining hours of blind Johnson Lee were to be few.

But it happened that the Three Men had reached the same decision in regard to Mr. Oberzohn, only there was some indecision as to the manner of his passing. Leon Gonsalez had original views.

===Chinese===
他就这样从她眼前消失了，也很可能就此从她的生命里消失——因为欧伯佐恩先生已经下了决断：瞎子约翰逊·李剩下的时辰屈指可数。

不想三义士对欧伯佐恩先生也已下了一样的决断，只是对于他该当如何「离世」，还有些拿不定主意。莱昂·冈萨雷斯自有一番别出心裁的见解。

## XIV The Pedlar / 十四 货郎
===Original===
The man with the pipe was standing within half a dozen paces of her. She was going back through the gate, when she remembered Aunt Alma’s views on the guardianship.

===Chinese===
抽烟斗的男人就站在离她不过六七步远的地方。她正要转身从院门里回去，忽然想起了阿尔玛姨妈对这班看守的一番议论。

===Original===
“Are you waiting here all day?” she asked.

“Till this evening, miss. We’re to be relieved by some men from Gloucester﻿—we came from town, and we’re going back with the nurse, if you can do without her?”

===Chinese===
「你打算在这儿等上一整天吗？」她问。

「等到今天傍晚，小姐。格洛斯特那边会来几个弟兄接我们的班——我们是从城里来的，回头还要同护士一道回去，如果你们离得开她的话？」

===Original===
“Who placed you here?” she asked.

“Mr. Gonsalez. He thought it would be wise to have somebody around.”

===Chinese===
「是谁把你们安插在这儿的？」她问。

「冈萨雷斯先生。他觉得左右有人照应着，方为万全。」

===Original===
“But why?”

The big man grinned.

===Chinese===
「可这是为什么？」

大个子咧嘴一笑。

===Original===
“I’ve known Mr. Gonsalez many years,” he said. “I’m a police pensioner, and I can remember the time when I’d have given a lot of money to lay my hands on him﻿—but I’ve never asked him why, miss. There is generally a good reason for everything he does.”

===Chinese===
「冈萨雷斯先生我认识许多年了。」他说。「我是领警队退休金过活的人，还记得当年那样的日子——情愿掏上一大笔钱，只求能把他捉拿到手——可我从来没有问过他为什么，小姐。他做的事，向来事事都有个充分的道理。」

===Original===
Mirabelle went back into the farmhouse, very thoughtful. Happily, Alma was not inquisitive; she was left alone in the drawing-room to reconstruct her exciting yesterday.

===Chinese===
米拉贝尔回到农舍里，满腹心事。所幸阿尔玛不好探人隐衷；她独自留在客厅里，把昨天那番惊心动魄的经历，在心头从头拼凑了一遍。

===Original===
Mirabelle harboured very few illusions. She had read much, guessed much, and in the days of her childhood had been in the habit of linking cause to effect. The advertisement was designed especially for her: that was her first conclusion. It was designed to bring her into the charge of Oberzohn. For now she recognized this significant circumstance: never once, since she had entered the offices of Oberzohn & Smitts, until the episode of the orangeade, had she been free to come and go as she wished. He had taken her to lunch, he had brought her back; Joan Newton had been her companion in the drive from the house, and from the house to the hall; and from then on she did not doubt that Oberzohn’s surveillance had continued, until﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
米拉贝尔心里的幻想本就不多。她读过许多，猜过许多，打孩提时候起就惯于把前因接上后果。那则广告是专为她设下的：这是她的第一个结论。设下它，为的是把她送到欧伯佐恩的手掌心里。因为此刻她认清了一层要紧的情节：自打她踏进欧伯佐恩-斯米茨商行的办公室，直到那杯橙水的一节为止，她竟没有一回能随心所欲地来去。是他带她去用的午饭，又是他送她回来的；从宅子出来兜风的一路，有琼·牛顿做她的同伴，从宅子去舞厅的一路也是；打那以后她毫不怀疑，欧伯佐恩的监视一直未曾间断，直到……

===Original===
Dimly she remembered the man in the cloak who had stood in the rocking doorway. Was that Gonsalez? Somehow she thought it must have been. Gonsalez, watchful, alert﻿—why? She had been in danger﻿—was still in danger. Though why anybody should have picked unimportant her, was the greatest of all mysteries.

===Chinese===
她影影绰绰记起那个披斗篷、立在摇晃门洞里的男人。那是冈萨雷斯吗？不知怎么，她觉得必定是他。冈萨雷斯，警醒着，提防着——为什么？她处境危险——如今仍处在危险之中。虽说为什么会有人偏偏挑中她这样个无足轻重的角色，乃是所有谜团里最大的一桩谜。

===Original===
In some inexplicable way the death of Barberton had been associated with that advertisement and the attention she had received from Dr. Oberzohn and his creatures. Who was Lord Evington? She remembered his German accent and his “gracious lady,” the curious click of his heels and his stiff bow. That was a clumsy subterfuge which she ought to have seen through from the first. He was another of her watchers. And the drugged orangeade was his work. She shuddered. Suppose Leon Gonsalez, or whoever it was, had not arrived so providentially, where would she be at this moment?

===Chinese===
巴伯顿之死，不知怎的竟同那则广告、同她从欧伯佐恩博士及其爪牙那里领受的殷勤牵连在一处。埃文顿勋爵是什么人？她记起他的德国口音，记起他那声「雍容的小姐」，记起他脚跟古怪的一磕和那僵硬的鞠躬。那是一套笨拙的障眼法，她本该打头一眼就识破的。他又是盯她的人之一。那杯下了药的橙水，便是他的手笔。她不寒而栗。倘若莱昂·冈萨雷斯——不管那是谁——没有那样凑巧地赶到，她此刻会是何等光景？

===Original===
Walking to the window, she looked out, and the sight of the two men just inside the gate gave her a sense of infinite relief and calm; and the knowledge that she, for some reason, was under the care and protection of this strange organization about which she had read, thrilled her.

===Chinese===
她走到窗前向外望去，望见院门里侧立着的那两个人，心头涌起一阵说不尽的宽慰与安宁；而想到自己不知为了什么缘故，正处于这个她读到过的奇特组织的照拂护佑之下，又不禁心头激荡。

===Original===
She walked into the vaulted kitchen, to find the kitchen table covered with fat volumes, and Aunt Alma explaining to the interested nurse her system of filing. Two subjects interested that hard-featured lady: crime and family records. She had two books filled with snippings from country newspapers relating to the family of a distant cousin who had been raised to a peerage during the war. She had another devoted to the social triumphs of a distant woman, Goddard, who had finally made a sensational appearance as petitioner in the most celebrated divorce suit of the age. But crime, generally speaking, was Aunt Alma’s chief preoccupation. It was from these voluminous cuttings that Mirabelle had gained her complete knowledge of the Four Just Men and their operations. There were books packed with the story of the Ramon murder, arranged with loving care in order of time, for chronology was almost a vice in Alma Goddard. Only one public sensation was missing from her collection, and she was explaining the reason to the nurse as Mirabelle came into the kitchen.

===Chinese===
她走进那间拱顶的厨房，只见厨房大桌上摊满了一册册厚本子，阿尔玛姨妈正对着听得津津有味的护士讲解她那套归档的法门。能叫这位面貌冷硬的太太上心的题目有两个：罪案，和家世记载。她有两本簿子贴满了乡间报纸的剪片，事关一位战时膺封爵位的远房表亲之家；另有一本，专贴一位远房女眷戈达德家的社交风光——这位太太后来在本时代最负盛名的那桩离婚案里以呈请人的身份登场，轰动一时。不过总的说来，阿尔玛姨妈最念念不忘的还是罪案。米拉贝尔对四义士及其行事的一整套完备知识，正是从这些卷帙浩繁的剪报里得来的。有好几本簿子贴满了拉蒙（Ramon）谋杀案的始末，按年月先后用心编次，井井有条——编年排序在阿尔玛·戈达德几乎算得一桩癖好。她的收藏里唯独缺了一桩轰动，米拉贝尔走进厨房时，她正向护士说明缘故。

===Original===
“No, my dear,” she was saying, “there is nothing about The Snake. I won’t have anything to do with that: it gives me the creeps. In fact, I haven’t read anything that has the slightest reference to it.”

===Chinese===
「没有的事，亲爱的。」她正说着，「关于『毒蛇』的一概没有。我不愿同那东西沾边：它叫我浑身发毛。说实话，凡是稍微沾着它一点边的文字，我一篇也没有读过。」

===Original===
