"Nothing; she will be kept here for a week. I'll swear to you that nothing will happen to her. At the end of a week she's to be released without a hair of her head being harmed."

===Chinese===
这是句老承诺了，从前也给过，从未兑现，却始终存着盼头。可这一回，它没能完全驱散她的不安。

「可你打算把那姑娘怎么样？」她问。

「不怎么样；她要在这儿关一个礼拜。我向你担保，她绝不会出事。一个礼拜到头，就把她放出去，头上连一根头发也伤不了。」

===Original===
She looked at him searchingly. As far as she was able to judge, he was speaking the truth. And yet—

"I can't understand it,"—she shook her head, and for once Monty Newton was patient with her.

===Chinese===
她审视地望着他。以她所能判断的而论，他说的是真话。然而——

「我弄不懂。」——她摇了摇头；而这一回，蒙蒂·牛顿竟对她耐住了性子。

===Original===
"She's the owner of a big property in Africa, and that we shall get, if things work out right," he said. "The point is that she must claim within a few days. If she doesn't, the property is ours."

===Chinese===
「她是一大片非洲产业的主人，只要事情顺当，那产业就到手了。」他说。「要紧的是她必须在几天之内出面认领。她若不认领，产业就是我们的了。」

===Original===
Her face cleared.

"Is that all?" She believed him, knew him well enough to detect his rare sincerity. "That's taken a load off my mind, Monty. Of course I'll stay and look after her for you—it makes it easier to know that nothing will happen. What are those baize things behind the furnace—they look like boxes?"

===Chinese===
她的脸色开朗了。

「就这些？」她信了他的话；她深知其人，辨得出他难得一露的诚恳。「这下我心里一块石头落了地，蒙蒂。我当然留下替你照看她——知道不会出事，心里就好受多了。炉子后头那些蒙着呢面的东西是什么——瞧着像箱子？」

===Original===
He turned on her quickly.

"I was going to tell you about those," he said. "You're not to touch them under any circumstances. They belong to the old man and he's very stuffy about such things. Leave them just as they are. Let him touch them and nobody else. Do you understand?"

===Chinese===
他霍地朝她转过身来。

「我正要跟你说那些呢。」他说。「无论如何你也别碰它们。那是老头子的东西，他对这类事顶顶讲究。原样放着别动。只许他碰，别人一概不许。你明白吗？」

===Original===
She nodded, and, to his surprise, pecked his cheek with her cold lips.

"I'll help you, boy," she said tremulously. "Maybe that trip will come off after all, if—"

"If what?"

===Chinese===
她点了点头，还——叫他吃了一惊——用冰凉的嘴唇在他脸颊上啄了一下。

「我会帮你的，伙计。」她颤声说。「说不定那趟旅行到头来真能成行，只要——」

「只要什么？」

===Original===
"Those men—the men you were talking about—the Four Just Men, don't they call themselves? They scare me sick, Monty! They were the people who took her away before, and they'll kill us—even Oberzohn says that. They're after him. Has he"—she hesitated—"has he killed anybody? That snake stuff… you're not in it, are you, Monty?"

===Chinese===
「那几个人——你说起过的那几个人——四义士，他们是这么自称的吧？他们把我吓得魂儿都没了，蒙蒂！先前把她带走的就是他们，他们会杀了我们的——连欧伯佐恩都这么说。他们正在追他。他有没有」——她迟疑了一下——「他杀过人没有？那套蛇的勾当……你没有掺和进去吧，蒙蒂？」

===Original===
She looked more like a child than a sophisticated woman, clinging to his arm, her blue eyes looking pleadingly into his.

"Stuff! What do I know about snakes?" He disengaged himself and came back to where Oberzohn was waiting, a figure of patience.

===Chinese===
她挽着他的胳膊，一双蓝眼睛哀恳地望着他，看上去不像个世故的女人，倒像个孩子。

「胡说！蛇的事我知道什么？」他挣脱开身子，回到欧伯佐恩等候的地方——那是一副耐心静候的身形。

===Original===
The girl was lying on the bed, her face in the crook of her arm, and he was gazing at her, his expression inscrutable.

"That is all, then. Good night, gracious ladies."

===Chinese===
姑娘躺在床上，脸埋在臂弯里；他凝视着她，神情高深莫测。

「如此便都妥当了。晚安，雍容的小姐们。」

===Original===
He turned and marched back towards the step and waved his hand. Monty followed. The girl heard the thud of the trap fall, the scrape of the old man's boots, and then a rumbling sound, which she did not immediately understand. Later, when in a panic, she tried the trap, she found that a heavy barrel had been put on top, and that it was immovable.

===Chinese===
他转身朝台阶开步走回，摆了摆手。蒙蒂跟了上去。姑娘听见活板门落下的闷响、老头靴底的刮擦声，跟着又是一阵隆隆声——她一时没能明白那是什么。后来她惊慌之下伸手去扳活板门，才发现上头压了一只沉重的桶，纹丝不动。

## XX Gurther Reports / 二十 格瑟复命

===Original===
Dr. Oberzhon had not been to bed for thirty-five years. It was his practice to sleep in a chair, and alternate his dozes with copious draughts from his favourite authors. Mostly the books were about the soul, and free will, and predestination, with an occasional dip into Nietzsche by way of light recreation. In ordinary circumstances he would have had need for all the philosophy he could master; for ruin had come. The destruction of his store, which, to all intents and purposes, was uninsured, would have been the crowning stroke of fate but for the golden vision ahead.

===Chinese===
欧伯佐恩博士已有三十五年不曾上床睡觉了。他的习惯是坐在椅中睡，打盹与大口畅饮心爱作家们的篇章轮换交替。那些书多半谈灵魂、谈自由意志、谈宿命，偶尔也翻一翻尼采，权当轻松的消遣。若在寻常境况，他本需要使出他所能掌握的全部哲学——因为大祸已然临头。他的货栈毁于一炬，而那货栈实际上等于没上保险——若非前头悬着那金色的幻景，这一场毁灭本该算是命运压顶的最后一击。

===Original===
Villa, that handsome half-breed, had arrived in England and had been with the doctor all the evening. At that moment he was on his way to Liverpool to catch the Coast boat, and he had left with his master a record of the claims that had already been pegged out on Monte Doro, as he so picturesquely renamed the new mountain. There were millions there; uncountable wealth. And between the Herr Doktor and the achievement of this colossal fortune was a life which he had no immediate desire to take. The doctor was a bachelor; women bored him. Yet he was prepared to take the extreme step if by so doing he could doubly ensure his fortune. Mirabelle dead gave him one chance; Mirabelle alive and persuaded, multiplied that chance by a hundred.

===Chinese===
维拉——那个相貌堂堂的混血儿——已经到了英格兰，整晚都陪在博士身边。这会儿他正赶往利物浦，去搭「海岸」航线的轮船；他给主人留下了一份记录，载明已经打桩圈定的各项矿权主张——全在蒙多尔山上，那座新山被他改成了这么个如画动听的名字。那里有千百万金，有数不清的财富。而横在博士先生与这笔泼天富贵之间的，是一条他眼下并不急于了结的性命。博士是个单身汉；女人叫他厌倦。然而，只要能叫这笔财富双倍保险，极端的一步他也预备迈出。米拉贝尔死了，给他一线机会；米拉贝尔活着并且被劝服，机会便百倍增长。

===Original===
He opened the book he was reading at the last page and took out the folded paper. It was a special licence to marry, and had been duly registered at the Greenwich Registrar’s Office since the day before the girl had entered his employment. This was his second and most powerful weapon. He could have been legally married on this nearly a week ago. It was effective for two months at least, and only five days separated him from the necessity of a decision. If the time expired, Mirabelle could live. It was quite a different matter, killing in cold blood a woman for whom the police would be searching, and with whose disappearance his name would be connected, from that other form of slaying he favoured: the striking down of strange men in crowded thoroughfares. She was not for the snake﻿—as yet.

===Chinese===
他把手头正读的书翻到最后一页，取出那张折起的纸。那是一纸特别结婚许可证，早在姑娘进他商行受聘的前一天，便已在格林尼治登记处正式注册。这是他的第二件、也是最厉害的一件武器。凭着它，他在将近一周之前便可以合法完婚。这证至少两个月有效，而距他必须作出决断之日，只隔着五天。若期限一过，米拉贝尔便可活命。冷血杀害一个警方势必全力搜寻、其失踪又必然牵连到他名下的女人，与他偏爱的另一种杀法——在熙来攘往的大街上放倒素不相识的男人——完全是两码事。她还轮不到「毒蛇」出手——至少眼下轮不到。

===Original===
He folded the paper carefully, put it back in the book and turned the page, when there was a gentle tap at the door and he sat up.

“Come in, Pfeiffer. March!”

The door opened slowly and a man sidled into the room, and at the sight of him Dr. Oberzohn gasped.

“Gurther!” he stammered, for once thrown out of his stride.

===Chinese===
他把那张纸仔细折好，放回书里，翻过一页——门口轻轻一叩，他坐直了身子。

「进来，法伊弗。开步走！」

门慢慢开了，一条人影侧身溜进屋来；一看见来人，欧伯佐恩博士倒抽了一口冷气。

「格瑟！」他结结巴巴地说，破天荒乱了方寸。

===Original===
Gurther smiled and nodded, his round eyes fixed on the tassel of the Herr Doktor’s smoking-cap.

“You have returned﻿—and failed?”

“The American, I think, is dead, Herr Doktor,” said the man in his staccato tone. “The so excellent Pfeiffer is also﻿—dead!”

===Chinese===
格瑟微笑点头，一双圆眼盯住博士先生吸烟帽上的流苏。

「你回来了——事败了？」

「那个美国人，我想，是死了，博士先生。」那人用他一顿一顿的腔调说。「那位好得不得了的法伊弗也——死了！」

===Original===
The doctor blinked twice.

“Dead?” he said gratingly. “Who told you this?”

“I saw him. Something happened﻿ ﻿… to the snake. Pfeiffer was bitten.”

===Chinese===
博士眨了两下眼睛。

「死了？」他沙着嗓子说。「这话是谁告诉你的？」

「我亲眼看见的。出了点事……蛇身上出了点事。法伊弗被咬了。」

===Original===
The old man’s hard eyes fixed him.

“So!” he said softly.

“He died very quickly﻿—in the usual manner,” jerked Gurther, still with that stupid smile.

“So!” said the doctor again. “All then was failure, and out of it comes an American, who is nothing, and Pfeiffer, who is much﻿—dead!”

===Chinese===
老头那双冷酷的眼睛盯住了他。

「哦。」他轻轻地说。

「他死得很快——照老样子死的。」格瑟一顿一顿地迸出这句，脸上仍挂着那副傻笑。

「哦！」博士又说。「这么说，到头来是一败涂地，败出来的却是一个美国人——一个无足轻重的人——和一个法伊弗——一个非同小可的人——死了！」

===Original===
“God have him in his keeping!” said Gurther, not lowering or raising his eyes. “And all the way back I thought this, Herr Doktor﻿—how much better that it should be Pfeiffer and not me. Though my nerves are so bad.”

===Chinese===
「愿上帝收留他！」格瑟说，眼皮不抬也不垂。「而且这一路上我一直在想，博士先生——死的是法伊弗而不是我，这该有多好。虽说我的神经坏得很。」

===Original===
“So!” said the doctor for the fourth time, and held out his hand.

Gurther slipped his fingers into his waistcoat pocket and took out a gold cigarette-case. The doctor opened it and looked at the five cigarettes that reposed, at the two halves of the long holder neatly lying in their proper place, closed the case with a snap and laid it on the table.

===Chinese===
「哦！」博士第四次说道，伸出了手。

格瑟把手指伸进马甲口袋，掏出一只金烟盒。博士打开烟盒，看了看安然躺着的那五支香烟，又看了看长烟嘴的两截整齐地躺在各自的原位上，随即啪的一声合上盒盖，把它搁在桌上。

===Original===
“What shall I do with you, Gurther? Tomorrow the police will come and search this house.”

“There is the cellar, Herr Doktor: it is very comfortable there. I would prefer it.”

===Chinese===
「叫我拿你怎么办呢，格瑟？明天警察就要来搜这宅子了。」

「有地窖在呀，博士先生：那里面可舒服了。我宁可待在那儿。」

===Original===
Dr. Oberzohn made a gesture like a boy wiping something from a slate.

“That is not possible: it is in occupation,” he said. “I must find a new place for you.” He stared and mused. “There is the boat,” he said.

Gurther’s smile did not fade.

===Chinese===
欧伯佐恩博士做了个手势，活像个学童从石板上抹去什么字迹。

「那可不行：里头已经有主了。」他说。「我得给你另找个地方。」他瞪着眼出神。「有一条船。」他说。

格瑟脸上的笑意并未消退。

===Original===
The boat was a small barge, which had been drawn up into the private dock of the O. & S. factory, and had been rotting there for years, the playing-ground of rats, the dosshouse of the homeless. The doctor saw what was in the man’s mind.

===Chinese===
那船是条小驳船，早年被拖进欧伯佐恩-斯米茨商行厂区私用的船坞，就此烂了多少年，成了老鼠的游乐场、无家可归者的寄宿窝棚。博士看出了这人心里的念头。

===Original===
“It may be comfortable. I will give you some gas to kill the rats, and it will only be for five-six days.”

“*Ja*, Herr Doktor.”

“For tonight you may sleep in the kitchen. One does not expect﻿—”

===Chinese===
「那上头兴许挺舒服。我会给你些毒气去熏老鼠，前后也就住个五六天。」

「*Ja*, Herr Doktor.」

「今夜你可以在厨房里睡。谁也不会想到——」

===Original===
There was a thunderous knock on the outer door. The two men looked at one another, but still Gurther grinned.

“I think it is the police,” said the doctor calmly.

===Chinese===
外门响起雷鸣般的敲门声。两个人对望了一眼，格瑟却照样咧着嘴笑。

「我想是警察。」博士平静地说。

===Original===
He got up to his feet, lifted the seat of a long hard-looking sofa, disclosing a deep cavity, and Gurther slipped in, and the seat was replaced. This done, the doctor waddled to the door and turned the key.

===Chinese===
他站起身，掀起一张样子硬邦邦的长沙发的坐面，露出一处深腔；格瑟一钻而入，坐面随即归位。做完这一切，博士才摇摇摆摆地走向门口，拧动钥匙开了锁。

===Original===
“Good morning, Inspector Meadows.”

“May I come in?” said Meadows.

Behind him were two police officers, one in uniform.

===Chinese===
「早安，米多斯探长。」

「我可以进来吗？」米多斯说。

他身后站着两名警官，其中一名穿制服。

===Original===
“Do you wish to see me? Certainly.” He held the door cautiously open and only Meadows came in, and preceded the doctor into his study.

===Chinese===
「你要见我？请便是了。」他谨慎地开了门，只放米多斯一人进来；米多斯走在博士前头，进了书房。

===Original===
“I want Mirabelle Leicester,” said Meadows curtly. “She was abducted from her home in the early hours of this morning, and I have information that the car which took her away came to this house. There are tracks of wheels in the mud outside.”

===Chinese===
「我要米拉贝尔·莱斯特。」米多斯生硬地说。「今天凌晨她在家中被人劫走，我得到线报，载走她的那辆汽车来过这宅子。外头泥地里还留着车轮印。」

===Original===
“If there are car tracks, they are mine,” said the doctor calmly. He enumerated the makes of machines he possessed. “There is another matter: as to cars having come here in the night, I have a sense of hearing, Mr. Inspector Meadows, and I have heard many cars in Hangman’s Lane﻿—but not in my ground. Also, I’m sure you have not come to tell me of abducted girls, but to disclose to me the miscreant who burnt my store. That is what I expected of you.”

===Chinese===
「要是有车轮印，那也是我的车留下的。」博士平静地说。他把自己名下那几辆车的牌子挨个报了一遍。「另有一桩：至于夜里有没有汽车来过此地，我是长着耳朵的，米多斯探长先生，绞刑吏巷上过往的汽车我听得多了——可没有一辆进过我的地界。再者，我确信你不是来对我讲什么被劫的姑娘的，而是来向我揭出烧掉我货栈的那个恶徒。我对你抱的正是这份指望。」

===Original===
“What you expect of me and what you get will be entirely different propositions,” said Meadows unpleasantly. “Now come across, Oberzohn! We know why you want this girl﻿—the whole plot has been blown. You think you’ll prevent her from making a claim on the Portuguese Government for the renewal of a concession granted in June, 1912, to her father.”

===Chinese===
「你指望的是什么和你将得到的是什么，完全是两回事。」米多斯不客气地说。「放明白点吧，欧伯佐恩！我们知道你为什么非要这姑娘不可——整个阴谋已经败露。你自以为能拦住她向葡萄牙政府主张续展一纸租契——那租契是 1912 年 6 月授予她父亲的。」

===Original===
If Dr. Oberzohn was shocked to learn that his secret was out, he did not show it by his face. Not a muscle moved.

===Chinese===
纵然听闻秘密败露叫欧伯佐恩博士心惊，他脸上也无一丝显露。一块肌肉都没有动。

===Original===
“Of such matters I know nothing. It is a fantasy, a story of fairies. Yet it must be true, Mr. Inspector Meadows, if you say it. No: I think you are deceived by the criminals of Curzon Street, W. Men of blood and murder, with records that are infamous. You desire to search my house? It is your privilege.” He waved his hand. “I do not ask you for the ticket of search. From basement to attic the house is yours.”

===Chinese===
「这类事情我一无所知。那是凭空的幻想，是神仙故事。不过，米多斯探长先生，既然你这么说了，那必定是真的。不：我看你是被西区柯曾街那伙罪犯欺骗了。一伙杀人流血的凶徒，个个都有一本臭名昭著的劣迹账。你想搜我的宅子？那是你的特权。」他摆了摆手。「我不向你要搜查的票证。从地窖到阁楼，这宅子归你。」

===Original===
He was not surprised when Meadows took him at his word, and, going out into the hall, summoned his assistants. They visited each room separately, the old cook and the half-witted Danish girl accepting this visitation as a normal occurrence: they had every excuse to do so, for this was the second time in a fortnight that the house had been visited by the police.

===Chinese===
米多斯说的当真就做，他并不意外。米多斯走到外面大厅里唤来手下，一间一间屋子分别查看；老厨娘和那个半傻的丹麦女仆把这一场查看当作寻常事受了下来：她们尽可以这样想——两个星期之内，警察光顾这宅子已是第二回了。

===Original===
“Now I’ll take a look at your room, if you don’t mind,” said Meadows.

His quick eyes caught sight of the box ottoman against the wall, and the fact that the doctor was sitting thereon added to his suspicions.

“I will look in here, if you please,” he said.

===Chinese===
「现在我要瞧瞧你的房间——你不介意吧。」米多斯说。

他那双锐利的眼睛一下就瞥见了靠墙放着的箱式软凳，而博士本人偏偏端坐在上头，这更添了他心头的疑窦。

「劳驾，这里面我要看一看。」他说。

===Original===
Oberzohn rose and the detective lifted the lid. It was empty. The ottoman had been placed against the wall, at the bottom of which was a deep recess. Gurther had long since rolled through the false back.

===Chinese===
欧伯佐恩站起身来，探长掀开了凳盖。里面空空如也。软凳是靠墙放着的，墙根上有一道深深的凹龛——格瑟早已从那扇活动的假后壁滚出去了。

===Original===
“You see﻿—nothing,” said Oberzohn. “Now perhaps you would like to search my factory? Perhaps amongst the rafters and the burnt girders I may conceal a something. Or the barge in my slipway? Who knows what I may place amongst the rats?”

===Chinese===
「你瞧——什么也没有。」欧伯佐恩说。「现在你或许乐意搜一搜我的厂子？说不定我会在椽木之间、烧焦的钢梁之间藏下一样什么东西。或者搜搜我滑道上的那条驳船？谁知道我会往老鼠堆里搁点什么呢？」

===Original===
“You’re almost clever,” said Meadows, “and I don’t profess to be a match for you. But there are three men in this town who are! I’ll be frank with you, Oberzohn. I want to put you where I can give you a fair trial, in accordance with the law of this country, and I shall resist, to the best of my ability, any man taking the law into his own hands. But whether you’re innocent or guilty, I wouldn’t stand in your shoes for all the money in Angola!”

===Chinese===
「你差不多算得上聪明。」米多斯说，「我也不敢冒充是你的对手。可这座城里有三个人是！我跟你打开天窗说亮话，欧伯佐恩。我要把你送到一个地方，好叫我能够依照本国的法律给你一场公正的审判；无论是谁想把法律攥进自己手里私行了断，我都将竭尽所能加以阻止。可无论你有罪无罪，就是把安哥拉全部的钱财都给我，我也不肯换到你的处境里去！」

===Original===
“So?” said the doctor politely.

===Chinese===
「是吗？」博士彬彬有礼地说。

===Original===
“Give up this girl, and I rather fancy that half your danger will be at an end. I tell you, you’re too clever for me. It’s a stupid thing for a police officer to say, but I can’t get at the bottom of your snake. They have.”

===Chinese===
「交出这姑娘，我倒觉得你的凶险便去掉了一半。告诉你，你太聪明，我不是对手。这话出自一个警官之口未免窝囊，可我实在探不到你那条蛇的底。他们探到了。」

===Original===
The old man’s brows worked up and down.

“Indeed?” he said blandly. “And of which snake do you speak?”

===Chinese===
老头的眉毛上下耸动。

「当真？」他温和地说。「那你说的是哪一条蛇呢？」

===Original===
Meadows said nothing more. He had given his warning: if Oberzohn did not profit thereby, he would be the loser.

===Chinese===
米多斯没有再多说。他已把警告放下了：欧伯佐恩若不从中取益，吃亏的便是他自己。

===Original===
Nobody doubted, least of all he, that, in defiance of all laws that man had made, independent of all the machinery of justice that human ingenuity had devised, inevitable punishment awaited Oberzohn and was near at hand.

===Chinese===
谁都不怀疑——米多斯最不怀疑——这场无视人类所立的一切法律、不借人类巧思所设的一切司法机器的惩罚，无可逃遁地等着欧伯佐恩，而且已迫在眉睫。

## XXI The Account Book / 二十一 账本
===Original===
It was five o’clock in the morning when the mud-spattered Spanz dropped down through the mist and driving rain of the Chiltern Hills and struck the main Gloucester Road, pulling up with a jerk before Heavytree Farm. Manfred sprang out, but before he could reach the door, Aunt Alma had opened it, and by the look of her face he saw that she had not slept that night.
“Where is Digby?” he asked.
“He’s gone to interview the Chief Constable,” said Alma. “Come in, Mr. Gonsalez.”
Leon was wet from head to foot: there was not a dry square centimetre upon him. But he was his old cheerful self as he stamped into the hall, shaking himself free of his heavy mackintosh.

===Chinese===
清晨五点钟，那辆溅满泥浆的「斯潘兹」穿过奇尔特恩丘陵（Chiltern Hills）的雾气与斜雨冲下山来，驶上通往格洛斯特的干道，在赫维特里农场门前一顿停住。曼弗雷德跳下车，还没走到门口，阿尔玛姨妈已把门开了；看她的脸色，他便知道她一夜没有合眼。
「迪格比呢？」他问。
「他去见郡警察总监（Chief Constable）了。」阿尔玛说。「请进，冈萨雷斯先生。」
莱昂浑身上下没有一处干的地方：找不出一平方厘米的干处。可他跺着脚走进门厅时，仍是那副乐呵呵的老样子，一面甩脱身上沉重的胶布雨衣。

===Original===
“Digby, of course, heard nothing, George.”
“I’m the lightest sleeper in the world,” said Aunt Alma, “but I heard not a sound. The first thing I knew was when a policeman came up and knocked at my door and told me that he’d found the front door open.”
“No clue was left at all?”
“Yes,” said Aunt Alma. They went into the drawing-room and she took up from the table a small black bottle with a tube and cap attached. “I found this behind the sofa. She’d been lying on the sofa; the cushions were thrown on the floor and she tore the tapestry in her struggle.”

===Chinese===
「迪格比当然是什么也没听见，乔治。」
「我是天底下睡觉最惊醒的人，」阿尔玛姨妈说，「可我连一点声响也没有听见。我知道的头一件事，就是一个警察上来敲我的门，告诉我他发现前门敞着。」
「一点线索也没留下？」
「有。」阿尔玛姨妈说。他们走进客厅，她从桌上拿起一只小黑瓶，瓶上连着一根管子和一个罩子。「这是我在沙发后头找到的。她当时正躺在沙发上；坐垫全给摔在地上，她挣扎时把挂毯都撕裂了。」

===Original===
Leon turned the faucet, and, as the gas hissed out, sniffed.
“The new dental gas,” he said. “But how did they get in? No window was open or forced?”
“They came in at the door: I’m sure of that. And they had a woman with them,” said Aunt Alma proudly.
“How do you know?”

===Chinese===
莱昂拧开旋塞，气体嘶嘶冒出，他嗅了嗅。
「新式的牙科麻醉气。」他说。「可他们是怎么进来的？窗户没有一扇开着，也没有一扇被撬过？」
「他们是从门进来的：这一点我有把握。而且他们还带了个女人来。」阿尔玛姨妈得意地说。
「您怎么知道？」

===Original===
“There must have been a woman,” said Aunt Alma. “Mirabelle would not have opened the door except to a woman, without waking either myself or Mr. Digby.”
Leon nodded, his eyes gleaming.
“Obviously,” he said.
“And I found the marks of a woman’s foot in the passage. It is dried now, but you can still see it.”
“I have already seen it,” said Leon. “It is to the left of the door: a small pointed shoe and a rubber heel. Miss Leicester opened the door to the woman, the men came in, and the rest was easy. You can’t blame Digby,” he said appealingly to George.

===Chinese===
「必定带了个女人来。」阿尔玛姨妈说。「换了不是女人，米拉贝尔绝不会开门——不然非吵醒我或迪格比先生不可。」
莱昂点了点头，眼里闪着光。
「明摆着的。」他说。
「我还在过道里发现了一个女人的脚印。现在已经干了，可你们还看得见。」
「我已经看过了。」莱昂说。「在门的左边：一只尖头小鞋，一只橡胶鞋跟。莱斯特小姐给那女人开了门，汉子们一拥而入，往下就都好办了。你可怪不着迪格比。」他这话是向着乔治说的，带着求情的口气。

===Original===
He was the friend at court of every agent, but this time Manfred did not argue with him.
“I blame myself,” he said. “Poiccart told me﻿—”
“He was here,” said Aunt Alma.
“Who﻿—Poiccart?” asked Manfred, surprised, and Gonsalez slapped his knee.
“That’s it, of course! What fools we are! We ought to have known why this wily old fox had left his post. What time was he here?”
Alma told him all the circumstances of the visit.

===Chinese===
手下凡是办事的人，他一概是替他们在东家面前说话的「朝中之人」；只是这一回，曼弗雷德没有同他争。
「我怪我自己。」他说。「波伊卡特提醒过我——」
「他来过这儿。」阿尔玛姨妈说。
「谁——波伊卡特？」曼弗雷德愕然问；冈萨雷斯一拍膝盖。
「正是这么回事！咱们多蠢！这只老奸巨猾的狐狸为什么擅离职守，咱们早该想到的。他几点钟来的？」
阿尔玛把那回造访的种种情形讲给他听。

===Original===
“He must have left the house immediately after us,” said Leon, with a wide grin of amusement, “caught the five o’clock train for Gloucester, taxied across.”
“And after that?” suggested Manfred.
Leon scratched his chin.
“I wonder if he’s back?” He took up the telephone and put a trunk call through to London. “Somehow I don’t think he is. Here’s Digby, looking as if he expected to be summarily executed.”
The police pensioner was indeed in a mournful and pathetic mood.

===Chinese===
「他准是紧跟着咱们后脚离开的宅子，」莱昂说，乐得咧开了嘴，「赶上五点钟去格洛斯特的火车，再叫出租汽车横穿过来。」
「再往后呢？」曼弗雷德提了一句。
莱昂挠了挠下巴。
「不知他回去了没有？」他拿起电话，往伦敦挂了个长途。「也不知怎么，我觉得他没有回去。迪格比来了——瞧他那副样子，活像等着就地正法似的。」
这位领警队退休金的看宅人，果然一副愁苦可怜的神气。

===Original===
“I don’t know what you’ll think of me, Mr. Manfred﻿—” he began.
“I’ve already expressed a view on that subject.” George smiled faintly. “I’m not blaming you, Digby. To leave a man who has been knocked about as you have been without an opposite number, was the height of folly. I didn’t expect them back so soon. As a matter of fact, I intended putting four men on from today. You’ve been making inquiries?”
“Yes, sir. The car went through Gloucester very early in the morning and took the Swindon road. It was seen by a cyclist policeman; he said there was a fat roll of tarpaulin lying on the tent of the trolley.”
“No sign of anybody chasing it in a car, or on a motor-bicycle?” asked Manfred anxiously.

===Chinese===
「我都不知道您会怎么看我，曼弗雷德先生——」他开口道。
「对这个题目，我已经发表过意见了。」乔治淡淡一笑。「我不是怪你，迪格比。让一个像你这样挨过一顿毒打的人独自值守、连个对班的人都没有，实在是愚蠢到了家。我没料到他们这么快就卷土重来。说实话，我本打算从今天起加派四个人。你去打听了？」
「打听了，先生。那辆车一大清早穿过格洛斯特，上了往斯温登去的路。有个骑自行车的巡警看见了它；他说车篷上躺着一卷鼓鼓囊囊的油布。」
「没见谁开车追它，或是骑摩托车追？」曼弗雷德焦灼地问。

===Original===
Poiccart had recently taken to motorcycling.
“No, sir.”
“You saw Mr. Poiccart?”
“Yes, he was just going back to London. He said he wanted to see the place with his own eyes.”
George was disappointed. If it had been a visit of curiosity, Poiccart’s absence from town was understandable. He would not have returned at the hour he was rung up.

===Chinese===
波伊卡特近来迷上了骑摩托车。
「没有，先生。」
「你见着波伊卡特先生了？」
「见着了，他正要回伦敦去。他说他要亲眼看看这地方。」
乔治好生失望。倘若那不过是一趟好奇的探访，波伊卡特不在城里倒还说得通；果真那样，人家摇电话找他的那个钟点，他原是不会已经回到伦敦的。

===Original===
Aunt Alma was cooking a hasty breakfast, and they had accepted her offer gratefully, for both men were famished; and they were in the midst of the meal when the London call came through.
“Is that you, Poiccart?”
“That is I,” said Poiccart’s voice. “Where are you speaking from?”
“Heavytree Farm. Did you see anything of Miss Leicester?”
There was a pause.
“Has she gone?”
“You didn’t know?”
Another pause.
“Oh, yes, I knew; in fact, I accompanied her part of the way to London, and was bumped off when the trolley struck a refuge on the Great West Road. Meadows is here: he has just come from Oberzohn’s. He says he has found nothing.”

===Chinese===
阿尔玛姨妈忙着张罗一顿仓促的早饭，两人感激地领了她的情——都饿坏了；饭吃到一半，伦敦的长途接通了。
「是波伊卡特吗？」
「是我。」波伊卡特的嗓音说。「你从哪儿打来？」
「赫维特里农场。莱斯特小姐的事，你见到什么没有？」
停了片刻。
「她走了？」
「你不知道？」
又停了片刻。
「哦，不，我知道；说实在的，我还送了她一程去伦敦的路，只是篷车在大西路上撞上安全岛时，把我颠了下来。米多斯在这儿：他刚从欧伯佐恩那儿过来。他说什么也没找着。」

===Original===
Manfred thought for a while.
“We will be back soon after nine,” he said.
“Leon driving you?” was the dry response.
“Yes﻿—in spite of which we shall be back at nine.”
“That man has got a grudge against my driving,” said Leon, when Manfred reported the conversation. “I knew it was he when Digby described the car and said there was a fat roll of mackintosh on the top. ‘Fat roll’ is not a bad description. Do you know whether Poiccart spoke to Miss Leicester?”
“Yes, he asked her if she grew onions”﻿—a reply which sent Leon into fits of silent laughter.

===Chinese===
曼弗雷德想了一会儿。
「九点刚过我们就回来。」他说。
「莱昂开车送你们？」回话的口吻干巴巴的。
「是——虽说他开车，九点我们照样回来。」
「那人对我的车技记恨在心。」曼弗雷德把通话转述之后，莱昂说。「迪格比形容那辆车、说车顶上躺着一卷鼓鼓囊囊的胶布雨衣时，我就知道是他。『鼓鼓囊囊的一卷』，形容得不坏。你知道波伊卡特同莱斯特小姐搭话没有？」
「搭了，他问她种不种洋葱」——这一答惹得莱昂无声地笑了一阵又一阵。

===Original===
Breakfast was over and they were making their preparations for departure, when Leon asked unexpectedly: “Has Miss Leicester a writing-table of her own?”
“Yes, in her room,” said Alma, and took him up to show him the old bureau.
He opened the drawers without apology, took out some old letters, turned them over, reading them shamelessly. Then he opened the blotter. There were several sheets of blank paper headed “Heavytree Farm,” and two which bore her signature at the bottom. Alma explained that the bank account of the establishment was in Mirabelle’s name, and, when it was necessary to draw cash, it was a rule of the bank that it should be accompanied by a covering letter﻿—a practice which still exists in some of the old West-country banking establishments. She unlocked a drawer that he had not been able to open and showed him a chequebook with three blank cheques signed with her name.

===Chinese===
早饭吃罢，两人正收拾动身，莱昂忽然出其不意地问：「莱斯特小姐自己有一张写字台吗？」
「有，在她房里。」阿尔玛说，便领他上楼去看那张旧写字台。
他也不打个招呼就拉开抽屉，取出几封旧信，一封封翻过来，读得毫无愧色。接着他翻开吸墨纸本。里头有几张空白信笺，抬头印着「赫维特里农场」，还有两张底下签着她的名字。阿尔玛解释说，这家庄户的银行账户开在米拉贝尔名下，需要提取现金时，照银行的规矩须随附一封签名函——这种做法，在西部各郡几家老式银行里至今犹存。她还打开一个他没能打开的抽屉，给他看一本支票簿，簿里有三张签好了她名字的空白支票。

===Original===
“That banker has known me since I was so high,” said Alma scornfully. “You wouldn’t think there’d be so much red-tape.”
Leon nodded.
“Do you keep any account books?”
“Yes, I do,” said Alma in surprise. “The household accounts, you mean?”
“Could I see one?”
She went out and returned with a thin ledger, and he made a brief examination of its contents. Wholly inadequate, thought Alma, considering the trouble she had taken and the interest he had shown.
“That’s that,” he said. “Now, George, *en voiture*!”

===Chinese===
「那位银行家打我这么点儿高的时候就认识我了。」阿尔玛不屑地说。「真想不到还有这许多繁文缛节。」
莱昂点了点头。
「家里记账本吗？」
「记的。」阿尔玛讶然说。「你是说家用账？」
「能给我看一本吗？」
她出去拿来一本薄薄的账本，他把里头的内容草草检视了一遍。想她费的那番心思、他先前显的那份兴致，这检视未免草率得可怜——阿尔玛心里这么想。
「行了，要办的办妥了。」他说。「走吧，乔治，*en voiture*（法语：上车啦）！」

===Original===
“Why did you want to see the account book?” asked Manfred as they bowled up the road.
“I am naturally commercial-minded,” was the unsatisfactory reply. “And, George, we’re short of juice. Pray like a knight in armour that we sight a filling station in the next ten minutes.”
If George had prayed, the prayer would have been answered: just as the cylinders started to miss they pulled up the car before a garage, and took in a supply which was more than sufficient to carry them to their destination. It was nine o’clock exactly when the car stopped before the house. Poiccart, watching the arrival from George’s room, smiled grimly at the impertinent gesture of the chauffeur.

===Chinese===
「你要看那本账本做什么？」汽车沿大路疾驰而上时，曼弗雷德问。
「我天生一副生意人的头脑。」回答得不能令人满意。「还有，乔治，咱们的汽油快没了。快像披甲的骑士那样祈祷吧，求上天保佑咱们在十分钟之内望见一座加油站。」
乔治若是真祈祷了，这祷告也应验了：气缸刚要断火，他们正好把车停在一家车行门前，加的油足够他们开到目的地还有富余。汽车在宅子门前停稳时，恰好九点整。波伊卡特正从乔治房里望着他们到达，见司机那放肆的手势，冷冷一笑。

===Original===
Behind locked doors the three sat in conference.
“This has upset all my plans,” said Leon at last. “If the girl was safe, I should settle with Oberzohn tonight.”

===Chinese===
三人闩上房门，密谈了一番。
「这一下把我的全盘计划都打乱了。」莱昂终于开口。「姑娘若是平安，今夜我就要同欧伯佐恩清账了。」

===Original===
George Manfred stroked his chin thoughtfully. He had once worn a trim little beard, and had never got out of that beard-stroking habit of his.
“We think exactly alike. I intended suggesting that course,” he said gravely.
“The trouble is Meadows. I should like the case to have been settled one way or the other, and for Meadows to be out of it altogether. One doesn’t wish to embarrass him. But the urgency is very obvious. It would have been very easy,” said Leon, a note of regret in his gentle voice. “Now of course it is impossible until the girl is safe. But for that”﻿—he shrugged his shoulders﻿—“tomorrow friend Oberzohn would have experienced a sense of lassitude. No pain﻿ ﻿… just a little tiredness. Sleep, coma﻿—death on the third day. He is an old man, and one has no desire to hurt the aged. There is no hurt like fear. As for Gurther, we will try a more violent method, unless Oberzohn gets him first. I sincerely hope he does.”
“This is news to me. What is this about Gurther?” asked Poiccart.
Manfred told him.

===Chinese===
乔治·曼弗雷德若有所思地抚着下巴。他从前蓄过一撮修剪得整整齐齐的小胡子，那抚须的习惯却一直没有丢掉。
「咱们所见完全相同。我正要提议这么办。」他正色说。
「麻烦在米多斯。我倒愿意这案子早已这样或那样地了结，让米多斯彻底置身事外。谁也不愿叫他左右为难。可是紧迫是明摆着的。那本来是很容易的，」莱昂说，温和的嗓音里带着一丝惋惜。「如今当然不行了——得等姑娘平安到手。若不是这一层」——他耸了耸肩——「明天，咱们的欧伯佐恩朋友就要尝到一阵倦乏了。没有痛苦……只有一点疲累。睡眠，昏迷——第三天上死。他是上了年纪的人，谁也不想难为老人。天底下再没有一种伤害赶得上恐惧。至于格瑟，咱们要换一个更激烈些的法子——除非欧伯佐恩先把他收拾了。我衷心盼着他收拾。」
「这可是新闻。格瑟是怎么回事？」波伊卡特问。
曼弗雷德讲给他听。

===Original===
“Leon is right now,” Poiccart nodded. He rose from the table and unlocked the door. “If any of you men wish to sleep, your rooms are ready; the curtains are drawn, and I will wake you at such and such an hour.”
But neither were inclined for sleep. George had to see a client that morning: a man with a curious story to tell. Leon wanted a carburetter adjusted. They would both sleep in the afternoon, they said.
The client arrived soon after. Poiccart admitted him and put him in the dining-room to wait before he reported his presence.

===Chinese===
「这么说，莱昂这回是对的。」波伊卡特点头。他从桌边起身，开了门锁。「你们哪位想睡的话，房间都备好了；窗帘也拉严了，到该叫的钟点我会叫醒你们。」
可两人都没有睡意。乔治上午要见一位委托人：那人有桩离奇的故事要讲。莱昂要调一调化油器。他们说，觉留到下午再睡。
委托人不久就到了。波伊卡特开门让他进来，先安置在饭厅里等候，才上去通报。

===Original===
“I think this is your harem man,” he said, and went downstairs to show up the caller.
He was a commonplace-looking man with a straggling, fair moustache and a weak chin.
“Debilitated or degenerate,” he suggested.

===Chinese===
「我想，这就是您那位『后宫』客人了。」他说着，下楼引客上来。
那是个相貌平平的男人，蓄着一撇稀稀拉拉的浅色小胡子，下巴绵软无力。
「不是先天羸弱，就是后天堕落。」他品评道。

===Original===
“Probably a little of both,” assented Manfred, when the butler had announced him.
He came nervously into the room and sat down opposite to Manfred.
“I tried to get you on the phone last night,” he complained, “but I got no answer.”

===Chinese===
「多半两样各占一点。」管家通报过来客的名姓，曼弗雷德附和道。
他怯生生地走进屋来，在曼弗雷德对面坐下。
「昨儿夜里我想打电话找您，」他抱怨道，「可是没人接。」

===Original===
“My office hours are from ten till two,” said George good-humouredly. “Now will you tell me again this story of your sister?”
The man leaned back in the chair and clasped his knees, and began in a singsong voice, as though he were reciting something that he had learned by heart.

===Chinese===
「我的办公时间是十点到两点。」乔治和颜悦色地说。「现在，请把你妹妹的事再给我讲一遍？」
那人往椅背上一靠，两手扣住膝盖，用一种念经似的单调声腔开了口，仿佛诵的是一篇烂熟于心的课文。

===Original===
“We used to live in Turkey. My father was a merchant of Constantinople, and my sister, who went to school in England, got extraordinary ideas, and came back a most violent pro-Turk. She is a very pretty girl and she came to know some of the best Turkish families, although my father and I were dead against her going about with these people. One day she went to call on Hymar Pasha, and that night she didn’t come back. We went to the Pasha’s house and asked for her, but he told us she had left at four o’clock. We then consulted the police, and they told us, after they had made investigations, that she had been seen going on board a ship which left for Odessa the same night. I hadn’t seen her for ten years, until I went down to the Gringo Club, which is a little place in the East End﻿—not high class, you understand, but very well conducted. There was a cabaret show after midnight, and whilst I was sitting there, thinking about going home﻿—very bored, you understand, because that sort of thing doesn’t appeal to me﻿—I saw a girl come out from behind a curtain dressed like a Turkish woman, and begin a dance. She was in the middle of the dance when her veil slipped off. It was Marie! She recognized me at once, and darted through the curtains. I tried to follow her, but they held me back.”

===Chinese===
「我们从前住在土耳其。我父亲是君士坦丁堡的商人，我妹妹在英国上的学，沾了一脑子稀奇古怪的主意，回来竟成了个顶顶激烈的亲土耳其派。她是个很好看的姑娘，结识了几家顶上等的土耳其人家，虽然我父亲同我都死活反对她同这些人来往。有一天她去拜望希马尔帕夏（Hymar Pasha），当夜就没有回来。我们上帕夏府去要人，他却说，她四点钟就走了。我们随后报了警，警方查访一番之后告诉我们，有人看见她登上了一艘当夜启航去敖德萨的船。此后我有十年没有见着她，直到我进了格林戈俱乐部（Gringo Club）——那是东区的一家小场子——不上等，您明白，可是经营得很规矩。过了半夜有一场卡巴莱歌舞，我正坐在那儿琢磨着回家——腻烦得很，您明白，那一套不对我的胃口——忽见一个姑娘从幕布后头出来，穿得像个土耳其女人，跳起舞来。跳到一半，她的面纱滑落下来。竟是玛丽（Marie）！她一眼就认出了我，一闪身钻回幕布后头去了。我想跟过去，他们却拦住了我。」

===Original===
“Did you go to the police?” asked Manfred.
The man shook his head.
“No, what is the use of the police?” he went on in a monotonous tone. “I had enough of them in Constantinople, and I made up my mind that I would get outside help. And then somebody told me of you, and I came along. Mr. Manfred, is it impossible for you to rescue my sister? I’m perfectly sure that she is being detained forcibly and against her will.”

===Chinese===
「你报警了没有？」曼弗雷德问。
那人摇了摇头。
「没报，警察有什么用？」他用单调的口吻接着说。「在君士坦丁堡，他们那套我领教够了；我拿定主意要找外界的帮手。后来有人对我提起您，我就来了。曼弗雷德先生，您就没法子救出我妹妹吗？我完全确信，她正被人强行扣押着，全然违背她自己的心意。」

===Original===
“At the Gringo Club?” asked Manfred.
“Yes,” he nodded.
“I’ll see what I can do,” said George. “Perhaps my friends and I will come down and take a look round some evening. In the meantime will you go back to your friend Dr. Oberzohn and tell him that you have done your part and I will do mine? Your little story will go into my collection of Unplausible Inventions!”
He touched a bell and Poiccart came in.

===Chinese===
「在格林戈俱乐部？」曼弗雷德问。
「是。」他点了点头。
「我瞧瞧能帮上什么忙吧。」乔治说。「兴许哪天晚上，我同我的几位朋友会去转一转。眼下嘛，请你回到你的朋友欧伯佐恩博士那儿去，告诉他：你的一份你已经办完了，我的一份我也会办。你这篇小故事，将收进我那部《难以置信的虚构集》里！」
他按了按铃，波伊卡特走了进来。

===Original===
“Show Mr. Liggins out, please. Don’t hurt him﻿—he may have a wife and children, though it is extremely unlikely.”
The visitor slunk from the room as though he had been whipped.
The door had scarcely closed upon him when Poiccart called Leon down from his room.
“Son,” he said, “George wants that man trailed.”

===Chinese===
「请送里金斯先生（Mr. Liggins）出去。别伤着他——他也许有妻儿老小，虽说这可能性微乎其微。」
那位来客溜出了房间，像是挨了一鞭子似的。
他前脚出门，波伊卡特后脚就把莱昂从楼上房里叫了下来。
「孩子，」他说，「乔治要你盯那人的梢。」

===Original===
Leon peeped out after the retiring victim of Turkish tyranny.
“Not a hard job,” he said. “He has flat feet!”
Poiccart returned to the consulting-room. “Who is he?” he asked.
“I don’t know. He’s been sent here either by Oberzohn or by friend Newton, the general idea being to bring us all together at the Gringo Club﻿—which is fairly well known to me﻿—on some agreeable evening. A bad actor! He has no tone. I shouldn’t be surprised if Leon finds something very interesting about him.”

===Chinese===
莱昂朝那位土耳其暴政的落难牺牲者远去的背影望了望。
「不是难活儿。」他说。「他是个扁平足！」
波伊卡特回到接待室。「他是什么人？」他问。
「不知道。不是欧伯佐恩、就是咱们的牛顿朋友打发他来的，总的意思是：挑某个称心的晚上，把我们全都聚到格林戈俱乐部去——那地方我相当熟。蹩脚的戏子！全无腔调。莱昂要是从他身上查出什么有趣的东西来，我一点也不奇怪。」

===Original===
“He’s been before, hasn’t he?”
Manfred nodded.
“Yes, he was here the day after Barberton came. At least, I had his letter the next morning and saw him for a few moments in the day. Queer devil, Oberzohn! And an industrious devil,” he added. “He sets everybody moving at once, and of course he’s right. A good general doesn’t attack with a platoon, but with an army, with all his strength, knowing that if he fails to pierce the line at one point he may succeed at another. It’s an interesting thought, Raymond, that at this moment there are probably some twenty separate and independent agencies working for our undoing. Most of them ignorant that their efforts are being duplicated. That is Oberzohn’s way﻿—always has been his way. It’s the way he has started revolutions, the way he has organized religious riots.”

===Chinese===
「他从前来过，是不是？」
曼弗雷德点了点头。
「来过，巴伯顿到伦敦的第二天他就来了。至少，第二天早晨我接到了他的信，白天还见了他一小会儿。古怪的魔鬼，这个欧伯佐恩！还是个勤快的魔鬼，」他补了一句。「他让所有人马一齐动起来，当然，他是对的。好的将军不用一个排进攻，而是用一支军队、用全部兵力去进攻，因为他知道，这一处打不穿防线，另一处也许就得手。雷蒙德，这么想挺有意思：此刻大约有二十路各自独立的人马，正为搞垮我们而奔走。其中多数还不知道，他们那份力气早有人在暗中白白重复。这就是欧伯佐恩的路数——向来是他的路数。他发动革命用的是这个路数，组织宗教暴乱用的也是这个路数。」

===Original===
After he had had his bath and changed, he announced his intention of calling at Chester Square.
“I’m rather keen on meeting Joan Newton again, even if she has returned to her normal state of Jane Smith.”
Miss Newton was not at home, the maid told him when he called. Would he see Mr. Montague Newton, who was not only at home, but anxious for him to call, if the truth be told, for he had seen his enemy approaching.

===Chinese===
他洗过澡、换了衣裳，便宣布要去切斯特广场拜访。
「我挺想再见见琼·牛顿，哪怕她已经回复了『简·史密斯』的本相。」
他上门时，女仆告诉他说牛顿小姐不在家。他愿不愿见一见蒙塔古·牛顿先生？此人不但在家——说句实话——还正巴望他来，因为他已望见他的对头逼近了。

===Original===
“I shall be pleased,” murmured Manfred, and was ushered into the splendour of Mr. Newton’s drawing-room.
“Too bad about Joan,” said Mr. Newton easily. “She left for the Continent this morning.”
“Without a passport?” smiled Manfred.

===Chinese===
「不胜荣幸。」曼弗雷德低声说，随即被引进牛顿先生那富丽堂皇的客厅。
「琼的事真不巧。」牛顿先生洒脱地说。「她今天早晨动身去欧洲大陆了。」
「没带护照？」曼弗雷德微笑道。

===Original===
A little slip on the part of Monty, but how was Manfred to know that the authorities had, only a week before, refused the renewal of her passport pending an inquiry into certain irregularities? The suggestion had been that other people than she had travelled to and from the Continent armed with this individual document.
“You don’t need a passport for Belgium,” he lied readily. “Anyway, this passport stuff’s a bit overdone. We’re not at war now.”

===Chinese===
这是蒙蒂出的一个小纰漏；可是曼弗雷德又哪里知道，就在一个星期之前，当局因某些违规情事正在进行查究，已经拒绝了她护照的续签？话里透出的意思是：拿着她这本独一无二的证件往返欧洲大陆的，另有其人。
「去比利时用不着护照。」他张口就撒了个谎。「再说，护照这一套也弄得过分了。咱们现在又不是交战的时候。」

===Original===
“All the time we’re at war,” said Manfred. “May I sit down?”
“Do. Have a cigarette?”
“Let me see the brand before I accept,” said Manfred cautiously, and the man guffawed as at a great joke.
The visitor declined the offer of the cigarette-case and took one from a box on the table.
“And is Jane making the grand tour?” he asked blandly.

===Chinese===
「咱们无时无刻不在交战。」曼弗雷德说。「我可以坐下吗？」
「请坐。来支烟？」
「先让我看看牌子再领受。」曼弗雷德慎重地说；那人哈哈大笑，仿佛听见了天大的笑话。
来客谢绝了递到面前的烟盒，从桌上的烟盒里自取了一支。
「那么简这是在做她的『大陆壮游』了？」他温文地问道。

===Original===
“Jane’s run down and wants a rest.”
“What’s the matter with Aylesbury?”
He saw the man flinch at the mention of the women’s convict establishment, but he recovered instantly.
“It is not far enough out, and I’m told that there are all sorts of queer people living round there. No, she’s going to Brussels and then on to Aix-la-Chapelle, then probably to Spa﻿—I don’t suppose I shall see her again for a month or two.”

===Chinese===
「简累坏了，需要静养。」
「艾尔斯伯里（Aylesbury）有什么不好？」
提起那座女子监狱，他看见对方瑟缩了一下，但那人随即恢复了常态。
「那地方躲得不够远，再说听说那一带住着各式各样的怪人。不，她要去布鲁塞尔，再往亚琛（Aix-la-Chapelle）去，然后多半去斯帕（Spa）——我想一两个月之内见不着她了。」

===Original===
“She was at Heavytree Farm in the early hours of this morning,” said Manfred, “and so were you. You were seen and recognized by a friend of mine﻿—Mr. Raymond Poiccart. You travelled from Heavytree Farm to Oberzohn’s house in a Ford trolley.”
Not by a flicker of an eyelid did Monty Newton betray his dismay.

===Chinese===
「今天凌晨，她在赫维特里农场，」曼弗雷德说，「你也在。你被我的一位朋友看见并认了出来——雷蒙德·波伊卡特先生。你乘一辆福特篷车，从赫维特里农场到了欧伯佐恩的宅子。」
蒙蒂·牛顿连眼皮都没有眨一下，没有露出半分惊惶。

===Original===
“That is bluff,” he said. “I didn’t leave this house last night. What happened at Heavytree Farm?”
“Miss Leicester was abducted. You are surprised, almost agitated, I notice.”
“Do you think I had anything to do with it?” asked Monty steadily.
“Yes, and the police share my view. A provisional warrant was issued for your arrest this morning. I thought you ought to know.”

===Chinese===
「这是唬人。」他说。「昨儿夜里我没有离开过这宅子。赫维特里农场出了什么事？」
「莱斯特小姐遭人绑架了。我看你很吃惊，几乎可以说是心慌意乱。」
「你认为我同这事有牵连？」蒙蒂沉住气问。
「是的，警方同我看法一致。今天早晨已经签发了一张拘捕你的临时逮捕令。我想你应当知道。」

===Original===
Now the man drew back, his face went from red to white, and then to a deeper red again. Manfred laughed softly.
“You’ve got a guilty conscience, Newton,” he said, “and that’s halfway to being arrested. Where is Jane?”

===Chinese===
这一下那人朝后一缩，脸色由红转白，又转为更深的红。曼弗雷德轻轻笑了。
「你良心有鬼，牛顿，」他说，「而良心有鬼，就等于被捕了一半。简在哪儿？」

===Original===
“Gone abroad, I tell you.”
He was thrown off his balance by this all too successful bluff and had lost some of his self-possession.
“She is with Mirabelle Leicester: of that I’m sure,” said Manfred. “I’ve warned you twice, and it is not necessary to warn you a third time. I don’t know how far deep you’re in these snake murders: a jury will decide that sooner or later. But you’re dead within six hours of my learning that Miss Leicester has been badly treated. You know that is true, don’t you?”

===Chinese===
「出国去了，我告诉你。」
这番过于奏效的唬诈打得他方寸大乱，几分镇定已经失守。
「她同米拉贝尔·莱斯特在一处：这一点我有把握。」曼弗雷德说。「我警告过你两次，用不着再警告第三次。这些毒蛇谋杀案你陷得有多深，我不知道：迟早有一天，陪审团会来裁决。可是只要我得知莱斯特小姐受了虐待，不出六个钟头，你就得死。你知道这话是真的，对不对？」

===Original===
Manfred was speaking very earnestly.
“You’re more scared of us than you are of the law, and you’re right, because we do not put our men to the hazard of a jury’s intelligence. You get the same trial from us as you get from a judge who knows all the facts. You can’t beat an English judge, Newton.”
The smile returned and he left the room. Fred, near at hand, waiting in the passage but at a respectful distance from the door, let him out with some alacrity.

===Chinese===
曼弗雷德说得极诚恳。
「你怕我们胜过怕法律，你怕对了，因为我们不把我们的人交到陪审团的智力底下去碰运气。你从我们这里受的审判，同你从一位洞悉全部案情的法官那里受的一模一样。你是斗不过一位英国法官的，牛顿。」
他脸上重又浮起笑意，离开了房间。弗雷德就在近旁，守在过道里、离房门隔着一段恭敬的距离，手脚麻利地送他出去。

===Original===
Monty Newton turned his head sideways, caught a fleeting glimpse of the man he hated﻿—hated worse than he hated Leon Gonsalez﻿—and then called harshly for his servant.
“Come here,” he said, and Fred obeyed. “They’ll be sending round to make inquiries, and I want you to know what to tell them,” he said. “Miss Joan went away this morning to the Continent by the eight-fifteen. She’s either in Brussels or Aix-la-Chapelle. You’re not sure of the hotel, but you’ll find out. Is that clear to you?”
“Yes, sir.”

===Chinese===
蒙蒂·牛顿侧过头去，飞快瞥了一眼那个他所憎恨的人——他恨此人甚于恨莱昂·冈萨雷斯——随即厉声唤他的仆人。
「过来。」他说，弗雷德依言上前。「他们要派人来回打探盘问的，我要你知道该怎么回话。」他说。「琼小姐今天早晨搭八点一刻的班车去欧洲大陆了。她不是在布鲁塞尔，就是在亚琛。旅馆你说不准，可你会去打听明白。听清楚了没有？」
「是，先生。」

===Original===
Fred was looking aimlessly about the room.
“What’s the matter with you?”
“I was wondering where the clock is.”
“Clock?” Now Monty Newton heard it himself. The tick-tick-tick of a cheap clock, and he went livid. “Find it,” he said hoarsely, and even as he spoke his eyes fell upon the little black box that had been pushed beneath the desk, and he groped for the door with a scream of terror.

===Chinese===
弗雷德正没头没脑地朝屋子四下里张望。
「你怎么了？」
「我在纳闷钟在哪儿。」
「钟？」这一下蒙蒂·牛顿自己也听见了。滴答、滴答、滴答，一只廉价钟表的走动声，他的脸色顿时铁青。「找出来。」他嗓音嘶哑地说；话音未落，他的目光已落在桌子底下那只被推进去的小黑盒上，他一声恐怖的尖叫，摸索着夺门而出。

===Original===
Passersby in Chester Square saw the door flung open and two men rush headlong into the street. And the little American clock, which Manfred had purchased a few days before, went on ticking out the time, and was still ticking merrily when the police experts went in and opened the box. It was Manfred’s oldest jest, and never failed.

===Chinese===
切斯特广场上的过路人看见那扇门砰地敞开，两条汉子没命地冲到街上。那只曼弗雷德几天前买来的美国小钟，兀自滴答滴答地报着时；等警方的专家进门打开盒子时，它还在欢快地走着。这是曼弗雷德最老的一个玩笑，从来没有不灵过。

## XXII In the Store Cellar / 二十二 货栈地窖

===Original===
It was impossible that Mirabelle Leicester could fail to realize the serious danger in which she stood. Why she had incurred the enmity of Oberzohn, for what purpose this man was anxious to keep her under his eye, she could not even guess. It was a relief to wake up in the early morning, as she did, and find Joan sleeping in the same room; for though she had many reasons for mistrusting her, there was something about this doll-faced girl that made an appeal to her.

===Chinese===
米拉贝尔·莱斯特不可能不明白自己身处何等凶险。她到底因何招致了欧伯佐恩的仇视，此人又图着什么，非要把她置于自己的眼皮底下，她连猜也猜不着。清晨一觉醒来——她也确是醒了——看见琼睡在同一间屋里，倒叫人松了口气；因为虽说她有千百条理由提防这姑娘，这张洋娃娃似的脸上却有一种东西，打动着她的心。

===Original===
Joan was lying on the bed fully dressed, and at the sound of the creaking bed she turned and got up, fastening her skirt.

“Well, how do you like your new home?” she asked, with an attempt at joviality, which she was far from feeling, in spite of Monty’s assurances.

“I’ve seen better,” said Mirabelle coolly.

“I’ll bet you have!” Joan stretched and yawned; then, opening one of the cupboards, took a shovelful of coal and threw it into the furnace, clanging the iron door. “That’s my job,” she said humorously, “to keep you warm.”

===Chinese===
琼和衣躺在床上；听得床架吱呀一响，她便翻身起身，扣好裙子。

「喏，你这新家住着还满意么？」她问，强打出一副快活劲儿——虽有蒙蒂的百般担保，她心里实在快活不起来。

「见过更好的。」米拉贝尔淡淡地说。

「那还用说！」琼伸了个懒腰，打了个哈欠；随后打开一口柜橱，铲了满满一锹煤投进炉膛，哐当一声带上铁门。「这就是我的差事，」她打趣道，「管你暖和。」

===Original===
“How long am I going to be kept here?”

“Five days,” was the surprising answer.

“Why five?” asked Mirabelle curiously.

“I don’t know. Maybe they’ll tell you,” said Joan.

===Chinese===
「要把我关在这儿多久？」

「五天。」这回答出人意料。

「为什么偏是五天？」米拉贝尔好奇地问。

「我不知道。兴许他们会告诉你。」琼说。

===Original===
She fixed a plug in the wall and turned on the small electric fire. Disappearing, she came back with a kettle which she placed on top of the ring.

“The view’s not grand, but the food’s good,” she said, with a gaiety that Mirabelle was now sure was forced.

===Chinese===
她把插头插上墙座，拧开那只小电炉，人出去了一趟，回来时端着一只水壶，搁在炉盘上。

「景致算不得气派，吃食倒不坏。」她说，那份高兴劲儿，米拉贝尔此刻已拿准是装出来的。

===Original===
“You’re with these people, of course﻿—Dr. Oberzohn and Newton?”

“Mister Newton,” corrected Joan. “Yes, I’m his fiancée. We’re going to be married when things get a little better,” she said vaguely, “and there’s no use in your getting sore with me because I helped to bring you here. Monty’s told me all about it. They’re going to do you no harm at all.”

“Then why﻿—” began Mirabelle.

“He’ll tell you,” interrupted Joan, “sooner or later. The old man, or﻿—or﻿—well, Monty isn’t in this: he’s only obliging Oberzohn.”

===Chinese===
「你自然是他们一伙的——欧伯佐恩博士，还有牛顿？」

「是牛顿先生。」琼纠正道。「对，我是他的未婚妻。等光景好一点儿，我们就结婚。」她含含糊糊地说，「再说，我帮着把你弄到这儿来，你跟我使性子也是白搭。蒙蒂全跟我讲过了。他们一点儿也不会伤害你。」

「那为什么——」米拉贝尔刚开口。

「他会告诉你的，」琼打断她，「早晚会。老头子会，或者——或者——唉，蒙蒂没掺和这档子事：他不过是给欧伯佐恩帮个忙。」

===Original===
With one thing Mirabelle agreed: it was a waste of time to indulge in recriminations or to reproach the girl for her supreme treachery. After all, Joan owed nothing to her, and had been from the first a tool employed for her detention. It would have been as logical for a convict to reproach the prison guard.

===Chinese===
有一点米拉贝尔是认的：此刻互相埋怨，或者痛责这姑娘彻头彻尾的背叛，纯属浪费时间。说到底，琼于她本无亏欠，打一开始就是被人使唤来拘禁她的一件工具。这就好比囚犯去埋怨狱卒，毫无道理。

===Original===
“How do you come to be doing this sort of thing?” she asked, watching the girl making tea.

“Where do you get ‘this sort of thing’ from?” demanded Joan. “If you suppose that I spend my life chaperoning females, you’ve got another guess coming. Scared, aren’t you?”

She looked across at Mirabelle and the girl shook her head.

“Not really.”

===Chinese===
「你怎么会干起这种事来的？」她问，一面看着姑娘沏茶。

「『这种事』——这话从何说起？」琼抢白道。「你要当我是靠给女人当陪护过活的，那就还得再猜一回。吓着了吧？」

她的目光扫向米拉贝尔，姑娘摇了摇头。

「也说不上。」

===Original===
“I should be,” confessed Joan. “Do you mind condensed milk? There’s no other. Yes, I should be writhing under the table, knowing something about Oberzohn.”

“If I were Oberzohn,” said Mirabelle with spirit, “I should be hiding in a deep hole where the Four Just Men would not find me.”

“Four Just Men!” sneered the girl, and then her face changed. “Were they the people who whipped Gurther?”

Mirabelle had not heard of this exploit, but she gave them credit with a nod.

===Chinese===
「换我就该怕了。」琼招认道。「炼乳你介意么？只有这一种。真的，晓得几分欧伯佐恩的底细，换我就该钻到桌子底下打颤了。」

「我要是欧伯佐恩，」米拉贝尔毫不示弱地说，「就该躲进一个四义士找不着的深窟窿里。」

「四义士！」姑娘嗤笑一声，随即脸色变了。「鞭打格瑟的，就是他们？」

这桩事迹米拉贝尔不曾耳闻，但她点了点头，把这份功劳记在了他们账上。

===Original===
“Is that so? Does Gurther know they’re friends of yours?” she asked significantly.

“I don’t know Gurther.”

“He’s the man who danced with you the other night﻿—Lord﻿—I forget what name we gave him. Because, if he does know, my dear,” she said slowly, “you’ve got two people to be extremely careful with. Gurther’s half mad. Monty has always said so. He dopes too, and there are times when he’s not a man at all but a low-down wolf. I’m scared of *him*﻿—I’ll admit it. There aren’t Four Just Men, anyway,” she went off at a tangent. “There haven’t been more than three for years. One of them was killed in Bordeaux. That’s a town I’d hate to be killed in,” said Joan irreverently.

===Chinese===
「当真？格瑟可知道他们是你的朋友？」她意味深长地问。

「我不认识格瑟。」

「就是那天晚上同你跳舞的那个人呀——天哪——咱们给他安的什么名字来着，我忘了。因为，亲爱的，他要是知道的话，」她慢条斯理地说，「你就得对两个人十二分地提防。格瑟是半个疯子，蒙蒂一直这么说。他还吸毒，有些时候他根本不是个人，是条下作的豺狼。我怕的正是*他*——这话我认了。再说了，压根儿也没有什么四义士，」她话锋一转。「这几年顶多只剩三个。有一个已经在波尔多（Bordeaux）送了命。那样一座城，换了我可不想把命丢在那儿。」琼没轻没重地说。

===Original===
An interval of silence followed whilst she opened an airtight tin and took out a small cake, and, putting it on the table, cut it into slices.

“What are they like?” she asked. Evidently the interval had been filled with thoughts of the men from Curzon Street.

“Monty says they’re just bluff, but I’m not so sure that Monty tells me all he thinks. He’s so scared that he told me to call and see them, just because they gave him an order﻿—which isn’t like Monty. They’ve killed people, haven’t they?”

===Chinese===
接下来静了好一阵，她开了一听密封的铁罐，取出一块小蛋糕，摆在桌上，切成一片片。

「他们都是些什么样的人？」她问。显然这一阵静默里，她心里想的全是柯曾街来的那几位。

「蒙蒂说他们不过是虚张声势，可蒙蒂是不是把心里话都告诉了我，我就拿不准了。他怕成那样，竟叫我去登门拜望他们，就因为他们给了他一纸命令——这可不像蒙蒂的为人。他们杀过人，是不是？」

===Original===
Mirabelle nodded.

“And got away with it? They must be clever.” Joan’s admiration was dragged from her. “Where do they get their money?”

That was always an interesting matter to Joan.

When the girl explained, she was really impressed. That they could kill and get away with it, was wonderful; that they were men of millions, placed them in a category apart.

===Chinese===
米拉贝尔点了点头。

「而且还安然脱身？那他们必定有两下子。」琼的钦佩之情是硬生生被拽出来的。「他们的钱是打哪儿来的？」

这个话题对琼向来有莫大的吸引力。

等姑娘解说了一番，她当真心折了。杀得了人还能全身而退，已然了不得；他们竟还是家资百万的人物，那就更属另一等人了。

===Original===
“They’ll never find you here,” said Joan. “There’s nobody living knows about this vault. There used to be eight men working here, sorting monkey hides, and every one of them’s dead. Monty told me. He said this place is below the canal level, and Oberzohn can flood it in five minutes. Monty thinks the old man had an idea of running a slush factory here.”

“What is a slush factory?” asked Mirabelle, open-mouthed.

“Phoney﻿—snide﻿—counterfeit. Not English, but Continental work. He was going to do that if things had gone really bad, but of course you make all the difference.”

===Chinese===
「他们永远也别想在这儿找到你。」琼说。「知道这地窖的活人一个也没有。从前这儿雇着八个人，分拣猴皮，如今一个个都死了。蒙蒂告诉我的。他说这地方在运河水位以下，欧伯佐恩五分钟就能把它淹掉。蒙蒂琢磨着，老头子原先起过心思，要在这儿开一家假币作坊。」

「什么是假币作坊？」米拉贝尔张着嘴问。

「假货——伪票——赃钞。不是英国的东西，是欧陆的手笔。他打的是这个主意，万一事情真砸了锅就这么干——不过嘛，如今有你，一切就大不相同了。」

===Original===
Mirabelle put down her cup.

“Does he expect to make money out of me?” she said, trying hard not to laugh.

The girl nodded solemnly.

“Does he think I have a great deal of money?”

“He’s sure.”

Joan was sure too. Her tone said that plainly enough.

===Chinese===
米拉贝尔放下了杯子。

「他还指望从我身上发财么？」她说，极力忍着不笑出来。

姑娘郑重其事地点了点头。

「他当我有大笔的钱？」

「他笃定得很。」

琼自己也笃定得很。她的口气把这一点说得再明白不过。

===Original===
Mirabelle sat down on the bed, for the moment too astonished to speak. Her own financial position was no mystery. She had been left sufficient to bring her in a small sum yearly, and with the produce of the farm had managed to make both ends meet. It was the failure of the farm as a source of profit which had brought her to her new job in London. Alma had also a small annuity; the farm was the girl’s property, but beyond these revenues she had nothing. There was not even a possibility that she was an heiress. Her father had been a comparatively poor man, and had been supported in his numerous excursions to various parts of the world in search of knowledge by the scientific societies to which he was attached; his literary earnings were negligible; his books enjoyed only a very limited sale. She could trace her ancestry back for seven generations; knew of her uncles and aunts, and they did not include a single man or woman who, in the best traditions of the storybooks, had gone to America and made an immense fortune.

===Chinese===
米拉贝尔在床沿坐下，一时惊得说不出话来。她自己的家底本不是什么秘密。父亲留下的遗产，每年给她带来一小笔进项，加上农场的出产，勉强做到收支相抵。正因为农场无利可图，她才到伦敦谋了那份新差事。阿尔玛也有一份小小的年金；农场是姑娘名下的产业，可除了这几宗进项，她再没有别的。她甚至连做女继承人的可能都谈不上。她父亲算得上清贫，一生无数次为求知远赴世界各地，靠的都是所属各家科学社团的资助；他的文字收入微不足道；他的著作销路寥寥。她的家世可以上溯七代；叔伯姨婶她都数得出来，其中并没有一个人——照言情小说里最正宗的路数——漂洋过海去美洲发了大财。

===Original===
“It is absurd,” she said. “I have no money. If Mr. Oberzohn puts me up to ransom, it will have to be something under a hundred!”

“Put you up to ransom?” said Joan. “I don’t get you there. But you’re rich all right﻿—I can tell you that. Monty says so, and Monty wouldn’t lie to me.”

Mirabelle was bewildered. It seemed almost impossible that a man of Oberzohn’s intelligence and sources of information could make such a mistake. And yet Joan was earnest.

===Chinese===
「这太荒唐了，」她说。「我根本没有钱。欧伯佐恩先生要拿我勒索赎金，怕是连一百镑也讹不出来！」

「拿你勒索赎金？」琼说。「这我就不懂了。可你确实有钱——这话我可以告诉你。蒙蒂说的，蒙蒂不会骗我。」

米拉贝尔糊涂了。以欧伯佐恩的智力和消息门路，竟会弄出这样的差错，简直叫人无法相信。可琼偏偏是一脸的认真。

===Original===
“They must have mistaken me for somebody else,” she said, but Joan did not answer. She was sitting up in a listening attitude, and her eyes were directed towards the iron door which separated their sleeping apartment from the larger vault. She had heard the creak of the trap turning and the sound of feet coming down the stairs.

Mirabelle rose as Oberzohn came in. He wore his black dressing-gown, his smoking-cap was at the back of his head, and the muddy Wellington boots which he had pulled over his feet looked incongruous, and would at any other time have provoked her to laughter. He favoured her with a stiff nod.

“You have slept well, gracious lady?” he said, and to her amazement took her cold hand in his and kissed it.

She felt the same feeling of revulsion and unreality as had overcome her that night at the dance when Gurther had similarly saluted her.

===Chinese===
「他们准是把我错当成别人了。」她说，琼却没有应声。她直起身子，侧耳细听，眼睛盯着那扇铁门——它把这间卧房同外头更大的地窖隔开。她听见了活板门转动的吱呀声，还有脚步下了台阶。

欧伯佐恩进来时，米拉贝尔站起身来。他身上还是那件黑晨袍，吸烟帽推在后脑勺上，脚上硬套着的那双沾泥威灵顿长靴看着不伦不类，若在别的场合，准会逗得她发笑。他赏了她一个生硬的点头。

「您睡得好么，雍容的小姐？」他说，随即——叫她大为惊愕地——把她冰凉的手握在自己手里，吻了一下。

她心头重新泛起那晚舞会上格瑟同样向她见礼时，曾攫住过她的那股憎恶与恍如梦寐之感。

===Original===
“It is a nice place, for young people and for old.” He looked round the apartment with satisfaction. “Here I should be content to spend my life reading my books, and giving my mind to thought, but”﻿—he spread his hands and shrugged﻿—“what would you? I am a business man, with immense interests in every part of the world. I am rich, too, beyond your dreams! I have stores in every part of the world, and thousands of men and women on my payroll.”

Why was he telling her all this, she wondered, reciting the facts in a monotonous voice. Surely he had not come down to emphasize the soundness of his financial position?

“I am not very much interested in your business, Mr. Oberzohn,” she said, “but I want to know why I am being detained here. Surely, if you’re so rich, you do not want to hold me to ransom?”

===Chinese===
「这是个好地方，于年轻人好，于老年人也好。」他满意地环顾这间屋子。「在这里，我本可以心满意足，终生读我的书，把心思交付沉思，可是」——他摊开双手，耸了耸肩——「有什么办法呢？我是个生意人，在世界每一个角落都有庞大的利益。我也富有，富得超出你的梦想！我在世界各地开有货栈，领我薪水的男男女女数以千计。」

他干吗要对她说这些呢，她纳闷着，听他用单调的嗓音历数这桩桩事实。他总不至于特地下来，强调自己财务状况的稳固吧？

「我对您的生意没有多大兴趣，欧伯佐恩先生，」她说，「可我想知道，为什么把我拘在这儿。您既然这么有钱，总不至于还想拿我勒索赎金吧？」

===Original===
“To ransom?” His forehead went up and down. “That is foolish talk. Did she tell you?” He pointed at the girl, and his face went as black as thunder.

“No, I guessed,” said Mirabelle quickly, not wishing to get her companion into bad odour.

“I do not hold you to ransom. I hold you, lovely lady, because you are good for my eyes. Did not Heine say, ‘The beauty of women is a sedative to the soul’? You should read Heine: he is frivolous, but in his stupidity there are many clever thoughts. Now tell me, lovely lady, have you all you desire?”

===Chinese===
「勒索赎金？」他的眉毛扬起又落下。「这是愚蠢的空谈。是她告诉你的？」他指着那姑娘，脸黑得像雷雨将至。

「不，是我猜的。」米拉贝尔急忙说，不愿连累同伴讨他的嫌。

「我不是拿你勒索赎金。我留着你，美丽的小姐，因为你对我的眼睛有益。海涅（Heine）不是说过：『女子的美，是心灵的镇静剂。』你应当读读海涅：他轻浮，可在他的愚蠢里有许多聪明的念头。现在告诉我，美丽的小姐，你所愿的一切都有了么？」

===Original===
“I want to go out,” she said. “I can’t stay in this underground room without danger to my health.”

“Soon you shall go.” He bowed stiffly again, and shuffled across the floor to the furnace. Behind this were the two baize-covered boxes, and one he lifted tenderly. “Here are secrets such as you should not pry into,” he said in his awkward English. “The most potent of chemicals, colossal in power. The ignorant would touch them and they would explode﻿—you understand?”

He addressed Mirabelle, who did not understand but made no answer.

“They must be kept warm for that reason. One I take, the other I leave. You shall not touch it﻿—that is understood? My good friend has told you?” He brought his eyes to Joan.

===Chinese===
「我想出去。」她说。「老关在这间地下屋里，保不住要伤身体。」

「很快你就可出去。」他又生硬地鞠了一躬，拖着脚步蹭过地板，走到炉子跟前。炉后是那两口蒙着呢面的箱子，他小心翼翼地抱起一口。「这里面是些你不该窥探的秘密，」他用他那口生硬的英语说。「最烈性的化学品，威力骇人。无知的人一碰，它们就要爆炸——你明白么？」

这话是对着米拉贝尔说的，她没有听懂，却也没有作声。

「因此必须让它们保持温暖。一口我带走，另一口我留下。你不许碰它——这可明白了？我的好朋友已告诉过你？」他把眼睛转向琼。

===Original===
“I understand all right,” she said. “Listen, Oberzohn: when am I going out for a walk? This place is getting on my nerves already.”

“Tonight you shall have exercise with the lovely lady. I myself will accompany you.”

“Why am I here, Mr. Oberzohn?” Mirabelle asked again.

===Chinese===
「明白，我明白。」她说。「听着，欧伯佐恩：我什么时候能出去遛遛弯儿？这地方已经把我的神经磨得受不了了。」

「今夜你将与美丽的小姐一同活动身体。我本人亲自陪同。」

「我为什么在这儿，欧伯佐恩先生？」米拉贝尔又问了一遍。

===Original===
“You are here because you are in danger,” said Oberzohn, holding the green box under his arm. “You are in very great danger.” He nodded with every word. “There are certain men, of all the most infamous, who have a design upon your life. They are criminal, cunning and wise﻿—but not so cunning or wise as Dr. Oberzohn. Because I will not let you fall into their hands I keep you here, young miss. Good morning.”

Again he bowed stiffly and went out, the iron door clanging behind him. They heard him climbing the stairs, the thud of the trap as it fell, and the rumble which Joan, at any rate, knew was made by the cement barrel being rolled to the top of the trap.

“Pleasant little fellow, isn’t he?” said Joan bitterly. “Him and his chemicals!” She glared down at the remaining box. “If I were sure it wouldn’t explode, I should smash it to smithereens!” she said.

===Chinese===
「你在这儿，是因为你正身处危险。」欧伯佐恩说着，把那口绿箱子夹在腋下。「你正身处极大的危险。」他一字一点头。「有那么几个男人，是万恶之尤，正谋算你的性命。他们邪恶、奸猾、聪明——却不如欧伯佐恩博士奸猾、聪明。因为我不肯让你落入他们的手心，我才把你留在这里，年轻的小姐。早安。」

他又生硬地鞠了一躬，走了出去，铁门在他身后哐当一响。她们听见他登上台阶，听见活板门落下的闷响，跟着是那阵隆隆声——至少琼知道，那是水泥桶被滚到了活板门上。

「可爱的小老头儿，是不是？」琼恨恨地说。「他，还有他那套化学品！」她狠狠瞪着剩下的那口箱子。「要是我拿得准它炸不了，我就把它砸个粉碎！」她说。

===Original===
Later she told the prisoner of Oberzohn’s obsession; of how he spent time and money in his search for the vital elixir.

“Monty thinks he’ll find it,” she said seriously. “Do you know, that old man has had an ox stewed down to a pint? There used to be a king in Europe﻿—I forget his name﻿—who had the same stuff, but not so strong. Monty says that Oberzohn hardly ever takes a meal﻿—just a teaspoonful of this dope and he’s right for the day. And Monty says﻿ ﻿…”

For the rest of that dreary morning the girl listened without hearing to the wise sayings and clever acts of Monty; and every now and again her eyes strayed to the baize-covered box which contained “the most potent chemicals,” and she wondered whether, in the direst extremity, she would be justified in employing these dread forces for her soul’s salvation.

===Chinese===
后来，她跟这位被囚的姑娘讲起欧伯佐恩的那桩痴念：讲他如何耗费光阴与钱财，追寻那剂长生灵药。

「蒙蒂觉得他找得着的。」她一本正经地说。「你可知道，那老头子曾把一整头牛熬成了一品脱！从前欧洲有个国王——名字我忘了——也服这同一种东西，只是没这么浓。蒙蒂说，欧伯佐恩几乎从不进餐——一小匙这种药下了肚，他一整天就都顶下来了。蒙蒂还说……」

在那个沉闷上午余下的时光里，姑娘心不在焉地听着蒙蒂的种种高明言语与干练事迹；她的眼睛时不时溜向那口蒙着呢面的箱子——里头装着「最烈性的化学品」——心里盘算着，倘若到了最绝望的关头，自己动用这股可怕的力量来拯救自己的灵魂，究竟算不算正当。

## XXIII The Courier / 二十三 信使

===Original===
Elijah Washington came up to London for a consultation. With the exception of a blue contusion beneath his right eye, he was none the worse for his alarming experience.
===Chinese===
伊莱贾·华盛顿为一场会商进了趟伦敦。除了右眼底下的一块青紫瘀伤，那场骇人的遭遇并没有在他身上留下别的损伤。

===Original===
Leon Gonsalez had driven him to town, and on the way up the big man had expressed views about snakebite which were immensely interesting to the man at the wheel. “I’ve figured it out this way: there is no snake at all. What happens is that these guys have extracted snake venom﻿—and that’s easy, by making a poison-snake bite on something soft﻿—and have poisoned a dart or a burr with the venom. I’ve seen that done in Africa, particularly up in the Ituri country, and it’s pretty common in South America. The fellow just throws or shoots it, and just where the dart hits, he gets snake-poisoning right away.”
===Chinese===
载他进城的是莱昂·冈萨雷斯；一路上，这位大个子就蛇咬发表的一番见解，叫握着方向盘的那位听得津津有味。「我是这么琢磨出来的：压根儿就没有什么蛇。实情是：这帮家伙抽取了蛇毒——那容易得很，叫一条毒蛇往什么软乎乎的东西上咬一口就是了——再用毒液喂一枚镖、或是一枚芒刺。这一手我在非洲见识过，尤其是在伊图里（Ituri）一带；在南美也寻常得很。那家伙只管把镖一掷、或是一射，镖尖扎在哪儿，哪儿当场就蛇毒发作。」

===Original===
“That is an excellent theory,” said Leon, “only﻿—no dart or burr has ever been found. It is the first thing the police looked for in the case of the stockbroker. They had the ground searched for days. And it was just the same in the case of the tramp and the bank clerk, just the same in the case of Barberton. A dart would stick some time and would be found in the man’s clothing or near the spot where he was struck down. How do you account for that?”
===Chinese===
「这个推断高明极了，」莱昂说，「只是——从来没有找到过什么镖或芒刺。股票经纪人那桩案子里，警方头一样找的就是它。他们把地面搜了整整好几天。流浪汉一案和银行职员一案一模一样，巴伯顿一案也是一模一样。一枚镖总会在身上黏上一阵子，总会在死者的衣裳里、或是在他被打倒的地方附近找出来。这一点你又怎么解释？」

===Original===
Mr. Washington very frankly admitted that he couldn’t account for it at all, and Leon chuckled.

“I can,” he said. “In fact, I know just how it’s done.”
===Chinese===
华盛顿先生坦率得很，承认自己全然解释不了；莱昂嘿然一笑。

「我解释得了。」他说。「不但如此，那手法是怎么弄的，我摸得一清二楚。

===Original===
“Great snakes!” gasped Washington in amazement. “Then why don’t you tell the police?”

“The police know﻿—now,” said Leon. “It isn’t snakebite﻿—it is nicotine poisoning.”
===Chinese===
「乖乖，真是活见蛇了！」华盛顿倒抽一口气，惊叹不已。「那你干吗不告诉警察？」

「警察已经知道了——如今知道了。」莱昂说。「那不是蛇咬——是尼古丁中毒。」

===Original===
“How’s that?” asked the startled man, but Leon had his joke to himself.

After a consultation which had lasted most of the night they had brought Washington from Rath Hall, and on the way Leon hinted gently that the Three had a mission for him and hoped he would accept.
===Chinese===
「这话怎么说？」那位吃惊的人问；莱昂却把这个玩笑留给了自己，不肯再往下讲。

那场会商几乎耗掉大半夜；会罢，他们载着华盛顿离开了拉思府。一路上，莱昂婉转地透了点口风：三位义士有一桩使命要托付他，盼他应承下来。

===Original===
“You’re much too good a fellow to be put into an unnecessarily dangerous position,” he said; “and even if you weren’t, we wouldn’t lightly risk your blessed life; but the job we should ask you to do isn’t exactly a picnic.”

“Listen!” said Mr. Washington with sudden energy. “I don’t want any more snakes﻿—not that kind of snake! I’ve felt pain in my time, but nothing like this! I know it must have been snake venom, but I’d like to meet the little wriggler who brews the brand that was handed to me, and maybe I’d change my mind about collecting him﻿—alive!”
===Chinese===
「你这个人太好了，好得叫我们不忍心把你搁进不必要的险地里去。」他说，「即便你不是这样的人，我们也不肯拿你那条宝贵的性命轻率冒险；只是我们要请你办的那桩差事，实在算不上一次郊游野餐。」

「听着！」华盛顿先生陡然来了精神。「蛇我可不要再领教了——再不要那一号的蛇了！我这辈子也疼过，可从没疼成这样！我知道那必是蛇毒无疑；可我倒想会一会那条扭来扭去的小东西——酿出我挨的那一味货色的正是它——没准儿我会改变主意，把他收作藏品——要活的！」

===Original===
Leon agreed silently, and for the next few moments was avoiding a street car on one side, a baker’s cart on another, and a *blah* woman who was walking aimlessly in the road, apparently with no other intention than of courting an early death, this being the way of *blah* women.
===Chinese===
莱昂默默赞同；接下来那几分钟里，他一边躲一辆有轨电车，一边躲一辆面包房的送货车，还要躲一个*blah*（傻叨叨）女人——那女人在马路当中没头没脑地逛荡，瞧那意思，除了巴望早些送命再没有别的打算；傻叨叨的女人们，向来就是这个走法。

===Original===
“Phew!” said Mr. Washington, as the car skidded on the greasy road. “I don’t know whether you’re a good driver or just naturally under the protection of Providence.”

“Both,” said Leon, when he had straightened the machine. “All good drivers are that.”

Presently he continued:
===Chinese===
「嚯！」汽车在滑腻的路面上打了个滑，华盛顿先生说。「我闹不清你到底是位好司机，还是天生有老天爷保佑。」

「两样都有。」莱昂把车身正了过来，说道。「凡是好司机，都是两样兼备。」

过了一会儿，他接着说：

===Original===
“It is snake venom all right, Mr. Washington; only snake venom that has been most carefully treated by a man who knows the art of concentration of its bad and the extraction of its harmless constituents. My theory is that certain alkaloids are added, and it is possible that there has been a blending of two different kinds of poison. But you’re right when you say that no one animal carries in his poison sac that particular variety of death-juice. If it is any value to you, we are prepared to give you a snake-proof certificate!”
===Chinese===
「那确确实实是蛇毒，华盛顿先生；只不过，是经一位行家极精心处理过的蛇毒——此人深谙一门技艺：把里头凶悍的成分加以浓缩，把无害的成分提取出去。依我的推断，里头还搀了某些生物碱，而且说不定是两种不同的毒兑在一起调和成的。不过你说得对：天下没有哪一种动物，毒囊里生就的就是那一号死亡毒液。这话若于你有用——我们预备发给你一纸蛇毒不侵证明书！」

===Original===
“I don’t want another experience of that kind,” Elijah Washington warned him; but Leon turned the conversation to the state of the road and the problems of traffic control.

There had been nothing seen or heard of Mirabelle, and Meadows’ activities had for the moment been directed to the forthcoming inquest on Barberton. Nowadays, whenever he reached Scotland Yard, he moved in a crowd of reporters, all anxious for news of further developments. The Barberton death was still the livliest topic in the newspapers: the old scare of the snake had been revived and in some degree intensified. There was not a journal which did not carry columns of letters to the editor denouncing the inactivity of the police. Were they, asked one sarcastic correspondent, under the hypnotic influence of the snake’s eyes? Could they not, demanded another, give up trapping speeders on the Lingfield road and bring their mighty brains to the elucidation of a mystery that was to cause every household in London the gravest concern? The Barberton murder was the peg on which every letter-writing faddist had a novel view to hang, and Mr. Meadows was not at that time the happiest officer in the force.
===Chinese===
「那一号的滋味，我不想再尝第二回了。」伊莱贾·华盛顿正色警告他；莱昂却把话头转到了路况和交通管制的问题上。

米拉贝尔至今音信全无、踪影皆无；米多斯的精力，暂时全都投向了即将开审的巴伯顿案死因研讯。这些日子，他一进苏格兰场，便有一群记者前呼后拥，个个急着打探案情的进一步进展。巴伯顿之死仍是报上最热闹的话题：「毒蛇」的旧恐慌死灰复燃，且又旺了几分。没有一家报纸不载着成栏成栏的读者来信，痛斥警方无所作为。有一位好挖苦的来信人问：他们莫不是叫蛇的眼睛催眠了？另一位则质问：他们就不能少在林菲尔德（Lingfield）路上设伏抓超速，把他们那些了不起的大脑挪过来，用在破解一桩势必要叫伦敦家家户户惶惶不安的疑案上？巴伯顿谋杀案成了一根挂钩，凡是好握管上书的怪论家，个个都要把自己的一番新奇见解往上面挂一挂。那阵子，米多斯先生算不得全警队里最如意的警官。

===Original===
“Where is Lee?” asked Washington as they came into Curzon Street.

“He’s in town for the moment, but we are moving him to the North of England, though I don’t think there is any danger to him, now that Barberton’s letters are in our possession. They would have killed him yesterday to prevent our handling the correspondence. Today I should imagine he has no special importance in the eyes of Oberzohn and Company. And here we are!”

Mr. Washington got out stiffly and was immediately admitted by the butler. The three men went upstairs to where George Manfred was wrestling with a phase of the problem. He was not alone; Digby, his head swathed in bandages, sat, an unhappy man, on the edge of a chair and answered Leon’s cheery greeting with a mournful smile.
===Chinese===
「李在哪儿？」车子驶进柯曾街时，华盛顿问。

「眼下还在城里，不过我们正要送他去英格兰北部；虽说巴伯顿的信如今已在我们手里，我看他已没有什么危险了。昨天他们本会杀他灭口，免得那批信函落到我们手上。到今天，我想在欧伯佐恩一伙眼里，他已算不得什么要紧人物了。咱们到了！」

华盛顿先生手脚僵硬地下了车，立刻被管家让进门去。三人上了楼，来到乔治·曼弗雷德正同这桩难题的某个侧面较劲的地方。屋里不止他一人：迪格比头上缠满绷带，愁眉苦脸地坐在一张椅子的边上，对莱昂兴冲冲的招呼只报以凄然一笑。

===Original===
“I’m sending Digby to keep observation on Oberzohn’s house; and especially do I wish him to search that old boat of his.”

He was referring to an ancient barge which lay on the mud at the bottom of Mr. Oberzohn’s private dock. From the canal there was a narrow waterway into the little factory grounds. It was so long since the small cantilever bridge which covered the entrance had been raised, that locals regarded the bridge floor as part of the normal bank of the canal. But behind the green water-gates was a concrete dock large enough to hold one barge, and here for years a decrepit vessel had wallowed, the hunting-ground of rats and the sleeping-place of the desperately homeless.

“The barge is practically immovable: I’ve already reported on that,” said Leon.
===Chinese===
「我派迪格比去盯欧伯佐恩的宅子；尤其要他去搜一搜他那条破船。」

他说的是一条烂在欧伯佐恩先生私人船坞底泥滩上的老驳船。从运河过来，有一条窄窄的水道通进那片小厂区。罩着入口的那座小悬臂桥，年深日久不曾吊起过，当地人早把桥面当作运河寻常的岸沿了。可在那两扇绿色水闸门的后头，藏着一座容得下一条驳船的混凝土船坞；多少年来，一条半死不活的破船一直瘫在里面打滚，是老鼠的猎场，也是走投无路的无家可归者的睡处。

「那条驳船基本动弹不得：这一点我已经报告过了。」莱昂说。

===Original===
“It certainly has that appearance, and yet I would like a search,” replied Manfred. “You understand that this is night duty, and I have asked Meadows to notify the local inspector that you will be on duty﻿—I don’t want to be pulled out of my bed to identify you at the Peckham police station. It isn’t a cheerful job, but you might be able to make it interesting by finding your way into his grounds. I don’t think the factory will yield much, but the house will certainly be a profitable study to an observer of human nature.”

“I hope I do better this time, Mr. Manfred,” said Digby, turning to go. “And, if you don’t mind, I’ll go by day and take a look at the place. I don’t want to fall down this time!”

George smiled as he rose and shook the man’s hand at parting. “Even Mr. Gonsalez makes mistakes,” he said maliciously, and Leon looked hurt.
===Chinese===
「看样子确是如此，可我还是想搜一搜。」曼弗雷德答道。「你明白，这是夜勤；我已托米多斯知会当地警局的督察，说你将在那一带执勤——我可不想半夜被人从被窝里拽出来，上佩卡姆（Peckham）警察局去把你认领出来。这差事不轻松，可你若能设法摸进他的地界，倒也能给它添些趣味。厂子那头我想淘不出多少东西；那宅子却肯定值得一位人性观察家好好研读一番，必有所获。」

「这一回，我指望干得漂亮些，曼弗雷德先生。」迪格比转身要走，说道。「您若不介意，我想白天先去把那地方看上一看。这回我可不想再栽跟头了！」

乔治含笑起身，临别握了握此人的手。「连冈萨雷斯先生都有失手的时候呢。」他话里带着促狭；莱昂一脸受了委屈的神气。

===Original===
Manfred tidied some papers on his desk and put them into a drawer, waiting for Poiccart’s return. When he had come: “Now, Mr. Washington, we will tell you what we wish you to do. We wish you to take a letter to Lisbon. Leon has probably hinted something to that effect, and it is now my duty to tell you that the errand is pretty certain to be an exceedingly dangerous one, but you are the only man I know to whom I could entrust this important document. I feel I cannot allow you to undertake this mission without telling you that the chances are heavily against your reaching Portugal.”

“Bless you for those cheerful words,” said Washington blankly. “The only thing I want to be certain about is, am I likely to meet Mr. Snake?”

Manfred nodded, and the American’s face lengthened.

“I don’t know that even that scares me,” he said at last, “especially now that I know that the dope they use isn’t honest snake-spit at all but a synthesized poison. It was having my confidence shaken in snakes that rattled me. When do you want me to go?”

“Tonight.”

Mr. Washington for the moment was perplexed, and Manfred continued: “Not by the Dover-Calais route. We would prefer that you travelled by Newhaven-Dieppe. Our friends are less liable to be on the alert, though I can’t even guarantee that. Oberzohn spends a lot of money in espionage. This house has been under observation for days. I will show you.”
===Chinese===
曼弗雷德把桌上的几份文件归置整齐，放进抽屉，等波伊卡特回来。人回来了，他才开口：「好了，华盛顿先生，我们要告诉你，我们盼你做的是一桩什么事。我们要你送一封信去里斯本。莱昂多半已朝这个意思透过了些口风；如今该由我来告诉你：这趟差事十有八九凶险异常。但在我认识的人里，这等重要文书托付得下去的，只有你一个。我总觉得，若不向你言明——你抵达葡萄牙的胜算实在悬得很——我就不能听凭你去承当这趟使命。」

「承您吉言，多谢这番提气的话。」华盛顿木然说。「我唯一想弄确实的是：我会不会碰上蛇先生？」

曼弗雷德点了点头；美国人的脸拉长了。

「闹不清连这个都吓不吓得住我。」他终于说，「尤其如今我已知道，他们使的那玩意儿根本不是地道的蛇唾沫，而是合成的毒药。叫我心神不定的，是我对蛇的信心动摇了。您要我几时动身？」

「今夜。」

华盛顿先生一时有些发懵；曼弗雷德接着往下说：「不走多佛尔—加来那条道。我们宁愿你取道纽黑文—迪耶普。咱们那几位朋友在那边严加提防的可能小些——虽说这一点我也不能打包票。欧伯佐恩在谍报上花的钱海了去了。这所宅子已被人盯了好几天。我说给你看。」

===Original===
He walked to the window and drew aside the curtain.

“Do you see a spy?” he asked, with a twinkle in his eye.

Mr. Washington looked up and down the street.

“Sure!” he said. “That man at the corner smoking a cigar﻿—”

“Is a detective officer from Scotland Yard,” said Manfred. “Do you see anybody else?”
===Chinese===
他走到窗前，拉开窗帘。

「你看得见暗探吗？」他问，眼里闪着一点狡黠。

华盛顿先生把那条街来来回回瞧了一遍。

「瞧见了！」他说。「拐角上那个抽雪茄的——」

「是苏格兰场的侦探警官。」曼弗雷德说。「你还看得见别人吗？」

===Original===
“Yes,” said Washington after a while, “there’s a man cleaning windows on the opposite side of the road: he keeps looking across here.”

“A perfectly innocent citizen,” said Manfred.

“Well, he can’t be in any of those taxis, because they’re empty.” Mr. Washington nodded to a line of taxis drawn up on the rank in the centre of the road.

“On the contrary, he is in the first taxi on the rank﻿—he is the driver! If you went out and called a cab, he would come to you. If anybody else called him, he would be engaged. His name is Clarke, he lives at 43, Portlington Mews; he is an ex-convict living apart from his wife, and he receives seven pounds a week for his services, ten pounds every time he drives Oberzohn’s car, and all the money he makes out of his cab.”

He smiled at the other’s astonishment.
===Chinese===
「看得见。」华盛顿看了一阵才说。「马路对面有个擦窗户的人：他眼睛一个劲儿往这边瞟。」

「那是个清清白白的良民。」曼弗雷德说。

「唔，反正他不在那几辆出租车里，车里全是空的。」华盛顿先生朝停在路中央候客位上的一列出租汽车点了点头。

「恰恰相反，他就在候客列的头一辆里——他就是司机！你要是出门叫车，应召而去的便是他。换了别人招他，他便是『已有雇主』。此人名叫克拉克（Clarke），住在波特林顿马厩巷（Portlington Mews）43 号；他是个释囚，与老婆分居，每周领七镑酬金，替欧伯佐恩开一趟车另收十镑，开出租挣下的钱也一概归他。」

他瞧着对方的惊愕，微微一笑。

===Original===
“So the chances are that your movements will be known; even though you do not call the cab, he will follow you. You must be prepared for that. I’m putting all my cards on the table, Mr. Washington, and asking you to do something which, if you cannot bring yourself to agree, must be done by either myself, Poiccart or Gonsalez. Frankly, none of us can be spared.”

“I’ll go,” said the American. “Snake or no snake, I’m for Lisbon. What is my route?”

Poiccart took a folded paper from his pocket.

“Newhaven, Dieppe, Paris. You have a reserved compartment on the Sud Express; you reach Valladolid late tomorrow night, and change to the Portuguese mail. Unless I can fix an aeroplane to meet you at Irun. We are trying now. Otherwise, you should be in Lisbon at two o’clock on the following afternoon. He had better take the letter now, George.”
===Chinese===
「所以说，你的行踪多半早已尽人皆知；纵然你不叫那辆车，他也会一路跟定你。这一点你须有个准备。我是把底牌全摊在桌上了，华盛顿先生；我请你办的这件事，你若实在应承不下，就只得由我、波伊卡特或冈萨雷斯三人中的一个亲自去办。老实说，我们三个，谁也抽不开身。」

「我去。」美国人说。「有蛇没蛇，里斯本我是去定了。我的路线怎么走？」

波伊卡特从口袋里取出一张折起的纸。

「纽黑文、迪耶普、巴黎。『南方快车』（Sud Express）上给你留了一间包厢；明天深夜你抵巴利亚多利德（Valladolid），换乘去葡萄牙的邮政列车——除非我能安排一架飞机到伊伦（Irun）接你，此事我们正在设法。不然的话，后天下午两点你该到里斯本。信顶好现在就交给他，乔治。」

===Original===
Manfred unlocked the wall safe and took out a long envelope. It was addressed to “**Senhor Alvaz Manuel y Cintra, Minister of Colonies**,” and was heavily sealed.

“I want you to place this in Senhor Cintra’s hands. You’ll have no difficulty there because you will be expected,” he said. “Will you travel in that suit?”

The American thought.

“Yes, that’s as good as any,” he said.

“Will you take off your jacket?”

Mr. Washington obeyed, and with a small pair of scissors Manfred cut a slit in the lining and slipped the letter in. Then, to the American’s astonishment, Leon produced a rolled housewife, threaded a needle with extraordinary dexterity, and for the next five minutes the snake-hunter watched the deft fingers stitching through paper and lining. So skilfully was the slit sewed that Elijah Washington had to look twice to make sure where the lining had been cut.
===Chinese===
曼弗雷德打开墙内保险柜，取出一只长信封。信封上写着「**葡萄牙殖民地大臣阿尔瓦兹·曼努埃尔·伊·辛特拉先生（Senhor Alvaz Manuel y Cintra, Minister of Colonies）**」，封得严严实实。

「我要你把这只信封亲手放到辛特拉先生手里。那一头你不会为难，因为自有人恭候。」他说。「你打算就穿这套衣裳上路？」

美国人想了想。

「嗯，就这套吧，跟哪套都一样。」他说。

「把上衣脱下来行吗？」

华盛顿先生依言脱下；曼弗雷德用一把小剪刀在衣里上割开一道缝，把信塞了进去。接着，叫美国人啧啧称奇的是，莱昂掏出一卷针线包，以出奇的手疾穿好针；随后五分钟里，这位捕蛇人只顾看那双巧手在信封纸与衣里之间穿针走线。那道口子缝得如此天衣无缝，伊莱贾·华盛顿不得不端详两遍，才认准衣里究竟是在哪里割开的。

===Original===
“Well, that beats the band!” he said. “Mr. Gonsalez, I’ll send you my shirts for repair!”

“And here is something for you to carry.” It was a black leather portfolio, well worn. To one end was attached a steel chain terminating in a leather belt. “I want you to put this round your waist, and from now on to carry this wallet. It contains nothing more important than a few envelopes imposingly sealed, and if you lose it no great harm will come.”
===Chinese===
「嗬，这手活儿真叫一绝！」他说。「冈萨雷斯先生，往后我的衬衫都送您这儿织补！」

「这儿还有一样东西要你随身带。」那是一只黑皮公文夹，已磨得半旧。一头拴着一条钢链，链子尽头连着一条皮带。「我要你把它系在腰上，从今往后随身带着这只皮夹。里头装的东西，无非是几封封得煞有介事的信封，丢了也无大碍。」

===Original===
“You think they’ll go for the wallet?”

Manfred nodded.

“One cannot tell, of course, what Oberzohn will do, and he’s as wily as one of his snakes. But my experience has been,” he said, “that the cleverer the criminal, the bigger the fool and the more outrageous his mistakes. You will want money.”

“Well, I’m not short of that,” said the other with a smile. “Snakes are a mighty profitable proposition. Still, I’m a business man﻿ ﻿…”

For the next five minutes they discussed financial details, and he was more than surprised to discover the recklessness with which money was disbursed.
===Chinese===
「您料定他们会冲这只皮夹下手？」

曼弗雷德点了点头。

「欧伯佐恩会出什么招，当然谁也说不好；他跟他养的蛇一样奸猾。不过依我的经验，」他说，「罪犯越是聪明，就越是个大傻瓜，出的岔子也越发离谱。你会用得着钱的。」

「唔，钱嘛，我倒不短。」对方微笑道。「蛇可是桩顶赚钱的买卖。话虽如此，我到底是个生意人……」

接下来的五分钟，他们商量起银钱细账；待发现他们撒起钱来那股不管不顾的豪阔手笔，他简直是惊讶到了极点。

===Original===
He went out, with a glance from the corner of his eye at the taximan, whose hand was raised inquiringly, but, ignoring the driver, he turned and walked towards Regent Street, and presently found a wandering taxi of an innocuous character, and ordered the man to drive to the Ritz-Carlton, where rooms had been taken for him.

He was in Regent Street before he looked round through the peephole, and, as Manfred had promised him, the taxi was following, its flag down to prevent chance hiring. Mr. Washington went up to his room, opened the window and looked out: the taxi had joined a nearby rank. The driver had left his box.

“He’s on the phone,” muttered Mr. Washington, and would have given a lot of money to have known the nature of the message.
===Chinese===
他出了门，眼角瞟了那出租车司机一眼——那人正扬起手来，探问要不要车；他却不予理会，转身朝摄政街（Regent Street）走去，不多时便寻着一辆四处游荡、瞧着毫无干系的出租汽车，吩咐司机开往丽思卡尔顿饭店——那里已替他订好了房间。

进了摄政街，他才回头从瞭望孔里张望：果如曼弗雷德所言，那辆出租车正跟在后头，旗牌放倒了，免得被顺路的主顾雇走。华盛顿先生上楼进了房间，推开窗朝外望：那辆出租车已排进了近旁的候客列。司机已离开驾驶座。

「他打电话去了。」华盛顿先生咕哝道；那通电话究竟说了些什么，他情愿掏一大笔钱，换一个明白。

## XXIV On the Night Mail / 二十四 夜行邮车
===Original===
A man of habit, Mr. Oberzohn missed his daily journey to the City Road. In ordinary circumstances the loss would have been a paralysing one, but of late he had grown more and more wedded to his deep armchair and his ponderous volumes; and though the City Road had been a very useful establishment in many ways, and was ill replaced by the temporary building which his manager had secured, he felt he could almost dispense with that branch of his business altogether.
Oberzohn & Smitts was an institution which had grown out of nothing. The energy of the partners, and especially the knowledge of African trading conditions which the departed Smitts possessed, had produced a flourishing business which ten years before could have been floated for half a million pounds.
Orders still came in. There were upcountry stores to be restocked; new, if unimportant, contracts to be fulfilled; there was even a tentative offer under consideration from one of the South American States for the armaments of a political faction. But Mr. Oberzohn was content to mark time, in the faith that the next week would see him superior to these minor considerations, and in a position, if he so wished, to liquidate his business and sell his stores and his trade. There were purchasers ready, but the half a million pounds had dwindled to a tenth of that sum, which outstanding bills would more than absorb. As Manfred had said, his running expenses were enormous. He had agents in every central Government office in Europe, and though they did not earn their salt, they certainly drew more than condiment for their services.
===Chinese===
欧伯佐恩先生是个凡事按老习惯行事的人，如今每日一趟的城市路之行去不成了，心里不免若有所失。若在寻常境况，这损失本足以叫他一蹶不振；只是近来他已愈发同他那把深陷的扶手椅、那几部沉甸甸的大书相依为命；况且城市路那爿字号虽说在许多方面都大有用处，如今由经理设法寻来顶替的临时场所实在不成模样——不过他觉得，生意上的这一支，他几乎可以索性整个撇开了。
欧伯佐恩-斯米茨商行是一门从无到有长起来的生意。两位合伙人的干劲，尤其是那位故去的斯米茨所具的非洲贸易行情知识，造就了这爿兴旺字号——十年之前，这本是一门可以作价五十万镑上市集资的生意。
订单仍源源而来。内地的铺栈有待补货；新的合同——虽说无关紧要——有待履行；甚至有一个南美国家正斟酌着一项试探性的出价，要为某个政治派别置办军火。但欧伯佐恩先生安于原地踏步，他笃信到下个星期，自己便将超然于这些琐屑考虑之上，届时他若愿意，尽可把生意清算收摊，铺面连同行当一总卖出。买主是现成的，只是那五十万镑已缩水到十分之一，而未清偿的账单足以把这笔钱吞没还有富余。正如曼弗雷德所言，他的日常开销大得惊人。欧洲各国中央政府的衙署里，处处有他安插的坐探；这些人虽不值自己吃的那份盐，领起「调味料」来倒着实绰绰有余。
===Original===
