He had spent a busy morning in his little workshop-laboratory, and had settled himself down in his chair, when a telegraph messenger came trundling his bicycle across the rough ground, stopped to admire for a second the iron dogs which littered the untidy strip of lawn, and woke the echoes of this gaunt house with a thunderous knock. Mr. Oberzohn hurried to the door. A telegram to this address must necessarily be important. He took the telegram, slammed the door in the messenger’s face and hurried back to his room, tearing open the envelope as he went.
There were three sheets of misspelt writing, for the wire was in Portuguese and telegraph operators are bad guessers. He read it through carefully, his lips moving silently, until he came to the end, then he started reading all over again, and, for a better understanding of its purport, he took a pencil and paper and translated the message into Swedish. He laid the telegram face downwards on the table and took up his book, but he was not reading. His busy mind slipped from Lisbon to London, from Curzon Street to the factory, and at last he shut his book with a bang, got up, and opening the door, barked Gurther’s name. That strange man came downstairs in his stockinged feet, his hair hanging over his eyes, an unpleasant sight. Dr. Oberzohn pointed to the room and the man entered.
===Chinese===
这天上午，他在那间作坊兼实验室的小屋里忙了半天，刚在椅中坐定，一个电报信差便推着自行车轧过粗糙的地面而来，停下脚，对散落在那溜邋遢草皮上的铁狗端详了一秒钟，跟着一记擂门，震得这座冷峻萧索的宅子回声四起。欧伯佐恩先生急忙赶去开门。送到这个地址来的电报，必定关系重大。他接过电报，当着信差的面砰地关上门，匆匆回到自己房里，一面走一面撕开信封。
信笺是三张满纸错字的手书——电文用的是葡萄牙文，而电报局的报务员向来不擅猜谜。他从头至尾细细读了一遍，嘴唇无声地翕动；读罢又从头再读一遍，为把文意吃得更透，他取来铅笔和纸，把电文译成了瑞典文。他把电报翻扣在桌上，拿起书，却一个字也没读进去。他那颗不肯闲着的心，从里斯本滑到伦敦，从柯曾街滑到厂区，末了他砰地合上书，站起身，开了门，厉声喝出格瑟的名字。那个怪人穿着袜子下了楼，头发披垂在眼前，一副叫人看了难受的样子。欧伯佐恩博士朝屋里一指，那人便进去了。
===Original===
For an hour they talked behind locked doors, and then Gurther came out, still showing his teeth in a mechanical smile, and went up the stairs two at a time. The half-witted Danish maid, passing the door of the doctor’s room, heard his gruff voice booming into the telephone, but since he spoke a language which, whilst it had some relation to her own, was subtly different, she could not have heard the instructions, admonitions, orders and suggestions which he fired in half a dozen different directions, even if she had heard him clearly.
This done, Dr. Oberzohn returned to his book and a midday refreshment, spooning his lunch from a small cup at his side containing a few fluid ounces of dark red liquid. One half of his mind was pursuing his well-read philosophers; the other worked at feverish speed, conjecturing and guessing, forestalling and baffling the minds that were working against him. He played a game of mental chess, all the time seeking for a check, and when at last he had discovered one that was adequate, he put down his book and went out into his garden, strolling up and down inside the wire fence, stopping now and again to pick a flower from a weed, or pausing to examine a rain-filled pothole as though it were the star object in a prize landscape.
===Chinese===
两人在闩死的门后谈了一个钟头，随后格瑟出来了，脸上依旧咧着那副机械的笑，两级一跨奔上楼去。那半傻的丹麦女仆打医生房门口经过，听见他粗哑的嗓门对着电话轰鸣；只是他讲的那种话虽与她自己的话沾亲带故，却又隐隐两样，故此莫说她听得不真切，就算句句听清，也听不懂他朝着六个方向劈头发出的一连串指示、告诫、命令与建议。
这一番办完，欧伯佐恩博士回到书卷与午间滋养跟前，用手边一只小杯一匙匙舀着吃他的午餐——杯中盛着几液盎司深红色的汁液。他半个心思追随着读得烂熟的哲人们，另外半个却以狂热的高速运转着，推测、臆度，抢在先机、挫败那些正与他作对的心思。他下着一盘意念中的棋，无时无刻不在寻一步将军；待到终于觅得一步足够分量的杀着，他放下书，踱进园子，在铁丝网篱之内来回蹀躞，不时停下脚，从一株野草上摘一朵花，或是俯身端详一个积满雨水的坑洼，仿佛那是获奖风景画中的头号景物。
===Original===
He loved this ugly house, knew every brick of it, as a feudal lord might have known the castle he had built, the turret, the flat roof with its high parapet, that commanded a view of the canal bank on the one side and the railway arches left and right. They were railway arches which had a value to him. Most of them were blocked up, having been converted into lockup garages and sheds, and through only a few could ingress be had. One, under which ran the muddy lane—why it was called Hangman’s Lane nobody knew; another that gave to some allotments on the edge of his property; and a third through which he also could see daylight, but which spanned no road at all.
===Chinese===
他爱这幢丑陋的宅子，熟悉它的每一块砖，正如一位封建领主熟悉自己一手建起的城堡——那座角塔，那片围着高耸胸墙的平屋顶，一侧控临运河的堤岸，左右尽收铁路拱桥。这些铁路拱桥于他自有其价值。桥洞大半封死了，改作了上锁的车房与棚屋，能通行人身的只剩寥寥几个：一个桥洞底下跑着那条泥泞的小巷——为何叫它绞刑吏巷，无人知晓；另一个通到他地界边缘的几块佃租份地；还有第三个，从那洞里他同样望得见天光，可它底下压根儿不跨任何道路。
===Original===
An express train roared past in a cloud of steam, and he scanned the viaduct with benignant interest. And then he performed his daily tour of inspection. Turning back into the house, he climbed the stairs to the third floor, opened a little door that revealed an extra flight of steps, and emerged on to the roof. At each corner was a square black shed, about the height of a man’s chest. The doors were heavily padlocked, and nearby each was a stout black box, equally weatherproof. There were other things here: great, clumsy wall-plugs at regular intervals. Seeing them, it might be thought that Mr. Oberzohn contemplated a night when, in the exultation of achievement, he would illuminate his ungainly premises. But up till now that night had not arrived, and in truth the only light usable was one which at the moment was dismantled in the larger of the four sheds.
===Chinese===
一列快车裹着蒸汽的云团轰隆驶过，他带着慈和的兴味把高架桥打量了一番。随后他开始每日一巡。折回宅内，他登上三楼，推开一扇小门，门后又露出一截楼梯，拾级而上便到了屋顶。屋顶四角各立着一座方形黑棚，高可及人胸。棚门都上了沉重的挂锁，每座棚旁还各有一只同样风雨不透的结实黑箱。这里还有些别的东西：间隔匀称的一排排粗大笨拙的墙壁插座。见了这些，人们兴许会以为欧伯佐恩先生在盼着某个夜晚——在大功告成的得意忘形之中，把他这处粗陋难看的宅业照得灯火通明。可直到如今那一夜也不曾降临；实情是，眼下唯一能用的一盏灯，正拆散了搁在四座棚中最大的一座里。
===Original===
From here he could look down upon the water cutting into the factory grounds; and the black bulk of the barge, which filled the entire width of the wharf, seemed so near that he could have thrown a stone upon it. His idle interest was in the sluggish black water that oozed through the gates. A slight mist lay upon the canal; a barge was passing down towards Deptford, and he contemplated the straining horse that tugged the barge rope with a mind set upon the time when he, too, might use the waterway in a swifter craft.
===Chinese===
从这儿他可以俯瞰那条嵌进厂区的水道；驳船黑黢黢的庞大身躯塞满码头的整幅宽度，看着近得他一抬手就能把石子抛上船去。他那份无所事事的兴味，全落在从闸门缝里渗出的滞缓黑水上。运河浮着一层薄雾；一条驳船正顺水往德普特福德（Deptford）去，他望着那匹绷紧了身子曳拉船缆的辕马出神，心里惦着有朝一日，他自己也要驾一艘更迅捷的船只走这条水路。
===Original===
London lay around him, its spires and chimneys looming through the thin haze of smoke. Far away the sun caught the golden ball of St. Paul’s and added a new star to the firmament. Mr. Oberzohn hated London—only this little patch of his had beauty in his eyes. Not the broad green parks and the flowering rhododendrons; not the majestic aisles of pleasure where the rich lounger rode or walked, nor the streets of stone-fronted stores, nor the pleasant green of suburban roads—he loved only these God-forgotten acres, this slimy wilderness in which he had set up his habitation.
===Chinese===
伦敦在他四周铺展开来，一城塔尖与烟囱在薄薄的烟霭里隐现。远处，日头照上圣保罗教堂（St. Paul’s）顶上的金球，给天穹添了一颗新星。欧伯佐恩先生恨伦敦——唯有他自己这一小块地盘，在他眼里才有美处。不是那开阔的绿荫公园与盛放的杜鹃；不是那闲富之辈驱车信步的堂皇游憩长街，不是那一街街石砌门面的商铺，也不是郊区道路赏心悦目的青翠——他爱的只是这几亩天弃之地，这片他筑巢而居的黏滑荒泽。
===Original===
He went downstairs, locking the roof door behind him, and, passing Gurther’s room, knocked and was asked to enter. The man sat in his singlet; he had shaved once, but now the keen razor was going across his skin for the second time. He turned his face, shining with cream, and grinned round at the intruder, and with a grunt the doctor shut the door and went downstairs, knowing that the man was for the moment happy; for nothing pleased Gurther quite so much as “dressing up.”
The doctor stood at the entrance of his own room, hesitating between books and laboratory, decided upon the latter, and was busy for the next two hours. Only once he came out, and that was to bring from the warm room the green baize box which contained “the most potent of chemicals, colossal in power.”
===Chinese===
他下了楼，随手锁上屋顶的门，经过格瑟的房间时叩了叩门，得了请进的应声。那人只穿着一件汗衫坐在那里；脸已刮过一遍，此刻那把锋快的剃刀正第二遍走过他的皮肤。他转过那张涂满剃须膏、油光光的脸，朝闯进来的人咧嘴一笑。医生哼了一声，带上门，径自下楼去了——他知道这人眼下心绪正好；因为再没有什么比「化装」更能逗格瑟欢喜的了。
医生站在自己房门口，在书卷与实验室之间踌躇片刻，选定了后者，跟着便忙了两个钟头。中间他只出来过一回——为的是从那间暖屋里取出那只绿呢面的箱子，里头装着「最烈性的化学品，威力骇人」。
---

===Original===
The Newhaven-Dieppe route is spasmodically popular. There are nights when the trains to Paris are crowded; other nights when it is possible to obtain a carriage to yourself; and it happened that this evening, when Elijah Washington booked his seat, he might, if it had been physically possible, have sat in one compartment and put his feet on the seat in another.
===Chinese===
纽黑文（Newhaven）至迪耶普（Dieppe）这条航线，时冷时热地受人青睐。有的夜里，开往巴黎的列车拥挤不堪；另一些夜里，你竟能独占一节车厢。这天傍晚伊莱贾·华盛顿订座时，恰巧赶上了后者——倘若人的身躯做得到，他满可以人坐这一间包厢，把脚搁到另一间的座位上去。
===Original===
Between the two great branches of the Anglo-Saxon race there is one notable difference. The Englishman prefers to travel in solitude and silence. His ideal journey is one from London to Constantinople in a compartment that is not invaded except by the ticket collector; and if it is humanly possible that he can reach his destination without having given utterance to anything more sensational than an agreement with some other passenger’s comment on the weather, he is indeed a happy man. The American loves company; he has the acquisitiveness of the Latin, combined with the rhetorical virtues of the Teuton. Solitude makes him miserable; silence irritates him. He wants to talk about large and important things, such as the future of the country, the prospects of agriculture and the fluctuations of trade, about which the average Englishman knows nothing, and is less interested. The American has a town pride, can talk almost emotionally about a new drainage system and grow eloquent upon a municipal balance sheet. The Englishman does not cultivate his town pride until he reaches middle age, and then only in sufficient quantities to feel disappointed with the place of his birth after he has renewed its acquaintance.
===Chinese===
盎格鲁-撒克逊种族的两大支脉之间，有一个显著的差别。英国人爱在孤独与沉默中旅行。他理想的旅程，是从伦敦坐到君士坦丁堡，一路包厢里除查票员外无人闯入；倘若人力许可，他竟一路到站，除了附和某位同车客对天气的一句评语之外再不曾吐出半句惊人之语，那他真是个幸福的人了。美国人爱作伴；他有拉丁人的贪求好问，又兼条顿人的雄辩美德。孤独叫他难过；沉默叫他冒火。他要谈论宏大而要紧的事——国家的前途、农业的展望、行情的涨落——这些事寻常英国人一概不知，兴味更是等而下之。美国人有乡土的自豪，谈起一套新的下水道系统几乎能动感情，对着一张市政资产负债表也能口若悬河。英国人不到中年不培养他的乡土自豪，而且培养的分量也刚够他在重新认得故乡之后，对那地方生出失望来。
===Original===
Mr. Washington found himself in an empty compartment, and, grunting his dissatisfaction, walked along the corridor, peeping into one cell after another in the hope of discovering a fellow-countryman in a similar unhappy plight. His search was fruitless and he returned to the carriage in which his bag and overcoat were deposited, and settled down to the study of an English humorous newspaper and a vain search for something at which any intelligent man could laugh.
===Chinese===
华盛顿先生发现自己独处一间空包厢，不满地哼了一声，顺着走廊一路走去，一格一格往那些小格子里张望，指望寻出一位同样陷于不幸处境的同邦人。搜寻一无所获，他只得回到那节搁着他的提包与大衣的车厢里坐定，埋头研究起一份英国滑稽报纸，徒劳地搜寻任何能让一个有脑筋的人发笑的东西。
===Original===
The doors of the coach were at either end, and most passengers entering had to pass the open entrance of Mr. Washington’s compartment. At every click of the door he looked up, hoping to find a congenial soul. But disappointment awaited him, until a lady hesitated by the door. It was a smoking carriage, but Washington, who was a man of gallant character, would gladly have sacrificed his cigar for the pleasure of her society. Young, he guessed, and a widow. She was in black, an attractive face showed through a heavy veil.
===Chinese===
车厢的门开在两头，上车的旅客大半都得经过华盛顿先生那间敞着门的包厢。门每咔哒响一声，他都抬起头，指望觅得一个投缘的灵魂。可等着他的只有失望——直到一位夫人在门边逡巡不前。这是节吸烟车厢，但华盛顿生性豪侠，为了能有她作伴的乐趣，甘愿把雪茄牺牲掉。年轻——他猜——而且是个寡妇。她一身黑衣，厚重的面纱后透出一张动人的脸。
===Original===
“Is this compartment engaged?” she asked in a low voice that was almost a whisper.
“No, madam.” Washington rose, hat in hand.
“Would you mind?” she asked in a soft voice.
“Why, surely! Sit down, ma’am,” said the gallant American. “Would you like the corner seat by the window?”
She shook her head, and sat down near the door, turning her face from him.
“Do you mind my smoking?” asked Washington, after a while.
“Please smoke,” she said, and again turned her face away.
“English,” thought Mr. Washington in disgust, and hunched himself for an hour and a half of unrelieved silence.
A whistle blew, the train moved slowly from the platform, and Elijah Washington’s adventurous journey had begun.
===Chinese===
「这间包厢有人订了吗？」她用低得近乎耳语的嗓音问。
「没有，夫人。」华盛顿手里捏着帽子，站起身来。
「可以吗？」她轻声问。
「哦，当然！请坐，太太。」这位豪侠的美国人说。「要不要靠窗的角落座位？」
她摇了摇头，在靠门的地方坐下，把脸偏开去。
「我抽支烟您介意吗？」过了一会儿，华盛顿问。
「请抽吧。」她说，又把脸偏了过去。
「英国人。」华盛顿先生厌恶地想着，缩起肩膀，预备捱过一个半钟头透不过气的沉默。
汽笛一响，列车缓缓驶离站台，伊莱贾·华盛顿的历险之旅开始了。
===Original===
They were passing through Croydon when the girl rose, and, leaning out, closed the little glass-panelled door.
“You should let me do that,” said Elijah reproachfully, and she murmured something about not wishing to trouble him, and he relapsed into his seat.
One or two of the men who passed looked in, and evidently this annoyed her, for she reached and pulled down the spring blind which partially hid her from outside observation, and after the ticket collector had been and had punched the slips, she lowered the second of the three blinds.
===Chinese===
车过克罗伊登（Croydon）时，那姑娘起身探出去，把那扇嵌着玻璃的小门关上。
「这个该让我来。」伊莱贾责备道；她低声含糊地说了句不愿劳烦他之类的话，他便缩回座位里坐好。
走过的一两个男人朝里张了张眼，这显然叫她着恼，因为她伸手拉下了弹簧卷帘，把自己半掩在过路人的视线之外；等查票员来剪过票，她又一气放下了三面帘子中的第二面。
===Original===
“Do you mind?” she asked.
“Sure not, ma’am,” said Elijah, without any great heartiness. He had no desire to travel alone with a lady in a carriage so discreetly curtained. He had heard of cases… and by nature he was an extremely cautious man.
===Chinese===
「介意吗？」她问。
「当然不，太太。」伊莱贾说，口气并不怎么热络。他可无意同一位夫人单独待在遮掩得这样周到的车厢里。这样的案子他有所耳闻……何况他天生是个极其谨慎的人。
===Original===
The speed of the train increased; the wandering passengers had settled down. The second of the ticket inspections came as they were rushing through Redhill, and Mr. Washington thought uncomfortably that there was a significant look in the inspector’s face as he glanced first at the drawn blinds, then from the lady to himself.
She affected a perfume of a peculiarly pleasing kind. The carriage was filled with this subtle fragrance. Mr. Washington smelt it above the scent of his cigar. Her face was still averted; he wondered if she had gone to sleep, and, growing weary of his search for humour, he put down the paper, folded his hands and closed his eyes, and found himself gently drifting to that medley of the real and unreal which is the overture of dreams.
===Chinese===
车速加快了；走廊里游荡的旅客也都各归各座。第二次查票来时，车正风驰电掣地穿过雷德希尔（Redhill），华盛顿先生老大不舒服地觉得，查票员脸上神情有异——先看看拉下的帘子，又从那位夫人看到他本人。
她惯用一种格外悦人的香水，整节车厢都浸在这微妙的芬芳里，华盛顿先生隔着雪茄的烟气也闻得出来。她的脸仍偏着；他疑心她睡着了，找笑料也找得乏了，便放下报纸，叠起双手，阖上眼睛，只觉得自己悠悠荡进那真境与幻境的杂糅之地——那正是梦的序曲。
===Original===
The lady moved; he looked at her out of the corner of his half-closed eyes. She had moved round so as to half face him. Her veil was still down, her white gloves were reflectively clasped on her knees. He shut his eyes again, until another movement brought him awake. She was feeling in her bag.
Mr. Washington was awake now—as wide awake as he had ever been in his life. In stretching out her hand, the lady had pulled short her sleeve, and there was a gap of flesh between the glove and the wrist of her blouse, and on her wrist was hair!
He shifted his position slightly, grunted as in his sleep, and dropped his hand to his pocket, and all the time those cold eyes were watching him through the veil.
===Chinese===
那位夫人动了动；他从半阖的眼角里看她。她已挪过身子，半面对着他。面纱仍旧罩着，一双戴白手套的手若有所思地扣在膝上。他又阖上眼，直到再一次的动静把他惊醒。她正在手包里摸索。
华盛顿先生这回是真醒了——这辈子从没这样清醒过。那位夫人伸手之际把袖子带短了一截，手套与衣袖腕口之间露出一线皮肉，而那腕子上有毛！
他微微挪了挪身子，像睡梦中似的哼了一声，手垂到口袋上——自始至终，那双冷酷的眼睛都在面纱后头盯着他。
===Original===
Lifting the bottom of the veil, she put the ebony holder between her teeth and searched the bag for a match. Then she turned appealingly to him as though she had sensed his wakefulness. As she rose, Washington rose too, and suddenly he sprang at her and flung her back against the door. For a moment the veiled lady was taken by surprise, and then there was a flash of steel.
From nowhere a knife had come into her hand and Washington gripped the wrist and levered it over, pushing the palm of his hand under the chin. Even through the veil he could feel the bristles, and knew now, if he had not known before, that he had to deal with a man. A live, active man, rendered doubly strong by the knowledge of his danger. Gurther butted forward with his head, but Washington saw the attack coming, shortened his arm and jabbed full at the face behind the veil. The blow stopped the man, only for an instant, and again he came on, and this time the point of the knife caught the American’s shoulder, and ripped the coat to the elbow. It needed this to bring forth Elijah Washington’s mental and physical reserves. With a roar he gripped the throat of his assailant and threw him with such violence against the door that it gave, and the “widow in mourning” crashed against the panel of the outer corridor. Before he could reach the attacker, Gurther had turned and sped along the corridor to the door of the coach. In a second he had flung it open and had dropped to the footboard. The train was slowing to take Horsham Junction, and the cat eyes waited until he saw a good fall, and let go. Staring back into the darkness, Washington saw nothing, and then the train inspector came along.
===Chinese===
她掀起面纱底边，把乌木烟嘴咬在齿间，在手包里摸火柴。随即她向他转过脸来，带着恳求的神气，仿佛已察觉他醒着。她一起身，华盛顿也起了身，猛然间他扑将过去，把她整个掼在包厢门上。披面纱的夫人一时间着了慌——跟着寒光一闪。
不知打哪儿，一柄刀已经跳进她手里。华盛顿扣住那截手腕，硬生生别过去，掌根直顶进对方颌下。隔着面纱他也摸得出那扎人的胡茬，于是他明白了——倘若先前还不明白——他应付的是个男人。一个活生生的男人，一个因自知身陷险境而加倍强悍的男人。格瑟低头一头撞将过来，华盛顿却觑准了这一招，收臂发短拳，一记结结实实捣在面纱后面那张脸上。这一拳只叫那人顿了一顿，他又扑了上来，这一回刀尖挂上了美国人的肩头，把上衣一路豁到肘弯。非到此时，不足以逼出伊莱贾·华盛顿身心两方面的全部后劲。他一声怒吼，掐住袭击者的咽喉，把那人往车门上掼得如此凶猛，门竟被撞开了，「披丧服的寡妇」一头磕在外侧走廊的壁板上。他还没来得及赶上前去，格瑟已转身顺着走廊直奔车厢尽头的门，眨眼间拉开门，跳落到踏板上。列车正减速进霍舍姆枢纽站（Horsham Junction），那双猫眼等着相中一处摔得下去的好地方，才松开了手。华盛顿回望黑暗，什么也没望见；随后，列车稽查走了过来。
===Original===
“It was a man in women’s clothes,” he said, a little breathlessly, and they went back to search the compartment, but Mr. Gurther had taken bag and everything with him, and the only souvenir of his presence was the heel of a shoe that had been torn off in the struggle.
===Chinese===
「那是个穿女人衣裳的男人。」他微微气喘着说。他们回去搜了那间包厢，可格瑟先生连提包带一切随身之物都带走了，他留下的唯一纪念品，是扭打中被撕落的一只鞋跟。

## XV Gurther Returns / 十五 格瑟归来

===Original===
The train was going at thirty miles an hour when Gurther dropped on to a ridge of sand by the side of the track, and in the next second he was sliding forward on his face. Fortunately for him the veil, though torn, kept his eyes free. Stumbling to his feet, he looked round. The level-crossing gates should be somewhere here. He had intended jumping the train at this point, and Oberzohn had made arrangements accordingly. A signalman, perched high above the track, saw the figure and challenged.

===Chinese===
列车正以时速三十英里疾驰，格瑟跳将下来，落在轨道旁一道沙脊上；下一秒钟，他已脸冲着地向前滑去。算他走运，面纱虽已撕裂，却还护得住他的眼睛。他跌跌撞撞地爬起身，四下张望。平交道口的栏杆门应该就在这一带。他原就打算在这一段跳车，欧伯佐恩也照此作好了安排。一个高踞在轨道上方的信号员望见了这条人影，出声喝问。

===Original===
“I’ve lost my way,” said Gurther. “Where is the level-crossing?”

“A hundred yards farther on. Keep clear of those metals﻿—the Eastbourne express is coming behind.”

===Chinese===
「我迷了路。」格瑟说。「平交道口在哪儿？」

「再往前一百码。离那铁轨远着些——后头伊斯特本特快就要过来了。」

===Original===
If Gurther had had his way, he would have stopped long enough to remove a rail for the sheer joy of watching a few hundred of the hated people plunged to destruction. But he guessed that the car was waiting, went sideways through the safety gates into a road which was fairly populous. There were people about who turned their heads and looked in amazement at the bedraggled woman in black, but he had got beyond worrying about his appearance.

===Chinese===
倘若由着格瑟的性子，他真肯耗上工夫撬走一根铁轨，单为看几百个他所仇视的人栽进毁灭，图一场快活。但他估摸着汽车正在等候，便侧身穿过安全栅门，走上了一条人烟颇密的大路。路上行人纷纷回头，惊愕地打量这个浑身泥水的黑衣女人，他却早已顾不得自己的仪表了。

===Original===
He saw the car with the little green light which Oberzohn invariably used to mark his machines from others, and, climbing into the cab (as it was), sat down to recover his breath. The driver he knew as one of the three men employed by Oberzohn, one of whom Mr. Washington had seen that morning.

===Chinese===
他望见了那辆汽车，车上亮着一盏小绿灯——欧伯佐恩向来用这个把自己的车同旁人的车分辨开来。他爬进那勉强算是驾驶室的所在，坐下来缓气。司机他认得，是欧伯佐恩雇用的三名司机之一；那天早晨，华盛顿先生还见过其中一个。

===Original===
The journey back to town was a long one, though the machine, for a public vehicle, was faster than most. Gurther welcomed the ride. Once more he had failed, and he reasoned that this last failure was the most serious of all. The question of Oberzohn’s displeasure did not really arise. He had travelled far beyond the point when the Swede’s disapproval meant very much to him. But there might be a consequence more serious than any. He knew well with what instructions Pfeiffer had been primed on the night of the attack at Rath House﻿—only Gurther had been quicker, and his snake had bitten first. Dr. Oberzohn had no illusions as to what had happened, and if he had tactfully refrained from making reference to the matter, he had his purpose and reasons. And this night journey with Elijah Washington was one of them.

===Chinese===
回城的一路漫长，不过以一辆公用车辆而论，这部机器已算跑得快的。格瑟乐得坐这一程。他又失了一回手，而他细想下来，这最后一回的失手，是历次之中最要命的。欧伯佐恩会否见怪，这个问题其实已不成其为问题——他早已走过了那道坎，瑞典人的不悦对他再没有多大分量。但后果也许比哪一次都严重。他心里透亮：围攻拉思府那夜，法伊弗领受的是怎样的嘱托——只是格瑟手脚更快，他的蛇先咬了下去。事情究竟如何，欧伯佐恩博士心里没有半点错觉；他若识趣地绝口不提，自有他的用意和缘由。同伊莱贾·华盛顿的这一趟夜间同行，便是其一。

===Original===
There was no excuse; he had none to offer. His hand wandered beneath the dress to the long knife that was strapped to his side, and the touch of the worn handle was very reassuring. For the time being he was safe; until another man was found to take Pfeiffer’s place Oberzohn was working single-handed and could not afford to dispense with the services of this, the last of his assassins.

===Chinese===
托词是没有的；他也拿不出什么托词。他一只手从裙裾底下摸过去，摸到缚在腰际的那把长刀，磨旧了的刀柄一上手，便令人安心。眼下他还不愁自身安全：在寻得接替法伊弗的人之前，欧伯佐恩是孤身一人做事，可舍不得撵走这最后一名刺客的效力。

===Original===
It was past eleven when he dismissed the taxi at the end of the long lane, and, following the only safe path, came to the unpainted door that gave admission to Oberzohn’s property. And the first words of his master told him that there was no necessity for explanation.

“So you did not get him, Gurther?”

“No, Herr Doktor.”

===Chinese===
十一点已过，他在长巷尽头打发了出租汽车，循着唯一稳妥的路径，来到那扇没上过漆的门前——由此进门，便是欧伯佐恩的产业。主人的第一句话便告诉他：解释大可不必。

「这么说，你没办成他，格瑟？」

「没办成，博士先生。」

===Original===
“I should not have sent you.” Oberzohn’s voice was extraordinarily mild in all the circumstances. “That man you cannot kill﻿—with the snake. I have learned since you went that he was bitten at the blind man’s house, yet lives! That is extraordinary. I would give a lot of money to test his blood. You tried the knife?”

“*Ja*, Herr Doktor.” He lifted his veil, stripped off hat and wig in one motion. The rouged and powdered face was bruised; from under the brown wig was a trickle of dried blood.

“Good! You have done as well as you could. Go to your room, Gurther﻿—march!”

===Chinese===
「我本不该派你去。」在此情此势之下，欧伯佐恩的嗓音温和得出奇。「那个人，你杀不死——用蛇杀不死。你走之后我得知，他在那盲人家里被咬过，居然还活着！这真是非同小可。我肯出一大笔钱，去验一验他的血。你试过刀子了？」

「*Ja*, Herr Doktor.」他掀起面纱，一手把帽子和假发齐齐褪了下来。那张涂了脂粉、抹了胭脂的脸带着淤伤；棕色假发底下，一缕干了的血迹。

「好！你已尽了力。回你的房间去，格瑟——开步走！」

===Original===
Gurther went upstairs and for a quarter of an hour was staring at his grinning face in the glass, as with cream and soiled towel he removed his makeup.

===Chinese===
格瑟上了楼，对着镜中自己那张龇牙嬉笑的脸足足瞪视了一刻钟，一面用冷霜和一条脏毛巾卸去脸上的妆。

===Original===
Oberzohn’s very gentleness was a menace. What did it portend? Until that evening neither Gurther nor his dead companion had been taken into the confidence of the two men who directed their activities. He knew there were certain papers to be recovered; he knew there were men to be killed; but what value were the papers, or why death should be directed to this unfortunate or that, he neither knew nor cared. His duty had been to obey, and he had served a liberal paymaster well and loyally. That girl in the underground room? Gurther had many natural explanations for her imprisonment. And yet none of them fitted the conditions. His cogitations were wasted time. That night, for the first time, the doctor took him into his confidence.

===Chinese===
欧伯佐恩这份和气本身便是一种威胁。它预示着什么？直到那天晚上，无论格瑟还是他那位亡故的同伴，都从未被指挥他们行动的那两个人引为心腹。他知道有几份文书要追回；他知道有几个人要杀掉；可那些文书价值何在、死亡为何偏偏派到这个倒霉蛋或那个倒霉蛋头上，他既不知道，也不在意。他的本分就是服从，而他也尽心尽忠地侍奉了一位出手阔绰的东家。地下屋子里的那个姑娘？对她的被囚，格瑟本想得出许多顺理成章的解释。然而没有一条对得上眼下的情形。他这番苦思冥想，纯属白费工夫。就在那一夜，博士破天荒头一遭向他交了底。

===Original===
He had finished dressing and was on his way to his kitchen when the doctor stood at the doorway and called him in.

“Sit down, Gurther.” He was almost kind. “You will have a cigar? These are excellent.”

He threw a long, thin, black cheroot, and Gurther caught it between his teeth and seemed absurdly pleased with his trick.

===Chinese===
他换好衣裳，正往他借宿的厨房去，博士已站在门口唤他进屋。

「坐下，格瑟。」他几乎是和气的。「来一支雪茄？这烟好得很。」

他抛过来一支细长的黑色方头雪茄，格瑟张口用牙接住，对这一手把戏显得乐不可支，傻气十足。

===Original===
“The time has come when you must know something, Gurther,” said the doctor. He took a fellow to the weed the man was smoking, and puffed huge clouds of rank smoke into the room. “I have for a friend﻿—who? Heir Newton?” He shrugged his shoulders. “He is a very charming man, but he has no brains. He is the kind of man, Gurther, who would live in comfort, take all we gave him by our cleverness and industry, and never say thank you! And in trouble what will he do, Gurther? He will go to the police﻿—yes, my dear friend, he will go to the police!”

He nodded. Gurther had heard the same story that night when he had crept soft-footed to the door and had heard the doctor discuss certain matters with the late Mr. Pfeiffer.

===Chinese===
「时候到了，你必得知道些事了，格瑟。」博士说。他给自己也取了一支与那人正抽的同款，朝屋里喷出一团团呛人的浓烟。「我有一位朋友——是谁呢？牛顿先生？」他耸了耸肩。「他是个非常讨人喜欢的男人，可惜没有脑子。他这种人，格瑟，日子要过得舒舒服服，凭咱们的聪明和勤勉挣来的东西他一概照单全收，却从不说一声谢！等到大难临头他会怎么做，格瑟？他会去找警察——是的，我亲爱的朋友，他会去找警察！」

他点了点头。那天夜里格瑟蹑手蹑脚摸到门外，听博士同已故的法伊弗先生商议某些事项，听的正是这一套。

===Original===
“He would, without a wink of his eyelash, without a snap of his hand, send you and me to death, and would read about our execution with a smile, and then go forth and eat his plum-pudding and roast beef! That is our friend Heir Newton! You have seen this with your own eyes?”

“*Ja*, Herr Doktor!” exclaimed the obedient Gurther.

===Chinese===
「他会眼睫毛也不眨一下、手指也不捻一下，就把你我送去送死，还会笑吟吟地读着咱们伏法的消息，然后出门去吃他的李子布丁和烤牛肉！咱们这位牛顿先生便是如此！这些你是亲眼见识过的？」

「*Ja*, Herr Doktor!」乖顺的格瑟应声喊道。

===Original===
“He is a danger for many reasons,” Oberzohn proceeded deliberately. “Because of these three men who have so infamously set themselves out to ruin me, who burnt down my house, and who whipped you, Gurther﻿—they tied you up to a post and whipped you with a whip of nine tails. You have not forgotten, Gurther?”

“*Nein*, Herr Doktor.” Indeed, Gurther had not forgotten, though the vacant smirk on his face might suggest that he had a pleasant memory of the happening.

===Chinese===
「他是个危险人物，缘由多多。」欧伯佐恩不慌不忙地说下去。「还因为有那三个人，那么歹毒地存心要毁掉我，烧了我的宅子，还鞭打了你，格瑟——他们把你捆在柱子上，用一条九尾鞭抽你。你没有忘吧，格瑟？」

「*Nein*, Herr Doktor.」的确，格瑟没有忘，虽然他脸上那副茫然含笑的神气，倒像是对这桩事存着一段愉快的回忆似的。

===Original===
“A fool in an organization,” continued the doctor oracularly, “is like a bad plate on a ship, or a weak link in a chain. Let it snap, and what happens? You and I die, my dear Gurther. We go up before a stupid man in a white wig and a red cloak, and he hands us to another man who puts a rope around our necks, drops us through a hole in the ground﻿—all because we have a stupid man like Herr Montague Newton to deal with.”

“*Ja*, Herr Doktor,” said Gurther as his master stopped. He felt that this comment was required of him.

===Chinese===
「一个组织里若混进蠢材，」博士俨然神谕附体般接着说，「就好比船身上一块劣质钢板，链条上一个脆弱的环节。一旦崩断，会怎么样？你我便都没命了，我亲爱的格瑟。咱们被押到一个头戴白色假发、身披红袍的蠢货跟前，他把咱们交给另一个人，那人往咱们脖子上套一条绳索，把咱们从地上的一个窟窿里放下去——全都因为咱们要对付的是蒙塔古·牛顿先生这样一个蠢货。」

「*Ja*, Herr Doktor.」主人住口时，格瑟应道。他觉得这时候理应有此一应。

===Original===
“Now, I will tell you the whole truth.” The doctor carefully knocked off the ash of his cigar into the saucer of his cup. “There is a fortune for you and for me, and this girl that we have in the quiet place can give it to us. I can marry her, or I can wipe her out, so! If I marry her, it would be better, I think, and this I have arranged.”

And then, in his own way, he told the story of the hill of gold, concealing nothing, reserving nothing﻿—all that he knew, all that Villa had told him.

===Chinese===
「现在，我把全部真相讲给你听。」博士小心翼翼地把雪茄灰弹进茶杯的托碟里。「有一笔大富贵等着你我，咱们藏在那个清净地方的姑娘，便能把它交到咱们手上。我可以娶她，也可以把她抹掉，就这么着！若娶了她，我想会更好些，这一层我已安排停当。」

随后，他用他自己的方式，把那座金山的故事讲了一遍，无所隐瞒，无所保留——他知道的全部，维拉告诉他的一切。

===Original===
“For three-four days now she must be here. At the end of that time nothing matters. The letter to Lisbon﻿—of what value is it? I was foolish when I tried to stop it. She has made no nominee, she has no heirs, she has known nothing of her fortune, and therefore is in no position to claim the renewal of the concession.”

===Chinese===
「如今她还须在这里待上三四天。过了这个期限，一切都不打紧了。那封寄往里斯本的信——它有什么价值？我先前想法子拦它，实在愚蠢。她没有指定过受益人，她没有继承人，她对这笔家财一无所知，因此根本无从主张租契的续展。」

===Original===
“Herr Doktor, will you graciously permit me to speak?”

The doctor nodded.

“Does the Newton know this?”

“The Newton knows all this,” said the doctor.

“Will you graciously permit me to speak again, Herr Doktor? What was this letter I was to have taken, had I not been overcome by misfortune?”

Oberzohn examined the ceiling.

===Chinese===
「博士先生，可否开恩准我说一句话？」

博士点了点头。

「这位牛顿知道这些吗？」

「这位牛顿全都知道。」博士说。

「可否再开恩准我说一句，博士先生？倘若不是我遭了厄运，我原该取走的那封信，究竟是怎样一封信？」

欧伯佐恩细细端详起天花板来。

===Original===
“I have thought this matter from every angle,” he said, “and I have decided thus. It was a letter written by Gonsalez to the Secretary or the Minister of the Colonies, asking that the renewal of the concession should be postponed. The telegram from my friend at the Colonial Office in Lisbon was to this effect.” He fixed his glasses, fumbled in his waistcoat and took out the three-page telegram. “I will read it to you in your own language﻿—

> “ ‘Application has been received from Leon Gonsalez, asking His Excellency to receive a very special letter which arrives in two days. The telegram does not state the contents of the letter, but the Minister has given orders for the messenger to be received. The present Minister is not favourable to concessions granted to England or Englishmen.’ ”

He folded the paper.

===Chinese===
「这件事我从各个角度都想过了。」他说，「我已这样定了案。那是冈萨雷斯写给殖民地部秘书或部长的一封信，请求将租契的续展予以展缓。我在里斯本殖民地部的那位朋友打来的电报，大意正是如此。」他扶正眼镜，在马甲里摸索一番，取出那份三页的电文。「我用你的母语念给你听——

> 「『兹悉莱昂·冈萨雷斯提出申请，请求阁下接收一封两日后送达之特急函件。电报未陈明信件内容，惟部长已谕令接待信使。现任部长不倾向于将租契授予英国或英国人。』」

他把纸折了起来。

===Original===
“Which means that there will be no postponement, my dear Gurther, and this enormous fortune will be ours.”

Gurther considered this point and for a moment forgot to smile, and looked what he was in consequence: a hungry, discontented wolf of a man.

===Chinese===
「这就是说，展缓是断然不会有的了，我亲爱的格瑟，这笔天大的富贵便将归咱们所有。」

格瑟把这一点掂量了一番，一时竟忘了微笑，露出了他在这般情形下的本相：一条饥饿而心怀不满的恶狼。

===Original===
“Herr Doktor, graciously permit me to ask you a question?”

“Ask,” said Oberzohn magnanimously.

“What share does Herr Newton get? And if you so graciously honoured me with a portion of your so justly deserved gains, to what extent would be that share?”

The other considered this, puffing away until the room was a mist of smoke.

“Ten thousand English pounds,” he said at last.

“Gracious and learned doctor, that is a very small proportion of many millions,” said Gurther gently.

===Chinese===
「博士先生，可否开恩准我问一句话？」

「问吧。」欧伯佐恩宽宏大量地说。

「牛顿先生得的是哪一份？倘蒙您开恩，从您那理所应得的巨额收益里赏赐我一星半点，那一份又会有多大？」

对方思忖起来，一口接一口地喷烟，喷得满屋子烟雾迷蒙。

「一万英镑。」他终于说。

「仁慈而博学的博士，这在千百万巨款之中，所占的比例未免太小了。」格瑟轻声说。

===Original===
“Newton will receive one half,” said the doctor, his face working nervously, “if he is alive. If misfortune came to him, that share would be yours, Gurther, my brave fellow! And with so much money a man would not be hunted. The rich and the noble would fawn upon him; he would have his lovely yacht and steam about the summer seas everlastingly, huh?”

Gurther rose and clicked his heels.

“Do you desire me again this evening?”

“No, no, Gurther.” The old man shook his head. “And pray remember that there is another day tomorrow, and yet another day after. We shall wait and hear what our friend has to say. Good night, Gurther.”

“Good night, Herr Doktor.”

===Chinese===
「牛顿将得一半，」博士说，脸神经质地抽动着，「只要他还活着。他若遭了不幸，那一份便归你，格瑟，我勇敢的家伙！有了这么多的钱，人就再不会被人追缉了。富人们、贵人们都要来奉承他；他会有他那条可爱的游艇，永永远远地荡漾在夏日的海上，嗯？」

格瑟站起身来，两脚跟咔的一声碰响。

「今晚您还用得着我吗？」

「不，不用了，格瑟。」老头摇了摇头。「还请你记住：明天另有一日，之后还有一日。咱们且等着，听听咱们这位朋友有什么说的。晚安，格瑟。」

「晚安，博士先生。」

===Original===
The doctor looked at the door for a long time after his man had gone and took up his book. He was deep in the chapter which was headed, in the German tongue: “The Subconscious Activity of the Human Intellect in Relation to the Esoteric Emotions.” To Dr. Oberzohn this was more thrilling than the most exciting novel.

===Chinese===
手下人走后，博士对着那扇门望了许久，才拿起他的书。他正读到深处——那一章的标题用德文写着：「人类智性之潜意识活动与神秘情感之关系」。对欧伯佐恩博士来说，这比最惊险的小说还要引人入胜。

## XVI In Captivity / 十六 身陷囹圄

===Original===

The second day of captivity dawned unseen, in a world that lay outside the brick roof and glazed white walls of Mirabelle Leicester’s prison-house. She had grown in strength and courage, but not so her companion. Joan, who had started her weary vigil with an almost cheerful gaiety, had sunk deeper and deeper into depression as the hours progressed, and Mirabelle woke to the sound of a woman’s sobs, to find the girl sitting on the side of her bed, her head in her wet hands.

===Chinese===

被囚的第二天破晓了，却无人得见——天亮在米拉贝尔·莱斯特那座囚牢的砖顶与釉面白墙之外的另一个世界里。她的气力和胆量都长进了，她的同伴却不然。琼当初开始这场令人倦怠的守望时，还带着几分近乎轻快的兴致；随着时辰一点点推移，她却愈陷愈深地坠入了消沉。米拉贝尔在一阵女人的抽泣声里醒来，只见那姑娘坐在床沿上，湿漉漉的双手捧着脑袋。

===Original===

“I hate this place!” she sobbed. “Why does he keep me here? God! If I thought the hound was double-crossing me﻿ ﻿… ! I’ll go mad if they keep me here any longer. I will, Leicester!” she screamed.

“I’ll make some tea,” said Mirabelle, getting out of bed and finding her slippers.

The girl sat throughout the operation huddled in a miserable heap, and by and by her whimpering got on Mirabelle’s nerves.

===Chinese===

「我恨死这个地方了！」她呜咽道。「他干吗把我扣在这儿？老天爷！我要是疑心那狗东西是在出卖我……！再把我关下去，我会疯的。真会的，莱斯特！」她尖声喊道。

「我去沏点茶。」米拉贝尔说着，下了床，摸到自己的拖鞋。

沏茶那一整套工夫里，那姑娘始终蜷作愁苦的一团坐着；渐渐地，她那抽抽噎噎的哭声搅得米拉贝尔心头发烦。

===Original===

“I don’t know why *you* should be wretched,” she said. “They’re not after *your* money!”

“You can laugh﻿—and how you can, I don’t know,” sobbed the girl, as she took the cup in her shaking hands. “I know I’m a fool, but I’ve never been locked up﻿—like this before. I didn’t dream he’d break his word. He swore he’d come yesterday. What time is it?”

“Six o’clock,” said Mirabelle.

It might as well have been eight or midday, for all she knew to the contrary.

===Chinese===

「我不明白，*你*有什么可愁的。」她说。「他们图的又不是*你的*钱！」

「你倒还笑得出来——你怎么笑得出来的，我可不明白。」姑娘双手哆哆嗦嗦地接过茶杯，抽泣着说。「我知道自己是个傻瓜，可我从没被关押过——这样子的关押从来没有过。我做梦也想不到他会食言。他赌过咒，说昨天一定来。这会儿几点了？」

「六点。」米拉贝尔说。

或许此刻已是八点、已是正午也未可知——她拿不出半点凭据说不是。

===Original===

“This is a filthy place,” said the hysterical girl. “I think they’re going to drown us all﻿ ﻿… or that thing will explode”﻿—she pointed to the green baize box﻿—“I know it! I feel it in my blood. That beast Gurther is here somewhere, ugh! He’s like a slimy snake. Have you ever seen him?”

“Gurther? You mean the man who danced with me?”

“That’s he. I keep telling you who he is,” said Joan impatiently. “I wish we could get out of here.”

===Chinese===

「这地方脏透了。」那歇斯底里的姑娘说。「我看哪，他们是要把咱们全都淹死……不然就是那玩意儿要炸了」——她指着那只绿呢箱子——「我知道！我这血里感觉得出来。那头畜生格瑟就在这附近什么地方，呸！他活像条黏糊糊的蛇。你见过他没有？」

「格瑟？你说的是跟我跳过舞的那个人？」

「就是他。我一再告诉过你他是什么人。」琼不耐烦地说。「真恨不得能离开这儿。」

===Original===

She jumped up suddenly.

“Come and see if you can help me lift the trap.”

Mirabelle knew it was useless before she set forth on the quest for freedom. Their united efforts failed to move the stone, and Joan was on the point of collapse when they came back to their sleeping-room.

===Chinese===

她霍地跳了起来。

「来，看看你能不能帮我抬那扇活板门。」

这场通向自由的探索还没上路，米拉贝尔便知道是无用功。两人合力也没能挪动那块石板；回到卧房时，琼已经濒临瘫倒。

===Original===

“I hope Gurther doesn’t know that those men are friends of yours,” she said, when she became calmer.

“You told me that yesterday. Would that make any difference?”

“A whole lot,” said Joan vehemently. “He’s got the blood of a fish, that man! There’s nothing he wouldn’t do. Monty ought to be flogged for leaving us here at his mercy. I’m not scared of Oberzohn﻿—he’s old. But the other fellow dopes, and goes stark, staring mad at times. Monty told me one night that he was﻿—” she choked﻿—“a killer. He said that these German criminals who kill people are never satisfied with one murder, they go on and on until they’ve got twenty or thirty! He says that the German prisons are filled with men who have the murder habit.”

===Chinese===

「但愿格瑟不知道那几个人是你的朋友。」她平静下来些后说。

「这话你昨天说过了。那会有什么分别吗？」

「分别可大了。」琼激愤地说。「那人一腔血冷得跟鱼一样！天下就没有他干不出来的事。蒙蒂把咱们撂在这儿任他摆布，真该拖去挨鞭子。欧伯佐恩我不怕——他老了。可另外那一个要嗑药，时不时疯得两眼发直、见神见鬼。蒙蒂有一晚告诉我，他是个——」她噎了一下——「杀人凶手。他说，这些杀人的德国罪犯绝不肯满足于一桩命案，他们会一路杀下去，直杀到二十条、三十条人命才罢！他还说，德国的监狱里关满了有杀人瘾的人。」

===Original===

“He was probably trying to frighten you.”

“Why should he?” said the girl, with unreasonable anger. “And leave him alone! Monty is the best in the world. I adore the ground he walks on!”

Very wisely, Mirabelle did not attempt to traverse this view.

===Chinese===

「他多半是想吓唬吓唬你。」

「他干吗要吓唬我？」姑娘无端动起气来。「还有，不许你说他！蒙蒂是天底下顶好的人，连他脚底下踩的土我都爱！」

米拉贝尔甚是知趣，并不试图驳倒这个看法。

===Original===

It was only when her companion had these hysterical fits that fear was communicated to her. Her faith was completely and wholeheartedly centred on the three men﻿—upon Gonsalez. She wondered how old he was. Sometimes he looked quite young, at others an elderly man. It was difficult to remember his face; he owed so much to his expression, the smile in his eyes, to the strange, boyish eagerness of gesture and action which accompanied his speech. She could not quite understand herself; why was she always thinking of Gonsalez, as a maid might think of a lover? She went red at the thought. He seemed so apart, so aloof from the ordinary influences of women. Suppose she had committed some great crime and had escaped the vigilance of the law, would he hunt her down in the same remorseless, eager way, planning to cut off every avenue of her escape until he shepherded her into a prison cell? It was a horrible thought, and she screwed up her eyes tight to blot out the mental picture she had made.

===Chinese===

只有同伴这样歇斯底里发作的时候，恐惧才会传到她身上。她的信赖完完全全、一心一意地系在那三个人身上——系在冈萨雷斯身上。她揣摩着他有多大年纪。有时他显得相当年轻，有时又像个上了年纪的人。他那张脸很难记得住；脸的模样多半全仗着他的神情——眼里含着的笑意，还有他说话时伴随着的那种古怪的、少年人似的热切手势与动作。她有点儿弄不懂自己：为什么她总在想着冈萨雷斯，像一个少女想着情人那样？想到这里她脸红了。他显得那样超然物外，对女人们惯常的那点影响无动于衷。设若她犯下过一桩大罪、逃脱了法律的耳目，他会不会也用同样毫不容情、兴头十足的法子追猎她，筹算着切断她一条条退路，直到把她像赶羊似的赶进一间牢房？这念头委实可怕，她紧紧闭起双眼，要把脑海里自己描出的那幅图景挤出去。

===Original===

It would have given her no ordinary satisfaction to have known how often Gonsalez’s thoughts strayed to the girl who had so strangely come into his life. He spent a portion of his time that morning in his bedroom, fixing to the wall a large railway map which took in the south of England and the greater part of the Continent. A red-ink line marked the route from London to Lisbon, and he was fixing a little green flag on the line just south of Paris when Manfred strolled into the room and surveyed his work.

===Chinese===

她若知道，冈萨雷斯的思绪有多少回飘向这个如此古怪地闯进他生命里的姑娘，心里得到的快慰必定非同小可。那天上午，他在自己卧室里消磨了一段时光，往墙上钉一幅大铁路图，图幅涵盖英格兰南部与欧陆的大半。一条红墨水线标出伦敦至里斯本的路线；他正往线上巴黎稍南的一处钉一面小绿旗，曼弗雷德踱进房来，端详他的活计。

===Original===

“The Sud Express is about there,” he said, pointing to the last of the green flags, “and I think our friend will have a fairly pleasant and uneventful journey as far as Valladolid﻿—where I have arranged for Miguel Garcia, an old friend of mine, to pick him up and shadow him on the westward journey﻿—unless we get the plane. I’m expecting a wire any minute. By the way, the Dieppe police have arrested the gentleman who tried to bump him overboard in mid-Channel, but the man who snatched at his portfolio at the Gare St. Lazare is still at liberty.”

“He must be getting quite used to it now,” said Manfred coolly, and laughed to himself.

Leon turned.

“He’s a good fellow,” he said with quick earnestness. “We couldn’t have chosen a better man. The woman on the train, of course, was Gurther. He is the only criminal I’ve ever known who is really efficient at disguising himself.”

===Chinese===

「『南方快车』大约走到了这儿。」他指着最末一面绿旗说。「我想咱们那位朋友到巴利亚多利德为止的这一程，会走得相当惬意、波澜不惊——我在那儿安排了米格尔·加西亚（Miguel Garcia），我的一位老朋友，去接应他，在西去的路上盯他的梢——除非咱们那架飞机办成了。电报随时会来，我正等着。顺带说一句，迪耶普警方已逮捕了那位在海峡中途想把他顶下海去的先生，可在圣拉扎尔车站（Gare St. Lazare）抢他公文夹的那位，眼下仍逍遥法外。」

「他如今对这一套想必相当习惯了。」曼弗雷德淡淡地说，自顾自笑了笑。

莱昂转过身来。

「他是条好汉。」他说得又急又恳切。「咱们再也挑不出更好的人选了。火车上那个女人，当然是格瑟。我识得的罪犯里，唯有他一个，易容改扮是真有两下子的。」

===Original===

Manfred lit his pipe; he had lately taken to this form of smoking. “The case grows more and more difficult every day. Do you realize that?”

Leon nodded. “And more dangerous,” he said. “By the laws of average, Gurther should get one of us the next time he makes an attempt. Have you seen the papers?”

Manfred smiled.

“They’re crying for Meadows’ blood, poor fellow! Which shows the extraordinary inconsistency of the public. Meadows has only been in one snake case. They credit him with having fallen down on the lot.”

===Chinese===

曼弗雷德点起烟斗；他近来迷上了这一路吸烟的做派。「这案子一天比一天棘手。你意识到没有？」

莱昂点了点头。「还更凶险。」他说。「照平均律来算，格瑟下回再动手，也该轮上咱们中的一个了。报纸你看了吗？」

曼弗雷德微微一笑。

「他们正嚷嚷着要米多斯的血呢，可怜的家伙！这可见公众出奇地前后不一。米多斯只经手过一桩『毒蛇』案，他们倒把整串案子的失手都记在他账上。」

===Original===

“They seem to be in remarkable agreement that the snake deaths come into the category of wilful murder,” said Gonsalez as they went down the stairs together.

Meadows had been talking to the reporters. Indeed, that was his chief offence from the viewpoint of the official mind. For the first article in the code of every well-constituted policeman is, “Thou shalt not communicate to the Press.”

===Chinese===

「他们仿佛意见出奇地一致，都认定这些毒蛇致死案该归入蓄意谋杀一类。」冈萨雷斯说。两人一同下了楼。

米多斯一直在跟记者们讲话。说实在的，在官场脑筋看来，这正是他的头一条罪过。因为每一个身心健全的警察，其守则里的第一诫便是：「不可向报界走漏风声。」

===Original===

Leon strolled aimlessly about the room. He was wearing his chauffeur’s uniform, and his hands were thrust into the breeches pockets. Manfred, recognizing the symptoms, rang the bell for Poiccart, and that quiet man came from the lower regions.

“Leon is going to be mysterious,” said Manfred dryly.

“I’m not really,” protested Leon, but he went red. It was one of his most charming peculiarities that he had never forgotten how to blush. “I was merely going to suggest that there’s a play running in London that we ought to see. I didn’t know that *The Ringer* was a play until this morning, when I saw one of Oberzohn’s more genteel clerks go into the theatre, and, being naturally of an inquisitive turn of mind, followed him. A play that interests Oberzohn will interest me, and should interest you, George,” he said severely, “and certainly should interest Meadows﻿—it is full of thrilling situations! It is about a criminal who escapes from Dartmoor and comes back to murder his betrayer. There is one scene which is played in the dark, that ought to thrill you﻿—I’ve been looking up the reviews of the dramatic critics, and as they are unanimous that it is not an artistic success, and is, moreover, wildly improbable, it ought to be worth seeing. I always choose an artistic success when I am suffering from insomnia,” he added cruelly.

===Chinese===

莱昂在屋里漫无目的地踱步。他穿着他那身司机制服，双手插在马裤口袋里。曼弗雷德看出了这些征候，摇铃唤波伊卡特；那位安静的先生从楼下上来了。

「莱昂又要故弄玄虚了。」曼弗雷德干巴巴地说。

「我哪是真卖关子。」莱昂分辩道，脸却红了。他有个顶可爱的癖性：从来不忘掉该怎么脸红。「我不过是想提个醒，伦敦眼下正演一出咱们该看的戏。直到今天早晨我才知道《敲钟人》（*The Ringer*）原是一出戏——我瞧见欧伯佐恩商行里一名比较斯文的职员走进那家戏院，我天生一副爱打听的脑筋，便跟了进去。欧伯佐恩感兴趣的戏，我就感兴趣，你乔治也理当感兴趣。」他正色道，「米多斯就更该感兴趣——满台都是惊心动魄的场面！讲的是一个罪犯从达特穆尔监狱（Dartmoor）脱逃，回来杀掉出卖他的人。有一场戏通场在黑暗里演，保管看得你心惊肉跳——我把戏剧评论家们的剧评都翻查过了，既然他们异口同声断定它算不上艺术上的成功之作，况且情节又荒诞不经，那就必定值一看。我犯失眠症的时候，专挑艺术上的成功之作看。」他又刻薄地补了一句。

===Original===

“Oberzohn is entitled to his amusements, however vulgar they may be.”

“But this play isn’t vulgar,” protested Leon, “except in so far as it is popular. I found it most difficult to buy a seat. Even actors go to see the audience act.”

“What seat did he buy?”

===Chinese===

「欧伯佐恩自有他消遣的权利，任凭那消遣多么俗气。」

「可这出戏并不俗气。」莱昂抗辩道，「要说俗，也只俗在它太叫座。我想买张座票，竟是难上加难。连演员们都跑去看观众演戏呢。」

「他买的是什么座？」

===Original===

“Box A,” said Leon promptly, “and paid for it with real money. It is the end box on the prompt side﻿—and before you ask me whence I gained my amazing knowledge of theatrical technique, I will answer that even a child in arms knows that the prompt side is the left-hand side facing the audience.”

“For tonight?”

Leon nodded.

“I have three stalls,” he said and produced them from his pocket. “If you cannot go, will you give them to the cook? She looks like a woman who would enjoy a good cry over the sufferings of the tortured heroine. The seats are in the front row, which means that you can get in and out between the acts without walking on other people’s knees.”

===Chinese===

「A 包厢。」莱昂应声答道，「而且是掏的真金白银。那是提词一侧的端头包厢——趁你还没问起，我这身戏剧学问的惊人来历打哪儿得来，我先自己招了：连怀里的娃娃都知道，提词一侧就是面对观众时的左手那一边。」

「是今晚的？」

莱昂点了点头。

「我弄到三张正厅票。」他说着，从口袋里掏了出来。「你们若是去不成，就把票送给厨娘好不好？她瞧着像那种肯为受尽折磨的女主角好好哭上一场的女人。座位在前排，这就是说，幕间进出，用不着踩着旁人的膝盖挤进挤出。」

===Original===

“Must I go?” asked Poiccart plaintively. “I do not like detective plays, and I hate mystery plays. I know who the real murderer is before the curtain has been up ten minutes, and that naturally spoils my evening.”

“Could you not take a girl?” asked Leon outrageously. “Do you know any who would go?”

“Why not take Aunt Alma?” suggested Manfred, and Leon accepted the name joyously.

===Chinese===

「我非去不可吗？」波伊卡特哀声问。「我不喜欢侦探戏，我讨厌悬疑戏。大幕升起不出十分钟，我就知道真凶是谁，这一来，我的夜晚自然就败了兴。」

「你就不能带个姑娘去？」莱昂促狭地问。「你认得肯去的姑娘吗？」

「何不带阿尔玛姨妈去？」曼弗雷德提议；莱昂欢天喜地地采纳了这名字。

===Original===

Aunt Alma had come to town at the suggestion of the Three, and had opened up the Doughty Court flat.

“And really she is a remarkable woman, and shows a steadiness and a courage in face of the terrible position of our poor little friend, which is altogether praiseworthy. I don’t think Mirabelle Leicester is in any immediate danger. I think I’ve said that before. Oberzohn merely wishes to keep her until the period of renewal has expired. How he will escape the consequences of imprisoning her, I cannot guess. He may not attempt to escape them, may accept the term of imprisonment which will certainly be handed out to him, as part of the payment he must pay for his millions.”

===Chinese===

阿尔玛姨妈是应三位义士之议进的城，已把道蒂庭院那套公寓开了起来。

「说实在的，她是位了不起的女人。面对咱们那位可怜的小朋友身处的可怕境地，她那份沉着与勇气，委实值得称道。我看米拉贝尔·莱斯特眼下并没有迫在眉睫的危险。这话我想从前说过。欧伯佐恩不过是想把她扣到续展期限届满为止。至于他囚禁她一事日后怎么脱身，我猜不着。他也可能压根儿不打算脱身，索性认下必定会判到他头上的那段刑期，算作他为那几百万身家必须缴付的一部分代价。」

===Original===

“Suppose he kills her?” asked Poiccart.

For a second Leon’s face twitched.

“He won’t kill her,” he said quietly. “Why should he? We know that he has got her﻿—the police know. She is a different proposition from Barberton, an unknown man killed nobody knew how, in a public place. No, I don’t think we need cross that bridge, only﻿ ﻿…” He rubbed his hands together irritably. “However, we shall see. And in the meantime I’m placing a lot of faith in Digby, a shrewd man with a sense of his previous shortcomings. You were wise there, George.”

===Chinese===

「要是他杀了她呢？」波伊卡特问。

一瞬间，莱昂的脸抽搐了一下。

「他不会杀她。」他静静地说。「他为什么要杀？我们知道她在他手里——警方也知道。她跟巴伯顿不是一码事：巴伯顿是个无名之人，在一处公共场所，被人用谁也说不清的法子杀了。不，我想这道桥咱们还犯不着现在就过，只是……」他烦躁地搓着双手。「不过，走着瞧吧。这期间我把很大的指望押在迪格比身上——他精明，又记着自己上回的短处。你在那一着上头是明智的，乔治。」

===Original===

He was looking at the street through the curtains.

“Tittlemouse is at his post, the faithful hound!” he said, nodding towards the solitary taxicab that stood on the rank. “I wonder whether he expects﻿—”

Manfred saw a light creep into his eyes.

“Will you want me for the next two hours?” Leon asked quickly, and was out of the room in a flash.

===Chinese===

他正隔着窗帘望着街面。

「『小耗子』（Tittlemouse）在他的岗位上呢，这条忠心的猎犬！」他说着，朝候客位上那辆孤零零的出租车点了点头。「不知他料没料到——」

曼弗雷德看见一道亮光悄悄爬进他的眼睛。

「往后两个钟头，你们用不着我吧？」莱昂飞快地问，话音未落人已闪出了房门。

===Original===

Ten minutes later, Poiccart and George were talking together when they heard the street door close, and saw Leon stroll to the edge of the pavement and wave his umbrella. The taxi-driver was suddenly a thing of quivering excitement. He leaned down, cranked his engine, climbed back into his seat and brought the car up quicker than any taxicab driver had ever moved before.

“New Scotland Yard,” said Leon, and got into the machine.

The cab passed through the forbidding gates of the Yard and dropped him at the staff entrance.

===Chinese===

十分钟后，波伊卡特和乔治正说着话，忽听得临街大门一响，只见莱昂踱到人行道边，扬了扬他的伞。那出租车司机霎时成了一团兴奋得发颤的东西。他俯下身去摇引擎，爬回座位，把车开了过来——那速度之快，天底下还没有哪个出租车司机赶得上。

「新苏格兰场。」莱昂说，钻进了汽车。

车子驶过苏格兰场那两扇门禁森严的大门，把他放在了职员入口处。

===Original===

“Wait here,” said Leon, and the man shifted uncomfortably.

“I’ve got to be back at my garage﻿—” he began.

“I shall not be five minutes,” said Leon.

Meadows was in his room, fortunately.

===Chinese===

「在这儿等着。」莱昂说；那司机浑身不自在地挪了挪。

「我可得回我的车行去——」他开口道。

「我用不上五分钟。」莱昂说。

还算凑巧，米多斯正在他屋里。

===Original===

“I want you to pull in this man and give him a dose of the third degree you keep in this country,” said Leon. “He carries a gun; I saw that when he had to get down to crank up his cab in Piccadilly Circus. The engine stopped.”

“What do you want to know?”

“All that there is to be known about Oberzohn. I may have missed one or two things. I’ve seen him outside the house. Oberzohn employs him for odd jobs and occasionally he acts as the old man’s chauffeur. In fact, he drove the machine the day Miss Leicester lunched with Oberzohn at the Ritz-Carlton. He may not have a cabman’s licence, and that will make it all the easier for you.”

===Chinese===

「我要你把这个人抓进来，给他灌上一剂你们贵国留存的三级审讯。」莱昂说。「他随身带着枪；他在皮卡迪利广场（Piccadilly Circus）不得不下车摇车那回，我就瞧见了。发动机熄了火。」

「你想知道些什么？」

「凡是能打听到的关于欧伯佐恩的事。兴许有一两件我漏了。我见过他守在这宅子外头。欧伯佐恩雇他打零差，偶尔还充当那老头儿的司机。实际上，莱斯特小姐在丽思卡尔顿饭店同欧伯佐恩吃午饭那天，开车的正是他。他未必有出租司机的执照，那你就更好办了。」

===Original===

A few minutes later, a very surprised and wrathful man was marched into Cannon Row and scientifically searched. Leon had been right about the revolver; it was produced and found to be loaded, and his excuse that he carried the weapon as a protection following upon a recent murder of a cabdriver, had not the backing of the necessary permit. In addition﻿—and this was a more serious offence﻿—he held no permit from Scotland Yard to ply for hire on the streets, and his badge was the property of another man.

“Put him inside,” said Meadows, and went back to report to the waiting Leon. “You’ve hit the bull’s-eye first time. I don’t know whether he will be of any use to us, but I don’t despise even the smallest fish.”

Whilst he was waiting, Leon had been engaged in some quick thinking.

===Chinese===

几分钟后，一个惊愕万状、怒气冲天的人被押进坎农街（Cannon Row），挨了一通科学搜身。那把左轮，莱昂说得分毫不差；枪搜了出来，一看还上了膛。他托辞说，近来有出租车司机遭人谋杀，带枪只为防身——可这话没有必备的持枪证作后盾。此外——这条罪名更重——他没有苏格兰场发给的上街揽客执照，那枚号牌还是旁人的东西。

「把他关起来。」米多斯说，随即回去向候着的莱昂回话。「你头一枪就正中靶心。这人日后于咱们有没有用，我说不准，可便是最小的鱼，我也不敢小看。」

候着的当儿，莱昂一直在飞快地转着念头。

===Original===

“The man has been at Greenwich lately. One of my men saw him there twice, and I needn’t say that he was driving Oberzohn.”

“I’ll talk to him later and telephone you,” said Meadows, and Leon Gonsalez went back to Curzon Street, one large smile.

“You have merely exchanged a spy you know for a spy you don’t know,” said George Manfred, “though I never question these freakish acts of yours, Leon. So often they have a trick of turning up trumps. By the way, the police are raiding the Gringo Club in the Victoria Dock Road tonight, and they may be able to pick up a few of Mr. Oberzohn’s young gentlemen who are certain to be regular users of the place.”

===Chinese===

「这人新近到过格林尼治。我的人两回在那儿看见他——不用说，他载的是欧伯佐恩。」

「回头我再审他，再给你去电话。」米多斯说；莱昂·冈萨雷斯便回柯曾街去了，脸上挂着老大一个笑容。

「你不过是拿一个知根知底的暗探，换了一个来历不明的暗探。」乔治·曼弗雷德说，「不过你这些怪招我从来不去多问，莱昂。它们往往有股冷不丁大获全胜的本事。顺带说一句，今晚警方要查抄维多利亚船坞路（Victoria Dock Road）的格林戈俱乐部，说不定能捞上欧伯佐恩先生手下的几位年轻绅士——那地方他们准是常客。」

===Original===

The telephone bell rang shrilly, and Leon took up the receiver, and recognized Meadows’ voice.

“I’ve got a queer story for you,” said the inspector immediately.

“Did he talk?” asked the interested Leon.

“After a while. We took a fingerprint impression, and found that he was on the register. More than that, he is a ticket-of-leave man. As an ex-convict we can send him back to finish his unexpired time. I promised to say a few words for him, and he spilt everything. The most interesting item is that Oberzohn is planning to be married.”

===Chinese===

电话铃刺耳地响了起来；莱昂拿起听筒，听出是米多斯的声音。

「我这儿有桩离奇事讲给你听。」探长劈头就说。

「他招了？」莱昂饶有兴味地问。

过了一阵子才招。我们取了他的指纹，发现他登在案犯档案上。不止如此，他还是个领着假释证的人。既然是释囚，咱们可以把他送回去，把未满的刑期坐完。我答应替他讲几句好话，他就一五一十全倒了出来。顶有意思的一条是：欧伯佐恩正打算成亲。

===Original===

“To be married? Who is this?” asked Manfred, in surprise. “Oberzohn?”

Leon nodded.

“Who is the unfortunate lady?” asked Leon.

There was a pause, and then: “Miss Leicester.”

===Chinese===

「成亲？这是谁呀？」曼弗雷德惊问。「欧伯佐恩？」

莱昂点了点头。

「哪位是那不幸的女士？」莱昂问。

电话那头顿了一顿，然后道：「莱斯特小姐。」

===Original===

Manfred saw the face of his friend change colour, and guessed.

“Does he know when?” asked Leon in a different voice.

“No. The licence was issued over a week ago, which means that Oberzohn can marry any morning he likes to bring along his bride. What’s the idea, do you think?”

===Chinese===

曼弗雷德看见他朋友的脸变了颜色，心里也猜着了几分。

「他知不知道日子？」莱昂换了一种嗓音问。

「不知道。许可证是一周多前签发的，这就是说，欧伯佐恩哪天早晨高兴把新娘带来，哪天早晨就能完婚。依你看，他打的是什么主意？」

===Original===

“Drop in this evening and either I or George will tell you,” said Leon.

He put the telephone on the hook very carefully.

“That is a danger I had not foreseen, although it was obviously the only course Oberzohn could take. If he marries her, she cannot be called in evidence against him. May I see the book, George?”

===Chinese===

「今晚过来坐坐，我或者乔治讲给你听。」莱昂说。

他把听筒极其小心地挂回钩子上。

「这一层危险我未曾料到，尽管那明摆着是欧伯佐恩唯一可走的路。他若娶了她，便再不能传她出庭作不利于他的证言了。那本册子给我看看行吗，乔治？」

===Original===

Manfred unlocked the wall safe and brought back a small ledger. Leon Gonsalez turned the pages thoughtfully.

“Dennis﻿—he has done good work for us, hasn’t he?” he asked.

“Yes, he’s a very reliable man. He owes us, amongst other things, his life. Do you remember, his wife was﻿—”

===Chinese===

曼弗雷德打开墙内保险柜，取来一本小小的名册。莱昂·冈萨雷斯若有所思地翻动书页。

「丹尼斯（Dennis）——他替咱们办过好事的，是不是？」他问。

「是，他这人十分可靠。他欠咱们的，除了别样，还有他这条命。你还记得么，他的太太原本是——」

===Original===

“I remember.” Leon scribbled the address of a man who had proved to be one of the most trustworthy of his agents.

“What are you going to do?” asked Manfred.

“I’ve put Dennis on the doorstep of the Greenwich registrar’s office from nine o’clock in the morning until half-past three in the afternoon, and he will have instructions from me that, the moment he sees Oberzohn walk out of a cab with a lady, he must push him firmly but gently under the wheels of the cab and ask the driver politely to move up a yard.”

Leon in his more extravagantly humorous moods was very often in deadly earnest.

===Chinese===

「我记得。」莱昂草草写下一个地址——那人已被证明是他手下最靠得住的密探之一。

「你打算怎么办？」曼弗雷德问。

「我已派丹尼斯守在格林尼治登记处的门阶上，从早晨九点守到下午三点半。他会从我这里领到指令：一看见欧伯佐恩偕一位女士走下汽车，就必须坚决而温柔地把他推到车轮底下去，再客客气气地请司机把车往前挪上一码。」

莱昂越是兴头十足、诙谐到十二分的时候，往往越是正经八百、字字当真。

## XVII Mr. Newton’s Dilemma / 十七 牛顿先生的两难

===Original===
The most carefully guided streaks of luck may, in spite of all precautions, overflow into the wrong channel, and this had happened to Mr. Montague Newton, producing an evening that was financially disastrous and a night from which sleep was almost banished. He had had one of his little card parties; but whether it was the absence of Joan, and the inadequacy of her fluffy-haired substitute, or whether the wine had disagreed with one of the most promising victims, the result was the same. They had played chemin de fer, and the gilded pigeon, whose feathers seemed already to be ornamenting the headdress of Monty Newton, had been successful, and when he should have been signing cheques for large amounts, he was cashing his counters with a reluctant host.

===Chinese===
纵是引导得最细心周到的鸿运，任你百般提防，也难免漫出堤岸、灌错沟渠；蒙塔古·牛顿先生正撞上了这样的事，招来了一个破财遭灾的夜晚，和一夜几乎被撵走睡眠的黑暗。他照例办了一场小小的牌局；可不知是琼不在场、她那满头蓬松头发的替手不够格呢，还是酒水同一位最有指望宰到手的「肥羊」犯了冲，结果反正是一样。他们玩的是「铁路」牌（chemin de fer）；那只镀金的鸽子——瞧那光景，它的羽毛俨然已在装点蒙蒂·牛顿的头冠了——居然大获全胜；本该在这个时辰签出一张张大额支票的人，这会儿正向着一位一百个不情愿的东道主兑取筹码。

===Original===
The night started wrong with Joan’s substitute, whose name was Lisa. She had guided to the establishment, via an excellent dinner at Mero’s, the son of an African millionaire. Joan, of course, would have brought him alone, but Lisa, less experienced, had allowed a young-looking friend of the victim to attach himself to the party, and she had even expected praise for her perspicacity and enterprise in producing two birds for the stone which Mr. Newton so effectively wielded, instead of one.

===Chinese===
这一晚，打从琼的替手起就走了岔道，那替手名叫丽莎。她先在梅罗餐厅安排了一顿上好的晚餐，借这一餐把一位非洲百万富翁的儿子引进了那处场子。若换作琼，自然只会把他一个人带来；丽莎历练不足，竟容许「肥羊」的一个面相年轻的朋友自行黏上了牌局，她甚至还有意讨赏——只道自己独具眼力、锐意进取，给牛顿先生那块使得出神入化的石头，送来的鸟儿不是一只，倒是两只。

===Original===
Monty did not resent the presence of the newcomer, and rather took the girl’s view, until he learnt that Lisa’s “find” was not, as she had believed, an officer of the Guards, but a sporting young lawyer with a large criminal practice, and one who had already, as a junior, conducted several prosecutions for the Crown. The moment his name was mentioned, Monty groaned in spirit. He was, moreover, painfully sober. His friend was not so favourably situated.

===Chinese===
蒙蒂起先并不嫌这位生客在场，反倒颇以为那姑娘的见识有理；直到听说丽莎「淘来」的这位，并非她以为的禁卫军军官，而是一个手里挂着一摊大宗刑案生意、性子又好赌的年轻律师——此人早年还以初级出庭律师的身份，替王室经办过好几桩公诉——名字一报出来，蒙蒂心里便暗暗叫苦。何况这人清醒得叫人绝望。他那位朋友的处境，倒没有这么「有利」了。

===Original===
That was the first of the awkward things to happen. The second was the bad temper of the player, who, when the bank was considerably over £3,000, had first of all insisted upon the cards being reshuffled, and then had gone banquo—the game being baccarat. Even this contretemps might have been overcome, but after he had expressed his willingness to “give it,” the card which Monty had so industriously palmed, slipped from his hand to the table, and though the fact was unnoticed by the players, the lawyer’s attention being diverted at the moment, it was impossible to recover that very valuable piece of pasteboard. And Monty had done a silly thing. Instead of staging an artistic exhibition of annoyance at remarks which the millionaire’s son had made, he decided to take a chance on the natural run of the cards. And he had lost. On top of that, the slightly inebriated player had decided that when a man had won a coup of £3,000 it was time to stop playing. So Monty experienced the mortification of paying out money, and accompanying his visitor to the door with a smile that was so genial and so full of good-fellowship that the young gentleman was compelled to apologize for his boorishness.

===Chinese===
这是当晚头一件别扭事。第二件，出在那位牌客的坏脾气上：庄上的赌本早已大大过了 £3,000，他却先一口咬定要把牌重洗一遍，随后又喊了一声「班科」（banco）——他们玩的这牌戏正是巴卡拉（baccarat）。就连这个岔子，原本也还过得去；可待他表示愿意「接这一注」，蒙蒂费了老大功夫藏进掌心的那张牌，竟从他手里滑脱，掉在了桌面上。牌客们谁也没瞧见——那位律师的注意力恰好一时岔开——可这张身价贵重的纸板，是无论如何也收不回来了。蒙蒂接着又干了件蠢事：百万富翁的儿子说了几句闲话，他本可以借题发挥、煞有介事地表演一场恼怒、就此散局，他却偏要赌一赌运气，任凭牌路自然而然地走下去。于是他输了。雪上加霜的是，那位带着三分醉意的牌客认定：一个人赢下一注 £3,000，就该见好就收。蒙蒂只得忍着满心屈辱往外掏钱，还满面春风地送客到门口，那笑容亲切至极、交情十足，倒逼得那位年轻绅士为自己的粗野赔了不是。

===Original===
“Come along some other night and give me my revenge,” said Monty.

“You bet I will! I’m going to South Africa tomorrow, but I shall be back early next year, and I’ll look you up.”

Monty watched him going down the steps and hoped he would break his neck.

===Chinese===
「改天晚上再来，让我报这一箭之仇。」蒙蒂说。

「那还用说！我明天就要去南非，不过明年一开年头就回来，到时候一定来找你。」

蒙蒂望着他走下台阶，心里巴望他摔断脖子。

===Original===
He was worried about Joan—more worried than he thought it possible for him to be about so light a girl. She was necessary to him in many ways. Lisa was a bungling fool, he decided, though he sent her home without hurting her feelings. She was a useful girl in many ways, and nothing spoils a tout quicker than constant nagging.

===Chinese===
他为琼心烦——烦心的程度，超出了他自料能为这么个轻浮姑娘所动的那条界线。她于他，许多方面都少不得。丽莎是个笨手笨脚的蠢货，他如此断定；不过送她回家时，倒半点没让她觉出委屈。这姑娘许多方面都得用，而要论毁掉一个揽客的托儿，什么都快不过成天的数落。

===Original===
He felt very lonely in the house, and wandered from room to room, irritated with himself that the absence of this featherbrained girl, who had neither the education nor the breed of his own class, should make such a big difference. And it did; he had to admit as much to himself. He hated the thought of that underground room. He knew something of her temperament, and how soon her experience would get on her nerves. In many respects he wished he did not feel that way about her, because she had a big shock coming, and it was probably because he foresaw this hurt, that he was anxious to make the present as happy as he could for her.

===Chinese===
他在这宅子里觉得说不出的孤单，从一间屋子踱到另一间屋子；叫他恼火的是他自己：不过是个既无他那一阶层的教养、也无那份血统的没头脑姑娘，不在跟前，竟会造成这么大的差别。差别确确实实存在；这一点他不能不向自己承认。他一想到那间地下室便觉得厌恶。她的性子他略知一二，也知道这段遭遇要多快就有多快地磨蚀她的神经。在许多方面，他倒但愿自己对她的心思不是这样；因为一场大刺激正在前头候着她，多半正因为预见了这份伤害，他才急着想让她眼下的日子过得尽量快活。

===Original===
After he had done what he was to do, there was no reason in the world why they should be bad friends, and he would give her a big present. Girls of that class soon forget their miseries if the present is large enough. Thus he argued, tossing from side to side in his bed, and all the time his thoughts playing about that infernal cellar. What she must be feeling! He did not worry at all about Mirabelle, because—well, she was a principal in the case. To him, Joan was the real victim.

===Chinese===
等他把该办的事办完，天底下再没有半点理由叫他们做不成朋友，他还要送她一份厚礼。那个阶层的姑娘，只要礼够重，转眼便把苦楚忘个干净。他躺在床上翻来覆去，就这样一条一条替自己圆着理；心思却始终绕着那座该死的地窖打转。她这会儿心里不知是什么滋味！至于米拉贝尔，他倒一丝儿也不挂心，因为——呃，她到底是本案的正主。在他看来，真正的受害人是琼。

===Original===
Sleep did not come until daybreak, and he woke in his most irritable frame of mind. He had promised the girl he would call and see her, though he had privately arranged with Oberzohn not to go to the house until the expiry of the five days.

===Chinese===
直到天蒙蒙亮他才睡着；一觉醒来，心境恶劣到了无以复加的地步。他答应过那姑娘要来看她，虽然他私下里同欧伯佐恩讲定，五天期满之前不进那所宅子。

===Original===
By lunchtime he could stand the worry no longer, and, ordering his car, drove to a point between New Cross and Bermondsey, walking on foot the remainder of the distance. Mr. Oberzohn expected the visit. He had a shrewd knowledge of his confederate’s mental outfit, and when he saw this well-dressed man picking a dainty way across the littered ground, he strolled out on the steps to meet him.

===Chinese===
挨到午饭时分，这桩心事他再也按捺不住，吩咐备车，一口气开到新克罗斯（New Cross）与伯蒙德赛（Bermondsey）之间的一处，剩下的路便靠两条腿走完。欧伯佐恩先生早料到有这次来访。他对同伙那副心智家当摸得透熟；望见这位衣着光鲜的人拣着下脚的地方、小心翼翼地穿过满地狼藉走来，便踱到台阶上迎他。

===Original===
“It is curious you should have come,” he said.

“Why didn’t you telephone?” growled Newton. This was his excuse for the visit.

“Because there are human machines at the end of every wire,” said Oberzohn. “If they were automatic and none could listen, but you and I, we would talk and talk and then talk! All day long would I speak with you and find it a pleasure. But not with Miss This and Miss That saying, ‘One moment, if you please,’ and saying to the Scotland Yard man, ‘Now you cut in’!”

===Chinese===
「你竟来了，真是稀奇。」他说。

「你干吗不打电话？」牛顿低吼道。这正是他此行预备好的托词。

「因为每一根电线的尽头，都坐着一架会喘气的机器。」欧伯佐恩说。「倘若它们是自动的，除你我之外谁也偷听不着，那咱们便要谈啊谈啊，谈了还要谈！我愿同你从早说到晚，还引为乐事。可架不住这位小姐、那位小姐一口一个『请稍等片刻』，又扭头对苏格兰场的人说：『现在，你接进来』！」

===Original===
“Is Gurther back?”

“Gurther is back,” said the doctor soberly.

“Nothing happened to that bird? At least, I saw nothing in the evening papers.”

“He has gone to Lisbon,” replied the doctor indifferently. “Perhaps he will get there, perhaps he will not—what does it matter? I should like to see the letter, because it is data, and data has an irresistible charm for a poor old scientist. You will have a drink?”

===Chinese===
「格瑟回来了么？」

「格瑟回来了。」博士正色道。

「那只鸟儿没出事吧？至少，晚报上我什么也没瞧见。」

「他上里斯本去了。」博士淡然答道。「兴许到得了，兴许到不了——有什么要紧？我倒很想看一看那封信，因为它是资料；对一位可怜的老科学家来说，资料自有一股无法抗拒的魅力。你要喝点什么？」

===Original===
Monty hesitated, as he always did when Oberzohn offered him refreshment. You could never be sure with Oberzohn.

“I’ll have a whisky,” he said at last, “a full bottle—one that hasn’t been opened. I’ll open it myself.”

The doctor chuckled unevenly.

“You do not trust?” he said. “I think you are wise. For who is there in this world of whom a man can say, ‘He is my friend. To the very end of my life I will have confidence in him’?”

===Chinese===
蒙蒂迟疑了一下——每逢欧伯佐恩请他用些饮品，他向来如此迟疑。跟欧伯佐恩打交道，你永远拿不准。

「我要威士忌。」他终于开口，「要一整瓶——没启过封的。我自己来开。」

博士干笑了几声，笑得断断续续。

「你不信任？」他说。「我想你是明智的。因为在这世上，还有哪一个人，能叫谁说得出口：『他是我的朋友。直到我此生的末一日，我对他都有信心』？」

===Original===
Monty did not feel that the question called for an answer.

He took the whisky bottle to the light, examined the cork and drove in the corkscrew.

“The soda water—that also might be poisoned,” said Dr. Oberzohn pleasantly.

===Chinese===
蒙蒂觉得这个问题不劳他回答。

他把威士忌瓶举到亮处，验过瓶塞，旋进开塞钻。

「苏打水——那个，兴许也有毒。」欧伯佐恩博士和颜悦色地说。

===Original===
At any other time he would not have made that observation. That he said it at all, betrayed a subtle but ominous change in their relationships. If Monty noticed this, he did not say a word, but filled his glass and sat down on the sofa to drink. And all the time the doctor was watching him interestedly.

===Chinese===
换了别的任何时候，这话他都不会说。这话居然出了口，便泄露出两人关系间一场微妙而不祥的变化。蒙蒂若有所觉察，也只字不提，只斟满杯子，坐到沙发上去喝。而博士自始至终，饶有兴味地注视着他。

===Original===
“Yes, Gurther is back. He failed, but you must excuse failure in a good man. The perfect agent has yet to be found, and the perfect principal also. The American, Washington, had left Paris when I last heard of him. He is to be congratulated. If I myself lived in Paris I should always be leaving. It is a frivolous city.”

===Chinese===
「是的，格瑟回来了。他失了手，可一个好人失手，你总得宽宥。十全十美的代理人还未曾问世，十全十美的主脑也一样。我最后听到的消息说，那个美国人，华盛顿，已离开巴黎。他值得道贺。倘使我本人住在巴黎，我便会永远处在离开之中。那是一座轻浮的城市。」

===Original===
Monty lit a cigar, and decided to arrive at the object of his visit by stages. For he had come to perform two important duties. He accounted as a duty a call upon Joan. No less was it a duty, and something of a relief also, to make his plan known to his partner.

===Chinese===
蒙蒂点上一支雪茄，决定一级一级、迂回着驶向此行的目的港。他这趟原是来办两件要差的。探望琼一趟，他算作一桩义务；把他的计划向合伙人和盘托出，同样是一桩义务——而且多少还是一桩宽慰。

===Original===
“How are the girls?” he asked.

“They are very happy,” said Dr. Oberzohn, who had not resumed his seat, but stood in an attitude somewhat reminiscent of Gurther, erect, staring, motionless. “Always my guests are happy.”

“In that dog-hole?” said the other contemptuously. “I don’t want Joan to be here.”

===Chinese===
「姑娘们都还好么？」他问。

「她们快活得很。」欧伯佐恩博士说。他未曾归座，只站在那里，那姿态颇叫人想起格瑟：笔挺、直瞪、纹丝不动。「我的客人从来都快活。」

「在那个狗窝里？」对方满含轻蔑地说。「我不愿意琼待在那种地方。」

===Original===
The Herr Doktor shrugged.

“Then take her away, my friend,” he said. “Why should she stay, if you are unhappy because this woman is not with you? She serves no purpose. Possibly she is fretting. By all means—I will bring her to you.” He moved to the door.

“Wait a moment,” said Monty. “I’ll see her later and take her out perhaps, but I don’t want her to be away permanently. Somebody ought to stay with that girl.”

===Chinese===
这位博士先生耸了耸肩。

「那就把她带走，我的朋友。」他说。「既然这女人不在你身边你便不痛快，她又何必留下？她派不上什么用场。说不定她正窝着火呢。尽管带走——我把她带来交你。」他举步朝门口走去。

「且慢。」蒙蒂说。「回头我自会见她，兴许还带她出去走走；可我不愿意她一去不返。总得有个人陪着那姑娘。」

===Original===
“Why? Am I not here?” asked Oberzohn blandly.

“You’re here, and Gurther’s here.” Monty was looking out of the window and did not meet the doctor’s eyes. “Especially Gurther. That’s why I think that Mirabelle Leicester should have somebody to look after her. Has it ever struck you that the best way out of this little trouble is—marriage?”

===Chinese===
「为什么？我不在此地么？」欧伯佐恩温温吞吞地问。

「你在此地，格瑟也在此地。」蒙蒂望着窗外，不与博士的目光相接。「尤其是格瑟。所以我才想，米拉贝尔·莱斯特身边总得有个人照应。你可曾想到过：摆脱这桩小小麻烦的最好出路——是结婚？」

===Original===
“I have thought that,” said the doctor. “You also have thought it? This is wonderful! You are beginning to think.”

The change of tone was noticeable enough now. Monty snapped round at the man who had hitherto stood in apparent awe of him and his judgments.

===Chinese===
「这一层，我想过。」博士说。「你也想到了？真是了不起！你开始会思想了。」

语气里的这番变化，如今已分明可辨。蒙蒂霍地转过身，直冲着那个人——此人在他面前，至今还一直貌似敬畏着他本人和他的裁断。

===Original===
“You can cut that sarcasm right out, Oberzohn,” he said, and, without preamble: “I’m going to marry that girl.”

Oberzohn said nothing to this.

“She’s not engaged; she’s got no love affairs at all. Joan told me, and Joan is a pretty shrewd girl. I don’t know how I’m going to fix it, but I guess the best thing I can do is to pretend that I am a real friend and get her out of your cellar. She’ll be so grateful that maybe she will agree to almost anything. Besides, I think I made an impression the first time I saw her. And I’ve got a position to offer her, Oberzohn: a house in the best part of London—”

===Chinese===
「你那套冷嘲热讽趁早收起来，欧伯佐恩。」他说；接着便开门见山：「我要娶那个姑娘。」

对此，欧伯佐恩一言不发。

「她没订过婚；连半段风流韵事也没有。琼告诉我的，琼是个相当有算计的姑娘。事情怎么撮合，我心里还没数；不过我想，我顶好的做法，是装成一个真心朋友，把她从你的地窖里搭救出去。她一感激，兴许什么都肯答应。再说，我头一回见她，想必已经给了她一个印象。而且我拿得出一份身份给她，欧伯佐恩：伦敦头等地段的一所宅子——」

===Original===
“My house,” interrupted Oberzohn’s metallic voice.

“Your house? Well, *our* house, let us say. We’re not going to quarrel about terms.”

“I also have a position to offer her, and I do not offer her any other man’s.”

===Chinese===
「我的宅子。」欧伯佐恩金属般的嗓音插了进来。

「你的宅子？唔，就算*咱们*的宅子吧。咱们犯不着为措辞伤和气。」

「我也有一份身份可以给她，而我给她的这份，不是从别的男人手里挪来的。」

===Original===
Oberzohn was looking at him wide-eyed, a comical figure; his elongated face seemed to stand out in the gloom like a pantomime mask.

“You?” Monty could hardly believe his ears.

“I, Baron Eruc Oberzohn.”

===Chinese===
欧伯佐恩瞪着一双圆眼望他，样子着实滑稽；那张拉长的脸在昏暗中凸现出来，活像一具哑剧面具。

「你？」蒙蒂几乎不信自己的耳朵。

「我，埃鲁克·欧伯佐恩男爵。」

===Original===
“A baron, are you?” The room shook with Monty’s laughter. “Why, you damned old fool, you don’t imagine she’d marry you, do you?”

Oberzohn nodded.

“She would do anythings what I tell her.” In his agitation his English was getting a little ragged. “A girl may not like a mans, but she might hate somethings worse—you understand? A woman says death is not’ing, but a woman is afeard of death, isn’t it?”

===Chinese===
「男爵，就凭你？」蒙蒂的笑声震得屋子直颤。「嘿，你这该死的老傻瓜，你总不至于真想她会嫁给你吧？」

欧伯佐恩点了点头。

「她是什么都会照我说的话做的。」他这一激动，英语便渐渐走了样。「姑娘家可以不喜欢一个男人，可她兴许更怕另一些东西——你懂么？女人嘴上说死不算一回事，女人心里可是怕死的，是不是？」

===Original===
“You’re crazy,” said Monty scornfully.

“I am crazy, am I? And a damned old fool also—yes? Yet I shall marry her.”

There was a dead silence, and then Oberzohn continued the conversation, but on a much calmer note.

===Chinese===
「你疯了。」蒙蒂语带鄙夷。

「我是疯了，是不是？还是个该死的老傻瓜——对么？可我终究要娶她。」

屋内死一般的静；随后欧伯佐恩重又续上话头，调子却平和了许多。

===Original===
“Perhaps I am what you call me, but it is not a thing worthy for two friends to quarrel. Tomorrow you shall come here, and we will discuss this matter like a business proposition, *hein*?”

Monty examined him as though he were a strange insect that had wandered into his ken.

===Chinese===
「兴许我正是你骂的那样东西，可这犯不上叫两个朋友拌嘴。明天你到这里来，咱们像议一桩买卖似的，把这件事议个明白，*hein*？」

蒙蒂端详他，如同端详一只误入他眼界的怪虫。

===Original===
“You’re not a Swede, you’re German,” he said. “That baron stuff gave you away.”

“I am from the Baltic, but I have lived many years in Sweden,” said Oberzohn shortly. “I am not German: I do not like them.”

More than this he would not say. Possibly he shared Gurther’s repugnance towards his sometime neighbours.

===Chinese===
「你不是瑞典人，你是德国人。」他说。「男爵那一套，露了你的馅。」

「我出自波罗的海，不过我在瑞典住了许多年。」欧伯佐恩简短地说。「我不是德国人：我不喜欢他们。」

再多的话他就不肯说了。多半他也怀着格瑟那份对旧日邻邦的憎恶。

===Original===
“We shall not quarrel, anyway,” he continued. “I am a fool, you are a fool, we are all fools. You wish to see your woman?”

“I wish to see Joan,” said Monty gruffly. “I don’t like that ‘your woman’ line of yours.”

“I will go get her. You wait.”

===Chinese===
「横竖咱们是不拌嘴的了。」他接着说。「我是傻瓜，你是傻瓜，咱们全是傻瓜。你想见你的女人？」

「我要见的是琼。」蒙蒂粗声粗气地说。「你那套『你的女人』的口吻，我不喜欢。」

「我去领她来。你等着。」

===Original===
Again the long boots came from under the table, were dragged on to the doctor’s awkward feet, and Monty watched him from the window as he crossed to the factory and disappeared.

===Chinese===
那双长靴又从桌子底下给拖了出来，套上博士那双笨拙的脚；蒙蒂立在窗前，望着他一路穿过去走进厂房，不见了。

===Original===
He was gone five minutes before he came out again, alone. Monty frowned. What was the reason for this?

“My friend,” panted Oberzohn, to whom these exertions were becoming more and more irksome, “it is not wise.”

===Chinese===
他进去了足足五分钟，才重新露面，而且是独自一人。蒙蒂皱起了眉。这是什么缘故？

「我的朋友，」欧伯佐恩喘着气说——这等奔波劳顿，于他正变得一日难过一日——「这样不聪明。」

===Original===
“I want to see her—” began Monty.

“Gently, gently; you shall see her. But on the canal bank Gurther has also seen a stranger, who has been walking up and down, pretending to fish. Who can fish in a canal, I ask you?”

“What is he to do with it?”

===Chinese===
「我要见她——」蒙蒂才起了头。

「轻声，轻声；你会见到她的。只是运河岸上，格瑟还望见了一个生人，在那儿走来踱去，假装钓鱼。我且问你，运河里能钓出什么鱼来？」

「他同这事有什么相干？」

===Original===
“Would it be wise to bring her in daylight, I ask you again? Do not the men think that your—that this girl is in Brussels?”

This had not occurred to Monty.

===Chinese===
「我再问你：大白天的把她领过来，聪明么？那些人岂不是都以为你的——以为这位姑娘在布鲁塞尔？」

这一层，蒙蒂倒不曾想到。

===Original===
“I have an idea for you. It is a good idea. The brain of old fool Oberzohn sometimes works remarkably. This morning a friend sent to me a ticket for a theatre. Now you shall take her tonight. There is always a little fog when the sun is setting and you can leave the house in a car. Presently I will send a man to attract this watcher’s attention, and then I will bring her to the house and you can call for her.”

===Chinese===
「我为你想了一个主意。一个好主意。老傻瓜欧伯佐恩的这颗脑袋，有时运转得出奇地好。今天早晨，一位朋友送来一张戏票。今晚你就带她去。日头沉西的时候总有一点薄雾，你们可以坐汽车离开此地。稍待片刻，我派一个人去引开那盯梢者的注意，随后我把她领到宅子里，你便来接她。」

===Original===
“I will wait for her.” Monty was dogged on this point.

And wait he did, until an hour later a half-crazy girl came flying into the room and into his arms.

Dr. Oberzohn witnessed the reunion unmoved.

“That is a pretty scene for me,” he said, “for one to be so soon married,” and he left them alone.

===Chinese===
「我就在这儿等她。」这一点上，蒙蒂执拗得很。

他也果真等了下去，直等到一个钟头之后，一个半疯的姑娘飞也似的闯进屋来，一头扑进他的怀里。

欧伯佐恩博士眼见这一场团聚，无动于衷。

「这光景于我真是好看得很，」他说，「于一个转眼便要成婚的人。」说罢，便撂下他们两人走了。

---

===Original===
“Monty, I can’t possibly go back to that beastly place tonight. She’ll have to stay by herself. And she’s not a bad kid, Monty, but she doesn’t know she’s worth a lot of money.”

===Chinese===
「蒙蒂，今晚我说什么也回不得那个鬼地方了。她只好独自待着了。她倒不是个坏丫头，蒙蒂，只是她自己不知道，她值着一大笔钱呢。」

===Original===
“Have you been talking to her?” he asked angrily. “I told you—”

“No, I’ve only just asked her a few questions. You can’t be in a poky hole like that, thrown together day and night, without talking, can you? Monty, you’re absolutely sure nothing can happen to her?”

===Chinese===
「你跟她讲话了？」他怒冲冲地问。「我不是叮嘱过你——」

「没有，我不过才问了她几个问题。两个人关在那么个憋屈的窟窿里，日夜滚在一处，总不能一句话不说吧，你说呢？蒙蒂，你能担保她绝对不会出事？」

===Original===
Monty cleared his throat.

“The worst thing that can happen to her,” he said, “is to get married.”

She opened her eyes at this.

“Does somebody want to marry her?”

===Chinese===
蒙蒂清了清喉咙。

「她所能遭上的最坏的事，」他说，「就是嫁人。」

她听了，睁大了眼睛。

「有人要娶她？」

===Original===
“Oberzohn,” he said.

“That old thing!” she scoffed.

Again he found a difficulty in speaking.

“I have been thinking it over, honey,” he said. “Marriage doesn’t mean a whole lot to anybody.”

===Chinese===
「欧伯佐恩。」他说。

「那件老东西！」她嗤之以鼻。

他又一次觉出了开口的艰难。

「我把这事前前后后想了一遍，宝贝儿。」他说。「婚姻这件事，对谁都说不上多要紧。」

===Original===
“It’ll mean a lot to me,” she said quietly.

“Suppose I married her?” he blurted.

“You!” She stepped back from him in horror.

“Only just a … well, this is the truth, Joan. It may be the only way to get her money. Now you’re in on this graft, and you know what you are to me. A marriage—a formal marriage—for a year or two, and then a divorce, and we could go away together, man and wife.”

===Chinese===
「对我要紧得很。」她低声说。

「倘若娶她的是我呢？」他脱口而出。

「你！」她惊骇地从他身边退开一步。

「只不过是个……唉，实话对你说了吧，琼。要弄到她那笔钱，这兴许是唯一的路。如今这桩买卖你既已入了伙，你也知道你在我心里占着什么位子。结一场婚——一场正正经经的婚——过上一两年，随后离婚，咱们便可以夫妻的名分，一道远走天涯。」

===Original===
“Is that what he meant?” She jerked her head to the door. “About ‘married so soon’?”

“He wants to marry her himself.”

“Let him,” she said viciously. “Do you think I care about money? Isn’t there any other way of getting it?”

===Chinese===
「他指的是这个么？」她朝门口猛一摆头。「什么『转眼便要成婚』？」

「他自己想娶她。」

「让他娶去。」她恶狠狠地说。「你以为我在乎钱？再没有别的法子弄到它了么？」

===Original===
He was silent. There were too many other ways of getting it for him to advance a direct negative.

“Oh, Monty, you’re not going to do that?”

“I don’t know what I’m going to do yet,” he said.

===Chinese===
他沉默了。弄钱的门路实在太多，他一时竟说不出口一句干脆的「不行」。

「哦，蒙蒂，你可别真要那么做？」

「我自己还不知道要怎么做呢。」他说。

===Original===
“But not that?” she insisted, clinging to him by his coat.

“We’ll talk about it tonight. The old man’s got us tickets for the theatre. We’ll have a bit of dinner up West and go on, and it really doesn’t matter if anybody sees us, because they know very well you’re not in Brussels. What is that queer scent you’ve got?”

===Chinese===
「可千万别是那样？」她攥住他的外衣，死不松口。

「今晚再谈。老头子给咱们弄到了戏票。咱们先上西区吃顿饭，然后看戏去。就算有人瞧见咱们，也全不打紧——他们心里明镜似的，你根本不在布鲁塞尔。你身上带着的这股怪香，是什么？」

===Original===
Joan laughed, forgetting for the moment the serious problem which faced her.

“Joss-sticks,” she said. “The place got so close and stuffy, and I found them in the pantry with the provisions. As a matter of fact, it was a silly thing to do, because we had the place full of smoke. It’s gone now, though. Monty, you do these crazy things when you’re locked up,” she said seriously. “I don’t think I can go back again.”

===Chinese===
琼笑了，一时忘掉了压在心头的那桩正经难题。

「线香。」她说。「那地方又闷又窒，我在食品间里搁口粮的地方翻到了几支。老实说，这事干得挺傻，弄得满屋子都是烟。好在这会儿散了。蒙蒂，人一给关起来，就会干出这些疯事来。」她正色说。「我看，我回不去了。」

===Original===
“Go back tomorrow,” he almost pleaded. “It’s only for two or three days, and it means a lot to me. Especially now that Oberzohn has ideas.”

“You’re not going to think any more about—about marrying her, are you?”

“We’ll talk of it tonight at dinner. I thought you’d like the idea of the graft,” he added untruthfully.

===Chinese===
「明天回去吧。」他几乎是在央告。「不过就两三天的事，于我却非同小可。何况如今欧伯佐恩起了别样的心思。」

「你不会再想那桩——那桩娶她的事了吧，是么？」

「吃晚饭时再谈。我还只当你中意这桩买卖的主意呢。」他补了一句，说的不是实话。

===Original===
Joan had to return to her prison to collect some of her belongings. She found the girl lying on the bed, reading, and Mirabelle greeted her with a smile.

===Chinese===
琼还须回她那座牢里去收拾几件随身物件。她进去时，见那姑娘正倚在床上看书；米拉贝尔含笑招呼她。

===Original===
“Well, is your term of imprisonment ended?”

Joan hesitated. “Not exactly. Do you mind if I’m not here tonight?”

Mirabelle shook her head. If the truth be told, she was glad to be alone. All that day she had been forced to listen to the plaints and weepings of this transfigured girl, and she felt that she could not well stand another twenty-four hours.

===Chinese===
「怎么样，你的刑期服满了没有？」

琼迟疑了一下。「算不得满。今晚我不在这儿，你介意么？」

米拉贝尔摇了摇头。说句实话，她正乐得独处。这一整天，她不得不听着这个脱胎换骨似的姑娘哭一阵、怨一阵，只觉得再这么熬上二十四个钟头，自己怕是撑不住了。

===Original===
“You’re sure you won’t mind being alone?”

“No, of course not. I shall miss you,” added Mirabelle, more in truth than in compliment. “When will you return?”

The girl made a little grimace. “Tomorrow.”

“You don’t want to come back, naturally? Have you succeeded in persuading your—your friend to let me out too?”

===Chinese===
「你真的不介意独自一人？」

「不，自然不。我会想你的。」米拉贝尔又添了一句——这话是实情，不是客套。「你几时回来？」

姑娘做了个小鬼脸。「明天。」

「你自然是不想回来的，对吧？你劝动你那位——你那位朋友，把我也放出去，成了没有？」

===Original===
Joan shook her head.

“He’ll never do that, my dear, not till…” She looked at the girl. “You’re not engaged, are you?”

“I? No. Is that another story they’ve heard?” Mirabelle got up from the bed, laughing. “An heiress, and engaged?”

“No, they don’t say you were engaged.” Joan hastened to correct the wrong impression. There was genuine admiration in her voice, when she said: “You’re wonderful, kid! If I were in your shoes I’d be quaking. You’re just as cheerful as though you were going to the funeral of a rich aunt!”

===Chinese===
琼摇了摇头。

「他决不肯的，亲爱的，除非等到……」她望着那姑娘。「你没有订过婚吧，有么？」

「我？没有。这又是他们听来的哪一出故事？」米拉贝尔从床上起身，笑起来。「一笔大宗家产的继承人，还订着婚？」

「没有，他们并没有说你订过婚。」琼忙不迭纠正这层误会。说出下面这话时，她声音里带着真心的赞叹：「你真了不起，小丫头！我要是换了你，早抖成一片了。你倒好，欢欢喜喜的，活像要去赴一位有钱姨妈的葬礼！」

===Original===
She did not know how near to a breakdown her companion had been that day, and Mirabelle, who felt stronger and saner now, had no desire to tell her.

“You’re rather splendid.” Joan nodded. “I wish I had your pluck.”

And then, impulsively, she came forward and kissed the girl.

===Chinese===
她并不知道，她这位同伴这一天离着精神崩溃只有一线之隔；而米拉贝尔此刻只觉气力更足、神志更清，也无意告诉她。

「你真是好样的。」琼点点头。「但愿我也有你这份胆气。」

接着，她一时情动，上前吻了吻那姑娘。

===Original===
