“Don’t feel too sore at me,” she said, and was gone before Mirabelle could make a reply.

The doctor was waiting for her in the factory.

“The spy has walked up to the canal bridge. We can go forward,” he said. “Besides,”—he had satisfaction out of this—“he cannot see over high walls.”

===Chinese===
「别把我恨得太深。」说罢，不等米拉贝尔回话，她人已经去了。

博士正在厂房里候她。

「那密探已踱到运河桥上去了。咱们可以走了。」他说。「再说，」——这话令他颇觉得意——「高墙，他是望不过去的。」

===Original===
“What is this story about marrying Mirabelle Leicester?”

“So he has told you? Also did he tell you that—that *he* is going to marry her?”

“Yes, and I’ll tell you something, doctor. I’d rather he married her than you.”

“So!” said the doctor.

===Chinese===
「娶米拉贝尔·莱斯特——这话是从何说起？」

「他已对你说了？他还告诉你没有——*他*自己要娶她？」

「说了。我也告诉你一件事，博士。他娶她，也胜过你娶她。」

「原来如此。」博士说。

===Original===
“I’d rather anybody else married her, except that snake of yours.”

Oberzohn looked round sharply. She had used the word quite innocently, without any thought of its application, and uttered an “Oh!” of dismay when she realized her mistake.

“I meant Gurther,” she said.

“Well, I know you meant Gurther, young miss,” he said stiffly.

===Chinese===
「随便换了哪一个娶她，我都情愿——只除了你那条蛇。」

欧伯佐恩霍地回过头来。她用这个字眼全出于无心，压根儿不曾想到它会落到谁的头上；待省悟到自己失言，她懊丧地「哦」了一声。

「我是说格瑟。」她说。

「哼，我知道你说的是格瑟，年轻的小姐。」他生硬地说。

===Original===
To get back to the house they had to make a half-circle of the factory and pass between the canal wall and the building itself. The direct route would have taken them into a deep hollow into which the debris of years had been thrown, and which now Nature, in her kindness, had hidden under a green mantle of wild convolvulus. It was typical of the place that the only beautiful picture in the grounds was out of sight.

===Chinese===
要回到宅子，他们须沿着厂房兜半个圈子，从运河围墙与厂房的夹缝之间穿行。抄直路走，便会陷入一处深坑——多年的碎砖烂瓦尽数倾倒在那里，幸而造化发了善心，替它罩上一袭野旋花织成的绿氅。这地方性子便是如此：满园子里唯一堪入画的一景，偏偏藏着不让人看。

===Original===
They were just turning the corner of the factory when the doctor stopped and looked up at the high wall, which was protected by a cheval de frise of broken glass. All except in one spot, about two feet wide, where not only the glass but the mortar which held it in place had been chipped off. There were fragments of the glass, and, on the inside of the wall, marks of some implement on the hard surface of the mortar.

===Chinese===
两人正拐过厂房墙角，博士忽地站住，仰头端详那堵高墙。墙头护着一道嵌满碎玻璃的拒马（cheval de frise）——只有一处例外，约莫两英尺宽：不但玻璃，连嵌住玻璃的砂浆也被凿得干干净净。地上留着玻璃碴子；墙的内侧，坚硬的砂浆面上，留着什么铁器磕划出的道道印子。

===Original===
“So!” said the doctor.

He was examining the scratches on the wall.

“Wait,” he ordered, and hurried back into the factory, to return, carrying in each hand two large rusty contraptions which he put on the ground.

One by one he forced open the jagged rusty teeth until they were wide apart and held by a spring catch. She had seen things like that in a museum. They were mantraps—relics of the barbarous days when trespass was not only a sin but a crime.

===Chinese===
「原来如此。」博士说。

他正细细察看墙上的划痕。

「等着。」他吩咐一声，匆匆折回厂房；再来时，每只手里各拎着两件锈蚀的大机括，搁到了地上。

他把那一口口参差的锈牙一件件硬生生掰开，掰到上下大张，由弹簧掣子咬住。这样的物件，她在博物馆里见识过。那是捕人铁夹（mantrap）——蛮荒年月的遗物；在那个年月，擅闯私地不但是罪孽，还是刑事罪名。

===Original===
He fixed the second of the traps on the path between the factory and the wall.

“Now we shall see,” he said. “Forward!”

===Chinese===
他把第二只铁夹安在厂房与围墙之间的那条小径上。

「如今且看分晓。」他说。「前进！」

===Original===
Monty was waiting for her impatiently. The Rolls had been turned out in her honour, and the sulky-looking driver was already in his place at the wheel.

===Chinese===
蒙蒂等她等得早不耐烦了。那辆劳斯莱斯已特意为她开出动过，那副哭丧着脸的司机早已端坐方向盘前。

===Original===
“What is the matter with that chauffeur?” she asked, as they bumped up the lane towards easier going. “He looks so happy that I shouldn’t be surprised to hear that his mother was hanged this morning.”

“He’s sore with the old man,” explained Monty. “Oberzohn has two drivers. They do a little looking round in the morning. The other fellow was supposed to come back to take over duty at three o’clock, and he hasn’t turned up. He was the better driver of the two.”

===Chinese===
「那位司机是怎么了？」汽车一路颠着驶出巷口、奔向好走的路面时，她问。「瞧他欢喜成那副模样，就算听说他娘今儿早晨上了绞架，我也不会吃惊。」

「他在跟老头子怄气。」蒙蒂解释道。「欧伯佐恩手底下有两名司机，每天早上出去转悠着四下望风。另一个本该三点钟回来接班，到这会儿还没露面。两人当中，数他车开得好。」

===Original===
The chauffeur was apparently seeking every pothole in the ground, and in the next five minutes she was alternately clutching the support of the arm-strap and Monty. They were relieved when at last the car found a metal road and began its noiseless way towards The Lights. And then her hand sought his, and for the moment this beautiful flower which had grown in such foul soil, bloomed in the radiance of a love common to every woman, high and low, good and bad.

===Chinese===
那司机分明存心把路面上的坑坑洼洼一个不落地拣着走；随后五分钟里，她一只手交替着，一会儿死死抓住扶手带的支柱，一会儿死死抓住蒙蒂。及至汽车终于驶上一条碎石路，无声无息地向着那片灯火辉煌的繁华地进发，两人才算松了口气。于是她的手摸索到了他的手；这朵自如此污浊的泥地里开出来的娇花，一时间，竟绽放在一片爱的光辉里——那是天下女子共通的爱，不分贵贱，无论善恶。

## XVIII At Frater's / 十八 在弗雷特剧院

===Original===
Manfred suggested an early dinner at the Lasky, where the soup was to his fastidious taste. Leon, who had eaten many crumpets for tea﻿—he had a weakness for this indigestible article of diet﻿—was prepared to dispense with the dinner, and Poiccart had views, being a man of steady habits. They dined at the Lasky, and Leon ordered a baked onion, and expatiated upon the two wasted years of Poiccart’s life, employing a wealth of imagery and a beauty of diction worthy of a better subject.
===Chinese===
曼弗雷德提议早早地在拉斯基餐厅用晚餐——那儿的汤最合他挑剔的口味。莱昂下午茶已消灭了不少烤面饼（crumpet）——他对这种不易消化的吃食素有痴嗜——本打算免了这顿正餐；波伊卡特却自有主张，他是个起居有恒的人。三人到底还是在拉斯基坐了席；莱昂点了一只烤洋葱，就波伊卡特平白虚掷的那两年大发议论，那满腹的譬喻、那漂亮的辞令，本配得上一桩像样些的题目。

===Original===
Manfred looked at his watch.

“Where are they dining?” he asked.

“I don’t know yet,” said Leon. “Our friend will be here in a few minutes; when we go out he will tell us. You don’t want to see her?”

Manfred shook his head.

“No,” he said.

“I’m going to be bored,” complained Poiccart.

“Then you should have let me bring Alma,” said Leon promptly.

“Exactly.” Raymond nodded his sober head. “I have the feeling that I am saving a lady from an unutterably dreary evening.”
===Chinese===
曼弗雷德看了看表。

「他们上哪儿用晚餐去了？」他问。

「眼下还不知道。」莱昂说。「咱们那位朋友几分钟内就到；等咱们出去，他自会讲明。你不想见见她？」

曼弗雷德摇了摇头。

「不想。」他说。

「我要闷出病来的。」波伊卡特抱怨道。

「那你早该让我把阿尔玛带来。」莱昂脱口便答。

「正是。」雷蒙德点了点他那颗沉稳的脑袋。「我此刻便有一种感觉：我正在把一位女士从一个乏味得难以言传的晚上里搭救出来。」

===Original===
There was a man waiting for them when they came out of the restaurant﻿—a very uninteresting-looking man who had three sentences to say sotto voce as they stood near him, but apparently in ignorance of his presence.
===Chinese===
他们步出餐厅时，早有一个男人候在外头——一个模样乏味、毫不起眼的男人；三人站到他近旁，一副显然不曾察觉他在场的样子，他却低声说出了三句话。

===Original===
“I did not wish to go to Mero’s,” said Manfred, “but as we have the time, I think it would be advisable to stroll in that direction. I am curious to discover whether this is really Oberzohn’s little treat, or whether the idea emanated from the unadmirable Mr. Newton.”

“And how will you know, George?” asked Gonsalez.

“By the car. If Oberzohn is master of the ceremonies, we shall find his machine parked somewhere in the neighbourhood. If it is Newton’s idea, then Oberzohn’s limousine, which brought them from South London, will have returned, and Newton’s car will be in its place.”
===Chinese===
「我本不打算去梅罗餐厅，」曼弗雷德说，「不过既然有的是工夫，我看还是朝那个方向遛达一趟为妥。我颇想弄个明白：这究竟真是欧伯佐恩摆下的这桌小小款待，还是这主意出自那位不足称道的牛顿先生。」

「那你怎么看得出来，乔治？」冈萨雷斯问。

「看车。若做东的是欧伯佐恩，咱们准能在左近什么地方寻着他那部车。若是牛顿的主意，那么把他们从伦敦南部载来的欧伯佐恩那辆豪华轿车早该回去了，原地换上的该是牛顿的车。」

===Original===
Mero’s was one of the most fashionable of dining clubs, patronized not only by the elite of society, but having on its books the cream of the theatrical world. It was situated in one of those quiet, old-world squares which are to be found in the very heart of London, enjoying, for some mysterious reason, immunity from the hands of the speculative property owner. The square retained the appearance it had in the days of the Georges; and though some of the fine mansions had been given over to commerce and the professions, and the lawyer and the manufacturer’s agent occupied the drawing-rooms and bedrooms sacred to the bucks and beauties of other days, quite a large number of the houses remained in private occupation.
===Chinese===
梅罗餐厅属最时髦的餐饮俱乐部之列，捧场的不仅有上流社会的精英，会员名册上更网罗着戏剧界的头面人物。它坐落在伦敦正中心那种幽静的老派广场之一；也不知什么神秘缘故，竟一直幸免于投机地产商的手掌心。广场至今保留着乔治王时代的旧观；虽有几座华宅已让给了商号与各行营生，律师和厂商经纪人占据了昔日专属于纨绔子弟与名媛佳丽的客厅卧房，仍有相当多的宅第由私家住用。

===Original===
There was nothing in the fascia of Mero’s to advertise its character. The club premises consisted of three of these fine old dwellings. The uninitiated might not even suspect that there was communication between the three houses, for the old doorways and doorsteps remained untouched, though only one was used.
===Chinese===
梅罗餐厅的门脸上，没有任何标榜自家底细的字样。俱乐部的场址由三座这样的老华宅连成。门外汉恐怕连三宅之间暗通曲径都想不到，因为旧门洞和旧门阶一概原封未动，虽则启用的只有一处。

===Original===
They strolled along two sides of the square before, amidst the phalanx of cars that stood wheel to wheel, their backs to the railings of the centre gardens, they saw Oberzohn’s car.
===Chinese===
他们沿着广场的两边遛达了两段，才在那一列车阵当中望见欧伯佐恩的车——车与车前轮贴着后轮地排开，车尾一律抵着中央花园的围栏。

===Original===
The driver sat with his arms folded on the wheel, in earnest conversation with a pale-faced man, slightly and neatly bearded, and dressed in faultless evening dress. He was evidently a cripple: one shoulder was higher than the other; and when he moved, he walked painfully with the aid of a stick.
===Chinese===
司机双臂交叠搭在方向盘上坐着，正同一个脸色苍白的男人恳切交谈；那人蓄着疏疏落落却修剪齐整的短须，穿一身挑不出毛病的晚礼服，一望便知身有残疾：一肩高，一肩低；一挪步，便得拄着手杖，走得艰难。

===Original===
Manfred saw the driver point up the line of cars, and the lame gentleman limped in the direction the chauffeur had indicated and stopped to speak to another man in livery. As they came abreast of him, they saw that one of his boots had a thick sole, and the limp was explained.
===Chinese===
曼弗雷德看见司机朝车列前方一指，那位瘸腿先生便循着所指的方向蹒跚而去，停下来同另一个穿号衣的汉子搭话。待三人走到与他并排，看清了他一只靴子的鞋底特别厚，那跛态的来由也就有了着落。

===Original===
“The gentleman has lost his car,” said Manfred, for now he was peering short-sightedly at the number-plates.
===Chinese===
「这位先生丢了车。」曼弗雷德说——他这会儿正眯起近视眼，辨认着一排车牌。

===Original===
The theft of cars was a daily occurrence. Leon had something to say on the potentialities of that branch of crime. He owned to an encyclopaedic knowledge of the current fashions in wrongdoing, and in a few brief sentences indicated the extent of these thefts.
===Chinese===
偷车是天天都有的事。莱昂对这门犯罪行当的前景颇有一番话讲。他自认于当今作奸犯科的时新花样有着百科全书般的学问，寥寥数语，便把这类窃案的规模点了个大概。

===Original===
“Fifty a week are shipped to India and the Colonies, after their numbers are erased and another substituted. In some cases the ‘knockers off,’ as they call the thieves, drive them straight away into the packing-cases which are prepared for every make of car; the ends are nailed up, and they are waiting shipment at the docks before the owner is certain of his loss. There are almost as many stolen cars in India, South Africa and Australia as there are honest ones!”
===Chinese===
「每礼拜有五十辆，抹去原号、另换一号，装船运往印度和各殖民地。有些案子里，那班他们唤作『敲车佬』（knockers off）的贼，索性把车径直开进为各牌各款汽车备好的装箱里；两头一钉，车主还没弄确实自己丢了车，货已经在码头候着装船了。印度、南非和澳大利亚的贼车，几乎跟清白车一样多！」

===Original===
They walked slowly past the decorous portals of Mero’s, and caught a glimpse, through the curtained windows, of soft table lamps burning, of bare-armed women and white-shirted men, and heard faintly the strains of an orchestra playing a Viennese waltz.
===Chinese===
他们缓步走过梅罗餐厅那气派端凝的大门，透过垂着帘的窗子，瞥见一盏盏柔亮的台灯，瞥见裸着臂膀的女人和穿白衬衫的男人，还依稀听见一支乐队奏着维也纳圆舞曲的曲调。

===Original===
“I should like to see our Jane,” said Gonsalez. “She never came to you, did she?”

“She came, but I didn’t see her,” said Manfred. “From the moment she leaves the theatre she must not be left.”

Leon nodded.

“I have already made that arrangement,” he said. “Digby﻿—”

“Digby takes up his duty at midnight,” said Manfred. “He has been down to Oberzohn’s place to get the lie of the land: he thought it advisable that he should study the topography in daylight, and I agreed. He might get himself into an awkward tangle if he started exploring the canal bank in the dark hours. Summer or winter, there is usually a mist on the water.”
===Chinese===
「我倒想见见咱们的简。」冈萨雷斯说。「她到底没上你那儿去，是不是？」

「来过，可我没见着她。」曼弗雷德说。「她一离开剧院，就一刻也不能没人照应。」

莱昂点了点头。

「这一层我已安排妥当。」他说。「迪格比——」

「迪格比到午夜才接班。」曼弗雷德说。「他已经下去把欧伯佐恩那地方的地势虚实摸了一遍：他认为最好趁天亮把地形踏勘清楚，我也赞同。若是摸黑去探运河岸，保不齐把自己缠进一团乱麻里。无论冬夏，那水面上总浮着一层雾。」

===Original===
They reached Frater’s theatre so early that the queues at the pit door were still unadmitted, and Leon suggested that they make a circuit of this rambling house of entertainment. It stood in Shaftesbury Avenue and occupied an island site. On either side two narrow streets flanked the building, whilst the rear formed the third side of a small square, one of which was taken up by a County Council dwelling, mainly occupied by artisans. From the square a long passageway led to Cranbourn Street; whilst, in addition to the alley which opened just at the back of the theatre, a street ran parallel to Shaftesbury Avenue from Charing Cross Road to Rupert Street.
===Chinese===
他们到弗雷特剧院时还很早，正厅后座门前的长队尚未放进场去，莱昂便提议绕着这座四下铺展的娱乐场子兜上一圈。剧院坐落在沙夫茨伯里大道上，独占一块四面临街的岛式地皮。楼身两侧各有窄街夹峙，背面则合成一座小广场的第三边；广场有一边叫郡议会的住宅楼占了，住户多为工匠。从广场有一条长长的通道直通克兰伯恩街；此外，除了正贴着剧院后身开口的那条小巷，另有一条街与沙夫茨伯里大道平行，从查令十字路一直通到鲁珀特街。

===Original===
The theatre itself was one of the best in London, and although it had had a succession of failures, its luck had turned, and the new mystery play was drawing all London.
===Chinese===
这座剧院本身便是伦敦数一数二的场子；虽说先前连栽过几出戏，如今时运回转，新排的那部悬疑剧正把整个伦敦都往里吸引。

===Original===
“That is the stage door,” said Leon﻿—they had reached the square﻿—“and those are emergency exits”﻿—he pointed back the way they had come﻿—“which are utilized at the end of a performance to empty the theatre.”

“Why are you taking such an interest in the theatre itself?” asked Poiccart.

“Because,” said Gonsalez slowly, “I am in agreement with George. We should have found Newton’s car parked in Fitzreeve Gardens﻿—not Oberzohn’s. And the circumstances are a little suspicious.”
===Chinese===
「那是舞台门。」莱昂说——这时他们已到了广场——「那些是太平门。」——他朝来路指去——「散场时便靠它们把满场的观众放空。」

「你怎么对这剧院本身这样上心？」波伊卡特问。

「因为，」冈萨雷斯慢条斯理地说，「我的看法与乔治一致。咱们本该看到停在菲茨里夫花园里的是牛顿的车——而不是欧伯佐恩的。况且眼下的情形，有点叫人起疑。」

===Original===
The doors of the pit and gallery were open now; the queues were moving slowly to the entrances; and they watched the great building swallow up the devotees of the drama, before they returned to the front of the house.
===Chinese===
这时，正厅后座与顶层楼座的门都开了；长队朝各个入口缓缓蠕动；他们望着那座大厦把一批批戏迷吞进腹中，方才折回剧院的正门前去。

===Original===
Cars were beginning to arrive, at first at intervals, but, as the hour of the play’s beginning approached, in a ceaseless line that made a congestion and rendered the traffic police articulate and occasionally unkind. It was short of the half-hour after eight when Manfred saw Oberzohn’s glistening car in the block, and presently it pulled up before the entrance of the theatre. First Joan and then Monty Newton alighted and passed out of view.
===Chinese===
汽车开始驶到，起初还隔着空当，待到开演的钟点逼近，便连成一线、川流不息，堵作一团，堵得交警打开了话匣子，偶尔还出言不逊。时钟刚过八点、还差几分才到半点的当儿，曼弗雷德在长龙里望见了欧伯佐恩那辆锃亮的车；不多时，它便在剧院门前停了下来。琼先下车，蒙蒂·牛顿随后也下了车，两人转眼走出视线。

===Original===
Gonsalez thought he had never seen the girl looking quite as radiantly pretty. She had the colouring and the shape of youth, and though the more fastidious might object to her daring toilette, the most cantankerous could not cavil at the pleasing effect.
===Chinese===
冈萨雷斯觉得从没见这姑娘出落得这般光彩照人。她有着青春的色泽与身段；讲究些的人或许要挑剔她那身大胆的装束，可再爱抬杠的人，也没法子挑那悦目效果的错儿。

===Original===
“It is a great pity,”﻿—Leon spoke in Spanish﻿—“a thousand pities! I have the same feeling when I see a perfect block of marble placed in the hands of a tombstone-maker to be mangled into ugliness!”
===Chinese===
「可惜啊，」——莱昂说的是西班牙语——「万分的可惜！我每见一块完美的大理石被塞到墓碑匠手里、要被活活糟践成丑物，心里涌起的便是这同一种滋味！」

===Original===
Manfred put out his hand and drew him back into the shadow. A cab was dropping the lame man. He got out with the aid of a linkman, paid the driver, and limped into the vestibule. It was not a remarkable coincidence: the gentleman had evidently come from Mero’s, and as all London was flocking to the drama, there was little that was odd in finding him here. They saw that he went up into the dress-circle, and later, when they took their places in the stalls, Leon, glancing up, saw the pale, bearded face and noted that he occupied the end seat of the front row.
===Chinese===
曼弗雷德伸出手，把他拉回暗影里。一辆出租汽车正把那瘸腿先生送到门前。他在一名执灯引路的侍者的搀扶下下了车，付了车钱，一瘸一拐地进了门厅。这算不得什么了不得的巧遇：这位先生分明是从梅罗餐厅过来的，而全伦敦都在往戏园子里涌，在此地撞见他，实在没什么可怪的。他们瞧见他上了二楼前座；稍后，三人自己在池座落了座，莱昂抬眼一扫，又见到那张苍白的蓄须的脸，并且留意到，他坐的正是头一排的末座。

===Original===
“I’ve met that man somewhere,” he said, irritated. “Nothing annoys me worse than to forget, not a face, but where I have seen it!”
===Chinese===
「那个人我在哪儿见过。」他懊恼地说。「再没有比这更叫我恼火的事了：忘掉的还不是那张脸，偏是我在哪儿见过它！」

===Original===
Did Gurther but know, he had achieved the height of his ambition: he had twice passed under the keen scrutiny of the cleverest detectives in the world, and had remained unrecognized.
===Chinese===
倘若格瑟自己心中有数，他该知道他已然攀上了抱负的绝顶：他两次从世上最聪明的侦探们锐利的审视底下走过，竟始终无一人将他认出。

## XXIX Work for Gurther / 二十九 格瑟的差事

===Original===
Gurther was sleeping when he was called for duty, but presented himself before his director as bright and alert as though he had not spent a sleepless night, nor yet had endured the strain of a midnight train jump.
===Chinese===
传他去当差时，格瑟正睡着；可在那位上司跟前露面时，他仍旧神采奕奕、机警伶俐，仿佛既不曾熬过一个不眠之夜，也不曾受过午夜跳车那一番惊扰。

===Original===
“Once more, my Gurther, I send you forth.” Dr. Oberzohn was almost gay. “This time to save us all from the Judas treachery of one we thought was our friend. Tonight the snake must bite, and bite hard, Gurther. And out into the dark goes the so-called Trusted! And after that, my brave boy, there shall be nothing to fear.”
===Chinese===
「再一次，我的格瑟，我派你出征。」欧伯佐恩博士几乎是欢天喜地的。「这一回，是要救我们大家脱离一个我们引为朋友之人的犹大式背叛。今夜，毒蛇必须出击，而且要狠狠出击，格瑟。那所谓的『亲信』，就此滚进黑暗里去！此后，我勇敢的孩子，就再没有什么可怕的了。」

===Original===
He paused for approval, and got it in a snapped agreement.

“Tonight we desire from you a chef d’oeuvre, the supreme employment of your great art, Gurther; the highest expression of genius! The gentleman-club manner will not do. They may look for you and find you. Better it should be, this time, that you﻿—”
===Chinese===
他顿了一顿，等候赞许；得到的答复是一声干脆的附和。

「今夜我们要你拿出一件杰作（chef d’oeuvre），要你那门伟大艺术的极致运用，格瑟；要天才的最高表达！绅士俱乐部式的做派可行不通。他们兴许会找你，兴许真找得着你。这一回，倒不如你——」

===Original===
“Herr Doktor, will you graciously permit me to offer a humble suggestion?” said Gurther eagerly.

The doctor nodded his head slowly.

“You may speak, Gurther,” he said. “You are a man of intelligence; I would not presume to dictate to an artist.”
===Chinese===
「博士先生，可否开恩准小人进一愚见？」格瑟急切地说。

博士缓缓点了点头。

「你可以说，格瑟。」他说。「你是个有头脑的人；对一位艺术家，我不敢妄加指点。」

===Original===
“Let me go for an hour, perhaps two hours, and I will return to you with a manner that is unique. Is it graciously permitted, Herr Doktor?”

“March!” said the doctor graciously, waving his hand to the door.
===Chinese===
「放我去一个钟头，兴许两个钟头，我回到您跟前时，自带一副独一无二的行头做派。可否开恩恩准，博士先生？」

「开步走！」博士宽仁地一挥手，朝门示意。

===Original===
Nearly an hour and a half passed before the door opened and a gentleman came in who for even a moment even the doctor thought was a stranger. The face had an unearthly ivory pallor; the black brows, the faint shadows beneath the eyes that suggested a recent illness, the close-cropped black beard in which grey showed﻿—these might not have deceived him. But the man was obviously the victim of some appalling accident of the past. One shoulder was hunched, the hand that held the stick was distorted out of shape, and as he moved, the clump of his club foot advertised his lameness.
===Chinese===
将近一个半钟头过去，门才开了，进来一位绅士——哪怕只是一瞬，连博士也当他是生客。那张脸泛着一股不似人间的象牙惨白；两道黑眉，眼底那暗示着新近一场病痛的淡淡阴影，一部透出灰白的黑色短须——这些兴许骗不过他。可这人分明是往日某场骇人事故的受害者。一肩高耸，握着手杖的那只手扭曲得走了形；他一挪步，那只畸形足沉重的声响便宣告着他的瘸腿。

===Original===
“Sir, you desire to see me﻿—?” began the doctor, and then stared open-mouthed. “It is not﻿ ﻿… !”

Gurther smiled.

“Herr Doktor, are you condescendingly pleased?”
===Chinese===
「先生，您要见我——？」博士开口道，随即目瞪口呆地怔住了。「这不会是……！」

格瑟微微一笑。

「博士先生，您屈尊满意了么？」

===Original===
“Colossal!” murmured Oberzohn, gazing in amazement. “Of all accomplishments this is supreme! Gurther, you are an artist. Some day we shall buy a theatre for you in Unter den Linden, and you shall thrill large audiences.”
===Chinese===
「宏伟之至！」欧伯佐恩喃喃道，不胜惊异地端详着。「种种绝技之中，这是登峰造极的一项！格瑟，你是位艺术家。有朝一日，咱们要在菩提树下大街（Unter den Linden）给你买下一座剧院，让你去骇倒满场的看客。」

===Original===
“Herr Doktor, this is my own idea; this I have planned for many months. The boots I made myself; even the coat I altered”﻿—he patted his deformed shoulder proudly.
===Chinese===
「博士先生，这是小人自己的主意；为这一着，小人筹划了好几个月。靴子是小人亲手做的；连这件上衣也是小人改的」——他骄傲地拍了拍自己那畸形的肩膀。

===Original===
“An eyeglass?”

“I have it,” said Gurther promptly.

“The cravat﻿—is it not too proper?”

Gurther fingered his tie.

“For the grand habit I respectfully claim that the proper tie is desirable, if you will graciously permit.”

The Herr Doktor nodded.
===Chinese===
「单片眼镜呢？」

「备下了。」格瑟应声答道。

「领结——是不是太板正了？」

格瑟抚了抚领结。

「既是盛装登场的行头，小人斗胆以为，板正的领结正属相宜，还望恩准。」

博士先生点了点头。

===Original===
“You shall go with God, Gurther,” he said piously, took a golden cigarette-case from his pocket and handed it to the man. “Sit down, my dear friend.”

He rose and pointed to the chair he had vacated.

“In my own chair, Gurther. Nothing is too good for you. Now here is the arrangement﻿ ﻿…”
===Chinese===
「愿上帝与你同行，格瑟。」他虔敬地说，从口袋里取出一只金烟盒，递给那人。「坐吧，我亲爱的朋友。」

他站起身，指了指自己刚空出来的椅子。

「坐我自己的椅子，格瑟。再好的东西给你都不为过。好了，安排是这样……」

===Original===
Step by step he unfolded the timetable, for chronology was almost as great a passion with this strange and wicked man as it was with Aunt Alma.
===Chinese===
他把那张时间表一步一步铺陈开来，因为编年记事于这个古怪而邪恶的人，几乎是与阿尔玛姨妈同等的一项癖好。

===Original===
So confident was Gurther of his disguise that he had gone in the open to speak to Oberzohn’s chauffeur, and out of the tail of his eye he had seen Manfred and Gonsalez approaching. It was the supreme test and was passed with credit to himself.
===Chinese===
格瑟对自己的易容自信到如此地步，竟大摇大摆走到明处去同欧伯佐恩的司机说话，又用眼角余光瞥见曼弗雷德和冈萨雷斯走近前来。这是至高无上的考验，他体体面面地通过了。

===Original===
He did not dine at Mero’s; Gurther never ate or drank when he was wearing a disguise, knowing just how fatal that occupation could be. Instead, he had called a taxi, and had killed time by being driven slowly round and round the Outer Circle of Regent’s Park.
===Chinese===
他没有在梅罗餐厅用晚餐；格瑟一身伪装在身时从来不吃不喝，深知那桩事有多性命攸关。他转而叫了一辆出租汽车，让车载着他在摄政公园（Regent’s Park）的外环道上一圈又一圈慢慢地兜，消磨时光。

===Original===
Gurther was doing a great deal of thinking in these days, and at the cost of much physical discomfort had curtailed his pernicious practices, that his head might be clear all the time. For if he were to live, that clear head of his was necessary.
===Chinese===
这些日子，格瑟的脑筋转得极多；他付出了许多肉体苦楚的代价，收敛了那些戕身的恶习，好让自己的脑袋时刻清明。因为若想活命，他那颗清明的脑袋是必不可少的。

===Original===
The prisoner in the cellar occupied his thoughts. She had an importance for two reasons: she was a friend of the men whom he hated with a cold and deadly malignity beyond description; she represented wealth untold, and the Herr Doktor had even gone to the length of planning a marriage with her. She was not to be killed, not to be hurt; she was so important that the old man would take the risks attendant upon a marriage. There must be an excellent reason for that, because Dr. Oberzohn had not a very delicate mind.
===Chinese===
地窖里的女囚盘踞在他心头。她的要紧有两个缘故：其一，她是那几个男人的朋友，而他对那几个人的恨，是一种冷冰冰、要人命、非言语所能形容的歹毒；其二，她代表着数不尽的家财，博士先生甚至已筹划到要与她成亲的地步。她不可杀，不可伤；她要紧到这个地步，连那老头儿都肯担下成亲所附带的风险。这背后必定有个极好的道理，因为欧伯佐恩博士的头脑可算不得多么精细。

===Original===
He seemed to remember that, by the English law, a wife could not give evidence against her husband. He was not sure, but he had a dim notion that Pfeiffer had told him this: Pfeiffer was an educated man and had taken high honours at the gymnasium.
===Chinese===
他仿佛记得，照英国的法律，做妻子的不能出面作证指控丈夫。他拿不准，只是模模糊糊觉得这话是法伊弗告诉他的：法伊弗是个受过教育的人，在文科中学（gymnasium）里拿过顶高的名次。

===Original===
Gurther was not well read. His education had been of a scrappy character, and once upon a time he had been refused a leading part because of his provincial accent. That fault he had corrected in prison, under the tuition of a professor who was serving a life sentence for killing two women; but by the time Gurther had been released, he was a marked man, and the stage was a career lost to him forever.
===Chinese===
格瑟读书不多。他所受的教育支离破碎；遥想当年，他曾因一口外省口音被拒了主角之位。这个毛病他是在监狱里矫正的，师从一位因杀死两个女人而服无期徒刑的教授；可等到格瑟出狱，他已是个挂号在案的人物，舞台这门生涯，与他永诀了。

===Original===
Oberzohn possessed advantages which were not his. He was the master; Gurther was the servant. Oberzohn could determine events by reason of his vast authority, and the strings which he pulled in every part of the world. Even Gurther had accepted this position of blind, obedient servant, but now his angle had shifted, even as Oberzohn’s had moved in relation to Montague Newton. Perhaps because of this. The doctor, in curtailing one confidence, was enlarging another, and in the enlargement his prestige suffered.
===Chinese===
欧伯佐恩拥有他所没有的种种优势。一个是主子，一个是仆役。欧伯佐恩凭他广大的权势、凭他在世界每一个角落牵动的提线，能左右大局。连格瑟也早已认下了这盲从听命的仆役位置，可如今他的视角偏移了，正如欧伯佐恩对蒙塔古·牛顿的视角也已偏移一样。兴许正是为了这个缘故。博士削减了一桩信任，便扩充了另一桩，而这一扩充，他的威望便吃了亏。

===Original===
Gurther was now the confidant, therefore the equal; and logically, the equal can always become the superior. He had dreamed dreams of a life of ease, a gratification of his sense of luxury without the sobering thought that somewhere round the corner was waiting a man ready to tap him on the shoulder﻿ ﻿… a white palace in a flowery land, with blue swimming pools, and supple girls who called him Master. Gurther began to see the light.
===Chinese===
格瑟如今是心腹，因而也是平起平坐者；而照逻辑讲，平起平坐者永远有朝一日能变成上位者。他做过那样的梦：一种安逸的生活，一种奢华之癖的满足，不必去想拐过哪个街角，便候着一个随时会上前拍他肩膀的人……一片繁花之地上的白色宫殿，蓝色的泳池，还有一个个柔顺的、唤他「老爷」的姑娘。格瑟开始看见那道亮光了。

===Original===
Until he had taken his seat in the theatre, he had not so much as glimpsed the man and the woman in the end box.
===Chinese===
直到在剧院里落了座，他才头一回望见端头包厢里那一男一女——此前他连两人的影子都不曾瞥过一眼。

===Original===
Joan was happy﻿—happier than she remembered having been. Perhaps it was the reaction from her voluntary imprisonment. Certainly it was Monty’s reluctant agreement to a change of plans which so exalted her. Monty had dropped the thin pretence of an accommodation marriage; and once he was persuaded to this, the last hindrance to enjoyment was dissipated. Let Oberzohn take the girl if he wanted her; take, too, such heavy responsibility as followed. Monty Newton would get all that he wanted without the risk. Having arrived at this decision, he had ordered another bottle of champagne to seal the bargain, and they left Mero’s club a much happier couple than they had been when they entered.
===Chinese===
琼很快活——比她记忆中任何时候都快活。这兴许是她那场自愿囚禁之后的反弹。而叫她如此飘然欲仙的，必定是蒙蒂那一番勉强的松口——他应允更改计划了。蒙蒂已撇下了那桩权宜婚姻的薄薄托词；一经劝到了这一步，享乐路上的最后一重障碍便烟消云散。欧伯佐恩想要那姑娘，只管让他要去；随之而来的沉重干系，也只管让他一并担去。蒙蒂·牛顿不必冒险，便能样样到手。打定了这个主意，他又叫了一瓶香槟，把这笔买卖封了口；两人离开梅罗俱乐部时，已比进门时快活了许多。

===Original===
“As soon as we’ve carved up this money, we’ll get away out of England,” he told her as they were driving to the theatre. “What about Buenos Aires for the winter, old girl?”
===Chinese===
「等咱们把这笔钱分派停当，就远走高飞，离开英国。」驱车去剧院的路上，他对她说。「上布宜诺斯艾利斯（Buenos Aires）过冬去，怎么样，宝贝儿？」

===Original===
She did not know where Buenos Aires was, but she gurgled her delight at the suggestion, and Monty expatiated upon the joys of the South American summer, the beauties of B.A., its gaieties and amusements.
===Chinese===
她不知道布宜诺斯艾利斯在哪儿，可一听这提议便咯咯地乐出了声；蒙蒂于是对南美之夏的种种乐处、布宜诺斯艾利斯的美景、它的繁华与消遣大发议论。

===Original===
“I don’t suppose there’ll be any kick coming,” he said, “but it wouldn’t be a bad scheme if we took a trip round the world, and came back in about eighteen months’ time to settle down in London. My hectic past would have been forgotten by then﻿—why, I might even get into Parliament.”
===Chinese===
「料想不会有什么闲话可挑。」他说，「不过咱们要能环游世界一趟，约莫十八个月后回伦敦安顿下来，倒也不失为一着好棋。到那时，我那段轰轰烈烈的往事早叫人忘干净了——嘿，说不定我还能进议会呢。」

===Original===
“How wonderful!” she breathed, and then: “What is this play about, Monty?”

“It’s a bit of a thrill, the very play for you﻿—a detective story that will make your hair stand on end.”
===Chinese===
「多妙啊！」她轻轻叹道，接着又问：「这出戏讲的是什么，蒙蒂？」

「有点儿惊险，正是给你预备的戏——一个保管叫你毛发倒竖的侦探故事。」

===Original===
She had all the gamin’s morbid interest in murder and crime, and she settled down in the box with a pleasant feeling of anticipation, and watched the development of the first act.
===Chinese===
她对凶杀与罪案怀着一股野丫头式的病态兴致，便带着满心惬意的期待在包厢里安顿下来，看第一幕渐渐铺展开来。

===Original===
The scene was laid in a club, a low-down resort where the least desirable members of society met, and she drank in every word, because she knew the life, had seen that type of expensively dressed woman who swaggered on to the stage and was addressed familiarly by the club proprietor. She knew that steady-eyed detective when he made his embarrassing appearance. The woman was herself. She even knew the cadaverous wanderer who approached stealthily at the door: a human wolf that fled at the sight of the police officer.
===Chinese===
场景设在一间俱乐部里——一个下等去处，社会上最不入流的分子在那里聚头。她一字一句都听得入骨，因为这种生活她识得：那一路衣着阔气、大摇大摆走上台、被俱乐部老板随口昵称的女人，她见过。那个目光沉稳的侦探一露那叫人发窘的面，她便认得。那女人就是她自己。连那个在门边悄悄蹭近的形容枯槁的游荡汉，她也识得：一条见了警官就逃的人形豺狼。

===Original===
The three who sat in the front row of the stalls﻿—how Leon Gonsalez secured these tickets was one of the minor mysteries of the day﻿—saw her, and one at least felt his heart ache.
===Chinese===
坐在池座前排的那三位——莱昂·冈萨雷斯这几张票是怎么弄到手的，是当日一桩小小的悬案——也望见了她，其中至少有一位，觉得心头作痛。

===Original===
Monty beamed his geniality. He had taken sufficient wine to give him a rosy view of the world, and he was even mildly interested in the play, though his chief pleasure was in the girl’s enchantment. He ordered ices for her after the first interval.
===Chinese===
蒙蒂满面和煦。他的酒喝得恰到好处，看世界都蒙上了一层玫瑰色；对这出戏他甚至有了几分淡淡的兴味，虽然他最大的乐趣，还在那姑娘的如痴如醉。第一次幕间休息过后，他给她叫了冰淇淋。

===Original===
“You’re getting quite a theatre fan, kiddie,” he said. “I must take you to some other shows. I had no idea you liked this sort of thing.”

She drew a long breath and smiled at him.

“I like anything when I’m with you,” she said, and they held hands foolishly, till the house lights dimmed and the curtain rose upon a lawyer’s office.
===Chinese===
「你快成个小戏迷了，丫头。」他说。「往后得带你去看几场别的戏。真没想到你竟喜欢这一路。」

她深深吸了一口气，朝他嫣然一笑。

「只要跟你在一起，什么都喜欢。」她说。两人傻乎乎地手握着手，直到场灯转暗，大幕升起，台上现出一间律师事务所。

===Original===
The lawyer was of the underworld: a man everlastingly on the verge of being struck off the rolls. He had betrayed a client with whom he had had dealings, and the man had gone to prison for a long term, but had escaped. Now the news had come that he had left Australia and was in London, waiting his opportunity to destroy the man whose treachery was responsible for his capture.
===Chinese===
那律师本是个黑道中人：一个永远濒临被除名的人。他出卖过一个与他有过往来的主顾，那人判了长刑，却越了狱。如今消息传来，说他已离开澳大利亚，到了伦敦，正候着机会，除掉那个因奸计出卖而使他落网的对头。

===Original===
Here was a note to which the heart of the girl responded. Even Monty found himself leaning forward, as the old familiar cant terms of his trade came across the footlights.
===Chinese===
这调子，正敲在那姑娘的心弦上。连蒙蒂也不觉把身子探了前去，因为脚灯那一头传来的，是他这一行里老熟的黑话切口。

===Original===
“It is quite all right,” he said at the second interval, “only”﻿—he hesitated﻿—“isn’t it a bit too near the real thing? After all, one doesn’t come to the theatre to see﻿ ﻿…”

He stopped, realizing that conditions and situations familiar to him were novel enough to a fashionable audience which was learning for the first time that a “busy” was a detective, and that a police informer went by the title of “nose.”
===Chinese===
「挺好挺好。」第二次幕间他说，「只是」——他迟疑了一下——「这是不是有点儿太贴近真事了？毕竟，人上戏院不是来看……」

他住了口，省悟到那些于他熟稔不过的情形与局面，对满场的时髦看客已够新奇——他们头一遭学到：「大忙人」就是侦探，而警方眼线的名号，唤作「鼻子」。

===Original===
The lights up, he glanced round the house, and suddenly he started and caught her arm.

“Don’t look for a moment,” he said, averting his eyes, “then take a glance at the front row. Do you see anybody you know?”

Presently she looked.
===Chinese===
灯光复明，他环顾全场，猛然一怔，抓住了她的手臂。

「先别看。」他避开目光说，「待会儿再朝前排瞟一眼。瞧见熟人了吗？」

过了一会儿，她望了过去。

===Original===
“Yes, that is the fellow you hate so much, isn’t it﻿—Gonsalez?”

“They’re all there﻿—the three of them,” said Monty. “I wonder,”﻿—he was troubled at the thought﻿—“I wonder if they’re looking for you?”

“For me? They’ve nothing on me, Monty.”
===Chinese===
「瞧见了，那不就是你恨得咬牙切齿的那家伙么——冈萨雷斯？」

「他们全在——三个都在。」蒙蒂说。「也不知」——这念头叫他心里发毛——「也不知他们是不是在找你？」

「找我？他们拿不住我什么把柄，蒙蒂。」

===Original===
He was silent.

“I’m glad you’re not going back to that place tonight. They’ll trail you sure﻿—sure!”
===Chinese===
他沉默了。

「今晚你不回那地方去，我倒是高兴。他们准会盯你的梢——准会！」

===Original===
He thought later that it was probably a coincidence that they were there at all. They seemed to show no interest in the box, but were chattering and talking and laughing to one another. Not once did their eyes come up to his level, and after a while he gained in confidence, though he was glad enough when the play was resumed.
===Chinese===
他后来想，他们会在场，多半只是巧合。他们似乎对那包厢毫无兴致，只顾彼此谈笑风生。他们的目光一次也不曾抬到他这个高度；过了一阵，他的胆气回涨了，虽说戏重新开演时，他还是大大地松了口气。

===Original===
There were two scenes in the act: the first was a police station, the second the lawyer’s room. The man was drunk, and the detective had come to warn him that The Ringer was after him. And then suddenly the lights on the stage were extinguished and the whole house was in the dark. It was part of the plot. In this darkness, and in the very presence of the police, the threatened man was to be murdered. They listened in tense silence, the girl craning her head forward, trying to pierce the dark, listening to the lines of intense dialogue that were coming from the blackness of the stage. Somebody was in the room﻿—a woman, and they had found her. She slipped from the stage detective’s grasp and vanished, and when the lights went up she was gone.
===Chinese===
这一幕共两场戏：头一场是警察局，第二场是那律师的房间。那男人喝醉了，侦探是来警告他，《敲钟人》已经盯上了他。骤然间，台上的灯全灭了，整座剧场陷进黑暗。这是剧情的一部分。就在这片黑暗里、就在警察的眼皮子底下，那受死亡威胁的人将被谋害。他们屏息凝神地听着，姑娘把头颈伸得老长，想望穿这片黑暗，听那从舞台黑影里传出的字字紧绷的道白。屋里有人——是个女人，他们发现她了。她从台上侦探的掌握里滑脱，没了踪影；灯再亮时，她已不见。

===Original===
“What has happened, Monty?” she whispered.

He did not answer.

“Do you think﻿—”
===Chinese===
「这是怎么了，蒙蒂？」她悄声问。

他没有作声。

「你说是不是——」

===Original===
She looked round at him. His head was resting on the plush-covered ledge of the box. His face, turned towards her, was grey; the eyes were closed, and his teeth showed in a hideous grin.

She screamed.

“Monty! Monty!”

She shook him. Again her scream rang through the house. At first the audience thought that it was a woman driven hysterical by the tenseness of the stage situation, and then one or two people rose from their stalls and looked up.

“Monty! Speak to me! He’s dead, he’s dead!”
===Chinese===
她回过头去看他。他的头歪在包厢那蒙着长毛绒的沿栏上。那张转向她的脸一片死灰；双眼紧闭，龇着牙，一副狰狞的笑相。

她尖叫起来。

「蒙蒂！蒙蒂！」

她摇撼着他。她的尖叫又一次响彻全场。起初观众还只当是个女人被台上紧张的情节逼出了歇斯底里，随后一两个人从池座里站起身，朝楼上张望。

「蒙蒂！跟我说句话！他死了，他死了！」

===Original===
Three seats in the front row had emptied. The screams of the hysterical girl made it impossible for the scene to proceed, and the curtain came down quickly.

The house was seething with excitement. Every face was turned towards the box where she knelt by the side of the dead man, clasping him in her arms, and the shrill agony in her voice was unnerving.
===Chinese===
池座前排空出了三个座位。那歇斯底里的姑娘尖叫不绝，戏再也没法演下去，大幕匆匆落了下来。

满场沸反盈天。一张张脸都转向那包厢：她跪在死人身边，把他紧紧搂在怀里，那声音里尖利的痛楚，令人心胆俱寒。

===Original===
The door of the box swung open, and Manfred dashed in. One glance he gave at Monty Newton, and he needed no other.

“Get the girl out,” he said curtly.

Leon tried to draw her from the box, but she was a shrieking fury.

“You did it, you did it!﻿ ﻿… Let me go to him!”

Leon lifted her from her feet, and, clawing wildly at his face, she was carried from the box.
===Chinese===
包厢门猛地荡开，曼弗雷德冲了进来。他朝蒙蒂·牛顿只看了一眼，便无须第二眼。

「把姑娘弄出去。」他简短地说。

莱昂想把她拉出包厢，她却狂怒得尖声嘶叫。

「是你们干的，是你们干的！……让我到他那儿去！」

莱昂把她整个人抱离了地面；她疯狂地抓挠他的脸，就这样被抱出了包厢。

===Original===
The manager was running along the passage, and Leon sent him on with a jerk of his head. And then a woman in evening dress came from somewhere.

“May I take her?” she said, and the exhausted girl collapsed into her arms.
===Chinese===
经理正顺着通道奔来，莱昂朝他一扬头，打发他继续照应去。这时，一个穿晚礼服的女人不知打哪儿出现了。

「让我来照应她，可以么？」她说；那精疲力竭的姑娘便瘫倒在她怀里。

===Original===
Gonsalez flew back to the box. The man was lying on the floor, and the manager, standing at the edge of the box, was addressing the audience.

“The gentleman has fainted, and I’m afraid his friend has become a little hysterical. I must apologize to you, ladies and gentlemen, for this interruption. If you will allow us a minute to clear the box, the play will be resumed. If there is a doctor in the house, I should be glad if he would come.”
===Chinese===
冈萨雷斯飞奔回包厢。那人躺在地板上，经理站在包厢边沿，正对观众说话。

「这位先生晕过去了，他的同伴恐怕也有些歇斯底里。诸位女士先生，这番打搅，我必须致歉。请容我们片刻工夫把包厢清理出来，演出随即继续。场内若有医生在座，若肯移步过来，我将不胜感激。」

===Original===
There were two doctors within reach, and in the passage, which was now guarded by a commissionaire, a hasty examination was made. They examined the punctured wound at the back of the neck and then looked at one another.

“This is The Snake,” said one.

“The house mustn’t know,” said Manfred. “He’s dead, of course?”

The doctor nodded.
===Chinese===
近旁就请到了两位医生；在此时已有一名门警把守的通道里，一场仓促的查验做完了。他们察看了颈后那个被刺穿的小孔，随即面面相觑。

「这是『毒蛇』干的。」其中一位说。

「不能让满场的人知道。」曼弗雷德说。「人自然是死了？」

医生点了点头。

===Original===
Out in the passage was a big emergency exit door, and this the manager pushed open, and, running out into the street, found a cab, into which all that was mortal of Monty Newton was lifted.

Whilst this was being done, Poiccart returned.
===Chinese===
通道尽头有一扇高大的太平门；经理把它推开，奔到街上寻着一辆出租汽车，蒙蒂·牛顿那具凡人的躯壳，便被抬了进去。

这边忙着的当儿，波伊卡特回来了。

===Original===
“His car has just driven off,” he said. “I saw the numberplate as it turned into Lisle Street.”

“How long ago?” asked Gonsalez quickly.

“At this very moment.”

Leon pinched his lip thoughtfully.

“Why didn’t he wait, I wonder?”
===Chinese===
「他的车刚刚开走。」他说。「拐进莱尔街（Lisle Street）的时候，我看见了车牌。」

「多久以前的事？」冈萨雷斯急问。

「就是这一刻。」

莱昂若有所思地捏了捏嘴唇。

「他为什么不等等呢，我倒纳闷。」

===Original===
He went back through the emergency door, which was being closed, and passed up the passage towards the entrance. The box was on the dress-circle level, and the end of a short passage brought him into the circle itself.

And then the thought of the lame man occurred to him, and his eyes sought the first seat in the front row, which was also the seat nearest to the boxes. The man had gone.

As he made this discovery, George emerged from the passage.
===Chinese===
他从那扇正要关上的太平门折了回去，顺着通道朝正门走。包厢设在二楼前座一层，一条短通道走到尽头，便进了楼座之中。

这时他想起那个瘸腿男人，目光便去寻二楼前排的头一个座位——那也是离包厢最近的一个座位。人已经不在了。

他这一发现刚落定，乔治从通道里走了出来。

===Original===
“Gurther!” said Leon. “What a fool I am! But how clever!”

“Gurther?” said Manfred in amazement. “Do you mean the man with the club foot?”

Leon nodded.
===Chinese===
「格瑟！」莱昂说。「我多蠢！可他多高明！」

「格瑟？」曼弗雷德惊道。「你说的是那个拐脚的男人？」

莱昂点了点头。

===Original===
“He was not alone, of course,” said Gonsalez. “There must have been two or three of the gang here, men and women﻿—Oberzohn works these schemes out with the care and thoroughness of a general. I wonder where the management have taken the girl?”
===Chinese===
「他当然不是孤身一人。」冈萨雷斯说。「这里必定还有帮中两三个人，男女都有——欧伯佐恩筹划这些局，用的是一位将军的缜密与周全。也不知剧院的人把那姑娘带到哪儿去了？」

===Original===
He found the manager discussing the tragedy with two other men, one of whom was obviously associated with the production, and he signalled him aside.

“The lady? I suppose she’s gone home. She’s left the theatre.”

“Which way did she go?” asked Gonsalez, in a sudden panic.

The manager called a linkman, who had seen a middle-aged woman come out of the theatre with a weeping girl, and they had gone down the side-street towards the little square at the back of the playhouse.
===Chinese===
他寻见经理正同另外两人商量这场悲剧，其中一位显然与这出戏的排演有干系；他招手把经理唤到一旁。

「那位女士？我看是回家了。她已经出了剧院。」

「她往哪边走的？」冈萨雷斯问道，心里猛地发慌。

经理唤来一名执灯侍者；那人看见一个中年妇人领着个泪人儿似的姑娘走出剧院，顺着边街往戏院后面的小广场去了。

===Original===
“She may have taken her home to Chester Square,” said Manfred. His voice belied the assumption of confidence.
===Chinese===
「她兴许把她送回切斯特广场的家了。」曼弗雷德说。他那副故作笃定的口吻，却被嗓音本身拆穿了。

===Original===
Leon had not brought his own machine, and they drove to Chester Square in a taxi. Fred, the footman, had neither heard nor seen the girl, and nearly fainted when he learned of the tragic ending to his master’s career.
===Chinese===
莱昂没把自己的车开来，两人便乘出租汽车去了切斯特广场。男仆弗雷德既没听过也没见过那姑娘；待得知主人这番事业落得这般惨淡收场，他险些晕了过去。

===Original===
“Oh, my God!” he groaned. “And he only left here this afternoon﻿ ﻿… dead, you say?”

Gonsalez nodded.

“Not﻿—not The Snake?” faltered the man.

“What do you know about the snake?” demanded Manfred sternly.

“Nothing, except﻿—well, the snake made him nervous, I know. He told me today that he hoped he’d get through the week without a snakebite.”
===Chinese===
「哦，我的老天爷！」他呻吟道。「他今天下午才离开这儿……你说，死了？」

冈萨雷斯点了点头。

「不是——不是『毒蛇』吧？」那人声颤地问。

「关于毒蛇，你知道些什么？」曼弗雷德厉声诘问。

「没什么，只是——唉，那毒蛇闹得他心神不安，这我知道。他今天还跟我说，但愿这个礼拜能平平安安过去，别挨上蛇咬。」

===Original===
He was questioned closely, but although it was clear that he knew something of his master’s illicit transactions, and that he was connected in business with Oberzohn, the footman had no connection with the doctor’s gang. He drew a large wage and a percentage of profits from the gaming side of the business, and confessed that it was part of his duties to prepare stacks of cards and pass them to his master under cover of bringing in the drinks. But of anything more sinister he knew nothing.
===Chinese===
他们细细盘问了他。虽说显然他对主人那些不法营生略有所知，在生意上也同欧伯佐恩有勾连，这男仆却与博士那一伙毫无牵涉。他领着高薪，还从这营生的赌台那一头抽取红利；他并且供认，他的职分之一，便是把一沓沓码好的纸牌备齐，借着送酒进屋的掩护递到主人手里。但再深一层的不祥勾当，他便一概不知了。

===Original===
“The woman, of course, was a confederate, who had been planted to take charge of the girl the moment the snake struck. I was in such a state of mind,” confessed Leon, “that I do not even remember what she looked like. I am a fool﻿—a double-distilled idiot! I think I must be getting old. There’s only one thing for us to do, and that is to get back to Curzon Street﻿—something may have turned up.”
===Chinese===
「那女人，自然是同党，是事先安插下的，毒蛇一出击，便把姑娘接了过去。我当时那副心绪，」莱昂坦承道，「竟连她长什么模样都不记得了。我是个傻瓜——一个蒸馏过两遍的十足蠢货！我看我是老了。眼下咱们只有一件事好做，就是回柯曾街去——那边说不定有了动静。」

===Original===
“Did you leave anybody in the house?”

Leon nodded. “Yes, I left one of our men, to take any phone messages that came through.”
===Chinese===
「宅子里你留了人么？」

莱昂点了点头。「留了，留了咱们一个人，接听打过来的电话。」

===Original===
They paid off the taxi before the house, and Leon sprinted to the garage to get the car. The man who opened the door to them was he who had been tied up by the pedlar at Heavytree Farm, and his first words came as a shock to Manfred:
===Chinese===
两人在宅邸门前付清了车钱，莱昂飞奔到车库去取车。给他们开门的，正是当初在赫维特里农场被那货郎捆起来的那个人；他的第一句话，便叫曼弗雷德吃了一惊：

===Original===
“Digby’s here, sir.”

“Digby?” said the other in surprise. “I thought he was on duty?”

“He’s been here since just after you left, sir. If I’d known where you had gone, I’d have sent him to you.”
===Chinese===
「迪格比来了，先生。」

「迪格比？」对方愕然道。「他不是该当值么？」

「你们前脚刚走他就到了，先生。早知道你们去了哪儿，我就打发他追你们去了。」

===Original===
Digby came out of the waiting-room at that moment, ready to apologize.

“I had to see you, sir, and I’m sorry I’m away from my post.”

“You may not be missing much,” said Manfred unsmilingly. “Come upstairs and tell me all about it.”
===Chinese===
正在这时，迪格比从待客室里走了出来，一副预备告罪的模样。

「我必须见您一面，先生，擅离了岗位，很是对不住。」

「你未必因此误了什么大事。」曼弗雷德不苟言笑地说。「上楼来，原原本本讲给我听。」

===Original===
Digby’s story was a strange one. He had gone down that afternoon to the canal bank to make a reconnaissance of ground which was new to him.
===Chinese===
迪格比讲的事颇为离奇。那天下午，他去了运河岸一带，踏勘一块他未曾识得的地界。

===Original===
“I’m glad I did too, because the walls have got broken glass on top. I went up into the Old Kent Road and bought a garden hoe, and prised the mortar loose, so that if I wanted, I could get over. And then I climbed round the water-gate and had a look at that barge of his. There was nobody about, though I think they spotted me afterwards. It is a fairly big barge, and, of course, in a terrible state, but the hold is full of cargo﻿—you know that, sir?”
===Chinese===
「亏得我去了，那墙头上镶着碎玻璃。我上到老肯特路（Old Kent Road），买了把园艺锄，把灰浆撬松了，这样我要想过去，就能过得去。随后我从水门那一面翻爬过去，把他那条驳船瞧了一眼。四下里没有人，不过我想他们后来还是察觉我了。那是条挺大的驳船，自然，破败得不成样子，可船舱里满载着货——这个您知道么，先生？」

===Original===
“You mean there is something in the barge?”

Digby nodded.

“Yes, it has a load of some kind. The after part, where the bargee’s sleeping quarters are, is full of rats and water, but the fore part of the vessel is watertight, and it holds something heavy too. That is why the barge is down by its head in the mud. I was in the Thames police and I know a lot about river craft.”
===Chinese===
「你是说，船里装着东西？」

迪格比点了点头。

「是，装着一船什么货。船尾那一截，船夫睡舱所在的地方，满是老鼠和积水；可船头那一截不漏水，里头也压着什么沉家伙。所以那条驳船才船头朝下，陷在泥里。我在泰晤士河警察当过差，河上的船只我懂得不少。」

===Original===
“Is that what you came to tell me?”

“No, sir, it was something queerer than that. After I’d given the barge a look over and tried to pull up some of the boards﻿—which I didn’t manage to do﻿—I went along and had a look at the factory. It’s not so easy to get in, because the entrance faces the house, but to get to it you have to go half round the building, and that gives you a certain amount of cover. There was nothing I could see in the factory itself. It was in a terrible mess, full of old iron and burnt-out boxes. I was coming round the back of the building,” he went on impressively, “when I smelt a peculiar scent.”
===Chinese===
「你来，就是为了告诉我这个？」

「不是，先生，还有比这更蹊跷的事。我把驳船里外看了一遍，又试着撬起几块舱板——没能撬动——便顺着走过去，把厂房瞧了一眼。进去不太容易，因为大门正对着那宅子，要走到门口，得绕着楼走上半圈，好在这样反倒有几分遮蔽。厂房里头，我看不出什么名堂。乱得一塌糊涂，堆满了废铁和烧空的箱子。我绕到楼后头的时候，」他说得郑重起来，「闻到了一股特别的香气。」

===Original===
“A perfume?”

“Yes, sir, it was perfume, but stronger﻿—more like incense. I thought at first it might be an old bale of stuff that had been thrown out, or else I was deceiving myself. I began poking about in the rubbish heaps﻿—but *they* didn’t smell of scent! Then I went back into the building again, but there was no smell at all. It was very strong when I returned to the back of the factory, and then I saw a little waft of smoke come out of a ventilator close to the ground. My first idea was that the place was on fire, but when I knelt down, it was this scent.”
===Chinese===
「香水？」

「是，先生，是香水味，可更冲——更像熏香。起初我当是有人扔出来的一包陈货，再不然就是我自己在骗自己。我便在垃圾堆里扒拉起来——可*它们*身上并没有香水味！随后我又回楼里去转了一圈，一点味儿也没有。等我再回到厂房后头，那气味浓得很，跟着我就看见一缕轻烟，从贴着地面的一个通风口里飘出来。我头一个念头是那地方着了火，可跪下去一闻，是这股香气。」

===Original===
“Joss-sticks?” said Poiccart quickly.

“That’s what it was!” said the detective. “Like incense, yet not like it. I knelt down and listened at the grating, and I’ll swear that I heard voices. They were very faint.”

“Men’s?”

“No, women’s.”

“Could you see anything?”

“No, sir, it was a blind ventilator; there was probably a shaft there﻿—in fact, I’m sure there was, because I pushed a stone through one of the holes and heard it drop some distance down.”
===Chinese===
「线香？」波伊卡特脱口问道。

「正是那玩意儿！」那密探说。「像熏香，又不像。我跪下去，贴着格栅听，我可以起誓，我听见了人声。很轻很轻。」

「男人的？」

「不，女人的。」

「能瞧见什么么？」

「瞧不见，先生，那是个不通光的死通风口；后头多半有口竖井——实际上我敢肯定有，因为我从一个窟窿里塞进一块石头，听见它落下去老深一段。」

===Original===
“There may be an underground room there,” said Poiccart, “and somebody’s burnt joss-sticks to sweeten the atmosphere.”

“Under the factory? It’s not in the plans of the building. I’ve had them from the surveyor’s office and examined them,” said George, “although surveyors’ plans aren’t infallible. A man like Oberzohn would not hesitate to break so unimportant a thing as a building law!”
===Chinese===
「那底下可能有间地下室，」波伊卡特说，「有人烧了线香，把里头的气味熏得好闻些。」

「厂房底下？楼房的图纸上可没有。我从测量官署调了图纸来，仔细看过。」乔治说，「虽说测量官的图纸也未必万无一失。欧伯佐恩那种人，区区一条建筑法这样无关紧要的东西，他毫不在乎就会违反！」

===Original===
Leon came in at that moment, heard the story and was in complete agreement with Poiccart’s theory.

“I wondered at the time we saw the plans whether we ought to accept that as conclusive,” he said. “The store was built at the end of 1914, when architects and builders took great liberties and pleaded the exigencies of the war.”
===Chinese===
莱昂正在这时进来，听了这番话，对波伊卡特的推断深表赞同。

「当初看图纸的时候我就犯嘀咕，该不该拿它当定论。」他说。「那座货栈是 1914 年年底建的，那年头，建筑师和营造商都放胆妄为，动不动就抬出战事紧急来当挡箭牌。」

===Original===
Digby went on with his story.

“I was going back to the barge to get past the water-gate, but I saw the old man coming down the steps of the house, so I climbed the wall, and very glad I was that I’d shifted that broken glass, or I should never have got over.”
===Chinese===
迪格比接着讲他的事。

「我本要回到驳船那边、打水门跟前过去，却看见那老头儿正从宅子的台阶上下来，我便翻墙出了来。多亏我先前撬掉了那些碎玻璃，不然压根儿休想翻得过去。」

===Original===
Manfred pulled his watch from his pocket with a frown. They had lost nearly an hour of precious time with their inquiries in Chester Square.

“I hope we’re not too late,” he said ominously. “Now, Leon﻿ ﻿…”

But Leon had gone down the stairs in three strides.
===Chinese===
曼弗雷德皱着眉，从口袋里掏出怀表。在切斯特广场的一番查问，已叫他们损失了将近一个钟头的宝贵工夫。

「但愿还不太迟。」他说，话音里透着不祥。「好了，莱昂……」

可莱昂早已三大步跨下楼去了。

## XXX Joan a Prisoner / 三十 琼沦为囚徒

===Original===

Dazed with grief, not knowing, not seeing, not caring, not daring to think, Joan suffered herself to be led quickly into the obscurity of the side-street, and did not even realize that Oberzohn’s big limousine had drawn up by the sidewalk.

===Chinese===

琼叫悲痛砸得昏昏沉沉，不知道，看不见，不在乎，也不敢想，任人牵着疾步走进侧街的暗影里，连欧伯佐恩那辆大型豪华轿车已经停靠在人行道边，她都没有察觉。

===Original===

“Get in,” said the woman harshly.

Joan was pushed through the door and guided to a seat by somebody who was already in the machine.

She collapsed in a corner moaning as the door slammed and the car began to move.

===Chinese===

「上车。」那女人厉声喝道。

琼被从车门里推进去，由车里先在的一个人引到座位上。

车门砰地关上，车开动了，她瘫在角落里呜咽着。

===Original===

“Where are we going? Let me get back to him!”

“The gracious lady will please restrain her grief,” said a hateful voice, and she swung round and stared unseeingly to the place whence the voice had come.

The curtains of the car had been drawn; the interior was as black as pitch.

“You﻿—you beast!” she gasped. “It’s you, is it?﻿ ﻿… Gurther! You murdering beast!”

===Chinese===

「这是上哪儿去？让我回到他身边去！」

「雍容的小姐还请节哀。」一个可憎的声音说道；她霍地转过身，朝那声音来处直瞪着，什么也看不见。

车帘早已拉严；车厢里黑得像沥青一般。

「你——你这畜生！」她倒抽一口气。「是你，是不是？……格瑟！你这杀人的畜生！」

===Original===

She struck at him feebly, but he caught her wrist.

“The gracious lady will most kindly restrain her grief,” he said suavely. “The Herr Newton is not dead. It was a little trick in order to baffle certain interferers.”

===Chinese===

她有气无力地朝他打去，却被他攥住了手腕。

「雍容的小姐还请大发慈悲，节住哀伤。」他温文地说道。「牛顿先生并没有死。那是一个小小的计谋，为的是摆脱几位碍事之辈。」

===Original===

“You’re lying, you’re lying!” she screamed, struggling to escape from those hands of steel. “He’s dead! You know he’s dead, and you killed him! You snake-man!”

===Chinese===

「你撒谎，你撒谎！」她尖叫着，拼命想挣脱那双钢铁般的手。「他死了！你知道他死了，是你杀了他！你这蛇人！」

===Original===

“The gracious lady must believe me,” said Gurther earnestly. They were passing through a public part of the town and at any moment a policeman might hear her shrieks. “If Herr Newton had not pretended to be hurt, he would have been arrested﻿ ﻿… he follows in the next car.”

===Chinese===

「雍容的小姐务必相信我。」格瑟一脸恳切地说。车子正穿行在城里的热闹地段，她的尖叫随时会落进警察的耳朵。「牛顿先生若不是假装负了伤，早就被逮起来了……他坐的正是后面那辆车。」

===Original===

“You’re trying to quieten me,” she said, “but I won’t be quiet.”

And then a hand came over her mouth and pressed her head back against the cushions. She struggled desperately, but two fingers slid up her face and compressed her nostrils. She was being suffocated. She struggled to free herself from the tentacle hold of him, and then slipped into unconsciousness.

===Chinese===

「你是想哄住我。」她说，「可我偏不罢休。」

接着，一只手捂上她的嘴，把她的头按回靠垫上。她拼命挣扎，可两根手指顺着她的脸颊滑上来，捏住了她的鼻孔。她正在被窒息。她拼命想挣脱他那触手一般的钳制，随后便滑进了昏迷。

===Original===

Gurther felt the straining figure go limp and removed his hands. She did not feel the prick of the needle on her wrist, though the drugging was clumsily performed in the darkness and in a car that was swaying from side to side. He felt her pulse, his long fingers pressed her throat and felt the throb of the carotid artery; propping her so that she could not fall, Herr Gurther sank back luxuriously into a corner of the limousine and lit a cigar.

===Chinese===

格瑟觉出那具紧绷绷挣动的身子瘫软了下去，便撤了手。针尖刺上她手腕，她毫无知觉——尽管这一针是在黑暗里、在一辆左右摇晃的汽车上笨手笨脚扎下去的。他搭了搭她的脉，长手指按着她的咽喉，试了试颈动脉的搏动；再把她架稳扶好、教她跌不下来，格瑟先生这才舒舒服服往豪华轿车的角落里一仰，点起一支雪茄。

===Original===

The journey was soon over. In a very short time they were bumping down Hangman’s Lane and turned so abruptly into the factory grounds that one of the mudguards buckled to the impact of the gatepost.

===Chinese===

路程很快走完。不多时，车子已颠簸着驶下绞刑吏巷，又那么急猛地拐进厂区，一只挡泥板撞上门柱，顿时撞得凹了进去。

---

===Original===

It must have been two hours after the departure of her companion, when Mirabelle, lying on her bed, half dozing, was wakened by her book slipping to the floor, and sat up quickly to meet the apprising stare of the man whom, of all men in the world, she disliked most cordially. Dr. Oberzohn had come noiselessly into the room and under his arm was a pile of books.

===Chinese===

同伴走后约莫两个钟头，米拉贝尔正躺在床上半睡半醒，手里的书滑落在地，把她惊醒了；她霍然坐起，正对上一双打量她的眼睛——这双眼睛的主人，恰恰是她在这世上男人当中最打心底厌恶的那一个。欧伯佐恩博士悄没声儿地进了屋，腋下夹着一摞书。

===Original===

“I have brought these for you,” he said, in his booming voice, and stacked them neatly on the table.

She did not answer.

“Novels of a frivolous kind, such as you will enjoy,” he said, unconscious of offence. “I desired the seller of the books to pick them for me. Fiction stories of adventure and of amorous exchanges. These will occupy your mind, though to me they would be the merest rubbish and nonsense.”

===Chinese===

「我把这些给你带来了。」他用他那轰鸣般的大嗓门说着，把书整整齐齐码在桌上。

她没有答话。

「轻浮一类的小说，你会喜欢的那种。」他说，浑然不觉此话开罪于人。「我特意嘱托卖书的替我挑拣。冒险的故事，还有谈情说爱的故事。这些可以占住你的心思，虽说在我眼里，它们纯属垃圾与胡话。」

===Original===

She stood silently, her hands clasped behind her, watching him. He was neater than usual, had resumed the frock-coat he wore the day she had first met him﻿—how long ago that seemed!﻿—his collar was stiffly white, and if his cravat was more gorgeous than is usually seen in a man correctly arrayed, it had the complementary value of being new.

He held in his hand a small bouquet of flowers tightly packed, their stems enclosed in silver foil, a white paper frill supplying an additional expression of gentility.

===Chinese===

她静静地站着，双手交叠在背后，望着他。他比平日更齐整，又穿上了她初次见他那天他穿的那件长礼服——那仿佛已是多久以前的事了！——领子浆得雪白挺硬；他那条领巾虽说艳丽得超出一个衣冠端正的男子身上惯见的式样，倒也另有一层可取之处：它是崭新的。

他手里拿着一小束扎得紧紧的鲜花，花茎裹在银箔里，一圈白纸褶边又为它平添几分斯文气派。

===Original===

“These are for you.” He jerked out his hand towards her.

Mirabelle looked at the flowers, but did not take them. He seemed in no way disconcerted, either by her silence, or by the antagonism which her attitude implied, but, laying the flowers on top of the books, he clasped his hands before him and addressed her. He was nervous, for some reason; the skin of his forehead was furrowing and smoothing with grotesque rapidity. She watched the contortions, fascinated.

===Chinese===

「这是给你的。」他把手朝她猛地一伸。

米拉贝尔看了看那束花，却没有接。她的沉默也罢，她的姿态所含的敌意也罢，似乎都丝毫没叫他乱了方寸；他把花搁在书堆顶上，双手交握在身前，对她讲起话来。不知怎的，他很紧张；额头上的皮正以一种怪诞的速度皱起又平复。她瞧着那番抽动，瞧得入了迷。

===Original===

“To every man,” he began, “there comes a moment of domestic allurement. Even to the scientific mind, absorbed in its colossal problems, there is this desire for family life and for the haven of rest which is called marriage.”

===Chinese===

「每一个男人，」他开腔道，「都会等到一个受家庭诱惑的时刻。即便是一颗沉潜于宏大难题的科学头脑，也存着这份对家庭生活的渴望，渴望那个名为婚姻的安息之港。」

===Original===

He paused, as though he expected her to offer some comment upon his platitude.

“Man alone,” he went on, when she did not speak, “has established an artificial and unnatural convention that, at a certain age, a man should marry a woman of that same age. Yet it has been proved by history that happy marriages are often between a man who is in the eyes of the world old, and a lady who is youthful.”

She was gazing at him in dismay. Was he proposing to her? The idea was incredible, almost revolting. He must have read in her face the thoughts that were uppermost in her mind, the loathing, the sense of repulsion which filled her, yet he went on, unabashed:

===Chinese===

他顿了顿，仿佛指望她对他的陈词滥调发表几句评点。

见她不作声，他又接着往下讲：「唯有人类，立下了一条人为的、悖逆自然的惯例：男人到了某个年纪，就该娶上同一年纪的女人。然而历史已然证明，美满的姻缘，往往缔结在世人眼中的老夫与青春的淑女之间。」

她惶惑地瞪着他。他这是在向她求婚？这念头匪夷所思，简直叫人作呕。她脑中翻腾得最凶的那些念头——那股憎恶、那阵充塞胸中的反感——必定都写在她脸上了，他却毫不知耻地讲了下去：

===Original===

“I am a man of great riches. You are a girl of considerable poverty. But because I saw you one day in your poor house, looking, gracious lady, like a lily growing amidst foul weeds, my heart went out to you, and for this reason I brought you to London, spending many thousands of pounds in order to give myself the pleasure of your company.”

===Chinese===

「我是极富有的人。你是相当清贫的姑娘。只因有一天，我在你那贫寒的宅子里望见了你，望上去，雍容的小姐，就像一株长在秽草丛中的百合，我的心便朝你飞去了；为了这个缘故，我把你带到伦敦，花去好几千镑，好让我自己享受与你作伴的乐趣。」

===Original===

“I don’t think you need go any farther, Dr. Oberzohn,” she said quietly, “if you’re proposing marriage, as I think you are.”

He nodded emphatically.

“Such is my honourable intention,” he said.

===Chinese===

「我看您不必再往下说了，欧伯佐恩博士。」她平静地说，「倘若您这是在求婚——我想您正是。」

他郑重其事地点了点头。

「这正是我光明正大的意图。」他说。

===Original===

“I would never marry you in any circumstances,” she said. “Not even if I had met you under the happiest conditions. The question of your age”﻿—she nearly added “and of your appearance,” but her natural kindness prevented that cruel thrust, though it would not have hurt him in the slightest degree﻿—“has nothing whatever to do with my decision. I do not even like you, and have never liked you, Mr. Oberzohn.”

===Chinese===

「无论什么境况，我都绝不嫁给您。」她说。「哪怕我是在最称心如意的境遇下认识您的，也绝不。您年纪的问题」——她差点要补上一句『还有您相貌的问题』，只是她天生仁厚，没让这句刻薄话出口，虽说它根本伤不着他分毫——「与我的决定毫无干系。我连喜欢您都谈不上，从来就没有喜欢过，欧伯佐恩先生。」

===Original===

“Doctor,” he corrected, and in spite of her woeful plight she could have laughed at this insistence upon the ceremonial title.

“Young miss, I cannot woo you in the way of my dear and sainted brother, who was all for ladies and had a beautiful manner.”

She was amazed to hear that he had a brother at all﻿—and it was almost a relief to know that he was dead.

===Chinese===

「是博士。」他纠正道；尽管自身处境凄惨，她险些被这份对礼敬头衔的死抠逗得笑出来。

「年轻的小姐，我没法像我亲爱的、圣徒一般的兄长那样向你求爱——他一心都在女士们身上，风度又优美。」

听说他居然有过一个兄长，她已够惊奇的——听说那兄长已不在人世，她几乎要松一口气。

===Original===

“Martyred, at the hands of wicked and cunning murderers, slain in his prime by the assassin’s pistol﻿ ﻿…” His voice trembled and broke. “For that sainted life I will some day take vengeance.”

It was not wholly curiosity that impelled her to ask who killed him.

===Chinese===

「殉了难，死在奸恶而狡诈的凶手手里，正当盛年，丧命于刺客的枪口之下……」他的声音颤抖着，哽住了。「为了那条圣徒般的性命，我总有一天要复仇。」

促使她问出是谁杀的，已不单是好奇。

===Original===

“Leon Gonsalez.” The words in his lips became the grating of a file. “Killed﻿ ﻿… murdered! And even his beautiful picture destroyed in that terrible fire. Had he saved that, my heart would have been soft towards him.” He checked himself, evidently realizing that he was getting away from the object of his call. “Think over this matter, young lady. Read the romantic books and the amorous books, and then perhaps you will not think it is so terrible a fate to drift at moonlight through the canals of Venice, with the moon above and the gondoliers.”

===Chinese===

「莱昂·冈萨雷斯。」这几个字到了他嘴上，便成了锉刀锉动的声响。「杀了……谋害了！连他那张漂亮的画像，也毁在那场可怕的大火里。他若救下了那张画，我的心对他原会软下来。」他猛地收住话头，显然察觉自己岔开了此来的正题。「这件事你细细思量，年轻的小姐。读一读那些传奇的书、情爱的书，到那时你或许便不觉得，在月光里随波荡过威尼斯的运河、头顶一轮明月、身边船夫摇橹，算得上多可怕的命运了。」

===Original===

He wagged his head sentimentally.

“There is no book which will change my view, doctor,” she said. “I cannot understand why you propose such an extraordinary course, but I would rather die than marry you.”

===Chinese===

他多愁善感地摇着脑袋。

「没有任何书能改变我的看法，博士。」她说。「我实在不明白，您为什么要提出这样一条异乎寻常的路数，可我宁可死，也不嫁给您。」

===Original===

His cold eyes filled her with a quick terror.

“There are worse things than death, which is but sleep﻿—many worse things, young miss. Tomorrow I shall come for you, and we will go into the country, where you will say ‘yes’ and ‘no’ according to my desire. I have many﻿—what is the word?﻿—certificates for marriage, for I am too clever a man to leave myself without alternatives.”

(This was true; he had residential qualifications in at least four counties, and at each he had given legal notice of his intended marriage.)

===Chinese===

他那双冷酷的眼睛，霎时叫她浑身生出一阵恐惧。

「比死更可怕的事有的是——死不过是场睡眠——比它可怕得多的事有的是，年轻的小姐。明天我来接你，我们到乡下去，到了那儿，你说『是』与『不是』，都得依我的心愿。我有许多——那个词怎么讲来着？——结婚证书，因为我是个太聪明的人，不会不给自己留下退路。」

（这倒不假：他至少在四个郡具备居住资格，而且在每一处都为拟办的婚事递过法定预告。）

===Original===

“Not tomorrow or any other day. Nothing would induce me.”

His eyebrows went almost to the top of his head.

“So!” he said, with such significance that her blood ran cold. “There are worse men than the Herr Doktor,”﻿—he raised a long finger warningly﻿—“terrible men with terrible minds. You have met Gurther?”

===Chinese===

「明天不去，哪一天也不去。什么都休想劝动我。」

他的两条眉毛几乎挑到了头顶。

「哦！」他这一声意味深长，直叫她血液发凉。「比博士先生更坏的男人有的是」——他警告似的竖起一根长长的手指——「可怕的男人，长着可怕的头脑。你见过格瑟的吧？」

===Original===

She did not answer this.

“Yes, yes, you danced with him. A nice man, is he not, to ladies? Yet this same Gurther﻿ ﻿… I will tell you something.”

He seated himself on a corner of the table and began talking, until she covered her ears with her hands and hid her white face from him.

===Chinese===

这一句她没有回答。

「见了，见了，你同他跳过舞。他对女士们岂不是位很可爱的人么？然而就是这个格瑟……我要告诉你一件事。」

他在桌角上坐下，开始讲起来，直讲得她双手捂住耳朵，把煞白的脸偏开不让他看见。

===Original===

“They would have killed him for that,” he said, when her hands came down, “but Gurther was too clever, and the poor German peasants too stupid. You shall remember that, shall you not?”

He did not wait for her answer. With a stiff bow he strutted out of the room and up the stairs. There came the thud of the trap falling and the inevitable rumble of the concrete barrel.

===Chinese===

「换了旁人，早为那件事叫他们弄死了。」等她的手放下来，他说道，「可格瑟太精明，那些可怜的德国乡下人太蠢。这一点你给我记住了，好不好？」

他不等她回答。僵硬地一躬之后，他昂首阔步出了屋，上了楼梯。接着传来活板门落下的闷响，和那阵照例响起的水泥桶的隆隆声。

===Original===

He had some work to do, heavy work for a man who found himself panting when he climbed stairs. And though four of his best and most desperate men were waiting in his parlour drinking his whisky and filling the little room with their rank cigar smoke, he preferred to tackle this task which he had already begun as soon as night fell, without their assistance or knowledge.

===Chinese===

他还有一件活计要做——对一个爬楼梯都要喘粗气的男人来说，是件重活。虽说有四名他手下最得力、最亡命的汉子正在他客厅里候着，喝他的威士忌，拿他们那股呛人的雪茄烟味灌满小屋，他还是宁可独自去接着干天一擦黑就已动手的那件活计——不要他们搭手，也不让他们知晓。

===Original===

On the edge of the deep hole in his grounds, where the wild convolvulus grew amidst the rusty corners of discarded tins and oil barrels, was a patch of earth that yielded easily to the spade. When the factory had been built, the depression had been bigger, but the builders had filled in half the hole with the light soil that they had dug out of the factory’s foundations.

He took his spade, which he had left in the factory, and, skirting the saucer-shaped depression, he reached a spot where a long trench had already been dug. Taking off his fine coat and waistcoat, unfastening cravat and collar and carefully depositing them upon the folded coat, he continued his work, stopping now and again to wipe his streaming brow.

===Chinese===

在他宅地里那个深坑的边上——野生旋花开在废弃铁皮罐和油桶锈烂的犄角之间——有一片土质松软、锹到即开的土地。当年建厂时，这凹坑还要更大，只是营造的人们用从厂房地基里挖出的浮土，把坑回填了一半。

他取了留在厂房里的那把铁锹，绕着碟子形的凹坑走，走到一处早已挖开一条长沟的地方。他脱下讲究的上衣和马甲，解开领巾与领子，把它们仔细安置在叠好的上衣上，接着干起活来，不时停下擦一擦汗涔涔的额头。

===Original===

He had to labour in the dark, but this was no disadvantage; he could feel the edges of the pit. In an hour the top of the trench was level with his chin, and, stooping to clear the bottom of loose soil, he climbed up with greater difficulty than he had anticipated, and it was only after the third attempt that he managed to reach the top, out of breath and short of temper.

He dressed again, and with his electric torch surveyed the pit he had made and grunted his satisfaction.

===Chinese===

他只得在黑暗里劳作，这却不算什么不便；坑的边沿他摸得着。一个钟头下来，沟沿已齐到他下巴的高度；他弯腰清净沟底的浮土，往上爬时却比预料的吃力得多，直到第三回才勉强爬上沟沿，气喘吁吁，心头火起。

他重新穿好衣裳，用手电筒照看自己挖成的坑，满意地哼了一声。

===Original===

He was keenly sensitive to certain atmospheres, and needed no information about the change which had come over his subordinates. In their last consultation Gurther had been less obsequious, had even smoked in his presence without permission﻿—absentmindedly, perhaps, but the offence was there. And Dr. Oberzohn, on the point of smacking his face for his insolence, heard a warning voice within himself which had made his hand drop back at his side. Or was it the look he saw on Gurther’s face? The man was beyond the point where he could discipline him in the old Junker way. For although Dr. Oberzohn contemned all things Teutonic, he had a sneaking reverence for the military caste of that nation.

===Chinese===

他对某些气氛素来敏感至极，手下人身上起了什么变化，无须谁来报信。上一回议事，格瑟就不那么卑躬屈节了，甚至未经许可就在他面前抽起烟来——兴许是一时出神，可冒犯总归是冒犯。欧伯佐恩博士正要为他这无礼掴他一个嘴巴，心里却响起一个警告的声音，叫他的手垂回了身侧。又或者，是他瞧见了格瑟脸上的那副神色？这个人已经越出了界限，不是他还能用老容克那一套加以训诫的了。因为欧伯佐恩博士尽管对条顿的一切统统鄙夷，对那个国度的军人阶级，却暗怀一分敬畏。

===Original===

He left the spade sticking in a heap of turned earth. He would need that again, and shortly. Unless Gurther failed. Somehow he did not anticipate a failure in this instance. Mr. Monty Newton had not yet grown suspicious, would not be on his guard. His easy acceptance of the theatre ticket showed his mind in this respect.

The four men in his room rose respectfully as he came in. The air was blue with smoke, and Lew Cuccini offered a rough apology. He had been released that morning from detention, for Meadows had found it difficult to frame a charge which did not expose the full activities of the police, and the part they were playing in relation to Mirabelle Leicester. Evidently Cuccini had been reproaching, in his own peculiar way and in his own unprincipled language, the cowardice of his three companions, for the atmosphere seemed tense when the doctor returned. Yet, as was subsequently proved, the appearance of discord was deceptive; might indeed have been staged for their host’s benefit.

===Chinese===

他把铁锹插在一堆翻起的土上。这物件他还要用，就在近期。除非格瑟失手。不知怎的，他不认为这一回会有闪失。蒙蒂·牛顿先生还没起疑心，也不会严加防备。他那样痛快地收下戏票，正见得他在这上头毫无戒心。

他进屋时，屋里那四个人毕恭毕敬地站起身来。空气叫烟熏得一片蓝，卢·库奇尼粗声粗气地道了个歉。他那天早晨刚从拘留中放出来——因为米多斯发现，很难给他立一个不致暴露警方全部活动、不致暴露警方在米拉贝尔·莱斯特一案中所扮角色的罪名。显而易见，库奇尼方才正以他特有的做派、他那毫无原则的言语，数落着三个同伙的怯懦，因为博士回来时，屋里的气氛显得剑拔弩张。然而——事后也得到了证明——这不睦的表象是骗人的；说不定压根就是演给他们东道主看的一出戏。

===Original===

“I’ve just been telling these birds﻿—” began Cuccini.

“Oh, shut up, Lew!” growled one of his friends. “If that crazy man hadn’t been shouting your name, we should not have gone back! He’d have wakened the dead. And our orders were to retire at the first serious sign of an alarm. That’s right, doctor, isn’t it?”

“Sure it’s right,” said the doctor blandly. “Never be caught﻿—that is a good motto. Cuccini was caught.”

===Chinese===

「我正跟这几只鸟说——」库奇尼开了腔。

「哦，闭嘴吧，卢！」他的一个同伙咆哮道。「要不是那个疯子扯着嗓子喊你的名字，我们根本不会回去！他那嗓子能把死人都喊醒。况且给我们的号令是：警讯一露真章，立刻撤退。是这么下的令吧，博士，嗯？」

「自然是这么下的。」博士和颜悦色地说。「万万不可被逮住——这是条好箴言。库奇尼就被逮住了。」

===Original===

“And I’d give a year of my life to meet that Dago again,” said Cuccini, between his teeth.

He was delightfully inconsistent, for he came into the category, having been born in Milan, and had had his early education in the Italian quarter of Hartford, Connecticut.

===Chinese===

「我情愿拿一年的阳寿，再会一会那个拉丁佬。」库奇尼从牙缝里说。

他这人不讲逻辑倒讲得可爱：他自己正归在这一类里——他生在米兰，早年受的教育，是在康涅狄格州哈特福德（Hartford, Connecticut）的意大利人街区得来的。

===Original===

“He’d have tortured me too﻿ ﻿… he was going to put lighted wax matches between my fingers﻿—”

“And then you spilled it!” accused one of the three hotly. “You talk about us bolting!”

“Silence!” roared the doctor. “This is unseemly! I have forgiven everything. That shall be enough for you all. I will hear no other word.”

===Chinese===

「他本来也要给我上刑……他要把点着的蜡梗火柴夹在我手指头缝里——」

「结果你就全招了！」三人中的一个怒冲冲地抢白。「亏你还好意思讲我们开溜！」

「肃静！」博士吼道。「这成何体统！我一概宽恕了。这对你们诸位尽够了。旁的话我一个字也不要再听。」

===Original===

“Where is Gurther?” Cuccini asked the question.

“He has gone away. Tonight he leaves for America. He may return﻿—who knows? But that is the intention.”

“Snaking?” asked somebody, and there was a little titter of laughter.

===Chinese===

「格瑟在哪儿？」库奇尼问。

「他走了。今夜他动身去美洲。兴许还会回来——谁知道呢？但用意是如此。」

「放蛇？」有人问，跟着响起一小阵窃笑。

===Original===

“Say, doctor, how do you work that stunt?” Cuccini leaned forward, his cigar between his fingers, greatly intrigued. “I saw no snakes down at Rath Hall, and yet he was bitten, just as that Yankee was bitten﻿—Washington.”

“He will die,” said the doctor complacently. He was absurdly jealous for the efficacy of his method.

===Chinese===

「哎，博士，那套戏法您是怎么弄的？」库奇尼探身向前，指间夹着雪茄，兴味浓极了。「在拉思府我可没见着一条蛇，可他就是中了蛇咬，跟那个美国佬一样——华盛顿那回。」

「他会死的。」博士踌躇满志地说。他对自家手法的效力，怀着一种近乎荒唐的护忌。

===Original===

“He was alive yesterday, anyway. We shadowed him to the station.”

“Then he was not bitten﻿—no, that is impossible. When the snakebites,”﻿—Oberzohn raised his palms and gazed piously at the ceiling﻿—“after that there is nothing. No, no, my friend, you are mistaken.”

===Chinese===

「反正他昨天还活着。我们盯他盯到了车站。」

「那他就没被咬——不，这不可能。蛇一出口咬了人，」欧伯佐恩举起双掌，虔敬地仰望天花板，「过后便万事皆休。不，不，我的朋友，你弄错了。」

===Original===

“I tell you I’m not making any mistake,” said the other doggedly. “I was in the room, I tell you, soon after they brought him in, and I heard one of the busies say that his face was all wet.”

“So!” said Oberzohn dully. “That is very bad.”

“But how do you do it, doctor? Do you shoot or sump’n’?”

===Chinese===

「我告诉您，我没弄错。」那人执拗地说。「他们把他抬进去没多久，我就在那屋里，我听见一个条子说，他满脸是水。」

「哦！」欧伯佐恩木然道。「那就很糟了。」

「可您到底是怎么弄的，博士？开枪，还是怎么的？」

===Original===

“Let us talk about eventual wealth and happiness,” said the doctor. “Tonight is a night of great joy for me. I will sing you a song.”

Then, to the amazement of the men and to their great unhappiness, he sang, in a thin, reedy old voice, the story of a young peasant who had been thwarted in love and had thrown himself from a cliff into a seething waterfall. It was a lengthy song, intensely sentimental, and his voice held few of the qualities of music. The gang had never been set a more difficult job than to keep straight faces until he had finished.

===Chinese===

「咱们来谈谈日后的财富与幸福吧。」博士说。「今夜于我是大喜之夜。我给你们唱一支歌。」

于是，令这几个人惊愕、也令他们苦不堪言的是，他用一种又细又尖、苇子似的苍老嗓音，唱起一个年轻农夫的故事：那人在爱情上受了挫，从悬崖上一头扎进了翻腾咆哮的瀑布。这支歌冗长之至，缠绵悱恻之至，而他的嗓音几乎不沾音乐的边。这伙人一生里再没领受过比这更难的差事：绷住脸不笑，直熬到他唱完。

===Original===

“Gee! You’re some artist, doctor!” said the sycophantic Cuccini, and managed to get a simulation of envy into his voice.

“In my student days I was a great singer,” said the doctor modestly.

===Chinese===

「嚯！您可真是位艺术家，博士！」惯会谄媚的库奇尼说，还硬往嗓音里搀进了几分艳羡。

「我学生时代是个了不起的歌唱家呢。」博士谦逊地说。

===Original===

Over the mantelpiece was a big, old clock, with a face so faded that only a portion of the letters remained. Its noisy ticking had usually a sedative effect on the doctor. But its main purpose and value was its accuracy. Every day it was corrected by a message from Greenwich, and as Oberzohn’s success as an organizer depended upon exact timing, it was one of his most valuable assets.

He glanced up at the clock now, and that gave Cuccini his excuse.

===Chinese===

壁炉台上方摆着一座老式大钟，钟面褪了色，只剩下零星几个字母。它那吵闹的滴答声平日对博士有种安神之效。但它的主要用途与价值在于走时精准。每天格林尼治发来的讯号都要给它校一回时刻；欧伯佐恩身为组织者，成败全系于分秒不差，这座钟便是他最贵重的资产之一。

这时他抬眼看了看钟，库奇尼便趁此寻着了由头。

===Original===

“We’ll be getting along, doctor,” he said. “You don’t want anything tonight? I’d like to get a cut at that Gonsalez man. You won’t leave me out if there’s anything doing?”

Oberzohn rose and went out of the room without another word, for he knew that the rising of Cuccini was a signal that not only was the business of the day finished, but also that the gang needed its pay.

===Chinese===

「我们这就该走啦，博士。」他说。「今晚您没别的吩咐吧？我倒想寻着那个姓冈萨雷斯的，砍他一刀。要有什么动静，您可别把我撇下？」

欧伯佐恩起身出了屋，再没说一句话，因为他知道，库奇尼一起身便是个信号：不但当天的正事已了，而且这伙人该领饷了。

===Original===

Every gang-leader attended upon Mr. Oberzohn once a week with his payroll, and it was usually the custom for the Herr Doktor to bring his cashbox into the room and extract sufficient to liquidate his indebtedness to the leader. It was a big box, and on payday, as this was, filled to the top with banknotes and Treasury bills. He brought it back now, put it on the table, consulted the little slip that Cuccini offered to him, and, taking out a pad of notes, fastened about by a rubber band, he wetted his finger and thumb.

===Chinese===

每个帮派头目每礼拜都要揣着工饷名册来见欧伯佐恩先生一回；惯常的规矩是，博士先生把钱箱提进屋来，取出足够的数目，清偿他欠下该头目的账。那是只大箱子，逢着发饷的日子——眼下正是——里头的钞票和国库券直满到箱口。这会儿他把箱子提了回来，放在桌上，照库奇尼递来的小纸条核对，取出一沓用橡皮筋箍着的钞票，蘸湿了拇指和食指。

===Original===

“You needn’t count them,” said Cuccini. “We’ll take the lot.”

The doctor turned to see that Cuccini was carelessly holding a gun in his hand.

===Chinese===

「您就不用数了。」库奇尼说。「我们整箱端走。」

博士一回头，只见库奇尼手里漫不经心地握着一支枪。

===Original===

“The fact is, doctor,” said Cuccini coolly, “we’ve seen the red light, and if we don’t skip now, while the skipping’s good, there’s going to be no place we can stay comfortable in this little island, and I guess we’ll follow Gurther.”

One glance the doctor gave at the pistol and then he resumed his counting, as though nothing had happened.

“Twenty, thirty, forty, fifty﻿…”

===Chinese===

「实情是，博士，」库奇尼冷冷地说，「咱们都瞧见红灯亮了。趁这会儿还溜得动不赶紧开溜，往后这座小岛上就再没有咱们待得安生的地界了。我琢磨着，我们就步格瑟的后尘吧。」

博士朝那支手枪瞥了一眼，便接着数他的钱，仿佛什么都没有发生。

「二十、三十、四十、五十……」

===Original===

“Now quit that,” said Cuccini roughly. “I tell you, you needn’t count.”

“My friend, I prefer to know what I am going to lose. It is a pardonable piece of curiosity.”

He raised his hand to the wall, where a length of cord hung, and pulled at it gently, without taking his eyes from the banknotes.

“What are you doing? Put up your hands!” hissed Cuccini.

===Chinese===

「快别数了。」库奇尼粗声说。「我跟您说了，用不着数。」

「我的朋友，我乐意知道自己将要损失多少。这点好奇心，情有可原。」

他抬手伸向墙上——那儿垂着一截绳索——轻轻拉了拉，眼睛却始终不离那些钞票。

「您干什么？举起手来！」库奇尼嘶声道。

===Original===

“Shoot, I beg.” Oberzohn threw a pad of notes on the table. “There is your pay.” He slammed down the lid of the box. “Now you shall go, if you *can* go! Do you hear them?” He raised his hand, and to the strained ears of the men came a gentle rustling sound from the passage outside as though somebody were dragging a piece of parchment along the floor. “Do you hear? You shall go if you can,” said the doctor again, with amazing calmness.

===Chinese===

「请开枪，我恳求。」欧伯佐恩把一沓钞票摔在桌上。「这是你们的饷钱。」他砰地扣上箱盖。「现在请走吧，要是你们*走得出*这扇门的话！听见了吗？」他抬起手来；那几个人屏息侧耳，只听得外面的走廊里传来一阵轻轻的窸窣声，仿佛有谁拖着一整张羊皮纸擦地而过。「听见了吗？走得出就请走。」博士又说了一遍，平静得惊人。

===Original===

“The snakes!” breathed Cuccini, going white, and the hand that held the pistol shook.

“Shoot them, my friend,” sneered Oberzohn. “If you see them, shoot them. But you will not see them, my brave man. They will be﻿—where? No eyes shall see them come or go. They may lie behind a picture, they may wait until the door is opened, and then﻿ ﻿… !”

Cuccini’s mouth was dry.

===Chinese===

「蛇！」库奇尼倒吸一口凉气，脸都白了，握枪的手直打颤。

「朝它们开枪呀，我的朋友。」欧伯佐恩讥笑道。「瞧见了就开枪。可你们瞧不见它们的，我的勇士。它们会待在——哪儿呢？任何眼睛都休想看见它们来、看见它们去。它们兴许趴在一幅画背后，兴许一直候到房门打开，那时候……！」

库奇尼嘴里发干。

===Original===

“Call ’em off, doctor,” he said tremulously.

“Your gun﻿—on the table.”

Still the rustling sound was audible. Cuccini hesitated for a second, then obeyed, and took up the notes.

The other three men were huddled together by the fireplace, the picture of fear.

===Chinese===

「把它们叫回去吧，博士。」他颤声说。

「你的枪——搁到桌上。」

那窸窣声仍旧听得见。库奇尼迟疑了一秒，还是照办了，拿起了钞票。

另外三个人缩作一团挤在壁炉边，活脱脱一幅恐惧的写照。

===Original===

“Don’t open the door, doc,” said Cuccini, but Oberzohn had already gripped the handle and turned it.

They heard another door open and the click of the passage light as it had come on. Then he returned.

“If you go now, I shall not wish to see you again. Am I not a man to whom all secrets are known? You are well aware!”

===Chinese===

「别开门，博士。」库奇尼说，可欧伯佐恩早已攥住门把，拧了开来。

他们听见另一扇门开了，又听见走廊的灯咔哒一声亮起。随后他回来了。

「你们这就走的话，我就不想再见你们了。我难道不是个洞悉一切机密的人么？你们是深知的！」

===Original===

Cuccini looked from the doctor to the door.

“Want us to go?” he asked, troubled.

Oberzohn shrugged.

“As you wish! It was my desire that you should stay with me tonight﻿—there is big work and big money for all of you.”

===Chinese===

库奇尼的眼睛从博士身上瞟到门上。

「您是要我们走？」他问，心里七上八下。

欧伯佐恩耸了耸肩。

「悉听尊便！我本是有意让你们今晚留在我这儿的——有大事要做，有大钱可赚，人人有份。」

===Original===

The men were looking at one another uneasily.

“How long do you want us to stay?” asked Cuccini.

“Tonight only; if you would not prefer﻿…”

===Chinese===

那几个人面面相觑，坐立不安。

「您要我们留多久？」库奇尼问。

「只今夜一夜；倘若你们不更愿意……」

===Original===

Tonight would come the crisis. Oberzohn had realized this since the day dawned for him.

“We’ll stay﻿—where do we sleep?”

For answer Oberzohn beckoned them from the room and they followed him into the laboratory. In the wall that faced them was a heavy iron door that opened into a concrete storehouse, where he kept various odds and ends of equipment, oil and spirit for his cars, and the little gas engine that worked a small dynamo in the laboratory and gave him, if necessary, a lighting plant independent of outside current.

===Chinese===

危机就在今夜要来了。打今天破晓起，欧伯佐恩便明白了这一点。

「我们留下——睡哪儿？」

作为回答，欧伯佐恩招手让他们出屋，领着他们进了实验室。迎面的墙上有一扇厚重的铁门，通向一间水泥库房；他在那儿存放着各式零碎器材、给汽车备的机油和酒精，还有那台小煤气机——它带动实验室里的小发电机，必要时还能供给他一套不依赖外电的照明设备。

===Original===

There were three long windows heavily barred and placed just under the ceiling.

“Looks like the condemned cell to me,” grumbled Cuccini suspiciously.

“Are the bolts on the inside of a condemned cell?” asked Oberzohn. “Does the good warden give you the key as I give you?”

===Chinese===

库房有三扇长窗，装着粗重的铁栏，紧贴着天花板开设。

「依我看，这倒像间死囚牢。」库奇尼疑神疑鬼地嘟囔。

「死囚牢的门闩，是闩在里头的么？」欧伯佐恩问。「慈善的狱监会把钥匙交给你们，像我这样交给你们么？」

===Original===

Cuccini took the key.

“All right,” he said ungraciously, “there are plenty of blankets here, boys﻿—I guess you want us where the police won’t look, eh?”

“That is my intention,” replied the doctor.

Dr. Oberzohn closed the door on them and reentered his study, his big mouth twitching with amusement. He pulled the cord again and closed the ventilator he had opened. It was only a few days before that he had discovered that there were dried leaves in the ventilator shaft, and that the opening of the inlet made them rustle, disturbingly for a man who was engaged in a profound study of the lesser known, and therefore the more highly cultured, philosophers.

===Chinese===

库奇尼接过了钥匙。

「行吧。」他没好气地说。「这儿毯子有的是，弟兄们——我琢磨，您是想把我们搁在警察不会来搜的地方，嗯？」

「我正是这个用意。」博士答道。

欧伯佐恩博士把他们关在门里，自己回到书房，那张大嘴角挂着得趣的抽动。他又拉了拉那截绳索，把方才敞开的通风口关上了。就在几天之前他才发现，通风井里积着些枯叶，进风口一开，枯叶便窸窣作响——对一个正潜心研读那些较少人知、因而也更见高深修养的哲学家们的人来说，这声响着实扰人。

## XXXI The Things in the Box / 三十一 箱中之物

===Original===

He heard the soft purr of engines, and, looking through the hall window, saw the dim lights of the car approaching the house, and turned out the hall lamp. There he waited in the darkness, till the door of the limousine opened and Gurther jumped out. "I respectfully report that it is done, Herr Doktor," he said.

===Chinese===

他听见发动机轻柔的嗡鸣，透过门厅的窗子望出去，看见汽车昏暗的灯光正朝宅子驶近，便熄了门厅的灯。他就在黑暗里等候，直到那辆豪华轿车的车门打开，格瑟跳了下来。「卑职谨报博士先生：事情办妥了。」他说。

===Original===

Oberzohn nodded.

"The woman of Newton—where is she?"

"She is inside. Is it your wish that I should bring her? She was very troublesome, Herr Doktor, and I had to use the needle."

"Bring her in—you!" He barked to the chauffeur. "Help our friend."

===Chinese===

欧伯佐恩点了点头。

「牛顿的那个女人——她在哪儿？」

「她在里边。可要我把她带来？她非常不驯，博士先生，我只好动了针。」

「把她抬进来——你！」他朝司机喝道。「帮咱们这位朋友一把。」

===Original===

Together they lifted the unconscious girl, but carried her no farther than the steps. At this point Oberzohn decided that she must return to the prison. First they sent the chauffeur away; the car was garaged at New Cross (it was one of Oberzohn's three London depots), where the man also lived. After he had gone, they carried Joan between them to the factory, taking what, to Gurther, seemed an unnecessarily circuitous route. If it was unnecessary, it was at least expedient, for the nearest way to the factory led past the yawning hole that the doctor had dug with such labour.

===Chinese===

两人合力抬起那失去知觉的姑娘，却只抬到台阶跟前为止。到了这地步，欧伯佐恩决定她必须送回那座牢房。他们先打发司机走：车要停进新克罗斯的车库（那是欧伯佐恩在伦敦的三处车场之一），那司机也住在那儿。他去之后，两人便一边一个架着琼往厂房走，走的路在格瑟看来绕得毫无必要。倘若果真不必绕，至少也算得上权宜，因为通往厂房最近的那条道，偏偏要经过博士费了好大力气掘出的那个张着大口的窟窿。

===Original===

There was no mistaking Oberzohn's arrival this time. The trap went up with a thud, and Mirabelle listened, with a quickly beating heart, to the sound of feet coming down the stone stairs. There were two people, and they were walking heavily. Somehow she knew before she saw their burden that it was Joan. She was in evening dress, her face as white as chalk and her eyes closed; the girl thought she was dead when she saw them lay her on the bed.

===Chinese===
